Collapsed nodes **fan their CONNECTED handles** out vertically around the node centre at a tight 11px spacing (`spreadTop()` in [CaNode.tsx](src/modeler/vpl/CaNode.tsx)) so the user can still tell which wire lands on which port without expanding; UNconnected handles stay stacked at `top: 50%` (they're only drag targets). The connected set is read from the connected-handles pub/sub (which now carries output handles too). Because the spread shifts handle positions WITHOUT a node-size change, CaNode calls `useUpdateNodeInternals(id)` whenever it's collapsed and the connected set (or collapse state) changes — otherwise React Flow wouldn't re-measure handle bounds and edges would render to stale positions. `isConnectingGlobal` triggers hover-to-uncollapse. The hover-expand is **disambiguation only** (`onMouseEnter`): it force-expands the collapsed node only when the COMPATIBLE side has >1 category-matching port (inputs when the drag origin is an output, outputs when it's an input). A single compatible port → no expand (releasing on the collapsed node lands on it directly), so approaching e.g. a one-input action node with a wire no longer pops it open.
- **Config-driven port-set changes re-measure handle bounds** ([CaNode.tsx](src/modeler/vpl/CaNode.tsx)): a node whose visible ports change via `hiddenPorts`/dynamic-port config — Vector Op's `op` (add `[a,b]` → scale `[a,s]`, or add → dot swapping the `result`→`value` OUTPUT), Get Agent Position's `mode`, Switch/Sequence dynamic ports — can keep the node HEIGHT the same, so React Flow's ResizeObserver never fires and the newly-shown handle keeps STALE/absent bounds → it silently **rejects connections** until something else forces a remeasure (the reported "Vector Op input port sometimes won't accept input" bug — H3-confirmed by the `vectorop-input-bug` workflow: NOT a validation issue, the connection paths resolve the ports fine). Fixed by a `useEffect` keyed on the visible port-id signature (`inputPorts`+`outputPorts` ids) that calls `updateNodeInternals(id)` on every port-set change — one place covering every config-driven port change (distinct from the collapsed-fan-out effect above, which handles the connected-set case).
- Group node RMB passthrough: CSS `:global(.react-flow__node-groupNode) { pointer-events: none !important; }` with `[data-drag-handle]` re-enabled
- Context menu: pane menu uses hover submenu (`.contextSubmenuTrigger` > `.contextSubmenu`); paste uses `pasteFlowPos` ref for right-click position
- ReactFlowProvider lifted to ModelerView (not inside GraphEditor) so NodeExplorer can access useReactFlow/useStore
- Simulator overlays: ALL overlay elements on the canvas (stats, transport bar, viewer bar, zoom controls, panel expand buttons) MUST have `data-sim-overlay` attribute. Mousedown handler sets `canvasBrushActive` flag; mousemove brush painting checks this flag to prevent accidental painting when interacting with overlays.
- Hide React Flow's persistent selection rect: CSS `:global(.react-flow__nodesselection-rect) { display: none !important; }`
- Groups use React Flow's native `parentId` — auto-resize requires manual bounding box computation in `handleNodesChange`
- Use `NodeResizer` for resizable nodes (comments, groups) — CSS `resize: both` conflicts with React Flow drag. NodeResizer updates `node.measured.width/height` and top-level `node.width/height` on resize end — NOT `node.style.width/height`. To persist across save/load: (1) `toRFNodes` seeds `rfNode.style = { width, height }` from `data` for the initial render, (2) the resizer's `onResizeEnd` callback writes back to `data` via `updateNodeData`, and (3) `toGraphNodes` reads `measured.width ?? node.width ?? style.width` (measured wins) when serializing. Without all three, mid-session resizes are lost on save.
- MacroNode, MacroInputNode, MacroOutputNode are hidden from Add Node menu via `HIDDEN_FROM_MENU` set
- Undo/redo: `graphHistory.ts` module-level undo/redo stacks (max 50 snapshots). Ctrl+Z undo, Ctrl+Shift+Z / Ctrl+Y redo. Snapshot pushed BEFORE each mutation. History cleared on scope change.
- `isMultiOutput()` helper in compile.ts replaces raw `MULTI_OUTPUT_TYPES.has()` — also checks `getModelAttribute` with `isColorAttr` config
- CaNode config: NEVER call `updateConfig()` twice in sequence — second call uses stale `nodeData.config`, losing the first update. Instead, build the merged config object and call `updateNodeData(id, { ...nodeData, config: newConfig })` once.
- CSS gotcha: `flex: 1` on buttons inside flex-column containers causes them to stretch vertically. Remove `flex: 1` from buttons that should have fixed height.
- Nullish coalescing: never mix `??` with `||` or comparison operators without explicit parens — Babel/esbuild will warn or error.
- Simulator lifecycle: SimulatorView is always-mounted (wrapped in `display:none` div when not visible). Simulation auto-pauses when leaving the tab. Canvas redraws via `requestAnimationFrame` when `visible` transitions to true. The `useEffect([model, compileModel])` fires on every model change (even while hidden), handling full reinit or soft recompile as appropriate. When `model.indicators` changes during soft recompile, an `updateIndicators` message is sent to the worker alongside the `recompile` message.
- Simulator save integration: FileMenu dispatches `genesis-capture-sim-state` CustomEvent with `detail.resolve` callback. SimulatorView captures worker state via `getState` and calls `resolve()` after `setSimulationState()`. FileMenu `await`s the Promise before serializing. 5-second safety timeout.
- Copy/paste: Ctrl+C/V/X + context menu. Module-level `clipboard` variable, strips macroInput/macroOutput, remaps IDs
- Group paste: parentId must be remapped to new IDs, children keep relative positions, groups sorted before children
- React StrictMode double-mount: effects run mount→cleanup→mount in dev. When terminating resources (Web Workers), always null out the ref (`workerRef.current = null`) after `.terminate()` so the second mount detects it needs a fresh init instead of reusing a dead reference.
- Indicator values use a ref (`indicatorValuesRef`) not React state — avoids extra re-renders on every worker step message. The existing `setGeneration` re-render reads the ref naturally.
- Linked indicator aggregation is always post-loop (not in-loop) to avoid async mode single-buffer corruption where mid-loop reads see a mix of old and new cell values.
- Neighborhood tags: `Neighborhood.tags?: Record<number, string>` maps coord index to tag name. Tags are optional per-cell labels for neighbor positions. **Tag keys are coord-array INDICES** — any code that removes coords MUST remap surviving tags or later tags silently shift onto the wrong cells. `toggleCellsChanges` in [NeighborhoodsPanelContent.tsx](src/modeler/panels/NeighborhoodsPanelContent.tsx) is the single batch-toggle helper that does this (the one-cell Point click routes through it too).
- Neighborhood shape tools (Point/Circle/Ring/Line in the grid editor): tool + paint mode (mark/unmark/toggle) + symmetry toggles (**h/v/d/d2** — horizontal, vertical, main-diagonal `r↔c`, anti-diagonal `r,c→-c,-r`; any three generate the full 8-fold D4) + staged clicks + hover live in component state; pure helpers `annulusCells` (euclidean disc/ring, clipped to margin box) / `lineCells` (integer Bresenham) / `shapeCells` / `expandSymmetry` (mirror closure over the enabled mirrors, mirrors of in-grid cells stay in-grid so no clipping) compute the affected set; everything applies through `applyCellsChanges(nbh, cells, mode)` as ONE updateNeighborhood (`cellEffect` decides add/remove/none per covered cell; a covered center drives `includeCentralCell` by the same mode; tag-index remap on removal lives here). Hover preview is outcome-split: `.gridCellPreviewAdd` (accent tint — visible on empty), `.gridCellPreviewRemove` (dim + warm outline — visible on filled), `.gridCellPreview` (covered no-op, faint outline); staged anchors `.gridCellStaged`; right-click cancels staging (falls through to the tag right-click only when nothing is staged). Center cell fills like a normal cell with a persistent inset ring (`.gridCellCenter`/`.gridCellCenterIncluded`).
- `inputToSources` (plural) map in compile.ts: collects ALL edges targeting the same value port. Used for multi-connection `isArray` ports on Aggregate node.
- Connection validation: `isValidConnection` allows multiple edges to the same target handle when the target port has `isArray: true`.
- Switch node dynamic ports: case output ports generated from `caseCount` + `case_N_value` config keys, similar to macro dynamic ports.
- Context menu: clamped to viewport bounds via `useLayoutEffect` + ref measurement after render. Initial render with `visibility: hidden`.
- Modeler PanelShell: resizable via drag handle on right edge (200-600px range). Pattern matches simulator right panel.
- Group shrink-to-fit: `resizeGroupsToFit(nds, allowShrink)` runs on graph load with `allowShrink=true`. Prevents stale bloated groups.
- Input drag fix: `stopDrag` callback checks `e.button === 0` (LMB only) to allow RMB pan through nodes. `stopAll` stops all buttons (for double-click). Body div uses `onDoubleClick={stopAll}` to prevent collapse; inline widgets use both `onMouseDown={stopDrag}` and `onDoubleClick={stopAll}`.
- Compiler: in `compileFlowChain`, EVERY `varName()` call MUST be preceded by `compileValueNode(source.nodeId)` to ensure the value variable is declared. This applies to ALL flow node handlers (conditional, loop, switch, regular). Missing this causes undefined variables at runtime.
- Model element cleanup: `ModelContext.tsx` reducer uses `patchAllNodes()` / `clearDeletedId()` helpers to update node configs when attributes/neighborhoods/mappings/indicators are deleted. Tag option deletion remaps indices in getConstant, switch, and setAttribute nodes. Always scan both `graphNodes` and `macroDefs[*].nodes`.
- Graph nodes are heterogeneous: comment nodes (`type: 'commentNode'`) have `data: { text }` and group nodes have `data: { label, width, height, nodeType: 'group', config: {} }`. Any code iterating `model.graphNodes` must guard against `n.data.config` being undefined (e.g., `patchNodes` in ModelContext).
- **Comment node editing** ([CommentNodeComponent.tsx](src/modeler/vpl/CommentNodeComponent.tsx)): the textarea is INERT (`readOnly`, `pointer-events: none`) until the user **double-clicks** to edit — only then does it get the `nodrag` class so native click-drag and shift-click text selection work (without `nodrag`, React Flow's drag swallows the pointer and selection is impossible). `onBlur` exits edit mode. A NEW comment (Add Comment from the context menu) is created `selected: true` with `data.autoEdit: true`; CaNode's effect focuses + select-all's the textarea and clears the one-shot flag via `updateNodeData(autoEdit: undefined)` so it never persists into a saved graph.
- Switch node: two modes (`conditions` = user-wired bool inputs per case; `value` = comparison ops per case with int/float/tag/**neighborIndex** types). `firstMatchOnly` toggle: true = if/else-if chain, false = independent if blocks with `_sw{id}` guard variable. Tag mode uses equality against tag index; int/float mode uses configurable comparison op (==,!=,>,<,>=,<=). **`neighborIndex` value type** (CaNode dynamic ports): the `value` port and each `case_N_val` port are WIRED (no inline widget — there's no packed-NI editor), equality-only (no op dropdown). Needs NO compiler change — it routes through the existing non-tag `value`-mode path: JS emits `===`, WASM compares as F64 (exact for all i32 NIs), WebGPU casts to i32. Wire NI sources (Get All Neighbor Indexes → Get Array Element, Neighbor Index From Tag/Offset, Get Facing Orientation, …) into the cases.
- Compare node (`statement`): a `compareType` config (`numerical` default / `bool` / `tag` / `neighborIndex`) swaps the inline operand widgets (CaNode extends the same dynamic `effectiveWidget` swap used by setAttribute-family: bool→`InlineBoolSelect`, tag→`InlineTagSelect` from the node's own `tagAttributeId`, neighborIndex→no widget/wired, numerical→number). Non-numerical types restrict the op dropdown to ==/!=, and changing the type resets `_port_x`/`_port_y`/`_port_y2` to type defaults. No emit change for numerical/bool/tag (`getInlineValue`/`parseInlineNum` already coerce `true`/`false`→1/0 and tag-index strings→numbers, and small ints are exact in f64/f32); neighborIndex adds an i32-equality branch in WebGPU only (JS `===` and WASM F64 are already exact for NIs). `nodeValidation` requires `tagAttributeId` when `compareType==='tag'`. Collapsed-node title formats the inline operands by `compareType` (`fmtOperand` in CaNode's collapsed block): tag→the `tagOptions` NAME (not the index), bool→`True`/`False` — mirrors Get Constant's collapsed resolution.
- PanelShell `side` prop: `'left'` (default) puts resize handle on right edge; `'right'` puts it on left edge with inverted drag math. NodeExplorer uses `side="right"`. Simulator left panel has its own resize handle (`.leftPanelResizeHandle`).
- Show Code: `buildFullCode()` in SimulatorView concatenates step + all inputColor + all outputMapping functions with section headers. Uses mapping names for readability.
- `inlineWidget: 'number'` ports on `setAttribute` / `updateAttribute` / `setNeighborhoodAttribute` / `setNeighborAttributeByIndex` / **`setAgentAttribute` / `setAgentsAttribute`** (the by-id agent writers) can carry `'true'`/`'false'` strings in config: CaNode dynamically swaps the rendered widget to a bool `<select>` (or a tag `<select>` from the picked attribute's `tagOptions`) when the chosen attribute is bool/tag, but the static port def stays `'number'`. The swap membership is a **hardcoded nodeType OR-chain in [CaNode.tsx](src/modeler/vpl/CaNode.tsx)** (~`effectiveWidget`, keyed on `port.id === 'value'`) — add a node type there to give it the type-adaptive value widget; the target attribute resolves via the graph-aware `ownAttrList` (= `model.agentAttributes` on the Agents graph, so the agent-only by-id writers resolve their AGENT attribute automatically). A neighborIndex/color/vector attribute falls to the `else → undefined` arm (wired-only, no widget). `getInlineValue` must map `'true'→'1'` / `'false'→'0'` BEFORE `parseFloat` (matching `parseInlineNum`) — otherwise NaN falls back to `defaultValue '0'` and every model that writes a bool attr via the inline widget breaks on ALL THREE targets (JS/WASM/WebGPU all chain through `getInlineValue` / the per-target `getInlineNum` clones, which key off the STATIC `'number'` inlineWidget and already accept `'true'`/`'false'`/tag-index — so the swap is **editor-only, no compiler change**). `setAgentsAttribute`'s array `agents` port is untouched (the swap only targets `value`).
- Inline `<input type="number">` widgets MUST set `step="any" lang="en" inputMode="decimal"`. Default `step="1"` makes decimals step-invalid; on some Chromium/locale combinations `.value` returns `""` for `0.2`-style entries → `getInlineValue` falls back to `defaultValue '0'` → "always-true" comparison bug (Compare's `>= 0.2` was the canonical reproducer).
- `onConnectEnd` signature in xyflow v12 is `(event, connectionState)`. `connectionState.toHandle == null` is the canonical "released not on a port" signal — works for releases on the pane AND on a node body. If onConnectEnd sets context-menu state (e.g., the connection-drop compatible-nodes menu), beware: the same LMB-up that fires onConnectEnd ALSO fires the editor wrapper's synthesized `click`, which closes the menu the moment it opens. Use a one-shot `suppressNextEditorClickRef` flag set in onConnectEnd and consumed by the wrapper's onClick.
- **Searchable connection-drop menu** (drag a wire onto empty canvas → compatible-nodes menu): a focused `.contextSearch` input + a persistent keyboard selection (`dropMenuSelIdx`) over the flat `dropMenuItems` list (Reroute first when origin is an output, then compatible nodes grouped by category). Same quick-add UX as the Palette: typing re-anchors the selection to the best LABEL match (prefix→substring→0), ↑/↓ wraps, mouse-move syncs, Enter → `commitDropMenuItem` (→ `addRerouteAndConnect` / `addNodeAndConnect`), Esc closes. The compatibility filter (`HIDDEN_FROM_DROP_MENU`, step/init singletons, `isNodeAvailable`, `nodeHasCompatiblePort`) is unchanged — search is layered on top. Items are flat with interleaved `.contextCategory` headers (flat index drives ↑/↓). **This same search/keyboard UI is now shared with the pane quick-add menu** (Spacebar / blank-canvas right-click) via the `renderQuickAddSearch` helper and the dual-target `dropMenuItems` / `commitDropMenuItem` — see "Unified add-node menu" above; the connection-drop branch just adds a `.contextTitle` and passes a wire origin (Reroute + compatibility filter), the pane branch passes no origin. DEV-only `window.__openConnectionDropMenu(origin,x,y)` test hook exists because React Flow's connection drag ignores synthetic pointer events (same limitation as box-select — see the synthetic-event gotchas).
- A new flow-control node with DYNAMIC flow outputs (Switch-style `case_N`, Sequence-style `then_N`) requires edits in ALL of: NodeTypeDef (+ defaultConfig counter), CaNode dynamic-port derivation + +/- UI, [effectivePorts.ts](src/modeler/vpl/effectivePorts.ts) (consumed by panel-drag + connection-drop helpers), `compile.ts` × 2 sites (compileInnerFlow + compileFlowChain), `wasm/compile.ts` × 2 sites (compileFlowChain + visitFlow), `webgpu/compile.ts` × 3 sites (preEmitValueNodes + compileFlowChain + analyzeAlwaysWritten), and `sinkAnalysis.ts` if the node belongs in `TRANSPARENT_FLOW_TYPES`.
- **Flow pass-through port (`next` — NEXT/DONE)**: every flow-input node except `sequence` + macro boundaries/instances has a static `next` flow OUTPUT in its def.ports (label NEXT on action nodes — their only output; label DONE on conditional/loop/forEachInArray/switch — rendered FIRST/top among outputs, aligned with the flow input so chains stay horizontal; the def lists `next` before the branch ports, and a CaNode+effectivePorts reorder re-hoists it to the FRONT above switch's dynamic `case_N`). Port order is purely visual — the compilers look ports up by id, so reordering is free. Semantics: targets run right after the node / after the whole construct, at the SAME scope, before the parent port's next sibling (depth-first, UE-style); `A.next → B` compiles byte-identically to `parent → [A, B]` fan-out on all three targets. Hooks (see [docs/IMPACT_MAP_FLOW_PASSTHROUGH.md](docs/IMPACT_MAP_FLOW_PASSTHROUGH.md)): each compiler's `compileFlowChain` walks `next` at the end of the per-target dispatch (+ the switch `caseCount===0` `continue` paths in WASM/WebGPU); `sinkAnalysis`/`volatileHoist` walk `next` at parentScope (transparent — no new scope); `asyncWriteHazard` walks it with prefix = entry ∪ own-writes (actions) / entry ∪ branch-body writes (control nodes) — its `writesInSubtree` covers next-subtrees automatically via the `${nodeId}:` prefix scan; WASM `visitFlow` + WebGPU `preEmitValueNodes` recurse into `next` (safe for forEach — the next chain is OUTSIDE the loop); WebGPU `analyzeAlwaysWritten` unions `next` for every target type (a loop's body gives no guarantee but its DONE chain always runs). A forEach with NO array wired skips body AND DONE (`continue` on both compilers). Any new flow-walk MUST handle `next` or chained nodes silently vanish from that analysis.
- **Loop `index` output (all compile surfaces)**: the Loop node exposes its 0-based iteration counter as an `index` value output — body-only scope, exactly like ForEachInArray's `element`/`index`. Per-surface wiring: **JS** `varName` maps `loop.index` → the `_li<id>` for-counter + the loop flow-emit activates the forEach-style body-emit context (`findElementDependents(node.id, ['index'])`); **sinkAnalysis** registers `loop` in `elementDependentsByForEach` and `hoistPastLoops` now consults it for `loopBody` scopes too (index-dependents stay pinned inside the loop; everything else still hoists past); **loopInvariant** `NEVER_INVARIANT` includes `loop` (its Count input is typically invariant, so the composite rule would otherwise hoist index consumers to the function preamble where `_li` is undefined); **WASM** caches the counter local via `setCachedPort(node.id, 'index')` inside the loop (mirrors forEach); **WebGPU** `loopIndexRef` mints the counter's WGSL name ON DEMAND (cached on the node's `index` port) so consumers compiled during `preEmitValueNodes` and the later `for (var …)` declaration agree — their `let`s flush inside the loop via the loopBody sink scope; **agentWasm/agentWebgpu** use a `loopStack` frame (mirrors `forEachStack`), seed `loop` in `computeVolatile` (consumers re-emit per iteration) and list it in `AGENT_VALUE_NO_HOIST`; **overseer** `refFor` resolves `_l<id>` with a `loopScopes` in-body guard (outside use = compile error). Index used outside the BODY chain is undefined-behaviour parity with forEach (JS ReferenceError / WGSL unknown identifier). **Range mode** (`config.mode: 'count' | 'range'`, default `'count'`): `hiddenPorts` swaps the Count port for `from`/`to` integer inputs, and the counter runs **From..To INCLUSIVE** (ascending; From > To = zero iterations) on every surface — JS `for (let _li = (from)|0, _liEnd = (to)|0; _li <= _liEnd; …)` (the `|0` matches the WASM/WebGPU i32 truncation), WASM `li = from` + exit-on-`OP_I32_GT_S` (count keeps `GE_S`), WGSL `for (var li = from; li <= to; …)`, agent compilers + overseer mirror it. COUNT-mode byte-identity is guarded by resolve-before-alloc ordering (WASM: the bound ref resolves BEFORE `li`/`lc` are allocated; agentWebgpu: the bound resolves before `fresh('lpI')` mints the counter — a lazily-compiled bound source mints its own locals/names, so swapping that order changes existing modules). Verified: [scripts/test-loop-index.mjs](scripts/test-loop-index.mjs) (JS+WASM runtime values + bit-identity for BOTH modes — count 25/7, range 3..7=25 + empty 5..2 skipped + −2..2=5; WGSL counter-inside-loop + inclusive headers; overseer driver runs log 0..2 and 2..4; agent WebGPU shader both modes) + the permanent `[synthetic] Loop index` entry in [scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs](scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs) (now includes a range loop, acc3 += 2..4); check-compile-identity: all 23 library models byte-identical (loops without index consumers / in count mode emit verbatim pre-change output on every surface).
- Worker `self.onmessage` is sync — `await` directly inside a `case` body is a syntax error. Wrap async work in `void (async () => { ... })()` IIFE (same pattern as `setUseWasm`), OR keep the await out of the message handler entirely.
- Module-level `let` exports from `graphState.ts` are LIVE bindings — reading the imported identifier inside a callback always sees the current value at call time (no ref needed). Used for `compatibleHandlesForDrag` and `currentModelElementDrag`. Safer than refs when the consumer is a pure function or non-React module.
- Panel-drag drop on a port (Attribute / Neighborhood / Mapping → canvas): `onPaletteDrop` in GraphEditor.tsx computes the snap target via `findNearestCompatibleHandle` (DOM query at the drop point), then calls `resolveDropCandidates(payload, snap)` to filter `RELATED_NODES` by port-compatibility. When `resolved.length === 1`, the menu is skipped and the node is auto-created via `addNodeAndConnect`. Position is computed by `computeSnapPosition` (heuristic: ~200px estimated new-node width, ~24px per port row, ~30px gap on the correct side), then `scheduleSnapRefinement` schedules a one-shot RAF that measures the actual port screen positions via `getPortScreenCentre` and applies a precise delta. Both single-option and multi-option (menu click) paths share the same snap+refine pair. `addNodeAtPosition` / `addNodeAndConnect` now return the new node id (`string | null`) instead of `boolean` so the RAF refinement can locate the just-added node — existing callers that only checked truthiness still work.
- Accessor CSE: all three compilers run [accessorCSE.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/accessorCSE.ts)'s `canonicalizeAccessorEdges` before sink analysis to dedup pure value-producing nodes (GetCellAttribute, GetNeighborsAttribute, arithmetic over them, etc.) that share a structural purity key. Sync-mode only (async-mode global no-op — single buffer means a read can change after an intervening write). See the "Accessor CSE" major section for purity rules and per-target wiring.
- **`valueSwitch` is a dual-mode value/array relay.** `result = cond ? ifValue : elseValue` is scalar when fed scalars, but an **array** when BOTH branches are array producers (it just selects one of the two branch arrays — output shape = input shape, which `nodeType` alone can't express). The editor already permits the wiring (`isValidConnection` checks only NI dataType compatibility, which `'any'` passes; it does NOT enforce array→scalar shape). **All three targets needed work** (the initial "JS is free" read was wrong — caught by inspecting emitted code, not just "does it compile"): on the producer side JS's untyped ternary `_v = cond ? ifVar : elseVar` already holds the selected array, BUT on the **consumer** side `valueSwitch.result` is a scalar-typed port, so JS's isArray-input resolution WRAPPED the relayed array as `[_v]` (a 1-element array) — silently making `pickRandomNeighbor` pick element 0 instead of a random element. Fixed by `sourceYieldsArray()` (compile.ts) — the JS analogue of `producesArray` (port-based + array-kind-getVariable + recursive valueSwitch relay, memoised+cycle-guarded); it passes an array-relay source straight through instead of wrapping (mirrors the pre-existing array-kind-getVariable pass-through). WASM + WebGPU gate array-input resolution on a type-based `isArrayProducer(nodeType)` allowlist that excludes `valueSwitch`, so both used to reject e.g. `valueSwitch → pickRandomNeighbor.indexes` and fall back to JS. Fix = shared [arrayRelay.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/arrayRelay.ts) `makeProducesArray({isArrayProducer, inputToSource, nodeMap})` → `ctx.producesArray(node)` (memoised, cycle-guarded; a valueSwitch is an array relay iff both `ifValue`+`elseValue` sources recursively produce arrays). It REPLACES every *source-node* `isArrayProducer(...)` check (the `resolveInputArray` gate + aggregate/groupOperator/groupStatement/groupCounting/setNeighbor*ByIndex/markCellUpdated disambiguators + the pre-emit walks). `valueSwitch` is now dual-registered in BOTH `VALUE_NODE_EMITTERS` and `ARRAY_NODE_EMITTERS` (like `getVariable`): **WASM** array emit = zero-copy `OP_SELECT` of the two branches' scratch offset/len (both arrays already materialised — "both inputs always evaluate"); **WebGPU** array emit = copy loop into a fresh `var<function>` result array (WGSL can't alias). Dispatch routes by `ctx.producesArray`: WASM `emitValuesForScope` emits an array-relay instance at its sink scope via `compileArrayNode` (it's pure — NOT skipped like volatile getVariable); the scalar value emitters guard with `producesArray` (defensive). `compileArrayNode`'s scalar-input pre-resolution skips ports whose source `producesArray` (valueSwitch's `ifValue`/`elseValue` are scalar-typed ports relaying arrays — resolved via `resolveInputArray` inside the emitter instead). WebGPU additionally: `compileValueNode`'s isArray-port *multi-source-scalar* pre-resolution (the block that warms `getRandom.options` / `aggregate` scalar sources) must skip an array-relay valueSwitch — otherwise it `compileValueNode`s the relay and hits the scalar emitter (the probe caught exactly this as the defensive guard firing). **NI-sentinel intentionally NOT recursed** through valueSwitch (the `NI_ARRAY_PRODUCERS` empty/OOB fallback in `arrayElement`) — JS already uses the `0` fallback for a valueSwitch-relayed NI array, so leaving WASM/WebGPU type-based keeps all three in parity; `pickRandomNeighbor` always uses `INVALID_NI` regardless, so the canonical case is unaffected. **Editor discoverability:** `PortDef.arrayCapable` (set on valueSwitch's `ifValue`/`elseValue`/`result`) makes the compatible-nodes menu + panel-drag highlight OFFER Value Switch in array contexts, BOTH drag directions (drag an array output onto If/Else; drag from If/Else to find an array source). `portsCompatible` (GraphEditor.tsx) + its `shapesMate` mirror (modelElementDrag.ts) relax the array-source→scalar-target rejection when the *target* side is `arrayCapable` (threaded via `ConnectionOrigin.arrayCapable` / `getOriginPortInfo`). Suggestion-layer ONLY — `isValidConnection` + the compilers are untouched (they already permit/relay the wiring). See [docs/IMPACT_MAP_VALUESWITCH_ARRAY_RELAY.md](docs/IMPACT_MAP_VALUESWITCH_ARRAY_RELAY.md).

### Modeler UX (master-detail panels + graph fixes)
- **Detail editor in a SECOND left panel**: the Attributes / Neighborhoods / Mappings panels are master-detail. The LIST stays in the primary `PanelShell`; the selected item's editor renders in a separate `PanelShell` mounted between the primary panel and the graph (so the user never scrolls past the list to reach the editor). Selection is lifted out of each panel into `ModelerDetailContext` ([ModelerDetailContext.tsx](src/modeler/ModelerDetailContext.tsx)) — `selectedByPanel: Partial<Record<PanelId, string|null>>` owned by `ModelerView`, exposed via `useDetailSelection(panel)` (used like `useState`). Each panel component takes a `mode?: 'list'|'detail'` prop and renders only that half (the same component is mounted twice). Variegated is a single form (not split). `ModelerView` shows the detail panel only when `activePanel ∈ {properties,attributes,neighborhoods,mappings}` AND its selected item still resolves (so deleting an item / closing the detail panel's ear-tab clears the selection and hides it). Per-panel selection survives ActivityBar tab switches. **Neighborhoods was converted from index-based (`selectedIdx`) to id-based selection** to fit the shared id-keyed context. **Local Variables stays INSIDE the Attributes panel** (no separate tab): `VariablesPanelSection`'s LIST renders in the primary Attributes panel and its editor in the SAME shared detail panel. The Attributes panel's selection slot is therefore a **discriminated `attr:<id>` / `var:<id>` string** — selecting an attribute or a variable clears the other (one shared detail panel). `VariablesPanelSection` is a controlled child (`mode` + `selectedId` + `onSelect` props); `selectedItemName` in `ModelerView` parses the prefix to title the detail panel. **Indicators are master-detail too, hosted by the Properties panel** (same controlled-child pattern as Variables-in-Attributes): the `IndicatorsPanelSection` LIST renders in the primary Properties panel and the selected indicator's editor renders in the SAME shared detail panel. `'properties'` is therefore in `MASTER_DETAIL_PANELS`, and Properties owns an `indicator:<id>` selection slot (`useDetailSelection('properties')`) threaded into `IndicatorsPanelSection` as `mode`/`selectedId`/`onSelect`; `PropertiesPanelContent` early-returns the indicator editor in `mode==='detail'`. `selectedItemName` resolves the `indicator:` prefix for the `Edit:` title.
- **Centered panel titles + group-header affordance**: PanelShell `.title` and the simulator's `.panelTitle` are `flex:1; text-align:center` (each header holds only the title; tabs are absolute), so all modeler + simulator panel titles are centered. Group nodes use the header strip as the drag handle, so [GroupNodeComponent.module.css](src/modeler/vpl/GroupNodeComponent.module.css) `.header` now reads as a subtle title bar — pulled to the group's inner edges via negative margins, faint `--color-overlay-row` tint, a 1px `--color-border-muted` bottom line, rounded top corners — marking the distinct interaction zone without being flashy.
- **Groups stay behind their children when selected**: React Flow's `elevateNodesOnSelect` (default on) rewrites a selected node's inline `z-index` to a high value, which popped a selected group in front of its contained nodes and swallowed every click. Fix is one CSS rule in [GraphEditor.module.css](src/modeler/vpl/GraphEditor.module.css): `:global(.react-flow__node-groupNode){ z-index:-1 !important; }` — `!important` beats the inline style, pinning groups at the `-1` toRFNodes assigns, regardless of selection. Targets groups only; caNode selection elevation is unchanged. (Tradeoff: a child overlapping a group's resize handle wins hit-testing — already true for unselected groups, accepted.)
- **Context menu dismiss on ANY outside pointer-press**: the pane/node context menu only closed on a synthesized `click`, which never fires when the user instead starts a box-select (LMB drag) or pan (RMB drag) — so it lingered. A capture-phase `document` `pointerdown` listener in [GraphEditor.tsx](src/modeler/vpl/GraphEditor.tsx) closes the menu whenever one is open and the press lands outside `contextMenuRef`, WITHOUT `preventDefault`/`stopPropagation` (so the press still starts the drag). The inside-`contextMenuRef` guard lets a menu item's own `onClick` run before the menu unmounts. (Note: `Node` is shadowed by xyflow's `Node` type in this file — cast `e.target as globalThis.Node`.)
- **Name-entry dialog (replaces native `window.prompt`)**: [NameInputDialog.tsx](src/components/NameInputDialog.tsx) is a small in-app dialog reusing `SaveProjectDialog.module.css` — transparent backdrop (no screen dim = popover feel; click-outside cancels), card anchored/**centered on the click** via a viewport-clamped `useLayoutEffect`, focus + select-all so a rename is one keystroke. GraphEditor drives it through a tiny promise controller: `const [namePrompt, setNamePrompt]` + `promptName(opts) => Promise<string|null>`; the four naming handlers (`renameNode`, `createGroup`, `createEmptyGroup`, `createMacroFromSelection`) are now `async` and `await promptName(...)` (null = cancelled), capturing coords/selection/bbox BEFORE `setContextMenu(null)` + the await. **Two non-obvious gotchas**: (1) `contextMenu.x/y` are `.react-flow`-relative but the dialog is `position:fixed`, so the render block adds the wrapper's `getBoundingClientRect()` offset to `anchorX/anchorY` (without it the card lands ~activity-bar-width up-left of the click). (2) focus must run in `useEffect([pos])` AFTER positioning — focusing during the initial `visibility:hidden` (pre-measure) frame silently no-ops. Rename prefills the node's current label (`data.label ?? getNodeDef(type)?.label`) with `allowEmpty:true` (clearing reverts to the default label); creates prefill `'Group'`/`'New Macro'` with `allowEmpty:false` (OK disabled while blank). The group inline double-click rename is a separate, unchanged path.

### Modeler UX & node additions (v1.15)
- **Group node interaction model**: LMB-drag on the group's **header strip** moves the group (React Flow `dragHandle` restricted to `[data-drag-handle="true"]`); LMB-drag on the **body** box-selects inner caNodes (driven through RF's `userSelectionRect` / `userSelectionActive` store + `triggerNodeChanges` on diff); LMB-click on the body selects the group; RMB on the body pans the pane (extended the edge RMB-passthrough handler). Header label is a span; double-click swaps in a focused input (select-all) for rename, blur/Enter/Escape exits.
- **Nested groups translate with their parent**: `onNodeDragStart` collects drag members by geometric containment (node center inside the dragged group's rect) and NO LONGER skips `groupNode` members — so a smaller group nested inside a larger one moves with it. The nested group's own children are collected independently (their centers are inside the parent rect too), so arbitrarily-deep nesting moves in lock-step with no recursion. Groups remain non-hierarchical (no `parentId`) — membership stays purely geometric, matching how caNodes are already gathered.
- **Group box-select modifiers**: snapshot pre-selected ids in `onDown`, keep a running `appliedIds` set; at threshold crossing only `resetSelectedElements` for plain (no-modifier) mode. Shift = pre ∪ box, Ctrl/Meta = pre \ box, plain = box. The capture-phase listener lives on `document` (NOT the wrapper) so it sits ABOVE React's root in capture order — otherwise RF's `Pane.onPointerDownCapture` treats Shift as its selectionKey and finalizes the group into the selection via `getSelectionChanges(…, mutateItem=true)` before our `stopPropagation` can fire. A `boxFromGroupRef`-gated filter in `handleNodesChange` strips the group from the selection as defense-in-depth. Inner-node drag uses `member.startPos + totalDelta` (delta from drag-start group position) — NOT `nodesRef.current.position + per_tick_dx`, which reads stale positions on fast pointer-move bursts (faster than React renders) and leaves inner nodes lagging.
- **`getNodesInside` force-include gotcha (box-select grabbing the whole group)**: `getNodesInside` (xyflow/system, used by `updateBoxSelect`) sets `forceInitialRender = !node.internals.handleBounds` and returns such nodes REGARDLESS of the box rect. Right after loading a library model with many (often **collapsed**) nodes, handles aren't measured yet → a box-select inside a group selected the ENTIRE graph. Fix: after `getNodesInside`, re-verify each candidate's **actual on-screen rect** (`wrapper.querySelector('.react-flow__node[data-id=…]').getBoundingClientRect()`) against the drag rectangle in client coords, so only truly-intersected nodes are kept. Client-rect comparison is robust whether or not handles are measured (the DOM elements are already laid out); replicating getNodesInside's internal flow-coord math via `pointToRendererPoint`/`nodeToRect` was tried first and over-filtered, so the DOM-rect approach is intentional.
- **Panel → port snap + single-add**: dropping a model element (attribute / neighborhood / mapping / indicator) onto a compatible port spawns the new node aligned with that port. `findNearestCompatibleHandle` (DOM query at drop point) → `resolveDropCandidates` filters `RELATED_NODES` by port-compatibility; when exactly one fits, the menu is skipped and the node auto-creates. `computeSnapPosition` does heuristic placement, then `scheduleSnapRefinement` measures actual port screen positions via a one-shot RAF and applies a precise delta. `addNodeAtPosition` / `addNodeAndConnect` now return the new node id (`string | null`, was `boolean`) so the RAF can locate the just-added node.
- **Set Cell Looks** ([SetCellLooksNode](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/SetCellLooksNode.ts)) — the unified appearance node (merged the former `setColorViewer` + `setCellGlyph`; both retired from the registry, migrated on load by [setCellLooksMigration.ts](src/model/setCellLooksMigration.ts)). Config `{mappingId, useGlyph, setBackground, fallbackToGlyphColor}`. Static ports: `do`/`next`, cell color `r`/`g`/`b`, `glyph` (inlineWidget `glyph` = the common-glyph picker), glyph color `glyphR`/`glyphG`/`glyphB`; `hiddenPorts` hides the glyph ports in plain mode and the cell-color ports in glyph mode without a background. **Emit (all three targets, viewer-guarded via `_isV_`/`activeViewer`, sentinel-bypassed):** write the RGBA `colors` buffer with `r/g/b` when `!useGlyph || setBackground` (the macro/all-zoom appearance — identical to the old setColorViewer); write `glyphCodes[idx]` + packed `glyphColors[idx]` from `glyph`/`glyphR/G/B` when `useGlyph` (identical to the old setCellGlyph). So the background reuses the always-present `colors` buffer (no new buffer); the glyph overlay draws on top when zoomed in. Per-cell u32 glyph codepoint + packed RGB tint buffers are allocated only when `hasGlyphsInModel` finds a `setCellLooks` with `useGlyph` ([glyphsUsage.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/glyphsUsage.ts)). Simulator overlays glyphs after the colour blit via a pre-rasterised tile cache keyed by (codepoint, colour, size); zoom-gated (hidden below ~6 px/cell). **"Glyph color when zoomed out"** (`fallbackToGlyphColor`) is a pure render concern (no compiler change): below the glyph threshold the simulator blits a bitmap where each glyphed cell is recolored to its glyph color — one composited blit — when the active viewer is in the model-scanned `glyphFallbackRef` set (sentinel → all viewers). Unlocks per-cell orientation indicators (GetOrientation → Switch → arrow glyphs). Lockstepped JS/WASM/WebGPU; verified cross-target compile parity (plain / glyph+bg+fallback / glyph-only) + an Amphiphile load showing species colors + arrow glyphs. Migration sets old setColorViewer → `{useGlyph:false, setBackground:true}` and old setCellGlyph → `{useGlyph:true, setBackground:false, fallbackToGlyphColor:false}` (its R/G/B → glyphR/G/B, edge handles + `_port_*` remapped), preserving each old node's behaviour byte-for-byte. See [docs/IMPACT_MAP_SET_CELL_LOOKS.md](docs/IMPACT_MAP_SET_CELL_LOOKS.md).
- **Inline widget primitives** ([InlineWidgets.tsx](src/modeler/vpl/widgets/InlineWidgets.tsx)): the inline port primitives (number / bool / tag / glyph) live in one component. The number widgets use `type=text` + `inputMode=decimal` with a draft/commit dual-state model — fixes the long-standing Chromium bug where typing `-` (or a partial decimal) into a controlled `type=number` input was wiped on every re-render. **`NumberField`** is the panel/simulator-facing variant of `InlineNumberInput` (numeric `onNumber` API + `min`/`max` clamping + `integer` rounding + optional `onClear`). Because `type=text` has no native spinbox, **NumberField renders its OWN up/down stepper column + honours ArrowUp/ArrowDown and the mouse wheel** (step = `step` prop, default 1; float drift de-fuzzed to the step's decimals) — restoring the increment/decrement the panel inputs lost when they moved off `type=number`. To keep this layout-safe across fixed-width (`.numberInput` 80px) AND flex (`.brushInput`) call sites, the caller's `className` (border/bg/width/flex) moves to a wrapper `<span>` and the `<input>` becomes a borderless transparent fill; focus border + `disabled` fade are re-applied inline (the class's `:focus`/`:disabled` can't target the wrapper). Pass `noSpinner` to hide the buttons in very tight spots (keyboard/wheel still work). **EVERY panel/simulator number entry MUST go through `NumberField` (numeric state) or `InlineNumberInput` (string-config state) — never a raw `<input type="number">`** (which both re-introduces the minus-sign bug and gives no centralized place for future fixes); the only exception is the DEV-only StyleReferenceView. For an OPTIONAL field whose empty state should become `undefined`, or one that should reset to a documented default on clear, pass `onClear` — without it, blanking the field restores the previous value (it never commits empty), so `Number(value) || default` empty-fallbacks from the old raw inputs must be ported to an `onClear` that re-commits the default.
- **isArray scalar→array compatibility**: `portsCompatible` / `shapesMate` now permit scalar source → array target connections (only array → scalar is rejected). Dragging from an `isArray` input port (Aggregate.values, GetRandom.options) now lists scalar-producing nodes in the compatible-nodes menu — matching what `isValidConnection` already accepted and what the compilers handle via `inputToSources` multi-scalar wiring.
- **Interactive minimap**: pannable + zoomable; a click jumps the viewport to the clicked spot (keeps current zoom, 200 ms ease). Was a static overview before.
- **RMB-through-edges**: a capture-phase `pointerdown` handler on the editor wrapper catches right-button pointerdowns inside `.react-flow__edge`, `stopPropagation`s, and re-dispatches `pointerdown` + `mousedown` on `.react-flow__pane` so d3-zoom's pan-on-drag starts as if the user had pressed empty canvas. LMB on edges (select / double-click delete) is untouched.
- **Models Library sort**: the plugin writes index.json alphabetically by display name (case-insensitive `localeCompare`), so the raw index stays stable across recompiles (was filesystem `readdir` order); the CLIENT then re-sorts per the user's persisted toolbar choice — see "Models Library browse tools" below.
- **Models Library browse tools + uniform cards** ([ModelsLibrary.tsx](src/library/ModelsLibrary.tsx); plan [docs/PLAN_LIBRARY_3D_VIEW_UX.md](docs/PLAN_LIBRARY_3D_VIEW_UX.md)): a toolbar with **search** (name/description/authors/tags substring), a **category `<select>`** over all tags (case-insensitive canonical + usage counts, frequency-sorted; a card's tag chip click toggles the same filter), an **All/2D/3D** segmented filter, the **CA Grid / Agents topology filters** — **independent TOGGLES, not a radio** (a model can run both layers at once; each toggle REQUIRES its layer, both on = models running both, e.g. the two field-bridge samples; fed by plugin-emitted `hasGrid`/`hasAgents` from `topologyMode`, absent → grid on/agents off), **sort** (Name A→Z default / Z→A / Newest / Oldest / Largest grid — grid size = the cell-count product parsed from `gridSize`), and a **Group by category** checkbox (sections keyed by each model's FIRST tag; untagged → "Other"; groups sorted by size). Prefs persist in `localStorage['genesisca_library_prefs']` (validated on load). The Vite plugin emits per-entry `modified` (file mtimeMs — powers date sort + the card date stamp), `dimension` (`'3d'` iff `properties.dimension==='3d' && gridDepth>1`, mirroring `is3dModel`) and a `WxHxD` gridSize for 3D — **additive index.json fields; a dev-server restart regenerates them** (an SW-cached index revalidates on the next fetch). **Cards are a FIXED 224px flex column** in a `minmax(308px, 1fr)` grid with TIGHT padding (`--space-5`/`--space-6`) and an **accent-coloured title** (name/authors 1-line ellipsis; the bottom meta block **WRAPS so EVERY tag stays visible — tags take layout priority over the description**, which shrinks via `flex:1`/`min-height:0`; `W×H · date` right-aligns on the last row via `margin-left:auto`) so the grid rows are uniform. **Synthetic "mode" tags** (`effectiveTags(entry)`): 3D (`dimension==='3d'`) and Agents (`hasAgents`) are injected as first-class tags — they render as coloured BADGES (3D = accent/amber `.dimBadge`, Agents = success/green `.agentBadge`; distinct in both themes) but otherwise **behave exactly like authored tags: clickable to filter (toggle `prefs.tag` to `'3d'`/`'agents'`), listed in the category `<select>` with counts, groupable, searchable** — every consumer (catalogue / filter / groups / card / search haystack) reads the per-entry `effTagsById` map, NOT `entry.tags`. Synthetic tags come FIRST (so a 3D/Agents model **groups under that mode**; a both-model groups under 3D) and **REPLACE the redundant hand-authored `3d`/`agents` tag chip** (`SYNTH_ALIASES` de-dup — the mode shows once, as the badge). Only the NON-default modes get a synthetic tag (2D + CA-Grid are the norm → badging them would be noise). The 2D/3D + CA-Grid/Agents toolbar quick filters stay as a separate axis (they also cover the DEFAULT side, which the badges don't). (Latent-bug fix in the same pass: the group-"Other" sentinel key `' other'` had a NUL byte instead of the space in the original write — harmless as a bucket key but flagged the file binary; now `'__other'`.) **`useAutoScrollOverflow`** (the shared scroll rule — card desc AND popover desc pane): overflowing text AUTO-SCROLLS while hovered — an rAF phase loop (rest 0.8 s → scroll ~25 px/s → hold 1.4 s → scroll back → repeat) writing `transform` directly (no React re-render; text that fits no-ops; a bottom fade hints clipping). The hover **popover is TWO same-size 320×240 panes side by side — [title + description | thumbnail] — CENTERED ON the hovered card (both axes, literally on top of it; viewport-clamped near edges; `pointer-events:none` passes hover/clicks through to the card beneath)**; measure-then-position on BOTH axes; the desc pane follows the same scroll rule (always-active while shown), a no-thumbnail model shows the desc pane alone, and a model with neither thumbnail nor description gets no popover. NB rAF is suspended in a hidden/occluded tab — verify the scroll via an rAF shim with synthetic timestamps, not by waiting.
- **Themes**: two shipped — **Nocturne** (**default**; `[data-theme="nocturne"]`, near-black surfaces + amber `#e8a13a` accent, softer radii/shadows; overrides every COLOUR token plus radii/shadows/scrollbar, and intentionally inherits spacing/fonts/layout-widths from `:root` — node category fills are theme-independent and kept identical) and **Blender** (`tokens.css` binds it to `:root` + `[data-theme="blender"]`, so `:root` is the omitted-token fallback base). [ThemeSwitcher.tsx](src/components/ThemeSwitcher.tsx) (a navbar `<select>`, reinstated) drives `html[data-theme]` + persists to `localStorage['genesisca_theme']`; the index.html boot script reads that key (validates against known themes, falls back to `nocturne`) synchronously before first paint to avoid a flash. **Default theme = the fallback in BOTH index.html (boot script) AND ThemeSwitcher's `readInitialTheme()`** — change both to re-default. Canvas-rendered charts recolor live via [useThemeTokens.ts](src/styles/useThemeTokens.ts) (MutationObserver on `data-theme`). **Adding a theme:** add a `[data-theme="<name>"]` block overriding every colour token (omitted tokens fall back to the Blender `:root` values), add it to `THEME_OPTIONS` + the boot-script allowlist. The GraphEditor's React-Flow CANVAS colors (Background grid + MiniMap) are tokenized via `--color-canvas-grid` / `--color-minimap-*` and read with `useThemeTokens` (reactive); edge value/flow colors (`#4cc9f0`/`#66bb6a`) are kept constant on purpose (semantic, like node category fills). The stray hardcoded `#2d4059` border in RightActivityBar is now `var(--color-border)`.
- **Native form-control theming**: a global `index.css` rule sets `accent-color: var(--color-accent)` on `input[type="range"|"checkbox"|"radio"]`. Without it, controls that don't set their own accent (e.g. the simulator model-attribute sliders) fall back to the BROWSER/OS default accent — which ignores the theme and even differs per machine (the canonical bug: model-attribute sliders showed blue on one browser, orange in the preview's embedded Chromium). New native controls inherit the theme accent for free; don't re-hardcode slider/checkbox colors.
- **Top navbar layout** ([App.tsx](src/App.tsx) / [App.module.css](src/App.module.css)): three flex groups — LEFT (`.navLeft`: logo from `public/icon.svg` + `GenesisCA` title/version + the **`<FileMenu/>` dropdown** (a `File ▾` button opening New/Save/Load) + a **Library** button + the model-name), CENTER (`.navCenter`: the two work **modes** Modeler / Simulator — the things you switch between WHILE working on a model — centered in the free space), RIGHT (`.navRight`: dev-only Style Reference + a **Help** button + the **keyboard-shortcuts** icon button (grouped as the help/reference cluster) + ThemeSwitcher + InstallButton + the **GitHub** link (rightmost)). Library is deliberately NOT a centered "mode" — it's a model picker, so it sits in the left group next to File. `.navCenter` uses `flex: 1 0 auto` + `justify-content:center` (grows to center the modes, but **never shrinks below the buttons** — else `justify-content:center` would spill them over the side groups on narrow widths); `.navLeft` is `min-width:0; overflow:hidden` so the model name truncates on narrow widths. NB: because `.navLeft` clips overflow, the FileMenu dropdown is rendered `position: fixed` (coords measured from the trigger) so it isn't clipped — see [FileMenu.tsx](src/components/FileMenu.tsx).
- **Discoverability — shortcuts overlay (navbar) + canvas fullscreen (on-canvas)**: the navbar **keyboard-shortcuts button** ([App.tsx](src/App.tsx)) — an inline keyboard SVG icon (the old `?` glyph was replaced: in Windows `?` reads as click-for-contextual-help, which isn't the behavior) — and the `?` key (suppressed while typing in a field) toggle [KeyboardShortcutsOverlay.tsx](src/components/KeyboardShortcutsOverlay.tsx) — a theme-aware cheat-sheet modal grouping the Global / Modeler / Simulator shortcuts (its own Esc handler is capture-phase so it closes the overlay instead of firing the simulator's Esc=reset). **Canvas fullscreen lives ON each canvas, not the navbar** (it's a canvas action): a `⛶` button in GraphEditor's bottom-left view-toggle cluster (next to grid/snap/port-labels) and in SimulatorView's zoom controls (next to gridlines/infinity). Both dispatch a `genesis-toggle-canvas-fullscreen` window event the active view handles by collapsing its side panels (exactly the `F` key's behavior — NOT browser F11). ModelerView and SimulatorView each extract their F-toggle into a `toggleCanvasFullscreen` callback shared by the key handler + the event listener (SimulatorView gates on `visibleRef` since it's always-mounted; GraphEditor's button just dispatches the window event).
- **Ear-tabs**: the Modeler's left + right panel-switch bars ([ActivityBar.tsx](src/modeler/ActivityBar.tsx) / [RightActivityBar.tsx](src/modeler/RightActivityBar.tsx), shared [ActivityBar.module.css](src/modeler/ActivityBar.module.css)) render as floating **ear-tab icon pills** — a thin (40px) TRANSPARENT gutter with the pills vertically centred, rather than a solid full-height rail. Each tab is a rounded pill (`--color-bg-panel` surface + `--color-widget-border`); the active one is accent-tinted (`--color-accent-soft` bg + `--color-accent` icon/border) instead of the old edge stripe. The left panels gained real SVG icons (Info/Properties/Attributes/Neighborhoods/Mappings/Variegated); the right already had them. **Docking is unchanged** — panels still push the graph; only the rail's look + width changed (the layout flow in ModelerView is untouched). `buttonActiveRight` is now a no-op kept for class-list compat.
- **Simulator mapping tooltips**: A→C viewer tabs and C→A brush tabs expose `mapping.description` as a native `title` attribute (matches the existing model-attribute / preset tooltips).
- **Cell inspector**: neighborIndex attribute values decode to `(dr X, dc Y)` offsets (INVALID_NI → `INVALID_NI (no neighbor)`) instead of the raw packed i32; variegated models gain an orientation row (`<int> (N/E/S/W)`) above the attribute rows.
- **`tagConstant` retired**: folded into `getConstant.tag` (identical picker, same i32 emit on all targets). [tagConstantMigration.ts](src/model/tagConstantMigration.ts) mirrors `colorScaleMigration`'s pair pattern and is wired into `LOAD_MODEL` + `macroImport` so saved `.gcaproj` files with the old node keep loading after its removal from the registry/emitters/validation.

---

## Indicators (Implemented)

### Architecture:
- Two kinds: **Standalone** (typed scalar, graph-writable) and **Linked** (auto-computed from cell attributes)
- Standalone indicators support all types: bool, integer, float, tag — stored as JS numbers in `_indicators` object
- Linked indicators aggregate cell attribute arrays: Frequency (count per value) or Total (sum)
- Both kinds have Accumulation Mode: per-generation (reset each step) or accumulated (running total, reset on simulator reset)
- **X-axis** (linked only): `generation` (default — classic time-history) or `rows`/`columns` (a live **spatial histogram** = chromatogram; see Spatial Indicators below). Standalone indicators are Generation-only (a graph-written scalar has no spatial extent).

### Standalone Indicator Nodes:
- `GetIndicatorNode` (value, `'data'`): reads `_indicators[indicatorId]`
- `SetIndicatorNode` (flow, `'output'`): writes `_indicators[indicatorId] = value`
- `UpdateIndicatorNode` (flow, `'output'`): modifies based on type — Bool: toggle/or/and; Int/Float: increment/decrement/max/min; Tag: next/previous
- All three have teal color `#00695c`

### Compiler Integration:
- `_indicators` parameter added after `activeViewer`, before optional `order` in `buildLoopParams`, `buildCellParams`, and output mapping params
- Step function loop-wrapped: `_indicators` is a shared object across all cell iterations within a single step — enables accumulation patterns

### Worker Integration:
- `cachedIndicators: Record<string, number>` — mutable during step function execution
- `standalonePerGenIds` — per-generation indicators reset to defaults before each step
- `computeLinkedIndicators()` — iterates typed arrays after each step (frequency, total, with float binning)
- `computeSpatialIndicators()` — the spatial (rows/columns) sibling: bins each cell by position and aggregates per bin using the SAME per-type branches; writes `Record<seriesKey, number[]>` into `linkedResults` (see Spatial Indicators)
- `linkedAccumulators` — running state for accumulated linked indicators
- Indicator values included in `stepped` message as `indicators: Record<string, number | Record<string, number> | Record<string, number[]>>` (the `number[]` arm carries spatial series)
- `initIndicators()` called on init, `resetIndicators()` on reset
- `updateIndicators` message rebuilds indicator state when definitions change

### Modeler UI:
- `IndicatorsPanelSection` LIST rendered inside `PropertiesPanelContent` (master-detail; the selected indicator's editor opens in the shared second detail panel — see the Modeler UX "Detail editor in a SECOND left panel" section). It's a controlled child: `mode`/`selectedId`/`onSelect` from Properties' `indicator:<id>` selection slot.
- Standalone: type selector, default value (type-specific), tag options editor
- Linked: attribute dropdown (cell attrs only), aggregation (type-dependent), **Value Bins** (float + frequency only — renamed from "Bin Count" to disambiguate from spatial bins), **Track Categories** (bool/tag frequency only — a checkbox checklist to chart a SUBSET of category values), **X Axis** (Generation/Rows/Columns), and when spatial: **Bin Mode** (Slices/Absolute) + Number of Slices / rows-per-bin
- Both: accumulation mode radio, watched toggle, delete button
- **Track Categories / `Indicator.trackedValues?: string[]`** (bool/tag frequency only; absent/empty = all): filter the per-category frequency map to a chosen subset so a dominant category doesn't flatten the rest on the shared chart Y-axis. Applied at ONE point — `sendColors` in [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts) (message assembly, post-aggregation) — so it uniformly covers JS-embedded / WASM-worker / WebGPU-reduced results AND generation + spatial axes; integer/float frequency (dynamic/range keys) and `total` (scalar) are untouched. UI = `TrackCategoriesEditor` in `IndicatorsPanelSection`; full/empty selection stores `undefined` (track all). SimulatorView threads `trackedValues` through BOTH the `init` and `updateIndicators` worker payloads (the worker `IndicatorDef` + internal `linkedDefs` both carry it).

### Simulator UI:
- `IndicatorDisplay` component in right panel below brush controls
- Scalar values (standalone, linked total): single numeric display + sparkline chart (always-mount-wrapper pattern — see Key Patterns)
- Frequency maps (linked frequency): three viz modes cycled via a header button — **Bars** (`IndicatorDisplay` inline bar chart, current gen only), **Lines** (`IndicatorMultiLineChart`, one coloured line per category over time), **Stack** (`IndicatorStackedAreaChart`, cumulative-sum bands). Preference persists per indicator via `indicatorVizModes: Record<id, 'bars'|'multiline'|'stacked'>` inside `genesisca_sim_settings` localStorage.
- **Legend click-to-hide (Lines/Stack/Spatial)**: clicking a legend entry dims/hides that series (line-through + opacity 0.4); click again to restore. Runtime, display-only state `indicatorHiddenCategories: Record<indicatorId, Set<category>>` owned in `SimulatorView`, persisted in `genesisca_sim_settings` as `Record<id, string[]>`, threaded via `IndicatorDisplay` to all three multi-series chart components (`IndicatorMultiLineChart` / `IndicatorStackedAreaChart` / `IndicatorSpatialChart`) via `hidden` + `onToggleCategory` props. The charts skip hidden series in the scaling + draw loops (Stack recomputes its cumulative bands + yMax from visible series only; Lines + Spatial rescale Y over visible). `colorFor()` keys the palette by the design-time series position (see "Stable series palette indexing" below), so hiding one series never recolors the others. **Distinct from `trackedValues`** (Track Categories): `trackedValues` is a design-time, model-level filter applied in the worker (changes which data reaches the chart); legend-hide is a per-session UI toggle (NOT saved into `.gcaproj`).
- **Chart settings (gear popover) / `Indicator.chartSettings?: IndicatorChartSettings`** — per-indicator chart display config: `yMin`/`yMax` (fixed axis bounds; absent = dynamic, each bound independent), `yTicks` (2–11 tick labels incl. min+max), `seriesColors` (per-category hex overrides; scalar charts use the single key `'value'`). TWO layers, field-level merge (override wins, colors merge per key): model-level DEFAULTS on the Indicator (edited via `ChartDefaultsEditor` in `IndicatorsPanelSection`, design-time-enumerable categories only — scalar `'value'`, bool `false/true`, tag options; numeric freq buckets are runtime-only) and simulator-side OVERRIDES (`indicatorChartOverrides: Record<id, IndicatorChartSettings>` in SimulatorView, edited via the ⚙ `ChartSettingsPopover` in `IndicatorDisplay` with runtime series keys, persisted in `genesisca_sim_settings` AND serialized into `SimulationState.indicatorChartOverrides` under "Simulator controls"; `applySimulationState` restores wholesale). Shared helpers in [indicatorChartSettings.ts](src/simulator/indicatorChartSettings.ts): `mergeChartSettings`, `applyAxisOverrides` (applied AFTER the 6% dynamic pad), `tickCount`, `drawIntermediateYTicks`, `hasFixedAxis` (charts clip data drawing to the plot rect when an axis bound is fixed — samples may fall outside the user's window), `chartSettingsKey` (redraw-effect dep). All four chart components take a `settings?: IndicatorChartSettings` prop (the EFFECTIVE merge); their `colorFor(idx, cat)` consults `seriesColors[cat]` before the index-keyed palette, so an override never recolors other series.
- **Stable series palette indexing (Track Categories fix)**: `designTimeSeriesKeys(ind, model)` (moved to [indicatorChartSettings.ts](src/simulator/indicatorChartSettings.ts), shared by the modeler's `ChartDefaultsEditor` AND the simulator charts) is the canonical design-time series order — scalar `'value'`, bool `false/true`, tag options (sorted, matching the charts' key sort); numeric freq buckets are runtime-only → `[]`. SimulatorView memoises `indicatorCategoryOrders: Record<id, string[]>` from it and threads it into `IndicatorDisplay` → the three multi-series charts + the gear-popover swatches as `categoryOrder`. Each chart's `colorFor(idx, cat)` now prefers `categoryOrder.indexOf(cat)` over the runtime `idx`, falling back to `idx` only for runtime-only keys. So **unticking a Track Categories value no longer shifts the surviving series onto different palette slots** (the bug was: filtering changed the runtime index, recoloring everything). Legend-hide (above) was already safe because it skip-draws rather than reindexes.
- History shape in `SimulatorView.indicatorHistoryRef` is polymorphic: `number[]` for scalars, `Record<category, number[]>` for frequency maps. Capped at 500 samples per series.
- `chartExpandedRef` is populated by `IndicatorDisplay`'s render-phase ref-compare notification. Do NOT reset it in `initWorker`'s useEffect — that runs AFTER the child's render and wipes the populated set, so the first stepped messages collect no history (symptom: scalar sparklines stay blank until manual collapse/expand).
- Eye icon per indicator toggles `watched` state
- Spatial indicators (`xAxis` rows/columns/layers) render `IndicatorSpatialChart` (per-series over position bins) — bypasses the scalar/freq branches. They have their OWN 2-state **Lines ⇄ Bars** style toggle (curves vs a per-bin histogram; multi-series bars overlay semi-transparent), stored in the SAME persisted `indicatorVizModes` slot (`'bars'` = bars, anything else = lines; `onSetVizMode` sets it directly — the 3-mode freq cycle doesn't fit a 2-state flip). Their ⌫ clear renders DISABLED with an explanatory tooltip (a spatial chart redraws from the current generation — no history to clear). **Header-control visibility is DEFINITION-based** (`isFreqDef`/`isScalarDef` from `kind`/`linkedAggregation`/`xAxis`), NOT value-shape-based — the viz-mode/clear buttons show from load instead of only after the first step delivers a value (the val-shape `isFreq`/`isScalar` still drive the chart rendering itself). `IndicatorDisplay` takes `gridWidth`/`gridHeight` props (live dims) to label the X-axis with real row/column positions.

### Spatial Indicators (chromatogram X-axis — purely a measurement/visualization layer):
- A spatial indicator plots value **per position bin** along the grid's `rows` or `columns` axis instead of over `generation` — reproducing the chromatogram plots in the Kier chromatography/enantiomer papers (population vs column position, one curve per species). **No graph nodes, no compiler changes, no per-cell position read** — it's one extra CPU pass in the worker, opt-in per linked indicator.
- **Why linked-only:** spatial binning = "scan all cells, bin each by position" = a cell-aggregation, which is exactly what linked indicators already do. Standalone indicators (a single graph-written scalar) have no spatial extent and stay Generation-only.
- **Data shape:** the per-step value is `Record<seriesKey, number[]>` — each array indexed by **position bin** (length = bin count). Each value-bucket the linked path already produces becomes a **series** (a curve): bool → `true`/`false`; tag → one per option (the chromatogram's per-species curves); integer freq → one per distinct value; integer/float **total** → single `total` (per-bin sum); float freq → one per **value-bin** (2-D value×position histogram). color/neighborIndex unchanged.
- **Two distinct bin counts — don't conflate:** `binCount` = **value** bins (float frequency only); `spatialBinCount` (slices mode) / `spatialBinSize` (absolute mode) = **position** bins. Only float+frequency uses both (value-bins → series, position-bins → array index).
- **Schema** (`Indicator`, all optional, additive — no migration): `xAxis?: 'generation'|'rows'|'columns'`, `spatialBinMode?: 'slices'|'absolute'` (default slices), `spatialBinCount?` (slices; default 50, clamped [2, axisLen]), `spatialBinSize?` (absolute; rows/cols per bin, default 1 → B = ceil(axisLen/size)).
- **Worker** (`computeSpatialIndicators` in [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)): mirrors `computeLinkedIndicatorsFromBuffer` (same per-type branches + sub-attr parent-match guard) but bins by `row = ⌊i/width⌋` / `col = i % width`. `linkedDefs` carry the xAxis + bin config; `hasSpatialIndicators` gates the per-step call. Writes `linkedResults[id]` as `Record<key, number[]>`.
- **Buffer/timing (the critical gotcha):** called **post-step, after the buffer swap/copy**, so `readAttrs` holds the just-computed generation on JS (ref-swap) AND WASM (w→r copy) — the same buffer a later `getState` reads, which is what makes the parity check exact. On WebGPU it runs inside `finalizeStepWebGPU` after the attrs readback; spatial source attrs (and their parents, for sub-attrs) are force-added to `watchedAttrIds` so the selective readback always pulls them even when a sibling generation-axis indicator on the same attr is GPU-reduced.
- **Never accumulated:** spatial is always a live per-step snapshot (the accumulation loop + history collection both skip it; SimulatorView detects array-valued entries structurally). End conditions exclude spatial indicators (a spatial value isn't a scalar/category count); the end-condition target dropdown filters them out.
- **WebGPU:** spatial indicators are CPU-only — `buildReductionPlan` ([webgpuReduce.ts](src/simulator/engine/webgpuReduce.ts)) skips any `xAxis` rows/columns def.
- **Verified** (tsc + cross-target parity): per-bin series match an independent `getState` re-bin with 0 mismatches and sum-of-bins == per-value total, on JS (columns) / WASM (rows) / WebGPU (rows), both slices and absolute modes.

### Manual Brush (runtime-only Input Mapping):
- Special "Manual" tab that always appears as the rightmost entry in the right-panel brush mapping strip, alongside the model's color-input mappings. Renders even when the model has zero color-input mappings (also auto-selected on load in that case). Does NOT appear in the Modeler's Mappings tab — it's purely a simulator UX layer with no model-schema counterpart.
- Selecting Manual swaps the color picker for a `<ManualBrushPanel>` (one row per cell attribute: `[Set checkbox] [name + sub-attr hint] [type-appropriate widget]`). Widgets reuse `InlineNumberInput` / `InlineBoolSelect` / `InlineTagSelect`; **neighborIndex** attributes use the `NeighborIndexValuePicker` grid widget (the same cell-matrix picker the Model Attributes panel uses — hint neighborhood resolved via `neighborhoodHintId`, dr/dc fallback inputs when no hint) instead of a raw packed-i32 number field, so picking a neighbour is a click on the offset grid. `ManualBrushPanel` takes `neighborhoods` for this. Unchecked rows are dimmed (`.manualBrushWidget.dim` — `opacity: 0.4; pointer-events: none`) and their attribute is skipped at paint time.
- Worker protocol: `paintManual` message — `{type: 'paintManual', cells: [{row, col}], sets: [{attrId, value}], activeViewer}`. UI pre-encodes string values into typed-array numerics via `encodeAttrValue()` in `src/model/attrValueEncoding.ts` (shared with worker's `defaultValue()`/`boundaryCellValue()`). Bypasses any compiled `InputColor` function. Worker handler mutates `readAttrs[attrId][idx]` directly (plus `writeAttrs[...][idx]` in sync mode for next-step consistency), works on JS, WASM, and WebGPU targets without per-target code paths — WebGPU branch readbacks attrs first when `gpuOwnsAttrs && needs parent-match read`, then patches per-cell via `patchWebGPUCells(idxs)`.
- Sub-attribute semantics: **per-cell skip**. For each sub-attr being Set, the worker computes the cell's *effective* parent value — the brush's parent value if the parent is itself in `sets` (`brushParentOverride` Map), else `readAttrs[parentId][idx]` — and suppresses the write when that value isn't in `parentValues`. Mirrors the schema's iteration semantics. `parentValueToInt` is now exported from `subAttribute.ts` for the worker to share.
- State: `manualBrush: Record<attrId, {enabled, value}>` in SimulatorView, persisted per-model in localStorage under `genesisca_manual_brush_v1:<model.properties.name>` (separate from the global `genesisca_sim_settings`; NOT saved into `.gcaproj` / `.gcastate` — Manual Brush is per-user UX). Signature-keyed merge effect (`cellAttrSig = "id1:type1|id2:type2|..."`) re-derives state when the attribute SET changes; live name/description edits don't reset values. New attrs seed `{enabled: true, value: defaultValue}`; deleted attrs vanish; type-changed attrs reset.
- Flush plumbing: `flushPaintBatch` ([SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx)) branches on the `MANUAL_BRUSH_MAPPING_ID` sentinel, snapshots `manualBrushRef.current` AT FLUSH TIME (so widget edits during a drag take effect mid-stroke), encodes values via `encodeAttrValue()`, posts `paintManual`. The existing mid-drag mapping-change flush path piggybacks on the sentinel — swapping in/out of Manual flushes correctly.
- Suppressed in Manual mode: Shift+RMB color popover (no color to set), Open Image button (image import requires a color mapping — disabled with explanatory tooltip), and the "Shift+RMB color" hint string. Ctrl+wheel cycle includes Manual as the rightmost entry.

### End Conditions & Stop Events:
- `ModelProperties.endConditions?: { enabled, maxGenerations?, indicatorConditions? }` — optional auto-pause rules evaluated on the main thread in `SimulatorView.evalEndConditions` after each `stepped` message; pauses play and shows a blue info notice.
- `IndicatorEndCondition.category?: string` — for linked-frequency indicators the comparison is `frequencyMap[category] <op> constant`. UI branches per linked attribute type: bool/tag → dropdown, integer → number input, float-binned → disabled with warning (bin keys aren't knowable at design time).
- Stop Event node compiles to a write into a shared `_stopFlag` Uint32Array (+ `layout.stopFlagOffset` for WASM). Worker reads the flag after every `runStep`, clears it at the top of each step, surfaces the message via a `stopEvent` message. Main thread pauses + shows the same blue notice. `stopMessages: string[]` passed via init/recompile; Stop Event config.`_stopIdx` is 1-based so 0 means "no stop requested".
- Saved state (.gcastate / embedded in .gcaproj) restores the grid configuration only. `generation` resets to 0 and indicators re-init on load — saved files represent starting configurations, not run snapshots. `serializeSimState` skips generation/indicators/linkedAccumulators on write; loader ignores them on read (back-compat with older files).

---

## Macro System (Implemented)

### Architecture:
- MacroInput/MacroOutput are boundary nodes inside macro subgraphs (teal, `#00897b`)
- MacroInput has OUTPUT ports (data flows into subgraph); MacroOutput has INPUT ports (data flows out)
- Ports are dynamic — derived from `MacroDef.exposedInputs`/`exposedOutputs` at render time
- Port editing UI on boundary nodes: add/remove/rename ports, value/flow category selector
- Changes propagate automatically — MacroNode's external handles re-derive from the same MacroDef arrays
- Boundary nodes cannot be deleted (filtered in `handleNodesChange` and `deleteSelection`)

### Create Macro from Selection:
- Auto-creates MacroInput (left of bbox) + MacroOutput (right of bbox) inside the subgraph. **Boundary positioning uses the selection's FULL-extent bbox** (`bMinX/bMinY/bMaxX/bMaxY` via `nodeSize(n)` → `position + measured width/height`), NOT bare `position.x`: MacroInput's right edge sits `BOUNDARY_GAP` (120px) left of the bbox left edge (`x = bMinX − GAP − EST_BOUNDARY_W`), MacroOutput's left edge sits `BOUNDARY_GAP` right of the bbox right edge (`x = bMaxX + GAP`), both vertically centred on the bbox. **Vertical centring estimates each boundary node's height from its exposed-port count** (`estBoundaryH(portCount) = 46 + max(1,portCount)*22`) — applied per side (`exposedInputs.length` for MacroInput, `exposedOutputs.length` for MacroOutput) so a tall many-port boundary node still centres on the selection instead of riding high. The old code used `maxX + 100` on the rightmost node's *top-left corner*, so MacroOutput landed ON TOP of that node whenever it was wider than 100px (nearly always — the user's "boundary nodes on top of the graph" report). Positions are stored relative to the selection centroid (`avgX/avgY`) like the internal nodes. DEV-only `window.__openSelectionMenu(nodeIds, x, y)` test hook ([GraphEditor.tsx](src/modeler/vpl/GraphEditor.tsx)) opens the selection context menu (box-select can't be driven by synthetic events — same limitation as `__openConnectionDropMenu`).
- `exposedInputs[i].internalNodeId` points to MacroInput node ID (not the actual internal target)
- Bridging edges connect MacroInput output ports → original internal targets
- Bridging edges connect original internal sources → MacroOutput input ports
- External edges are sorted by `internalNode.position.y * 1000 + portIdx` BEFORE building exposedInputs/exposedOutputs, so port order matches the visual top-to-bottom layout instead of arbitrary edges-array order. Port index resolves via `def.ports.filter(p => p.kind === kind).findIndex(p => p.id === portId)`, falling back to MacroDef.exposedInputs/Outputs for nested macros.

### Default Macros & Import/Export:
- `.gcamacro` file format: `{ schemaVersion: 1, name, description?, macroDef: MacroDef }`. Single MacroDef per file.
- `public/macros/*.gcamacro` is auto-indexed into `public/macros/index.json` by `macrosLibraryPlugin()` in vite.config.ts (mirrors `modelsLibraryPlugin`). Palette's "Default Macros" section fetches from `index.json` and lists each one as draggable.
- Right-click macro node → "Export Macro…" downloads a `.gcamacro` (filename derived from macro.name).
- Right-click canvas → "Import Macro…" (top-level item) opens a hidden `<input type="file">` and inserts the imported macro at the right-click position.
- `cloneMacroWithFreshIds(raw)` in `src/model/macroImport.ts` is REQUIRED for any macro import path. Regenerates: MacroDef.id, every internal node.id (with parentId remap), every edge.id, MacroPort.internalNodeId references, AND `config.macroDefId` on `macroInput`/`macroOutput` boundary nodes (this last one is easy to forget — without it, boundary nodes still point at the old MacroDef and macros break across imports).
- `importMacro(raw): string` action on ModelContext wraps clone + addMacro and returns the new id.
- MacroNode `data.label` is the user-facing name shown above the "Macro" header. `createMacroFromSelection` sets it; palette drops and file imports also pass `name` as the label argument to `addNodeAtPosition`. Always set the label when creating a macro instance.
- Project Macros section in palette filters `model.macroDefs` to only those referenced by at least one MacroNode (in `model.graphNodes` OR in any `model.macroDefs[*].nodes`). Stale defs (last instance deleted) don't appear; no auto-cleanup of model state itself.

### Undo Macro:
- Filters out boundary nodes and bridging edges when restoring
- Traces through bridging edges to find actual internal nodes for edge reconnection
- **Positioning** (`undoMacro` in GraphEditor.tsx): restored nodes are centered on the macro node by aligning the internal cluster's bounding-box center to the macro node's center (`offset = macroCenter − clusterCenter`). Do NOT just add `macroNode.position` to each internal `n.position` — that assumes internal coords are relative to (0,0), which only holds for `createMacroFromSelection` (stores `pos − centroid`). Macros from `.gcamacro` import or palette drop carry absolute coords, so the naive offset flung them far from the macro node. The bbox-center alignment is origin-independent.

### Macro Compilation (shared `expandMacros`, all three targets):
- **All three compilers flatten macros UP FRONT** via the shared `expandMacros` ([macroExpand.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/macroExpand.ts)) before any analysis. Each `macro` instance is replaced by its MacroDef's internal nodes (ids prefixed `m<instanceId>_`) + rewritten bridge edges; boundary nodes (`macroInput`/`macroOutput`) are dropped; recurses with a depth-20 guard. After expansion NO `macro`/`macroInput`/`macroOutput` nodes remain, so sink analysis, loop-invariance, accessor-CSE AND volatile hoisting all see one uniform flat graph — there is no "inside a macro" special case on any target.
- **JS used to have a SEPARATE lazy macro-inliner** (`inlineMacroValues` / `inlineMacroFlow` / `inlineNestedMacroValues` + `emitMacroLine` + `expandingMacroDefs`) that ran NONE of those analyses on macro internals. It was DELETED in favour of `expandMacros` (call at the top of `compileGraph`, before reroute-collapse / linked-OM / CSE / `buildAdjacency`). This closed two real bugs: (a) the lazy flow-inliner never emitted a flow node's internal value-input deps (a macro of `getNeighbors→count → setVariable` referenced undeclared `_m…_count` vars); (b) macro internals bypassed sink/loop-invariance/volatile analysis entirely.
