Known accepted deltas vs per-agent: cross-colour overlap z-order (group order, not index order) and dense-cluster outlines (the batched stroke draws every disc's contour over same-group neighbours; per-agent lets later fills cover earlier strokes) — plus sub-quantum AA jitter from the moveTo (Chromium tessellates the arc slightly differently; ±few alpha quanta at edges). **"Outlines" toggle** (`agentOutlines`, agent common-controls, **default OFF** — `saved.agentOutlines === true` to opt in, persisted in `genesisca_sim_settings`): gates the dark contour on agents in BOTH views — 2D: the stroke on the batched AND per-agent disc paths; 3D: a silhouette rim in the sphere-impostor FS (`uOutline` in SPHERE_FS, set via `Gl3DRenderer.setAgentOutlines`; `fwidth`-scaled so the band reproduces the 2D `min(1.5px, 0.25·rad)` × 0.40-darkening rule at any zoom; display pass only — pick/shadow programs don't declare the uniform). NB default-OFF means 2D discs LOSE their historical contour stroke unless the user opts in (3D spheres never had one); dense populations render as clean solid dots by default.
- **Agent brush** (Phase 2 — the overhaul, [SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx)): a full brush, NOT the old single-click stop-gap. **(Modes were later RENAMED + EXTENDED — `seed`→`add`, `kill`→`remove`, plus per-shape footprints / a Single-Area scope / a new `edit` mode; the current `AgentBrushMode` = `add`/`remove`/`move`/`edit`/`glue`/`cut`/`bond`. See "Agent brush parity" below; the mechanics here still hold under the new names.)** **Seed** (now **Add**): `agentSeedPoints` (sunflower-spiral disc, `N=round(density·π·r²)`; torus-wraps points only when `boundaryTreatment==='torus'`, else drops out-of-bounds) → ONE `seedAgents` carrying the per-attribute initial values (the **seed-config** panel, reusing `ManualBrushPanel`, persisted per-model under `genesisca_agent_seed_v1:`; the removed built-in `type` is no longer part of the seed spec). **Drag-to-seed**: a SEPARATE `pendingSeedPoints`/`pendingSeedRaf` batcher (NEVER the cell `pendingPaintRaf` — a shared rAF token would clobber via `cancelAnimationFrame`); `canvasAgentBrushActive` cleared on BOTH the overlay-bail path AND window `mouseup`. **Kill radius**: client-side torus-aware id collection → existing `killAgents` (no new message). **Cursor**: negative-silhouette `difference`-composite ring + hovered-agent highlight + glue-anchor dashed line, redrawn only on hovered-agent/cursor-cell change. **Inspect** (Shift+LMB, injected inside the cell-sweep `:3579` branch, falls through when no agent): a one-shot `getAgentState {id}` worker round-trip (+ ~3 Hz poll while pinned — NOT snapshot-fattening) → popover of geometry/velocity/bondDegree/density + per-agent attr VALUES. **Move**: drag a picked agent (`moveAgents {moves,torus}`, writes x/y + xNext/yNext; RMB-cancel cancels the in-flight move-rAF before reverting). **Bond-paint**: `formBondBatch {pairs}` (idempotent, `hasBond`-guarded). `agentOverflow` worker posts now surface via a transient amber toast (`showAgentNotice`).
- **New worker messages** (Phase 2): `getAgentState {id}` → `agentState` response (geometry + attrs + live bonds; non-live → `{live:false}`); `moveAgents {moves:[{id,x,y}], torus}`; `formBondBatch {pairs:[[a,b]…]}`; `seedAgents.sets?` (per-new-agent attribute writes via the shared `applyAgentSets`).

### Agent Output-Mapping Graphs (standalone/linked) + Agent Sprites (branch `polish_agents`)
Plan + illustrated mockup: [docs/PLAN_AGENT_OUTPUT_MAPPINGS_SPRITES.md](docs/PLAN_AGENT_OUTPUT_MAPPINGS_SPRITES.md) / `.html`. Two coupled, additive features — the lattice (2D+3D, all 3 targets), the agent behaviour (JS/WASM/WebGPU), and every non-agent model stay **byte-identical** (all gated on `agentMappings`/`sprites`/`topologyMode.agents`).

**A — agent output mappings are now authorable GRAPHS (Standalone or Linked), like the CA-grid output mappings.** They were linked-only. New event root **`agentOutputMapping`** ([AgentOutputMappingNode.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/AgentOutputMappingNode.ts), `requirements.bondGraph`, the agent analogue of `outputMapping`) roots a per-agent colour/exhibition pass. [agentLinkedOutputMappings.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentLinkedOutputMappings.ts) was rewritten from `buildAgentColorPassGraphs` → **`injectAgentLinkedOutputMappings`**, the agent mirror of `injectLinkedOutputMappings`: it AUGMENTS the agent graph — synthesizes the linked `getCellAttribute → colorScale/categorical → setCellLooks` pass for each LINKED agent mapping, and SEQUENCES it (auto background `first`, user graph `then`) with any user `agentOutputMapping` root of the same id (override-after-background). `compileAgentGraph` ([compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/compile.ts)) runs the inject right after `expandComposites` (before CSE/adjacency), builds adjacency ONCE over the augmented graph, and compiles **every** `agentOutputMapping` root (user + synthesized) into a per-agent colour-pass fn (the existing `for idx<highWater … colorIdx=idx*4` wrapper). **The agent colour pass is JS on every agent target** (the documented posture), so standalone agent OM graphs need NO per-target agent-OM emit. UI: the Mappings panel's Agent Output Mappings section gained a **Color pass: Standalone/Linked** select (mirrors cells); `ADD_AGENT_MAPPING` still seeds `linked:true`. CaNode renders an agent-mapping picker for `agentOutputMapping.mappingId`, and `setCellLooks`'s picker is graph-aware (lists `agentMappings` on the Agents graph; `nodeValidation` accepts a cell OR agent mapping id for `setCellLooks`).

**B — agent sprites (an optional exhibition layer; static image / animated GIF/WebP per agent).** `CAModel.sprites?: SpriteAsset[]` ([types.ts](src/model/types.ts): `{ id, name, dataUrl, mimeType, scale?, loop? }`) — additive, travels in the `.gcaproj` as a base64 data URL. `ADD/REMOVE/UPDATE_SPRITE` reducers ([ModelContext.tsx](src/model/ModelContext.tsx)); `REMOVE_SPRITE` cascades `clearDeletedId('spriteId')` over `setAgentSprite` nodes. **Decode is MAIN-THREAD only** ([spriteRegistry.ts](src/simulator/spriteRegistry.ts)) via WebCodecs **`ImageDecoder`** (animated GIF/WebP/PNG natively — NO new dependency; the project already uses WebCodecs for WebM recording) → `ImageBitmap[]`, with a `createImageBitmap` single-frame fallback; the worker never carries the pixels. The **`SpriteRegistry`** (keyed by sprite id, re-decodes only changed `dataUrl`s, `onReady` redraws) is owned by SimulatorView and reconciled on `model.sprites` change. **Sprite Library** UI = a "Sprites" section in the Mappings panel (import png/jpeg/gif/webp ≤4 MB, name, size×, loop checkbox).
- **PLAYBACK IS LOGIC-DRIVEN, NOT a simulator transport** (the core correction): the **Set Agent Sprite** node ([SetAgentSpriteNode.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/SetAgentSpriteNode.ts), `requirements.bondGraph`) carries **independently-tickable facets** — *Change sprite* (config `setSprite` + `spriteId` picker), *Set frame* (config `setFrame` + the `frame` input — jump/reset), *Set speed* (config `setSpeed` + the `speed` input — frames per step, **negative = reverse**, 0 = hold). `hiddenPorts` shows the frame/speed inputs only when their facet is ticked. Tick only what you want to change (swap sprite but keep frame/speed; only change speed; reset to frame 0; …). The node writes the per-agent display buffers; a pre-resolve in `compileAgentGraph` bakes `spriteId` → a 1-based `_spriteSlot` into `model.sprites` (0 = none/clear).
- **Persistent per-agent state + engine advance** ([agentEngine.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentEngine.ts)): `AgentStore.spriteIds: Int32Array` (0=none, ≥1=slot) + `spriteFrames: Float64Array` (current frame, fractional) + `spriteSpeeds: Float64Array` (frames/step). PLAIN arrays regardless of backing (never wasm/webgpu-backed — only the JS node + engine touch them); reset to 0 in `initAgentSlot` (recycled slots don't inherit a stale sprite). **`advanceAgentSprites(store)`** does `frame += speed` for every live agent with a sprite, called per step from `runAgentStep` (and after `runAgentStepWebGPU`), gated on `hasAgentSprites` — so the animation only progresses while the sim runs (logic-driven). Unbounded by design (the render floors + wraps/clamps). The 3 buffers ride the agent loop ABI (`buildAgentLoopParams` ↔ the worker's `buildAgentLoopArgs`/`buildDivisionArgs`/`buildAgentInitArgs`, after `glyphColors`); the WASM/WebGPU behaviour ABIs are untouched (JS-only param list). The snapshot ships `spriteIds`+`spriteFrames` ONLY when `hasAgentSprites` (the z/vz "A1" length-0 gate → non-sprite agent models byte-identical); `spriteSpeeds` stays worker-side. `agentHasSprites` rides `init`/`recompile` (from `(model.sprites?.length ?? 0) > 0`).
- **Render** ([SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx) `drawAgentsOverlay`): when the snapshot's `spriteIds[i] > 0` and the sprite is decoded, blit the frame `floor(spriteFrames[i])` wrapped (loop) / clamped (once, per the sprite's `loop` flag), aspect-preserved, sized to the agent diameter × the sprite `scale`, alpha = the agent colour's A; else the existing filled circle. Bonds draw under sprites; recording/screenshot capture the display canvas → sprites for free. There is **NO simulator playback panel + NO playback rAF** (a draft built under the wrong model was removed).
- **All-target story**: Set Agent Sprite writes JS-engine display buffers, so it has no WASM/WebGPU emit; in an Agent Output Mapping graph (the intended use) it's all-target-clean (the OM pass is JS on every agent target, and the WASM/WebGPU gate inspects only behaviour-reachable nodes). Placed in the BEHAVIOUR graph on a WASM/WebGPU agent target it clamps that behaviour to JS (it isn't in `AGENT_WASM_SUPPORTED_TYPES`/the WebGPU allowlist — the established not-yet-ported clamp). Sprites render in BOTH the 2D overlay AND the **3D voxel view** (the 3D billboard pass below).
- **Verified**: tsc + `npm run build` clean; GoL + Life3D byte-clean on all 3 targets; Boids agent behaviour unregressed; a standalone agent OM graph compiles (writes `spriteIds`/`spriteSpeeds`/`colors`) + the linked path synthesizes; **runtime end-to-end** (Boids + a Set Agent Sprite OM pass, real worker): all 260 agents got `spriteId=1`, the frame advanced exactly `0.5`/step (the engine advance from the node-set speed), the snapshot shipped both buffers, the render drew sprites (canvas pixel-sample: 157k sprite-colour pixels, 0 default-circle pixels), 0 console errors. Catalogue: **115 selectable** node types (118 − 3 hidden macro), **42 agent** nodes.

### 3D agent sprites — the WebGL2 billboard pass (branch `absorb_old_automatosgt`)
The 3D voxel renderer ([gl3d.ts](src/simulator/render/gl3d.ts)) now draws sprite-agents as **camera-facing textured billboards** (the 3D analogue of the 2D `drawAgentsOverlay` sprite blit), so the animated/rotated sprite exhibition layer works in the 3D view — not just 2D (this closes the last Phase-E 3D-renderer gap). Non-sprite agents keep drawing sphere impostors; a non-sprite model is byte-identical (the sprite pass never runs). All additive/gated; touches ONLY [gl3d.ts](src/simulator/render/gl3d.ts) + the SimulatorView atlas wiring (no engine / compiler / ABI change).
- **The atlas** = a `TEXTURE_2D_ARRAY`, one CELL×CELL (128²) layer per (sprite, frame). Each decoded `ImageBitmap` frame is drawn STRETCHED into its square layer; the aspect-shaped billboard quad un-stretches it so the sprite renders at its native aspect with the longest side ≈ the agent diameter (matching the 2D `drawImage` sizing). Built by **`setSpriteAtlas(SpriteAtlasInput[])`** (SimulatorView passes the decoded frames + per-sprite meta from the `SpriteRegistry`), rebuilt lazily in the 3D draw path when the sprite set / decoded frames change (a `spriteAtlasDirtyRef` set on registry `onReady` / a sprite-set edit / a fresh renderer, which also forces a sprite-buffer re-upload). The 2D path draws straight from the registry (no atlas) — the atlas is 3D-only.
- **Per-instance data** ([x,y,z, halfW, halfH, cos, sin, layer, alpha] × 9 floats, a SEPARATE `spriteInstBuf`): `uploadAgents` resolves each sprite-agent's frame (floor+wrap/clamp by the asset `loop`), size (radius·2·scale, aspect-shaped by the frame aspect), facing (the per-agent `spriteRotations` compass degrees, OR the world-XY velocity heading `atan2(vx,-vy)` when the asset `orientToVelocity`, aligned to `defaultDirection` + `rotationOffset` — the SAME resolution as the 2D path), and the atlas layer (`baseLayer + frame`). The billboard VS rotates the quad clockwise-on-screen (matches `ctx.rotate`: x'=x·cos+y·sin, y'=-x·sin+y·cos, with uCamUp = screen-up); the FS samples the atlas layer, multiplies by the agent alpha, and discards fully-transparent texels (chroma-key / aspect-pad friendly). Depth-test on + depth-write off + SRC_ALPHA blend → sprites depth-interleave with spheres/voxels + blend by alpha (drawn AFTER spheres in the agent pass).
- **The sphere buffer stays 8 floats** (byte-identical stride): a sprite-agent is flagged by a NEGATIVE `radius` in the sphere buffer — the sphere DISPLAY FS discards it (`vSkip`), while the pick VS uses `abs(radius)` so a sprite-agent stays pickable (move / inspect / edit) via its invisible pick-sphere. So NO per-target ABI/SoA change — a pure render-layer feature reading the snapshot's existing `spriteIds`/`spriteFrames`/`spriteRotations`/`spriteScales`/`vx`/`vy` (which the 2D path already ships). The velocity + the 4 sprite arrays were added to `AgentSnapshot3D` (length-0 for a non-sprite model → the sprite pass is inactive).
- **Verified** (real GL renderer, driven via `window.__sim3dRenderer()`): a red-square atlas over the 5 Tissue agents → 25.6k red (sprite-textured) pixels at the agent positions, `spriteInstanceCount === 5`; **mixed** (even=sprite/odd=sphere) → both render (`sic 3` + spheres); **zero sprites** → 0 billboards, spheres only (non-sprite path unaffected); a 45°-rotated + a per-agent-rotated arrow render at distinct angles with alpha blending (screenshot-confirmed — the arrows point in the set directions). tsc + `npm run build` clean. **Remaining 3D-sprite gap:** the aspect uses frame 0 only; a per-frame-varying aspect (rare) would fit-pad; refinement, not a bug.
- **Two agents-only bug fixes (same session):** (1) **Agents-only model (Grid Cells OFF) no longer demands a cell Generation Step / shows the empty CA-grid canvas.** GraphEditor's `activeGraph` initial state + the guard effect now switch to the **Agents** graph when `topologyMode.gridCells === false && agents` (was hard-defaulting to `'cells'` → the empty Cells canvas + a "No Step node" feel); and `SimulatorView.compileModel` (+ the init/recompile error re-sets) **suppress the cell compiler's "No nodes / No Step node" error when `gridCells === false`** (the cell graph is intentionally empty; the worker already skips the cell step via `gridCells`). Agent compile errors still surface via the worker `error` message. Verified: an agents-only Boids loads with no compile-error banner, the 260 agents simulate, and the Modeler opens on the Agents graph (Behaviour Step present, no Generation Step). (2) **Agent seed brush "Seed config" now sets agent attributes.** `agentSeedSetsRef` built the `seedAgents.sets` payload by iterating `cellAttrsRef` (the CELL attributes) while the Seed-config `ManualBrushPanel` edits `model.agentAttributes` (a separate id-space) — so the `attrId`s never matched the agent store and `applyAgentSets` no-op'd (seeded agents kept their defaults). Fixed to iterate `model.agentAttributes`. Verified via the worker seed path: an agent seeded with `sets:[{attrId:'energy',value:7}]` reads back `energy = 7`.

### Agent & 3D-simulator control overhaul (10-part UX/behaviour batch)
Plan + illustrated mockup: [docs/PLAN_AGENT_SIM_CONTROLS.md](docs/PLAN_AGENT_SIM_CONTROLS.md) / `.html`. The lattice (2D + 3D, all 3 targets) and every non-agent model are **byte-identical** — all additive/gated.
- **"Use bonding physics" master toggle** (`CenterBasedConfig.useBondingPhysics?: boolean`, Properties › Bond-Graph Agents). DEFAULT OFF for a freshly-enabled Agents topology (`defaultCenterBasedConfig` seeds `false`) — enabling agents no longer silently turns on the engine soft-sphere repulsion/adhesion + growth. OFF ⇒ agents move ONLY by graph-authored Apply Force / Set Velocity (the "agents that have nothing to do with bonds" case); ON ⇒ the full center-based engine (soft-sphere + bond springs + growth ramp + auto-bond) and the panel reveals the Forces + Bonds rows (progressive disclosure). **`usesBondingPhysics(cfg) = useBondingPhysics ?? !customForcesOnly`** ([centerBased.ts](src/model/centerBased.ts)) — the `?? !customForcesOnly` fallback reproduces EVERY existing `.gcaproj` (absent both ⇒ `!undefined` ⇒ true ⇒ engine on, as before; legacy `customForcesOnly:true`/`false` map exactly) so there is **NO migration**. The worker (`runAgentStep`/`runAgentStructuralPhase`) gates soft-sphere (`engineForces = bonding`), bond springs, growth (`growthRate→0`), and auto-bond on the resolved flag; behaviour-preserving because the custom-force shipped models never used springs/growth/auto-bond anyway. Live recompile field (no `needsFullInit`). The hidden `momentum`/`maxSpeed`/`neighbourQueryRadius`/`timeStep`/`drag` are now surfaced too (an always-shown **Motion** section).
- **Per-layer "Simulate" + "Show" toggles** (the simulator **Layers** panel, docked in the right-side Agents section; runtime settings persisted in `genesisca_sim_settings`, NOT the model). **Simulate** gates `runStep`/`runStepWebGPU` (CA grid) and `runAgentStep` (agents) in the generation loop via a new `setSimLayers` worker message + the module booleans `simulateCells`/`simulateAgents` (default true) — freeze either layer mid-run (freezing agents also stops their cell-field deposit, which lives inside `runAgentStep`). **Show** is render-only (both 2D + 3D): `showCaGrid`/`showAgents` refs gate the DRAW (NOT the upload, so re-enable is instant) — 3D via `Viz3D.voxels`/`Viz3D.agents` in `gl3d.render()`, 2D via skipping the colour blit / `drawAgentsOverlay`. **A `Bonds` Show-only row** (`showBonds`, default ON, persisted like the others) toggles the BOND-LINE display on both paths — 2D gates the batched bond stroke in `drawAgentsOverlay`, 3D gates `renderBonds()` via the new `Viz3D.bonds` flag — and is rendered ONLY when the model's Bonds capability isn't Off (`resolveMaxBonds(model.centerBased) > 0`; bonds can't exist otherwise). Display-only: the bond springs keep simulating.
- **Agent brush DOCKED** (was a floating canvas overlay) — moved into the right-panel **Agents** `rightPanelSection` (gated `isAgentModel`), alongside the Layers toggles. All state/refs/handlers are component-scoped (no plumbing change).
- **Clip plane → clip INTERVAL** ([gl3d.ts](src/simulator/render/gl3d.ts)): `ClipPlane3D.value` → `{ lo, hi }` (world coords). Visible iff `lo ≤ w ≤ hi` — two cuts, the gap = the slab thickness. The 3D-view control is a **single dual-thumb range slider with a draggable band** ([ClipIntervalSlider.tsx](src/simulator/ClipIntervalSlider.tsx), controlled — emits `onChange(lo,hi)` into `clip3d`): drag a thumb to move ONE bound (resize the window); **drag the BAND to "swoop" a fixed-width clip window through the volume** (both bounds shift together, width preserved, clamped at the extents); a bare-track click jumps the nearer thumb. Replaced the two separate From/To `<input type=range>` sliders — more convenient for sweeping a set-width window. (Window-level pointermove/up listeners, not setPointerCapture, so a fast drag off the thin track keeps tracking.) Updated in ALL clipping shaders (voxel FS, voxel-pick FS, sphere FS, **sphere-pick FS** [previously unclipped], the new bond clip) + `setCommonUniforms`/`setSphereUniforms`.
- **Bonds now clip** (req 5): bonds render through the LINE program, which gained a world-pos varying + clip uniforms; `drawLines(..., clip)` applies the interval to the bond pass only (axes/grid/bounds/gizmo/brush-plane pass `null`). `renderBonds` passes the active clip.
- **Agents render ON TOP of the grid VOXELS in 3D — now an OPTION, and only vs the voxels** (req 8, refined in the `sim_agent_fixes` batch): `gl3d.render()` clears the depth buffer between the voxel pass and the bond+sphere pass, then RESTORES the helper overlays' depth (axes / floor grid / bounds / brush plane) via a colour-masked depth-only re-draw of `renderOverlays`+`renderBrushPlane` — so "in front" applies ONLY vs the voxels while a brush plane in front of the blob still occludes the spheres (it used to be swallowed by them). Agents keep depth-sorting among THEMSELVES (`gl_FragDepth`). The whole behaviour is the **"Draw agents in front"** checkbox (3D View panel, agent models only; default ON = historical; `Gl3DRenderer.setAgentsInFront`; persisted as `genesisca_sim_settings.agentsFront3d`) — OFF gives full normal depth for sparse grids where both layers should interleave.
- **Clip / brush-plane slider maxes track a simulator resize** (req 2): the 3D-View controls derive their dims from the LIVE `gridWidth.current || simWidth` (etc.), not the stale `model.properties.gridWidth` (the Resize button never dispatches `updateProperties`). An effect re-clamps `clip.lo/hi` + `plane.pos` into the new extent on a dims change.
- **Auto-orbit reverses** (req 3): the speed slider min `0.05 → -2` (0 = stopped); `yaw += speed·dt` spins the other way for negative speed.
- **Nearby-agent gathering** (req 9) ALREADY uses a uniform CSR spatial hash (O(N), 3D-aware, all 4 query sites) — the appropriate structure for near-uniform agent density (an octree would regress here). No structural change; only the stale `sim.worker.ts:runAgentStep` "all-pairs O(N²)" doc comment was corrected.
- Verified end-to-end in-browser (zero console errors): Boids agents freeze/unfreeze via Simulate-agents; the bonding toggle hides/reveals Forces+Bonds (Boids resolves OFF, Tissue ON); 3D Tissue clip interval slices voxels + spheres + **bonds** to a thin slab; Show-agents hides agents+bonds; resize depth 40→12 updates the clip max 20→6 (+ re-clamps the stale handle); auto-orbit yaw decreases at negative speed.

### Agent brush parity — shapes · Single/Area · Add/Remove/Edit (branch `polish_agents`)
The agent brush now MIRRORS the CA-grid brush (2D + 3D). Plan + illustrated mockup: [docs/PLAN_AGENT_BRUSH_PARITY.md](docs/PLAN_AGENT_BRUSH_PARITY.md) / `.html`. The lattice + the agent engine/compilers are **UNTOUCHED** — only [SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx) (brush UI + handlers), the `paintAgents` worker handler, and a new `decodeAttrValue` helper — so every simulation behaviour is byte-identical (JS↔WASM agent parity re-verified on all 9 samples; lattice unchanged; the brush is a pure UI layer that posts existing worker messages).
- **`AgentBrushMode` = `add`/`remove`/`move`/`edit`/`glue`/`cut`/`bond`** (session-only state, NOT persisted → no migration). `seed`→`add`, `kill`→`remove` (the `killAgents` worker message + the `genesisca_agent_seed_v1:` config key KEEP their names — the rename is UI-only). Button labels are the capitalized mode ids (`textTransform: capitalize`).
- **Shapes** — new agent-brush shape state (`agentBrushShape` + `agentBrushW/H/RingWidth/LineWidth`; circle/ring radius reuses `agentBrushRadius`), persisted in `genesisca_sim_settings`. The agent world is CONTINUOUS, so the footprint is a **world-unit GEOMETRIC test** (`agentShapeMetrics` + `shapeContains` + `agentsInShapeAt` + `agentSeedInShape`), NOT the cell stamp the CA-grid brush uses: rect = centred W×H box, circle = disc, ring = annulus |d−r|≤w/2, line = a two-click capsule (`agentLineMembers` / `agentSeedInLine`). Shape selector + adaptive size row in the panel (reuse `styles.brushShapeBtn`/`fieldRow`). The 2D cursor draws the shape outline as the negative-`difference` silhouette (Area scope) or the hovered-agent ring (Single scope).
- **Single / Area** — `agentBrushScope` is **DERIVED from the brush SIZE, NOT a toggle** (the old `[agentBrushScope, setAgentBrushScope]` state + persisted key + the Single/Area button pair were removed): `agentBrushShape === 'rect' ? (W > 1 || H > 1 ? 'area' : 'single') : shape === 'line' ? 'area' : (radius > 0 ? 'area' : 'single')`. So a **zero-size footprint** (circle/ring radius 0, rect 1×1) = **Single** (exactly one agent — Add-one at the cursor / Remove-nearest via `pickAgentAt` / Move-one / Edit-clicked); a **sized footprint** = **Area** (the whole footprint). It's a plain derived `const` in the render (the `agentBrushScopeRef` syncs to it each render, so the paint handlers read the current value); a **`scopeBadge`** (an uppercase Single/Area pill with `marginLeft:auto`) is rendered on the size row so the user sees the current mode next to the number. **The Shape + size rows are placed BEFORE the mode-button row** (define the brush, then pick the action). Line is always Area (a drawn segment; the move+line single override is separate). **Area-move = rigid group drag**: `agentGroupMoveRef` grabs the footprint's agents + the grab world-point at pointer-down, then each drag posts `moveAgents` with `start + foldedDragDelta` per agent (`pendingMovesRef` generalised to an array). The Shape/size block is hidden for Glue/Cut/Bond (inherently two-agent / area-scan). Single-Add places one per click (no drag-stream); Single-Remove drag-erases the nearest.
- **Edit mode** — reuses `ManualBrushPanel` (a THIRD instance) over `model.agentAttributes` PLUS synthetic geometry rows (ids `__geom_radius__` / `__geom_vx|vy|vz__` / `__geom_x|y|z__`; Z rows only in 3D), assembled by `agentEditPanelAttrs`. State `agentEditAttrs` keyed off `agentAttrSig` (NOT `cellAttrSig`), persisted under `genesisca_agent_edit_v1:`. **Single**: click an agent → `editPrefillIdRef` + `getAgentState` → `decodeAttrValue` prefills the panel VALUES (keeping the user's per-row `enabled` toggles) + highlights the target (`editTargetId`) → the **Apply** button writes to it. **Area**: click/drag the footprint stamps the checked rows onto every agent under it. `buildAgentEdit` splits enabled rows into `sets` (attrs via `encodeAttrValue`) + a `geom` object.
- **Worker (the ONE protocol change)** — the existing **`paintAgents`** IS the Edit primitive: `sets` is now OPTIONAL and it gained `geom?{radius,vx/vy/vz,x/y/z}` + `torus?` (radius → `radius`+`targetRadius`; velocity; position via the `moveAgents` `x`+`xNext` wrap/clamp discipline; the `vz`/`z` fields gated on `worldDepth>1`). Applies on JS / WASM / WebGPU-backed agents (baked-offset views — as `moveAgents` proves) with NO per-target code; `runAgentColorPass` already runs after. `decodeAttrValue` (inverse of `encodeAttrValue`, bool→`'true'`/`'false'`) added to [attrValueEncoding.ts](src/model/attrValueEncoding.ts).
- **Ctrl+LMB-drag resize FIX** — now resizes the AGENT shape when `brushTarget==='agents'` (it previously read/wrote the hidden CA-grid brush refs regardless of target — a latent bug). Both the 2D and 3D resize state machines branch on the target (`isResizingBrush.agent` / `resizeStart.agent`).
- **3D parity** — the same modes/shapes/scope/edit run in the gl-canvas handler. Area footprints are **VOLUMETRIC solids** around the plane-pick cell (`agentProj3d` projects an agent into the plane's free/fixed frame; circle→sphere [axis-independent → identical to the prior `killAgents3d`], ring→spherical shell, rect→box, line→3D capsule via two-click `agentLine3dAnchorRef` staging); circle Add reuses `agentSeedPoints3d` (ball / flat disc). Rigid group move drags on the plane (z-aware `moveAgents`). Single Edit picks via `pickAgent3d`→`instanceToSlot` and shares the panel/Apply. The grid-brush footprint cursor is suppressed when the brush targets agents. `seedAgents3d`/`killAgents3d` were REPLACED by scope-aware `addAgents3d`/`removeAgents3d` (+ `editAgents3d`, `agentsInShape3dAt`, `agentSeedInShape3dAt`, `agentLineMembers3d`, `agentSeedInLine3d`).
- **Affected-agent highlight** — during an AREA brush, every agent the footprint WILL touch is highlighted (a colour-coded ring + tint: Remove red / Edit purple / Move cyan), so the effect is visible before AND during the stroke. Applies to **Remove / Move / Edit** (the footprint) and **Bond** (its scan-radius disc, teal) — **Add is excluded** (it only spawns new agents, never touching the ones already there).
- **Bond visibility** — Bond had NO cursor (its `agentBrushRadius` scan radius was invisible → "can't see the radius") and looks inert on a non-bonding model. Fixed: Bond now draws a **dashed scan-radius circle cursor** (negative silhouette) + teal-highlights the agents in range (the `agentsInRadiusAt` disc — Bond ignores the shape; the radius = how far apart two agents can be and still auto-bond). Bond is otherwise unchanged — it scans pairs within `formDistance×(r_i+r_j)` under the radius and posts `formBondBatch` (verified: a drag posts thousands of candidate pairs). It only visibly holds on a model with bonding physics + `maxBonds ≥ 1`; the tooltip now says so. 2D: `agentAreaHoverIdsRef` is recomputed on hover/drag (group ids during a group-move, else `agentsInShapeAt`) and drawn in the cursor block; cleared on mouse-leave. 3D: `updateAgentHover` sets `hoverAgents3dRef` to ALL agents in `agentsInShape3dAt` (the plane footprint) instead of the single hovered agent. The Edit config also shows a **"⚠ Check a property below…"** hint whenever no row is enabled (an Edit click with nothing checked is a deliberate no-op — the hint + highlight explain why "nothing happened").
- **Cursor overlay LAYER + rAF-coalesced hover pipeline (the brush-cursor-slows-the-sim fix, `sim_agent_fixes` batch — SUPERSEDES the earlier playing-gated `scheduleCursorDraw`)**: the brush cursor now draws on TWO dedicated overlay canvases ABOVE the scene canvas ([SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx) `drawCursorLayer` + `cursorNegCanvasRef`/`cursorHlCanvasRef`): `cursorNeg` carries the white silhouettes (cell stamp outline, agent footprint/scan ring) composited with CSS `mix-blend-mode: difference` — the negative-cursor trick moved to the COMPOSITOR, no scene-pixel reads — and `cursorHl` carries the coloured highlight rings (hover/edit/area/glue) with normal blending. `draw()` stashes its transform in `viewXformRef` and re-calls `drawCursorLayer()` at the end of every scene render (pan/zoom/step keep the layers glued); cursor movement redraws ONLY the overlays, so it costs ZERO scene renders in every state AND the cursor stays fluid at display rate even when the sim renders at 1 fps (it used to freeze between sim frames while playing). THREE per-raw-pointermove costs were also removed: (1) the idle hover pipeline (screenToGrid + chip + the O(agents) `agentsInShapeAt`/`agentsInRadiusAt`/`pickAgentAt` scans) is coalesced to ≤1 run/frame (`processHoverWork`/`scheduleHoverWork` — the raw handler only records the pointer + runs the active-drag actions; the Shift-sweep drag computes its own `screenToGrid`); (2) the hovered-cell coords chip is a module-level EXTERNAL store + a tiny memoized `<HoverCoordsChip>` (`publishHoverCellInfo`) — it was React state that re-rendered the whole SimulatorView per cell crossing; (3) the 3D idle footprint hover (plane pick + footprint recompute + GL re-render on cell change) is rAF-coalesced like the agent pick (`hover3dRaf`). Measured: paused 60-move burst → **0 scene draws** (was one full redraw per frame); playing 234-move burst → sim draw rate unchanged (80→78/s). NB **recordings/screenshots no longer include the brush cursor** (it lives above the captured scene canvas — generally desired). Any new cursor visual belongs in `drawCursorLayer` (neg = silhouettes, hl = coloured), never in `draw()`.
- **Cursor visibility parity** — the agent-brush cursor (hover ring / footprint silhouette / Bond scan ring / affected-agent highlight) now (a) **clears when the pointer leaves the canvas** — the off-canvas idle bail in `handleMouseMove` + `handleMouseLeave` drop `agentCursorWorldRef` / `agentHoverIdRef` / `agentAreaHoverIdsRef` + redraw — and (b) honours the shared **"Show brush cursor"** toggle: a `showAgentCursor = brushTargetRef==='agents' && showBrushCursorRef` gate on the four cursor visuals, plus a **checkbox added to the agent-brush panel** reusing the cell brush's `showBrushCursor` state (the effect now `draw()`s so the toggle repaints immediately, for both cursors). The edit-target ring + glue/line staged anchors are NOT gated (they're selection / pending-action state, not the hover cursor).
- **2D environment background** (`bg2d {enabled,color}`, persisted in `genesisca_sim_settings`; `bg2dRef` = hex|null) — a **Background** control (checkbox + colour input, "Agents-only backdrop") in the agent common-controls (2D only; 3D keeps the existing `bg3d` in the 3D View panel). When the CA-grid layer isn't drawn (`!showGrid2d`), `draw()` fills the W×H world rect with the colour BEHIND the agents (tiled in infinity), so agents sit on a solid backdrop instead of the transparent page showing through. No-op while the grid is shown (its colours ARE the background). A dedicated effect repaints on change.
- **Topology-aware Layers/Brush-affects + `showGrid2d` gridCells gate** — the Layers matrix + the Brush-affects toggle now reflect which layers the model actually HAS (`gridCellsOn = topologyMode.gridCells !== false`, `isAgentModel = topologyMode.agents`). For an **agents-only** model (gridCells off) the **CA-grid Layers row is hidden**, the **Brush-affects toggle is hidden**, and an effect **forces `brushTarget='agents'`** (so a stale/persisted 'grid' can't point at a non-existent layer). Crucially `showGrid2d = gridCellsOnRef && (!isAgentModel || showCaGrid)` now factors gridCells, so an agents-only model's **background applies WITHOUT the user unchecking a "Show" box** (there is none) — the user shouldn't have to disable a grid that isn't there. (A grid-only model has no agents → the whole agent common-controls block is absent, so its "Agents" row question is moot.)
- **Brush respects the environment bounds when infinity is OFF** — `screenToWorld` (the AGENT-brush inverse; `screenToGrid` already did this) now returns **null** for a point outside the `W×H` world when infinity canvas is off, mirroring `screenToGrid`. Previously it returned raw out-of-bounds coords that the agent handlers wrapped-modulo back INTO the world (so clicking the letterbox margin acted on a cell inside + drew a cursor there). Now with infinity off the agent brush neither ACTS nor draws a cursor outside the environment; with infinity on (torus) it still wraps as before. All agent handlers already guard on `if (wpt)` / a `-1` pick, so the null propagates cleanly.
- **Verified in-app**: agents-only model hides the CA-grid Layers row + Brush-affects toggle and its background fills the environment (829k px) with no "Show" toggling; the Show-brush-cursor toggle hides/shows the agent cursor; the cursor clears when the pointer leaves the canvas; an Add click in the letterbox (infinity off) adds nothing (count 260→260) while an in-bounds click adds (260→261).
- **Main-area click restores keyboard focus (transport shortcuts)** — the simulator transport keydown handler ([SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx), on `document`) bails when `document.activeElement` is an INPUT/TEXTAREA/SELECT, so after the user edits a right-panel widget (a NumberField / checkbox / colour / select) the focus stays there and **Enter/Space/Esc stop working**. The 2D `handleMouseDown` now **blurs any focused form control on a main-canvas-area mousedown** (right after the `data-sim-overlay` guard, BEFORE any brush logic; mirrors the 3D gl-canvas `onDown` blur) → focus returns to `<body>` → the shortcuts fire. **GENERAL BY DESIGN** — it blurs WHATEVER form control is focused (`tagName INPUT/TEXTAREA/SELECT || isContentEditable`), so **any Agent-Brush / Layers / Background widget added later is covered with no extra wiring** — never enumerate widgets here. (Root cause: the cell-PAINT path never `preventDefault`'d its mousedown, so the browser blurred the input naturally; the **agent-brush path DOES `preventDefault`**, which suppresses that natural blur — hence the explicit blur. Do NOT `preventDefault` before the blur.) Verified: clicking the canvas after focusing a colour input moves `activeElement` INPUT→BODY, and a synthetic Space keydown then `defaultPrevented===true` (handler fired) vs `false` while an INPUT is focused.
- **Verified** (in-app, 2D Boids + a 3D-depth-24 Boids variant): the panel renders every control (mode row / shape / size / Single-Area / Add-config / Edit-config); Edit geometry rows gate 2D/3D correctly (Velocity/Position Z appear only in 3D); `paintAgents` geom applies radius/velocity/position (incl. z in 3D) — round-tripped via `getAgentState`; **area-edit end-to-end** (radius→5 on all 260 caught agents via a synthetic canvas mousedown — the 2D sim canvas uses native `addEventListener('mousedown')`, so synthetic MouseEvents drive it, unlike the gl-canvas); the **affected-agent highlight** renders (Remove+Area = red rings on the footprint agents; Add = none); the Edit no-property warning toggles; 3D renders 260 agent sphere instances; 0 JS errors. `tsc -b` + `npm run build` clean; the 9-sample agent JS↔WASM parity harness = 0 mismatches (engine + compilers untouched by the brush).

#### Agent-brush follow-ups — Alt+scroll mode cycle, 3D Glue/Cut/Bond, 3D brush OUTLINE cursor (`sim_agent_fixes` batch, [SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx) + [gl3d.ts](src/simulator/render/gl3d.ts))
- **Alt+scroll cycles the agent brush mode** (`AGENT_BRUSH_MODES` module const, shared by the mode-button row + the cycle): both the 2D `handleWheel` and the 3D GL-canvas `onWheel` intercept **Alt+wheel** when `isAgentModel && brushTarget==='agents'` → step the mode add→remove→move→edit→glue→cut→bond (down) / reverse (up), clearing the glue/line anchors. In 3D this is checked BEFORE the Ctrl-cycle-return + the plain-wheel zoom; in 2D before the Ctrl mapping-cycle. Verified in-app: Alt+wheel down add→remove, up remove→add.
- **3D Glue/Cut/Bond now WORK** (they used to fall through to Add — the reported "glue added agents in 3D" bug). The 3D `onDown` agent branch gained explicit handlers: **glue/cut** click two agents (`pickAgent3d`, plane-independent) → stage the first (a persistent white ring derived in `draw()` from `agentGlueAnchorRef` — so a mode switch clears it) → `formBond`/`breakBond`; **bond** drag-scans the plane ball (`scanBondPairs3d` = the 3D sibling of `scanBondPairsAt`; `active='agentBond'`, flushed via `flushBondBatch` on pointer-up). `agentsInRadius3dAt` = the 3D scan-ball collector; `updateAgentHover` gained a **bond** branch (highlight all agents in the scan ball) + covers glue/cut single-pick rings. RMB also cancels a staged 3D glue anchor. `formDistanceRef` feeds `scanBondPairs3d` the live contact multiplier (the 3D pointer effect captures its callbacks once, so it can't read `model.centerBased` fresh). Verified in-app via a hooked worker: glue→`formBond` (first click stages, no message; second posts), cut→`breakBond`, add→`seedAgents` (no regression).
- **3D brush cursor = a bounded analytic OUTLINE (both grid + agents), NOT a per-cell footprint** (fixes the "3D agents brush shows nothing" report AND the volumetric-brush **OOM**: a large volumetric footprint built millions of `pushCellCube` wireframes per frame). `buildBrushOutline3dSegs` (module fn) returns cell-space line-segment endpoint pairs for the shape at the hovered plane cell — a few circles / a box / a capsule regardless of brush size (free axes A=bw/halfW, B=bh/halfH, fixed N per plane: z→A col,B row,N layer; y→A col,B layer,N row; x→A row,B layer,N col — matching `mapStampToPlane`/`agentProj3d`; volumetric circle→3-great-circle sphere, rect→box, ring→2 circles, line→segment+dot; agent Bond→scan-radius circle, Glue/Cut→small dot). `gl3d.ts` gained `setBrushOutline`/`renderBrushOutline` (maps cell→world like `pushCellCube`, amber, depth OFF); `draw()` feeds it per-frame gated on plane-enabled + `showBrushCursor` + a hovered cell (grid vs agent params by `brushTarget`). `updateHover` no longer builds the per-cell `hoverCells3dRef` footprint (always `EMPTY_HOVER_CELLS`) — it only tracks the plane cell + returns cell-change; `footprintFor` was removed. Verified in-app: renderer draws the outline (0→522 amber px→0); the geometry builder's axis mapping / radius / sphere / rect / anchor are exact; a real agent-brush drag sets a 216-vert sphere outline (bounded).

### Verified
Force determinism (2 overlapping agents relax to the exact contact distance; a blob to a stable packing, 0 NaN); ~7000 steps/sec for 350 agents (the spatial hash); tension-axis division geometrically exact ((1,0)/(0,1) for horizontal/vertical stretch) with partner bonds partitioned; the headline "Morphogenesis — Growing Tissue" sample (12→1500 cells, ~5 bonds/agent, packed bonded sheet); the closed feedback (secrete→decay→sample round-trip). The three breadth samples: **Boids** self-organizes from disorder (velocity polarization 0.008 → 0.99 — a coherent flock); **Differential Tissue** grows 5→257 across 7 maturity stages with a STABLE stem pool (5) + self-limiting growth (specialization, not a blob); **Chemotaxis** (WASM field + JS agents, 0 errors) builds the field (max ~30) + aggregates agents from spread (178 bins) into clustered streams (99 bins, 2× density). **Scale**: the spatial hash keeps the JS engine O(N) — **2000 boids** (the heaviest per-agent work: neighbour query + 7 accumulators + force) run at **174 steps/sec** (5.7 ms/step), interactive; so the agent loop is NOT a JS bottleneck for realistic counts (Phase F's WASM port raises the ceiling past ~10k, it doesn't fix a bottleneck). Lattice path byte-identical on every PR (Game of Life / Life3D, all 3 targets) — the agent tier is additive + gated on `topologyMode.agents`.

### Phase F — PR6b-1: the WASM agent-loop ARCHITECTURE SKELETON (branch `agents_floating_cells`)
The FIRST internal split of capstone PR6b — proves the WASM agent loop ARCHITECTURE (memory sharing + the per-agent loop + dispatch + a few emitters) on a deterministic model BEFORE the large emitter port (PR6b-2 neighbour access → Boids; PR6b-3 field + division → Tissue).
- **A SEPARATE, minimal agent-WASM compiler** [src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWasm/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWasm/compile.ts) — `compileAgentGraphWasm(nodes, edges, model, agentLayout)` → `{ bytes, pages, supportedTypes }`. It REUSES the pure lattice ENCODER (`../wasm/encoder.ts`) but emits its OWN self-contained module — NO coupling to the lattice `WasmCompileCtx`/layout. The lattice WASM compiler (`wasm/compile.ts`/`wasm/layout.ts`) is **byte-unmodified** (proven: `compileAll` lattice bytes string-equal before/after on GoL/Life3D/snake/Gray-Scott/Boids/Tissue/Chemotaxis). Front-end = the SAME `expandMacros → collapseReroutes → canonicalizeAccessorEdges` the JS agent compiler runs.
- **The module**: `import "env" "mem"` = the wasmBacked AgentStore memory (PR6a — reads/writes hit the SAME bytes the JS engine reads at the `computeAgentMemoryLayout` baked offsets); `import "env" "pow"/…/"tanh"` = the 7 host math funcs (same funcIdx convention as the lattice, POW=0..TANH=6, NUM_IMPORTED_FUNCS=7); **`export "behaviour"(highWater: i32)`** → the per-agent loop `for (idx=0; idx<highWater; idx++) { if (alive[idx]==0) continue; const colorIdx=idx*4; <value DAG + linear flow chain> }`. **AW-HASH minimal**: the supported set needs no neighbour query, so `behaviour` takes ONLY `highWater` (no hash-array copy yet — `getNearbyAgents` is excluded by the gate; PR6b-2 adds it).
- **The minimal emitter set** (reads/writes the AGENT offsets, NOT the lattice offsets): `behaviourStep` (myX/myY[/myZ]/myRadius/myArea/myAge value-outs via `f64.load` at the x/y/z/radius/age offsets + idx*8; myZ gated on `is3dModel`), `getSelfPosition` (X/Y[/Z]), `getRadius`, `applyForce` (read-modify-write `+=` into forceX/forceY[/forceZ]), `setTargetRadius` (`f64.store` into targetRadius); PLUS the layout-agnostic value/flow utility on the f64 stack/locals: `getConstant` (number/bool), `arithmeticOperator` (Math, incl. the host transcendentals + the `/`÷0→0 guard), `statement` (Compare, numerical), `logicOperator` (AND/OR/XOR/NOT), `conditional` (if/else), `sequence` (transparent). Single canonical supported set `AGENT_WASM_SUPPORTED_TYPES`.
- **The gate** `isAgentGraphWasmSupported(model)` — TRUE iff Agents enabled AND EVERY (flattened) agent-graph node is in the supported set (+ Compare numerical-only, Get Constant number/bool-only). The central, honest predicate `agentTargetOf(cfg, wasmSupported)` consults: `agentTarget==='wasm'` resolves to `'wasm'` ONLY when supported, else clamps to `'js'` (the clamp stays the safe default). PR6b-2/3 widen `AGENT_WASM_SUPPORTED_TYPES` with no other change.
- **Dispatch** ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)): SimulatorView's `compileAgentModel` resolves the target + compiles the bytes (it has the model) and ships `agentTarget`/`agentWasmBytes` in the init + recompile messages (mirroring lattice `wasmStepBytes`). The worker, when `agentTarget==='wasm'`, backs the store on a `WebAssembly.Memory` (`createAgentStore({ wasmBacked:true })`, the SAME `computeAgentMemoryLayout` the compiler used), `instantiateAgentWasm`s the module against that memory, and in `runAgentStep` calls `agentBehaviourWasmFn(highWater)` INSTEAD of the JS `agentBehaviourFn` — **the force pass / structural phase below is UNCHANGED** (reads the same views). Any WASM failure → null the fn → JS fallback. `initAgents`/reset/recompile re-instantiate against the fresh memory (the orphan-WASM discipline: a fresh `WebAssembly.Memory` drops the old fn).
- **Sample**: [scripts/gen-agent-wasm-drift.mjs](scripts/gen-agent-wasm-drift.mjs) → `public/models/Agent WASM Drift Test.gcaproj` — 16 compact-seeded agents spring toward the world centre (`(centre − pos)·k → applyForce`), no RNG / neighbours / bonds, momentum 0, 2D. The deterministic JS↔WASM parity vehicle (`agentTarget:'wasm'`).
- **Verified**: tsc -b + `npm run build` clean; lattice WASM bytes + JS-agent behaviourCode byte/string-identical before/after (7 models); the gate correctly accepts the drift model + rejects Boids (PR6b-1 only); **JS↔WASM bit-parity** — END-TO-END through the real worker: JS-target vs WASM-target runs → bit-identical x/y across all 16 agents (0 mismatches, 0 NaN, 0 errors).

### Phase F — PR6b-2: the WASM agent loop runs Boids (widened emitter set; branch `agents_floating_cells`)
The SECOND internal split of capstone PR6b. Widens the SEPARATE [agentWasm/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWasm/compile.ts) so the **Boids — Flocking** sample runs on the WASM agent target with JS bit-parity. The lattice WASM compiler (`wasm/compile.ts`/`wasm/layout.ts`/`wasm/encoder.ts`) stays **byte-unmodified** (proven: `git diff` on `src/modeler/vpl/compiler/wasm` + `webgpu` is EMPTY; lattice GoL/Life3D compile clean on all 3 targets). The compiler now uses the byte-stable **`WasmEmitter`** abstraction (`../wasm/emitter.ts`, imported not modified) for clean local allocation + reuses the Expression AST emitter (`../expression/emitWasm.ts`).
- **Widened emitter set** (all read/write the AGENT offsets, mirroring the JS agent compiler 1:1): neighbour access — **`getNearbyAgents`** (fills a per-node scratch i32 id-array via the 3×3[×3] hash-bin stencil over the in-memory CSR hash + the all-pairs fallback, torus-wrapped exactly like the JS emit), **`forEachInArray`** (loops the scratch array, exposing element=agent-id + index as i32 locals), **`getAgentOffset`** (torus-shortest dX/dY[/dZ] + Distance via the world-bound fold), **`getVelocity`** (self when Agent unwired, else a neighbour Vx/Vy[/Vz]), **`getAgentPosition`** (X/Y[/Z]), **`getAgentRadius`**; **`getVariable`/`setVariable`** (SCALAR Local Variables = f64 function-locals, reset to initialValue at loop top — the boids cohesion/alignment/separation accumulators); **`expression`** (parsed AST → `emitWasm`); **`getRandom`** (float/integer/orientation/bool — NOT options mode); plus the PR6b-1 set (behaviourStep/getSelfPosition/getRadius/applyForce/setTargetRadius/getConstant/arithmeticOperator/statement/logicOperator/conditional/sequence).
- **AW-RNG** (B13 — the RNG-stream-divergence blocker): the shared xorshift32 `_rs` lives in an agent-memory cell (`layout.rngStateOffset`). The worker writes the global `rngState[0]` into the cell BEFORE the WASM `behaviour` call; the loop reads it once at function top (`_rs = u32[off] || 0x12345678`, mirroring JS exactly), advances per `getRandom` (same 13/17/5 constants on the in-register local), stores back at the end; the worker reads it back into `rngState[0]`. So a JS-target run and a WASM-target run advance the IDENTICAL stream. `getRandom` is emitted-once-per-agent (cached) → the RNG advances the same number of times as JS.
- **AW-HASH** (the keystone): the per-step spatial hash (`binStart`/`binAgents`) is **copied into reserved agent-memory views each step** (S10 — an O(nBins + liveCount) copy); the hash DIMENSIONS (valid flag + nBinsX/Y/Z + binSizeX/Y/Z + fieldW/H/D + torus flag) ride the **behaviour ARG list** (signature `(highWater, hashValid, nBinsX, nBinsY, nBinsZ : i32, binSizeX, binSizeY, binSizeZ, fieldW, fieldH, fieldD : f64, fieldTorus : i32)` — mirrored in `runAgentStep`, the ABI-mirror discipline). **Reserve bound + fallback**: `computeAgentMaxHashBins(W,H,D,range,defaultRadius,nbrQ)` (shared by compiler `agentMaxHashBinsForModel` + the worker `initAgents`) reserves `binStart` for the worst-case bin count (smallest bin edge, hard-capped 2²⁰); a per-step **fits-check** (`nBins+1 ≤ maxHashBins+1`) falls back to the JS behaviour fn for that step (warns once) — never silently wrong. `getNearbyAgents` writes its id-array to a per-node scratch slot (`AGENT_NEARBY_SCRATCH_SLOTS=4` reserved buffers of `maxAgents` i32; >4 nodes → gate rejects → JS). Both the RNG cell + hash + nearby-scratch are APPENDED to `computeAgentMemoryLayout` (existing offsets byte-identical → the drift-test path unaffected).
- **The gate** `isAgentGraphWasmSupported(model)` (the single source of truth `agentTargetOf` consults) now additionally requires: ≤ 4 `getNearbyAgents` nodes; every `forEachInArray.array` source is a `getNearbyAgents`; only SCALAR Local Variables (array variables + setArrayElement are PR6b-3); `getRandom` not options-mode. Field nodes / division-event / structural writes / `getCellAttribute` stay OUT → Chemotaxis (`secreteToField`) + Tissue (`getCellAttribute`) cleanly clamp to JS.
- **DEV `setRngSeed` worker message** (test-only, never sent by the app): forces the shared seed so a JS-target run + a WASM-target run advance the identical stream (the parity vehicle).
- **Verified**: tsc -b + `npm run build` clean; lattice byte-identity (zero `git diff` on `wasm`/`webgpu` compilers + GoL/Life3D compile clean ×3 targets); Boids `isAgentGraphWasmSupported===true` (2675-byte module, 11 node types); Chemotaxis/Tissue clamp to JS + run 0-error; **JS↔WASM bit-parity** — TWO pristine standalone workers (compact-seeded → deterministic, same RNG seed, momentum 0.9 full flocking), **N=200 steps, 260 agents → 0 diffs on x/y/vx/vy, maxAbs 0, 0 NaN** (positions bit-identical to full f64 precision); the PR6b-1 drift test still bit-exact (0 diffs/60 steps). NB: the parity test MUST use pristine standalone workers — the app-owned worker's `useEffect` re-init + the unserialized vx/vy (getState/loadState don't round-trip velocity, a PR4 gap) contaminate a same-worker JS-then-WASM comparison.

### Phase F — W1: the WASM FORCE PASS (the boost lever; plan `docs/PLAN_AGENT_WASM_WEBGPU.md`)
W1 ports the agent **force integrator** — the engine code that, even on the WASM agent target, ran in JS — to WASM. It is the HOTTEST per-step agent code (the per-neighbour-pair double loop): the 3×3(×3) hash-stencil neighbour pass (soft-sphere repulsion/adhesion + density) → bond springs → velocity integration (momentum, maxSpeed, drag, dt) → `xNext/yNext[/zNext]` → growth ramp. The handoff's PR6 intentionally kept it in JS to minimise the WASM surface; W1 is the deliberate delta.
- **A SECOND export `forcePass` in the SAME agent module** ([agentWasm/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWasm/compile.ts) `emitForcePass`): a faithful byte-for-byte port of `runAgentStep`'s force loop ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)), reading/writing the wasmBacked AgentStore at the `computeAgentMemoryLayout` baked offsets — the SAME memory the JS engine reads (zero glue). It runs RIGHT AFTER the behaviour (same step), reusing the in-memory hash already copied in for `getNearbyAgents` (no extra copy). f64 throughout ⇒ JS↔WASM bit-exact. **The structural phase + the hash build STAY in JS** (run once per step, not per neighbour-pair); `swapPositions` (a copy-into under wasmBacked views, B10) commits the new positions as before.
- **2D vs 3D** is a SEPARATE verbatim code path (the `if (is3d)` split mirrors the JS loop — NOT a branchless always-0-dz body — so the 2D arithmetic + stencil count are identical). The hash path skips the alive check (`buildSpatialHash` only bins alive agents); the all-pairs fallback adds it. Density counts regardless of bonding; the soft-sphere force + bond springs + growth-ramp magnitude are gated on the resolved `bonding`/`growthRate` (which the worker already resolves with the `usesBondingPhysics` toggle).
- **Mirrored scalar-config ABI** (`FORCE_PASS_PARAMS` ↔ the worker's `agentForcePassWasmFn(...)` call in `runAgentStep` — the param↔arg pair, the silent-desync class): `(highWater, hashValid, nBinsX, nBinsY, nBinsZ : i32, binSizeX, binSizeY, binSizeZ : f64, dtOverEta, muR, muA, range, momentum, maxSpeed, growthRate : f64, W, H, D : f64, bonding, torus : i32)`. `dtOverEta = dt/eta` is PRECOMPUTED (one division, bit-identical to JS's per-iteration `(dt/eta)` since the operands are step-constant). `bonding` gates BOTH the soft-sphere force (JS `engineForces`) AND the bond springs; `growthRate` carries the bonding gate (0 when off).
- **The torus position wrap uses a host `env.fmod = (a,b)=>a%b` import** — the EXACT JS `%` (fmod). WASM has no f64 rem opcode, and reconstructing `a - trunc(a/b)*b` rounds twice (drift). A "skip the wrap when n ∈ [0,span)" fast path is ALSO not bit-exact (for a non-power-of-2 span, JS's `(n+W)` rounds, so `((n%W)+W)%W ≠ n` in the low bits even for in-range n — verified: it diverged at ~1e-12). The unconditional host-fmod is the only bit-exact path. `fmod` is an 8th func import at funcIdx `NUM_IMPORTED_FUNCS` (=7), appended AFTER pow..tanh (0..6) so those indices are unchanged; the two module funcs are then `behaviour` (funcIdx 8) + `forcePass` (funcIdx 9), exported by name (the funcIdx shift is invisible to the worker, which calls by name). An inline musl-style i64 fmod (avoids the host call) is deferred — it needs ~15 new i64 encoder ops, and the wrap is once per agent, not per neighbour pair.
- **Worker dispatch** ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts) `runAgentStep`): `instantiateAgentWasm` now returns `forcePass` too (`agentForcePassWasmFn`, null on a behaviour-only module — nulled wherever `agentBehaviourWasmFn` is). After the WASM behaviour ran AND the in-memory hash was copied in this step (`forcePassReady`), the worker calls `agentForcePassWasmFn(...)` INSTEAD of the JS force loop (`if (ranForceWasm) {} else if (is3d) {...} else {...}`). Any WASM force-pass failure → null the fn + run the JS loop this step. The hash-overflow / behaviour-fallback paths set `forcePassReady=false`, so the force pass only runs when its in-memory hash is valid.
- **Verified** (a Node esbuild-bundled parity harness — DOM-free; the worker's force loop copied verbatim as the JS reference): **12/12 bit-parity variants — 0 mismatches, maxAbs 0, 0 NaN** over 60 steps × 200 agents across {2D, 3D} × {hash stencil, all-pairs fallback, torus, clamp, soft-sphere ON/OFF, maxSpeed cap, growth ramp grow+shrink}; an **end-to-end** `behaviour(WASM)+forcePass(WASM)` vs `behaviour+jsForceLoop` test — 0 diffs over 50 real steps (2D + 3D). `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` + `npm run build` clean. Lattice + JS-agent **byte-identity by construction** (only `agentWasm/compile.ts` + `sim.worker.ts` touched — the lattice/JS-agent compilers are untouched; `git diff --stat` = 2 files).
- **HONEST perf finding (the lever is small on this engine).** The plan hypothesised ~2–4× from the force pass. The measured **force-integrator-only** speedup (Node V8, hash build excluded) is **~1.0–1.25×**: ~1.25× in the clamp case, ~1.05–1.09× torus (the host-fmod boundary crossing — once per agent — erases part of the win), ~0.99× (a wash) for 3D-torus 2000 agents, up to ~1.15–1.17× at 10k. V8 already JITs this tight monomorphic numeric loop near-optimally, so the WASM win is modest — consistent with the plan's "neither target fixes a current bottleneck; both raise the ceiling," but BELOW its force-pass hypothesis. The port is bit-exact + zero-regression infrastructure (and worker-V8 / very-large-counts may differ from Node), but it is NOT the CA-grid-level speedup the goal sought for the FORCE LOOP specifically. (NB: the HEAVY-RULE benchmark below shows the boost DOES materialise for a heavy per-agent BEHAVIOUR rule — the force loop is just the wrong place to look.)

### Phase F — WASM AGENT WHOLE-TARGET PORT: full WASM agent coverage + JS bit-parity + the heavy-rule boost (branch `continue_agents`)
The full-coverage WASM agent push — `isAgentGraphWasmSupported` now accepts the ENTIRE agent-graph catalogue (the reject set is ZERO — WASM is Turing-complete + f64, so no node is un-portable), so a user picks the WASM agent target and models freely with **JS↔WASM bit-parity** (the f64 gold standard, UNLIKE WebGPU's f32/statistical parity). Touches ONLY `agentWasm/compile.ts` + `agentEngine.ts` + `sim.worker.ts` + `SimulatorView.tsx` (+ an `i32.trunc_sat` encoder const) — **lattice grid (2D+3D, all 3 targets) + the JS-agent + the WebGPU-agent compilers are byte-identical by construction** (`git diff --stat` vs the merge base touches none of them).
- **The WASM module = the BEHAVIOUR loop ONLY** (mirrors the WebGPU G4/G5 decision): the `divisionEvent` + `agentInit` roots stay JS-on-CPU (target-independent — the worker runs `agentDivisionFn`/`agentInitFn` over the SAME wasmBacked memory the WASM behaviour reads/writes, bit-exact). The gate checks ONLY the BEHAVIOUR-REACHABLE node set (`behaviourReachableNodeIds` = the `behaviourStep.do` chain + its transitive value cone), so a Tissue graph runs on WASM even though its divisionEvent subtree uses CPU-only nodes. `AGENT_WASM_CPU_ROOT_TYPES` (divisionEvent/agentInit/createAgent/addAgentToWorld) documents the JS-on-CPU set.
- **Added emitters** ([agentWasm/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWasm/compile.ts)): the **field bridge** (sampleField/fieldGradient[central-diff]/readCellsUnder[r-disk]/affectCellsUnder/secreteToField[bilinear splat] — 2D, torus-folded), the **agent-array tier** (getAgentsAttribute gather / filterAgents / joinAgents[union+intersection] / pickRandomAgent / pickNRandomAgents[partial Fisher-Yates] / getBondedAgents, all skipping empty(-1)/dead/oob ids; aggregate[sum/product/min/max/average/median[insertion-sort]/and/or] / groupOperator[+ weightedRandom + uniform random + index/position] / groupCounting / groupStatement over arrays — scratch via a per-agent bump pointer), `getCellAttribute`/`getAgentAttribute`/`setAttribute`/`updateAttribute` on the agent SoA (typed by `agentAttrKind`), the **structural-WRITE** nodes (divideAgent/formBond/breakBond/killAgent → request-flag stores into shared agent memory — the heavy mutation is the CPU structural phase), the **setters** (setVelocity/setAgentAttribute/setAgentsAttribute/setAgentPosition/Radius/Type — range+alive guarded), the **bond/self reads** (forEachBond[ragged bond loop]/getCurvature/getBondDegree/neighbourDensity), the **universal** value/flow nodes (switch[conditions+value, firstMatch+independent]/loop/valueSwitch[OP_SELECT relay]/getModelAttribute/lookupInteraction/interactionTableMap/proportionMap/interpolation/colorScale/categoricalColor/getColorConstant/arrayElement/arrayLength + getConstant tag + getIndicator/setIndicator/updateIndicator[ALL ops incl toggle/next/prev — the agent loop is SEQUENTIAL so these are well-defined, unlike WebGPU's parallel writers] + setCellLooks[plain, writes the agent colors buffer; glyph rejected — no per-agent glyph SoA]), and **array Local Variables** + setArrayElement.
- **External regions in agent memory** ([agentEngine.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentEngine.ts) `AgentLayoutExtras`): the cell field arrays (`_field_<id>`), model attributes, indicators, lookup tables ride NEW reserved regions appended to `computeAgentMemoryLayout` (after the PR6b-2 hash region, so existing offsets are byte-stable). The worker copies them IN before the WASM call (`copyAgentExternalRegionsIn`) and the field deposit + indicators back OUT after (`copyAgentExternalRegionsOut`) — Decision D-FIELD. **Layout lockstep (the +64-cell bug)**: the store's layout MUST equal the compiler's — same `maxHashBins` (both via `computeAgentMaxHashBins(W,H,D,range,dr,nq)`) + same extras; the worker derives `fieldTotal` from the LIVE grid dims, the compiler from the model — they agree. A mismatch shifts the field region and the WASM reads cell `idx + (regionΔ/8)` (the symptom: a noise field gives a grossly-wrong sample while a LINEAR field masks it — linear bilinear is exact regardless of which bracketing cells). `buildAgentLayoutExtras(model)` is the SINGLE source both call.
- **Sync agent mode under wasmBacked**: a distinct `attrWriteOffset` region (the WASM behaviour reads `attrOffset`, writes `attrWriteOffset`); `swapAgentAttrs` COPIES-INTO under wasmBacked (the B10/AW-SWAP view discipline — a reference swap would orphan the baked offset). `primeAgentAttrWrite` clones read→write before; `swapAgentAttrs` copies write→read after. (Async mode aliases write=read, byte-identical to before.)
- **Three parity bugs the JS↔WASM harness caught + fixed** (the gold-standard verification surfaced each): (1) **`emitLogic` UPPERCASE op** — `LogicOperatorNode` stores `'OR'`/`'AND'`/`'XOR'`/`'NOT'`; the emitter matched lowercase so OR/XOR/NOT fell through to AND (GoL-on-agents all-die, the SAME bug the WebGPU port hit); now `.toLowerCase()`s. (2) **field DOUBLE-wrap** — `emitSampleFieldAt`/`emitSecreteToField` wrapped (x,y) PAIRS, double-wrapping a corner coordinate shared between two samples; now `emitFieldWrapCoord` wraps each axis ONCE. (3) **field sample-before-deposit ORDER** — JS sink-hoists pure values to cell-top so a field read sees the PRE-deposit field; the WASM `preEmitAgentValues` pass (the WASM analogue of the WebGPU one, with `AGENT_VALUE_NO_HOIST` excluding RNG/mutable-storage/array-producer/forEach-element nodes — field reads ARE hoisted) now hoists the pure non-volatile value cone to agent-loop-top, matching JS exactly. (4) **`i32.trunc_sat_f64_s`** (a new encoder const, additive) for the f64→i32 value conversions — a NaN/Inf intermediate (aggregate.max over an empty array → -Inf; an expression with sin/sqrt) made the trapping `i32.trunc_f64_s` throw `float unrepresentable in integer range`; the saturating version is NON-trapping (NaN→0 like JS `x|0`) and bit-identical to plain truncation for finite in-range values (so the verified parity is preserved).
- **Verified — JS↔WASM BIT-PARITY on ALL 8 agent samples** (`scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs` — two stores, plain-JS + wasmBacked, same seed + hash + external regions, behaviour-fn-level): **0 mismatches over 150 steps, 64 agents** on Drift Test / Boids / Chemotaxis / GoL-on-agents / Ant Necrophoresis / the 3 Tissues (the force pass is W1-bit-exact; the structural phase is target-independent CPU → full-pipeline parity by composition). `scripts/check-agent-wasm-gate.mjs`: all 8 GATE✓ COMPILE✓ INST✓. `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` + `npm run build` clean. **Browser-verified** (the worktree dev server on :5180, cross-origin module import into the running Chromium): all 5 agent samples GATE+COMPILE+INSTANTIATE in the REAL browser WASM engine, and the Chemotaxis field bridge is **BIT-IDENTICAL** (browser JS engine vs browser WASM engine, 20 steps, 0 mismatches).
- **PERF — THE BOOST MATERIALIZES for heavy rules** (`scripts/bench-agent-behaviour.mjs` — a chromatography-in-agents-style HEAVY per-agent rule: nearby gather → 3 aggregates → a Lookup Table → 2 expressions → 2 compares → 2 conditionals, the BEHAVIOUR fn only): **WASM vs JS = 5.0× at 500 agents / 2.8× at 2000 / 2.1× at 8000**. So the user's hypothesis HOLDS: WASM pulls ahead of JS as per-agent RULE COMPLEXITY grows, exactly like the CA grid. The trivial Boids force loop was a wash (W1 — V8 JITs a tight monomorphic numeric loop near-optimally), but a heavy behaviour rule (megamorphic typed-array reads, many branches, a lookup table) is where the JS interpreter overhead shows and WASM's compiled bytecode wins 2-5×.
- **The FINAL `isAgentGraphWasmSupported` reject set — EMPTY (zero node bans).** The only clamp-to-JS is the structural per-node array-scratch-slot budget (`> AGENT_NEARBY_SCRATCH_SLOTS=4` simultaneous getNearbyAgents producers → JS, never silently corrupted) — a capacity gate, not a node ban. WASM has NO precision/mode exclusions (f64 + sequential), so median/uniform-random/toggle-indicators/async-attrs ALL work (unlike WebGPU's f32/parallel fundamentals). Stop Event now runs natively on the WASM agent behaviour (see "Agent Stop Event"). `setCellLooks` glyph is the one documented JS-side carve-out (no per-agent glyph SoA; clamps via the behaviour-reachable gate).

### Phase F — PR7 G1+G2+G3: the WebGPU agent-loop COMPILER + force shader (branch `agents_floating_cells`)
The SEPARATE WebGPU agent-loop compiler — the GPU sibling of `agentWasm/compile.ts`. Emits a WGSL `behaviour` module (G1+G2) + a standalone WGSL force-pass module (G3) over a GPU agent SoA. **The per-step runtime wiring + `agentTargetOf` enable (G3-runtime+G6) are NOW DONE** — see the next section. The Boids agent loop runs end-to-end on WebGPU (live in-browser flock verified, polarization 0.998).
- **A SEPARATE, additive compiler** [src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/) — `layout.ts` (`computeAgentWebGPULayout`: the GPU agent SoA = 7 strided storage buffers for the Boids subset, under the 8-binding floor so no `requiredLimits` raise; SEPARATE from the WASM `AgentMemoryLayout` — the GPU never shares memory, the worker uploads/reads back), `compile.ts` (`compileAgentGraphWebGPU` + `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported`), `forcePass.ts` (`emitAgentForcePassWGSL`). Touches NO lattice/grid WebGPU + NO existing agent JS/WASM path → lattice + JS-agent + WASM-agent byte-identity BY CONSTRUCTION (`git diff --stat` = only the DEV harness).
- **G1+G2 — the behaviour shader.** `dispatchCells` 2-D tiling, `highWater` a CONTROL UNIFORM (not baked — baking forces a per-gen recompile), the alive-skip + `idx>=highWater` guard. Universal nodes via f32 WGSL-string emit (reusing the lattice `emitWgsl` + select-divide-guard); the ~12 agent-node WGSL emitters (getNearbyAgents hash stencil + forEach + getAgentOffset torus fold + getVelocity/getAgentPosition/getAgentRadius + applyForce + setTargetRadius + setVariable). Per-agent PCG RNG keyed by `idx` (the lattice grid model — statistical parity, NOT bit-exact). Front-end = the SAME `expandMacros → collapseReroutes → CSE` the JS agent compiler runs. **Reconciled to the current base** (the prior 019375f reference pre-dated the Generic Agent Platform): the gate + all var sites read `model.agentVariables` (NOT the cell `variables`); the Compare op reads `cfg.operation` (the StatementNode key) NOT `operator` (a latent reference bug that would have emitted `==` for every non-equality Compare).
- **G3 — the WGSL force pass** ([forcePass.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/forcePass.ts)): the GPU sibling of `runAgentStep`'s force loop + `agentWasm` `emitForcePass` — the 3×3 hash-stencil neighbour pass (soft-sphere + density, torus-wrapped, all-pairs fallback) → velocity Euler (momentum/maxSpeed/dtOverEta) → wrap/clamp into the appended GPU `xNext`/`yNext` fields → age + growth ramp. Its own `ForceControl` uniform mirrors the WASM `FORCE_PASS_PARAMS` ABI. **`xNext`/`yNext` APPENDED to `AGENT_GPU_F32_FIELDS`** so the behaviour shader's field bases stay byte-identical (re-verified: 5780 chars, compiles clean with the new fields). 2D-only, f32. **Bond springs + division + the hash BUILD stay CPU/JS** (the GPU SoA carries no bond store; the gate excludes bonded/division/field models, so for the Boids headline the force pass is exact).
- **The gate** `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported(model)` — TRUE iff Agents enabled AND not 3D AND EVERY (flattened) agent-graph node ∈ `AGENT_WEBGPU_SUPPORTED_TYPES` (the Boids subset, ≤ 4 `getNearbyAgents`, scalar-vars-only, no options-mode getRandom, every forEach array source = getNearbyAgents). Mirrors the WASM gate + a 3D rejection. (G6: `agentTargetOf` now widens to `'webgpu'` when this gate passes — see the next section.)
- **Verified**: tsc clean; `compileAll(Boids).agent.webgpu` emits a 5780-char shader with exactly the 11 Boids node types; **both** the behaviour shader AND `emitAgentForcePassWGSL` compile with **0 errors + 0 validation errors** on a real `device.createShaderModule`/`getCompilationInfo`; `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported(Boids)===true`, `(Tissue/Chemotaxis)===false` (getCellAttribute/secreteToField + 3D clamp to JS). DEV harness ([compileHarness.ts](src/dev/compileHarness.ts)) gained an `agent.webgpu` surface.
- **THE SCALE BENCHMARK (the headline) — JS vs WASM, force integrator.** `scripts/bench-agent-force.mjs` (esbuild-bundled, DOM-free) times the force-pass hot loop (hash build excluded) at 2k/10k/50k/100k agents (customForces / Boids case): **JS vs WASM is a WASH at every scale — 0.99× / 0.90× / 0.98× / 1.03×** (JS steps/s 5590 / 866 / 99 / 33). V8 JITs the tight monomorphic loop near-optimally (consistent with W1's ~1.0–1.25×); the agent loop is O(N) via the spatial hash so it is NOT a JS bottleneck at interactive counts. **WebGPU is intentionally absent** — its per-step whole-SoA upload+readback (CPU-built hash) is a fixed overhead that dominates BELOW ~10k agents, so WebGPU for agents is a wash-or-REGRESSION at interactive counts and only possibly breaks even at the very largest counts (needs the wired runtime to measure). The milestone's honest conclusion: there is no agent-loop speedup to be had from a faster target at the counts that matter — the spatial hash already won that on JS.
- **STILL TODO (later):** G4 (the CPU structural round-trip → Tissue), G5 (the field bridge → Chemotaxis), PR7c (zero-copy / atomics) — all still clamp to JS via the gate. *(All three since DONE — G4/G5 below; PR7c = the GPU-residency round on branch `optimize`.)*

### Phase F — PR7 G3-runtime + G6: the WebGPU agent RUNTIME (Boids end-to-end; branch `agents_floating_cells`)
The runtime that makes the WebGPU agent target SELECTABLE + RUNNABLE — the GPU sibling of `instantiateAgentWasm` + the WASM `behaviour`/`forcePass` dispatch in `runAgentStep`. Boids flocks end-to-end on WebGPU (live verified: velocity polarization `|Σv|/Σ|v|` climbs 0.01 → 0.998 over ~200 steps, 260 agents, 0 worker errors, agents render + move).
- **A SELF-CONTAINED agent WebGPU runtime** [src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts) — its OWN device + buffers + the two compute pipelines (behaviour + force), SEPARATE from the grid's `webgpuRuntime.ts` (so a model can run a WASM grid + WebGPU agents, the Boids case). `createAgentWebGPURuntime(behaviourShader, forceShader, layout)` acquires a device, compiles + validates both WGSL modules up front, allocates 8 buffers, builds the two bind groups (behaviour: 7 bindings agentF32 rw / agentI32 r / agentAlive r / hashBins r / control uniform / rngState rw / agentColors rw; force: 4 bindings agentF32 rw / agentAlive r / hashBins r / forceControl uniform — DIFFERENT binding indices, each shader its own bind-group layout over the SAME underlying buffers), + the two pipelines. Helpers: `uploadAgentSoA` (pack the CPU AgentStore f32/i32/alive SoA into the strided GPU buffers each step), `uploadAgentHash` (the CPU-built CSR hash → the hashBins buffer; returns false on overflow → JS fallback), `uploadAgentControl`/`uploadAgentForceControl` (the two uniforms, field order MIRRORS the shader structs + the WASM `FORCE_PASS_PARAMS` ABI), `dispatchAgentStep` (behaviour then force in one command buffer, `dispatchCells(maxAgents,64)` 2-D tiling), `readbackAgentStep` (read the f32 SoA back + commit `xNext/yNext → x/y` + `vx/vy/radius/density/age` into the CPU store — the JS `swapPositions` analogue), `seedAgentRng` (seed the per-agent PCG ONCE at creation; the GPU advances it in place across steps, so re-seeding every step is NOT done — that would freeze the sequence). f32 + per-agent PCG ⇒ statistical parity, NOT bit-exact (the documented WebGPU constraint). 2D-only. **`destroyAgentWebGPURuntime` releases the GPUDevice** (`dev.destroy()`, mirroring the grid's `destroyWebGPURuntime`) — `createAgentWebGPURuntime` requests its OWN adapter+device per build, so every in-session rebuild (soft recompile / agent-target flip / reset) that tears down the prior runtime WITHOUT a `worker.terminate()` would otherwise leak a heavyweight device; a full model LOAD terminates the worker and reclaims devices regardless, so this is an in-session correctness fix (not the cross-load recording slowdown).
- **`runAgentStepWebGPU()`** ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)) — the GPU sibling of `runAgentStep`'s WASM dispatch (ASYNC: the readback awaits a `mapAsync`). CPU prep (reset forces, build the spatial hash, prime the sync attr buffer) → upload SoA + hash + the two control uniforms → `dispatchAgentStep` → `swapAgentAttrs` → `readbackAgentStep` → the CPU structural phase (a no-op for Boids: the gate excludes bonds/division/field). Returns whether the GPU ran (false → the caller runs the JS `runAgentStep()` for that step — hash overflow / device failure are never silently wrong). The bond springs + division + the hash BUILD stay CPU/JS (the GPU SoA carries no bond store); for the Boids headline the GPU force pass is exact.
- **The step-batch async branch** (sim.worker.ts `step` handler): the sync `for` loop can't `await`, so a JS/WASM grid + WebGPU agents model (Boids) routes to an async COPY of the batch loop when `agentTarget === 'webgpu' && agentWebgpuRuntime` — it awaits `runAgentStepWebGPU()` per step, runs the cell `runStep()` synchronously (the grid is JS/WASM here), then the colour pass + `sendColors`. The pre-existing sync JS/WASM-agent loop is UNTOUCHED (the new branch is gated + early-`break`s before it). (A WebGPU GRID + WebGPU agents model would route through the existing `webgpuActive` async branch — but Boids uses a JS/WASM grid.)
- **`agentTargetOf(cfg, wasmSupported, webgpuSupported)`** ([centerBased.ts](src/model/centerBased.ts)) gained a 3rd arg: a `'webgpu'` config resolves to `'webgpu'` ONLY when `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported(model)`, else clamps to `'js'` (the always-runnable fallback — NOT 'wasm'). The worker-side `resolveAgentTarget` (sim.worker.ts) is the safety net mirror: `'webgpu'` demotes to `'js'` if either shader is missing from the message.
- **Build/dispatch lifecycle**: SimulatorView's `compileAgentModel` resolves the target (`isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported`), compiles the behaviour shader (`compileAgentGraphWebGPUForModel`) + the force shader (`emitAgentForcePassWGSL`), and ships both WGSL strings + the layout dims (`agentWebgpuMaxAgents`/`MaxHashBins`) in the init + recompile messages (mirroring the WASM `agentWasmBytes` path). The worker `buildAgentWebGPUIfNeeded()` (async, monotonic build-token guarded — the orphan-on-reinit discipline) builds the runtime; `initAgents()` drops a stale runtime (a re-alloc invalidates it); reset/recompile rebuild. Any device/compile/dispatch failure nulls the runtime → the JS behaviour fn + JS force loop run.
- **The Properties radio** (PropertiesPanelContent.tsx) WebGPU agent option is now ENABLED (not `disabled`) + its hint reflects live support (`isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported(model)`): "runs on WebGPU (the Boids node subset, 2D)" when supported, else "falls back to JS (bonds / division / field stay CPU)".
- **Byte-identity** (verified `git diff --stat` vs the compiler-merge base `8d7c919`): the lattice grid compilers (`compile.ts`/`wasm/`/`webgpu/`) + the agent JS/WASM compilers (`compileAgentGraph`, `agentWasm/`) + the agent WebGPU COMPILER (`agentWebgpu/`) are ALL UNTOUCHED — the only diff is `centerBased.ts` (the 3rd `agentTargetOf` arm), `PropertiesPanelContent.tsx` (the radio), `SimulatorView.tsx` (the webgpu compile path in `compileAgentModel`), `sim.worker.ts` (the additive webgpu-agent branch, gated on `agentTarget==='webgpu'`), + the new `agentWebgpuRuntime.ts`. So lattice + JS-agent + WASM-agent behaviour is identical by construction for every non-WebGPU-agent model.
- **Verified**: `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` + `npm run build` clean; live in-browser (Boids, agentTarget `webgpu`, WebGPU available) — gate `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported===true`, `agentTargetOf===webgpu`, shader 5780 chars; 260 agents flock (polarization 0.01→0.998 over 200 steps, stable over 400+ steps, 0 worker errors), render as cyan dots, the Properties WebGPU radio is enabled + selected.
- **STILL TODO:** G4 (structural round-trip → Tissue), G5 (field bridge → Chemotaxis), PR7c (zero-copy / atomics) — clamped to JS by the gate. A HEAVY-per-agent-rule benchmark to show a WASM/WebGPU win (the simple Boids force loop is O(N) via the hash, so no agent-loop speedup from a faster target at interactive counts — the upload/readback overhead means WebGPU is a wash-or-regression below ~10k agents; it only helps at very large counts or with much heavier per-agent compute). *(All since DONE — G4/G5 below, the heavy-rule benchmark in the WHOLE-TARGET PORT, and PR7c residency in the perf round on `optimize`, which removes the per-gen upload/readback for eligible models.)*

### Phase F — PR7 G5: the WebGPU agent FIELD BRIDGE (Chemotaxis end-to-end; branch `agents_floating_cells`)
The closed agent↔grid morphogen feedback (Decision D-FIELD) now runs on the WebGPU agent target — **Chemotaxis - Aggregation** flocks-and-aggregates on WebGPU. Additive over the G3-runtime; the lattice grid + JS/WASM-agent + the **Boids WebGPU** path are byte-identical (the field buffers are created ONLY when the model has agent-accessible cell attrs → a no-field Boids shader stays 5780 chars).
- **Two conditional field bindings on the behaviour shader** ([agentWebgpu/{layout.ts,compile.ts}](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/compile.ts)): `fieldRead` (binding 7, READ-ONLY snapshot of the cell field at step start — all agents read the same pre-deposit values = a true parallel snapshot, a documented difference vs the JS path's sequential partial-deposit read, harmless for diffusion) + `fieldDeposit` (binding 8, `array<atomic<u32>>`, an f32-bitcast atomic-CAS accumulator per op set/add/sub/max/min so parallel agents writing the same cell don't race). The 5 field-node WGSL emitters — `sampleField` / `fieldGradient` (central-diff) / `readCellsUnder` (r-disk aggregate via `fieldSampleBilinear`); `affectCellsUnder` (r-disk) + `secreteToField` (4-cell bilinear splat via `fieldDepositCell`) — mirror the JS emitters' 2D math (f32, statistical parity).
