- **Runtime** ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts)): the two buffers + `uploadAgentField` (snapshot → fieldRead, prime fieldDeposit with the current field) + `readbackAgentField` (deposit → the cell read buffer). The worker's `runAgentStepWebGPU` ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)) does the CPU round-trip: upload the field BEFORE the dispatch, read the deposit back into `readAttrs[id]` AFTER (gated on `fieldReadLen||fieldWriteLen > 0`) so the cell CA step incorporates it. `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported` now accepts the 5 field nodes; the worker derives the field spec from `fieldSpecs` (= `cellFieldAttrsOf`; readWrite subset = `cellFieldWriteAttrsOf`), the SAME order the shader compiled against.
- **Live-verified** (in-browser, `agentTarget:'webgpu'`): gate `true` + `agentTargetOf==='webgpu'`, shader 2258 chars (`secreteToField`+`fieldGradient` present); the field BUILDS and the 220 agents AGGREGATE (220 spread bins → ~93 clustered, local density 1 → 8, ~2.4× concentration), **0 worker errors**; **Boids re-verified still flocks on WebGPU (polarization 0.998)**. (Recovery note: the implementing session crashed on its final API response with the work uncommitted-but-correct; recovered + independently re-verified.)
- **STILL DEFERRED** (after G4 — most since DONE by the WHOLE-TARGET PORT + the FULL-COVERAGE CLOSE-OUT): the agent-array tier + `getAgentAttribute`, 3D agents on WebGPU, and the 3D field bridge are all DONE; a glyph setCellLooks is now a parity no-op (un-gated). `stopEvent` on the agent behaviour is now DONE (see "Agent Stop Event"). PR7c (GPU residency) SHIPPED in the perf round on `optimize` — see "Agent-engine performance review round".

### Phase F — PR7 G4: the WebGPU agent STRUCTURAL ROUND-TRIP (Tissue end-to-end; branch `agents_floating_cells`)
The CPU structural phase (bonds / division eigensolve / death / auto-bond) + the division-event fn are **target-INDEPENDENT** (pure CPU/TS in [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts) `runAgentStructuralPhase`/`runDivisionEvent` + [agentEngine.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentEngine.ts) `divideAgent`) and run unconditionally. So G4 = (a) emit the structural-WRITE nodes on the GPU as per-agent REQUEST-FLAG stores + (b) read the request buffers back to CPU before the structural phase, mirroring how G5 added the field round-trip. **Morphogenesis - Differential Tissue** (division + bonds + maturity differentiation) now runs on the WebGPU agent target. The eigensolve + the divisionEvent fn stay JS on every target (NO WGSL division shader).
- **Request buffers ride the existing `agentF32` (binding 0) — ZERO new bindings** ([agentWebgpu/layout.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/layout.ts)): the 9 structural-request fields (`divideRequest`/`divideAxisX`/`divideAxisY`/`divideAsym`/`bondFormReq`/`bondFormL`/`bondFormK`/`bondBreakReq`/`killRequest`, `AGENT_GPU_REQUEST_FIELDS`) are appended to `AGENT_GPU_F32_FIELDS` AFTER `xNext`/`yNext` (so the force-shader bases stay byte-identical), stored as f32 (flags are small ints / an agent-id+1, exact ≤2²⁴; the axis/asym are float). The behaviour-shader emitters ([agentWebgpu/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/compile.ts) `compileFlowNode`): `divideAgent`→`agentF32[drBase+idx]=1.0` + axis (`0.0` when unwired → the engine resolves the tension axis: `divideAgent` resolves it whenever the axis is non-finite OR `(0,0)`, so `0.0` is behaviour-equivalent to the JS path's NaN default — and MUST NOT be a NaN literal/bitcast, see the gotcha in Verified) + asym; `formBond`/`breakBond`→`(target|0)+1` + restLength/stiffness; `killAgent`→`1.0`.
- **User AGENT attributes on the GPU** ([layout.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/layout.ts) `agentAttrIds`/`agentAttrBase`): each `agentAttrsOf` id gets its own `agentF32` run appended after the request fields. `getCellAttribute`→`agentF32[attrBase+idx]` (f32; int/tag/bool round-trip exactly); `setAttribute`→write back with `round(...)` for int/tag/bool. `computeAgentWebGPULayout` gained a 4th `agentAttrIds` param; the SimulatorView compile (`compileAgentGraphWebGPUForModel`, uses `agentAttrsOf(model)`) + the worker rebuild (`buildAgentWebGPUIfNeeded`, uses `store.attrSpecs`) derive the SAME ordered list (the layout-match invariant).
- **Universal nodes Tissue's behaviour uses** (new emitters): `categoricalColor` (multi-output palette select, shares the lattice `readCategoricalEntries`/`readCategoricalDefault`); `setCellLooks` (per-agent packed `agentColors[idx]` = `r|g<<8|b<<16|a<<24`, PLAIN mode only — a glyph setCellLooks clamps to JS; the `__current__`/any mapping writes unconditionally since the agent loop has one viewer); `neighbourDensity`→`agentF32[densityBase+idx]`; `getBondDegree`→`f32(bondCount)`.
- **Runtime** ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts)): `uploadAgentSoA` zeroes the request runs each step (a fresh per-step request slate) + uploads the agent attrs from `s.attrRead`; `readbackAgentStep` now ALSO copies the f32 SoA back into the engine's CPU request arrays (`s.divideRequest`/`s.bondFormReq`/…, flags rounded to int), the agent-attr runs into `s.attrWrite` (int kinds rounded), and the packed `agentColors` (a 2nd `copyBufferToBuffer`) into `s.colors`. The worker's `runAgentStepWebGPU` reorders to **dispatch → readback (attrs→attrWrite) → swapAgentAttrs (→attrRead) → structural phase** (the swap MUST follow the attr readback; in async agent mode `attrWrite` aliases `attrRead` so the swap is a no-op and the readback lands directly in `attrRead` — Tissue is async).
- **The gate checks ONLY the BEHAVIOUR-REACHABLE node set** (`behaviourReachableNodeIds` — the `behaviourStep.do` flow chain + its transitive value cone): the `divisionEvent` + `agentInit` roots are compiled SEPARATELY on CPU/JS (target-independent), so a Tissue graph whose divisionEvent subtree uses a node the shader can't emit (e.g. an extra `setAttribute`/`expression` on a daughter) still runs on WebGPU. The compiler's defensive loop + the getNearbyAgents-slot assignment are likewise scoped to reachable nodes. (Mirrors how the JS/WASM compilers walk one root at a time.)
- **`migrateForHarness`** ([compileHarness.ts](src/dev/compileHarness.ts)) now runs `migrateAgentAttributeSplit` + `migrateVariableScopeSplit` (mirrors LOAD_MODEL) — so the dev harness sees the split agentAttributes (Tissue's `maturity` moves cell→agent), matching the loaded model.
- **Byte-identity**: lattice grid + JS/WASM-agent + the no-structural-node Boids/Chemotaxis WebGPU shaders are unchanged (the request/attr runs only EMIT when a structural / Get-Set-Attribute node is reached; the layout's bigger `f32Len` is a buffer-size change, not a shader-string change). The force shader reads only the leading static fields, whose bases are unchanged.
- **Verified**: tsc + `npm run build` clean; the Tissue WebGPU shader compiles (gate `true`, target `webgpu`; `maturity` read/write at `agentF32[46200u+idx]`, the `divideRequest` flag store + NaN axis, the categorical palette select, `agentColors[idx]=` packed write, `neighbourDensity` read all present); Boids + Chemotaxis still gate `true` / compile with no request emit; the engine structural primitives consume the request values (the `(0,0)` GPU axis → the eigensolve resolves a finite tension axis on a bonded cluster, division grows the store, bonds partition, daughter inherits maturity). **LIVE-verified in the browser** (`agentTarget:'webgpu'`): gate `true`, target `webgpu`, behaviour shader compiles + builds with NO fallback; Tissue **GROWS 5 → ~305 agents** (bonds 5 → ~841, self-limiting at ~290-305 = contact inhibition), **maturity differentiates** (the categorical green-stem→magenta-mature coloring renders), 0 worker errors; Boids (0.998) + Chemotaxis (aggregation) re-verified still run on WebGPU (no regression — the structural emit is gated). **GOTCHA (the bug live-verification caught — the headless check could not):** the unwired divide axis MUST emit `0.0`, NOT a NaN — `bitcast<f32>(0x7fc00000u)` has no literal `nan` in the shader STRING (so a string scan passes), but **WGSL/Naga CONSTANT-FOLDS the bitcast into a NaN constant and rejects it** at `device.createShaderModule` ("value nan cannot be represented as 'f32'"), so the behaviour shader silently failed to build and **fell back to JS**. Any future "magic sentinel" in a WGSL agent/grid emitter must be a representable f32 (the engine's `non-finite OR (0,0)` check makes `0.0` the safe sentinel here). A passing TS-compile / shader-string check is NOT proof a shader RUNS — only a real `device.createShaderModule` (i.e. a browser run) is.

### Phase F — PR7 WHOLE-TARGET PORT: full WebGPU agent coverage (70 node types; branch `continue_agents`)
The full-coverage push — `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported` now accepts the ENTIRE agent-graph catalogue (**70** node types after `setAgentType`'s removal) minus the genuine fundamentals, so a user picks the WebGPU agent target and models freely (the CA-grid end state). Touches ONLY the agent-WebGPU files + the SimulatorView/worker agent-webgpu wiring — **lattice grid (2D+3D, all 3 targets) + JS/WASM-agent byte-identity by construction** (`git diff --stat` = `agentWebgpu/{compile,forcePass,layout}.ts` + `agentWebgpuRuntime.ts` + the gated SimulatorView/worker branches).
- **Added emitters** ([agentWebgpu/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/compile.ts)): the rest of the agent-array tier (`getBondedAgents`), `getCurvature`, `forEachBond` (the ragged bond store); the agent setters (`setVelocity`/`setAgentAttribute`/`setAgentsAttribute`/`setAgentPosition`/`setAgentRadius` — `setAgentType` was **REMOVED**), `updateAttribute`; the **universal** value/flow nodes — `groupOperator` (sum/product/min/max/avg/count/`weightedRandom`), `groupCounting`, `groupStatement`, `switch`, `loop`, `valueSwitch`, `getModelAttribute`, `lookupInteraction`, `proportionMap`, `interpolation`, `colorScale`, `getColorConstant`, `arrayElement`, `arrayLength`, `getConstant` tag, `getIndicator`/`setIndicator`/`updateIndicator` (atomics); **array Local Variables** + `setArrayElement`.
- **3D agents** ([agentWebgpu/layout.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/layout.ts) `AGENT_GPU_F32_FIELDS_3D` + `forcePass.ts`): the z fields (`z`/`vz`/`forceZ`/`zNext`/`divideAxisZ`) appended only when `gridDepth>1` (2D byte-identical); the getNearbyAgents/force-pass hash stencil becomes 3×3×3 with the `(nbz·nBinsY+nby)·nBinsX+nbx` bin index; `getAgentOffset`/`getSelfPosition`/`getAgentPosition`/`getVelocity`/`applyForce` get the z axis + dz torus fold against `control.fieldD`; the Control + ForceControl uniforms gain `maxBonds`/`nBinsZ`/`binSizeZ`/`fieldD`. The 3D agent loop runs on WebGPU; the 3D RENDERER (gl3d spheres) is still Phase E, so 3D agents are headless-correct but not yet drawn.
- **New conditional GPU bindings** ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts)): `auxF32` (9 — model attrs + lookup tables, the SoA-flat upload), `indicators` (10 — `array<atomic<u32>>`), `bondStore` (11 — interleaved `[partnerId, restLengthBits]` per slot, stride `maxBonds·2`). **Declared + bound ONLY when an emitter REFERENCES it** (the compiler ships `usesBondStore`/`usesIndicators`/`usesAux`; SimulatorView→worker→`createAgentWebGPURuntime` thread them) — NOT merely when the layout reserved the region: a declared-but-unused storage global is stripped by Naga, so its bind-group entry mismatches the pipeline's reflected layout (**the GoL-on-agents bug** — `maxBonds=2` reserved a bond store the totalistic rule never touches). The runtime adds `uploadAgentAux`/`uploadAgentIndicators`/`readbackAgentIndicators`/`uploadAgentBondStore`; the SoA upload/readback cover the 3D z fields.
- **`preEmitAgentValues`** (the cross-branch WGSL scoping fix): the agent compiler emits values inline at first use, so a PURE value read in MULTIPLE sibling branches (an Ant Necrophoresis `readCellsUnder` tested in both an `if`-branch and its `else`) was declared block-scoped inside the FIRST branch → "unresolved value" at `createShaderModule`. The pass walks the behaviour value cone and hoists every PURE, non-volatile node (incl. the hoistable sub-cone under an RNG-tainted consumer) to function-top, so the value cache serves every branch; skips RNG / mutable-storage / array-producer / forEach-element nodes; multi-output nodes pre-emit each consumed output port.
- **`emitLogic` OR→AND bug** (the GoL-on-agents all-die): `LogicOperatorNode` stores its op UPPERCASE (`'AND'`/`'OR'`/`'XOR'`/`'NOT'`) but `emitLogic` matched lowercase → every `OR`/`XOR`/`NOT` fell through to `AND`. GoL's birth rule `(sum==3) OR (sum==2 AND alive)` silently became `(sum==3) AND (…)` = always false → all cells die. Now `.toLowerCase()`s the op. (Boids/Tissue/Chemotaxis use AND, so it stayed masked.)
- **SimulatorView ships the FULL `r.layout`** (not just the dims) + `usesI32Write` + the usage flags, so the worker binds/uploads against the EXACT compiled layout (no recompute mismatch) — the layout carries the universal-node region bases (auxF32/indicators/bondStore/3D z).
- **The FINAL reject set — only the documented fundamentals** (post the 3D-field + glyph close-out): (1) `aggregate`/`groupOperator` `median` + uniform `random` (no sort / per-cell-pick path — same as the lattice WebGPU grid; `weightedRandom` IS supported); (2) `updateIndicator` `toggle`/`next`/`previous` (order-dependent under parallel writers — same as the grid); (3) > 6 agent-array producers (the `var<function>` register budget — a CAPACITY gate, not a node ban); (4) behaviour-reachable **cross-agent OVERWRITE writers with a WIRED non-spawn-handle id** (`setAgentAttribute`/`setAgentsAttribute`/`setAgentPosition`/`setAgentRadius` — sequential-order-dependent writes into another thread's slot; added with the Cross-Agent Write Semantics gate, see that section). The orientation/variegation nodes never reach the Agents graph (variegation is grid-only); `stopEvent` on the agent behaviour is now SUPPORTED (a dedicated stopFlag atomic buffer, binding 13, atomicCompareExchangeWeak + readback merge — see "Agent Stop Event"). f32 precision bars NO node; async agent mode WORKS (Tissue is async; the agent loop is single-buffer — on the GPU the mode only affects CPU buffering + residency eligibility; a parallel dispatch is snapshot-reads + thread-own-writes either way). **Glyph `setCellLooks` + 3D field nodes are NO LONGER rejected — see the FULL-COVERAGE CLOSE-OUT below.**
- **GPU-LIVE-verified in the browser** (real `device.createShaderModule` + 0 validation errors AND the running worker on `agentTarget:'webgpu'`, 0 worker errors, NO JS fallback): **Boids 2D** flocks (polarization 0.999); **3D Boids** flocks (3D pol 0.023→0.982, z spans the 32-deep volume, force pass integrates z); **Chemotaxis** aggregates (occupied bins 174→106, sum-of-squares 318→670 ≈ 2× clustering via the field bridge); **GoL-on-agents** evolves like Conway (344→155→107 alive, stabilising into still-lifes/blinkers — the OR-fix); **Tissue** grows 5→274 agents / 745 bonds with maturity differentiation (7 distinct colours, self-limiting at contact inhibition); **Ant Necrophoresis** runs 0-error + matches the JS baseline. The lattice grid (Game of Life 2D + Life3D) compiles clean on all 3 targets (no regression).

### Phase F — PR7 FULL-COVERAGE CLOSE-OUT: WebGPU agents 3D field bridge + glyph setCellLooks (branch `continue_agents`)
The last two WebGPU-agent gaps closed (the FULL-COVERAGE mandate). Touches ONLY `agentWebgpu/compile.ts` (+ a defensive guard in `agentWasm/compile.ts`) — lattice grid (all 3 targets) + JS/WASM-agent + the agentWebgpu RUNTIME are byte-identical by construction.
- **3D field bridge (trilinear) — DONE + GPU-live-verified.** The 2D bilinear field bridge already ran on WebGPU; the layout (`fieldTotal = W·H·D`, `fieldReadBase`/`fieldWriteBase` strided by `fieldTotal`) + the control struct (`fieldD`) + the runtime round-trip (`uploadAgentField`/`readbackAgentField` copy the whole flat field array) were ALREADY 3D-correct — only the WGSL emitter MATH was 2D-locked. `emitFieldHelpers(is3d)`: a 3D model emits **`fieldSampleTrilinear`** (8-corner read, index `(z·H+y)·W+x`, z-axis torus/clamp) instead of `fieldSampleBilinear`; `fieldSampleCall()` picks bilinear/trilinear + threads the agent z. `sampleField`/`fieldGradient` (the latter emits the `dz` central-difference port in 3D — the node already exposes `dz`) sample trilinearly. `readCellsUnder`/`affectCellsUnder`: the r-disk becomes an r-**SPHERE** (a layer loop + 3D torus-SHORTEST membership fold + the 3D field index, mirroring the 3D JS nodes; the 2D paths stay byte-identical). `secreteToField` = an 8-cell trilinear splat (weights sum to 1). `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported` dropped the field-in-3D clamp. The mirror 3D math matches SampleFieldNode/FieldGradientNode/ReadCellsUnder/AffectCellsUnder/SecreteToFieldNode JS (statistical parity — f32/per-cell PCG). **GPU-live-verified** (3D Chemotaxis 32×32×16 on `agentTarget:'webgpu'`): gate `true`, `agentTargetOf===webgpu`, the behaviour shader (incl. `fieldSampleTrilinear` + z-splat) AND the 3D force pass compile **0 errors / 0 validation** on a real device; 0 worker errors, the 3D field BUILDS (chemical max ~4.5, 16368/16384 cells non-zero — the trilinear secrete spread through the volume) and the 220 agents span the full z depth (z 0.13..15.74).
  - **3D spatial-hash reserve fix (surfaced by the GPU run):** `agentMaxHashBinsForModelGPU` computed `nx·ny` only — it DROPPED the Z dim, so the 3D 3×3×3 hash (108 bins) overflowed the 36-bin reserve and the worker's per-step fits-check fell back to JS EVERY step. Fixed to `nx·ny·nz` (mirrors the shared `computeAgentMaxHashBins`); 2D (D=1→nz=1) byte-identical. Propagates through `r.layout.maxHashBins` → the shipped `agentWebgpuLayout` → the worker fits-check.
  - **WASM-agent 3D field is NOW PORTED — see "Phase F — WASM AGENT 3D FIELD BRIDGE" below.** (The old defensive `isAgentGraphWasmSupported` field-in-3D clamp is removed; a 3D-field WASM-agent model runs on WASM with JS bit-parity.)
- **Glyph `setCellLooks` un-gated (parity no-op) — DONE + GPU-live-verified.** Key finding: the AGENT render path (`drawAgentsOverlay`) draws filled circles ONLY — NO glyph overlay on ANY target. On JS/WASM a glyph setCellLooks writes the per-AGENT glyph buffer, which for the agent loop is the **length-0 `GLYPH_NOOP` buffer** (a silently-dropped typed-array write) = a no-op. So a glyph setCellLooks is HARMLESS, not a fundamental — it was only the WebGPU gate that banned the glyph-without-background case. `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported` dropped that reject; `emitSetCellLooks` is unchanged (writes the background colour when `setBackground`, and the glyph-only-no-background case early-returns = the SAME no-op as JS/WASM — NO per-agent glyph buffers added, so NO cross-target rendering divergence). Adding real per-agent glyph rendering would be a NEW cross-target feature (AgentStore/snapshot/render + JS/WASM/WebGPU parity), not a coverage gap. **GPU-live-verified**: a Boids model with a glyph (no-background) setCellLooks — the previously-BANNED case — now gates `true`, `agentTargetOf===webgpu`, the shader compiles 0 errors (with NO `glyphCode`/`glyphColor` buffer references — the no-op confirmed), and Boids flocks end-to-end on WebGPU (polarization 0.81+, 260 agents, 0 worker errors).
- **Byte-identity**: `git diff --stat` vs `continue_agents` = `webgpu/compile.ts` (grid median/random) + `agentWebgpu/compile.ts` (3D field + glyph + hash-reserve) + `agentWasm/compile.ts` (the +10-line 3D-field guard). NO lattice grid (`compile.ts`/`wasm/`/`webgpu/encoder.ts`/`layout.ts`) + NO JS-agent compiler + NO `agentEngine.ts`/`agentWebgpuRuntime.ts` touched. **THE COMPILE MATRIX IS NOW FULL COVERAGE** — every per-target gate rejects ONLY the genuine fundamentals (WebGPU async-update-mode + order-dependent indicator ops [+ the agent-array-producer capacity gate]).

### Phase F — WASM AGENT 3D FIELD BRIDGE (the last WASM-agent gap; branch `continue_agents`)
The WASM-agent field bridge was 2D-only (`emitFieldIdx` = `row·W+col`), so a 3D-field WASM-agent model defensively clamped to JS (the one documented WASM follow-up after the whole-target port). NOW PORTED to 3D — a 3D-field WASM-agent model runs on WASM with **JS↔WASM bit-parity** (f64). The WebGPU agent 3D field bridge (the prior close-out) is the structural model. Touches ONLY [agentWasm/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWasm/compile.ts) (+ the parity harness) — lattice grid (all 3 targets) + JS-agent + WebGPU-agent (compiler AND runtime) are byte-identical by construction (`git diff --stat` = `agentWasm/compile.ts` + `scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs`).
- **All 5 field emitters branch on `ctx.is3d`** (the agent loop is already 3D-aware — getNearbyAgents / getAgentOffset / the force pass), mirroring the JS field nodes (SampleField / FieldGradient / ReadCellsUnder / AffectCellsUnder / SecreteToField) bit-for-bit:
  - `emitFieldIdx(row, col, layer?)` — 3D index `(layer·H+row)·W+col` (2D `row·W+col` unchanged).
  - `emitFieldWrapCoord(cL, axis)` — axis selector `'x'`/`'y'`/`'z'` (z wraps/clamps against `_fieldD`); `pushFieldDInt`.
  - `emitSampleFieldAt(…, pushPZ?)` — 3D 8-corner trilinear via the SAME **NESTED lerp** the JS node uses (lerp_x → lerp_y → lerp_z). NB: a flat `Σ c·wx·wy·wz` is mathematically equal but NOT bit-identical to the nested form (different f64 rounding) — the nested lerp is required for parity.
  - `emitFieldGradient` — emits the `dz` central-difference port (the node already exposes `dz` in 3D).
  - `emitSphereLoop` + `emitTorusDeltaFold` — the 3D r-SPHERE scan: a layer loop + 3D **torus-SHORTEST membership fold** (the 3D JS uses the fold; the 2D `emitDiskLoop` uses the raw delta) + the 3D index. `readCellsUnder`/`affectCellsUnder` dispatch sphere (3D) vs disk (2D).
  - `emitSecreteToField` — 8-cell trilinear splat in 3D (flat `rate·wx·wy·wz`, matching the JS flat splat; weights sum to 1).
- **The gate** (`isAgentGraphWasmSupported`) dropped the field-in-3D clamp — a 3D-field agent model now compiles to WASM.
- **Two parity bugs the comprehensive harness caught + fixed** (the gold-standard f64 verification surfaced both): (1) `emitSampleFieldAt` 3D first used a flat 8-term sum — bit-divergent from the JS nested lerp (fixed to the nested form); (2) `emitTorusDeltaFold` computed `-half` as `(half - 0)` instead of `(0 - half)` (WASM `f64.sub` is `a-b`; operands were reversed), so the `d < -half` branch fired for nearly every cell and added the full span to the delta → the r-sphere membership matched ZERO cells (read returned 0). Fixed the operand order.
- **Verified — JS↔WASM bit-parity on ALL 9 agent samples** (8 shipped + a SYNTHETIC 3D-field model built in-memory by [scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs](scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs)'s `build3DFieldModel`, exercising ALL 5 field nodes in 3D): 0 mismatches over 150 (+ 300) steps, 64 agents, incl. the readWrite `field_` arrays (so the 3D secrete splat + affect scatter write bit-identical cells too). The 8 shipped samples are unregressed; `check-agent-wasm-gate.mjs` GATE✓ COMPILE✓ INST✓ on all + the synthetic. `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` + `npm run build` clean. The 3D-field model is **synthetic (in-harness)** — not shipped to the Models Library (the existing samples don't cover 3D field; this keeps permanent regression coverage without a library card). **THE WASM AGENT TARGET IS NOW FULL 2D+3D COVERAGE.**

### Production-hardening pass (branch `polish_agents` — full agents+3D audit, all findings fixed)
A six-agent review swept the agent engine, the three agent compile targets, the ~42 agent nodes, the 3D grid/renderer, the UI lifecycle, and the docs; every confirmed finding was fixed. Verified: tsc + `npm run build` clean; ALL 21 library models compile clean across JS/WASM/WebGPU (sweep via the dev harness); the 9-entry JS↔WASM agent parity harness = 0 mismatches; `check-agent-wasm-gate.mjs` all ✓. The load-bearing changes:
- **Agent state persists in `.gcastate` / Save-Project-with-grid** (was silently dropped — a hand-seeded agent board vanished on save/load). `SimulationState.agents?: SerializedAgentState` (+ `activeAgentViewer?`), base64-generic over the worker `AgentStatePayload` via `serializeAgentState`/`deserializeAgentState` ([fileOperations.ts](src/model/fileOperations.ts)); `applySimulationState` forwards it to the worker `loadState`; a state WITHOUT an agent payload re-seeds the agent layer (loadState else-branch) instead of keeping the old population. `AgentStatePayload` also gained `vx`/`vy` + the 3 sprite arrays (zeroed-then-loaded on deserialize — momentum models keep their motion; loading over a running session can't inherit stale slot velocities).
- **Async read-after-write hazards now cover AGENT roots on all three targets** ([asyncWriteHazard.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/asyncWriteHazard.ts) gained the agent reader/writer tables + synthetic `__agentVelocity__`/`__agentPosition__`/`__agentRadius__` keys): a behaviour `Set Attribute → read later in flow` reads post-write instead of a hoisted stale snapshot (async agent mode; division events ALWAYS — the division ABI's `w_` block now ALIASES attrRead, fixing sync-mode division writes being clobbered by the next step's `primeAgentAttrWrite`). JS pins via the existing volatile machinery; the agent WASM/WebGPU compilers consume the SAME shared `computeVolatileHoist` emitBefore plan (`ctx.hazardPinned`/`hazardEmitBefore` — emit ONCE at the LCA, NOT re-emit-per-use, which diverged on multi-use reads with a write between uses; the Ant pickup-then-drop shape caught it). Field reads stay a loop-top snapshot on every target (JS `routeValueEmit` forces them to CELL_TOP on agent roots so branch-sinking can't see a same-step deposit the WebGPU fieldRead snapshot can't). The agent WASM/WebGPU branch emitters also clear the volatile cache at conditional/switch/loop BRANCH boundaries (a getVariable-derived value cached from one branch left the sibling reading a stale local on WASM / an unresolved name on WGSL).
- **Agent flow-DIAMOND value scope — `enterCacheScope`/`exitCacheScope` (agentWasm + agentWebgpu)**: the branch-boundary `clearVolatileCache` above covers VOLATILE (forEach-element-derived) values but NOT the general class. `compileFlowChain` has NO visited guard (the documented "flow graphs are DAGs, not trees" discipline), so a flow **DIAMOND** — a node reached from >1 SIBLING branch (a `conditional` `then` AND `else` both flowing into a shared downstream chain; also `switch` cases, or two nodes' flow-outs targeting the same node) — inlines that shared node's body ONCE PER branch walk. A value in the shared body that is NOT hoisted to function-top by `preEmitAgentValues` (an impure cone — canonically a **getRandom-tainted expression**, since getRandom is in `AGENT_VALUE_NO_HOIST`) emits its `let`/`local.set` + caches inside the FIRST branch's block; the SECOND branch's walk hits the cache and references the first block's `let` — on **WebGPU** an out-of-scope WGSL "unresolved value" compile error (→ silent JS fallback), on **WASM** a stale function-scoped local reused (the else branch never re-draws its random) = silently wrong + a broken RNG stream (JS↔WASM parity break). **Fix**: a per-flow-block cache-scope snapshot/restore — `enterCacheScope` (snapshot value+array cache keys) on entering a `{…}` flow block (conditional then/else, switch cases+default, loop/forEach/forEachBond body), `exitCacheScope` (drop entries ADDED inside the block) on exit — so a shared body re-emits its non-hoisted values in each sibling branch's own scope. The snapshot is taken BEFORE the entry `clearVolatileCache` (volatile handling unchanged); pure cross-branch values pre-exist at snapshot time (function-top hoisted) → survive → **byte-identical** for models with no diamond (check-compile-identity: all library models unchanged). The **JS agent compiler needs no equivalent** — it uses the lattice sink analysis, which emits each value at the LCA of its uses (a diamond's shared getRandom lands ABOVE the branch, in-scope for both). Guarded permanently by the `[synthetic] Flow diamond` entry in [scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs](scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs) (conditional → shared getRandom chain; proven to FAIL without the WASM fix). When adding ANY new block-scoped flow emit to an agent compiler, wrap its body in enter/exitCacheScope — a diamond into it will otherwise reuse a first-branch local.
- **WASM agent emitter fixes** (all silent-wrong before): Compare `between`/`notBetween` (fell through to `==`), Math `%` (fell through to `+`; now host `env.fmod` + the ÷0 guard — `FMOD_FUNC_IDX` is module-scope), options-mode Get Random (fell into the float branch; now emits array-pick + Fallback, inputs resolved BEFORE the RNG advance), bool-mode Get Random (double i32→f64 convert = module VALIDATION failure → silent JS fallback), `filterAgents`/`joinAgents` `count`-port standalone consumption (threw → silent JS clamp; now the lattice count-dispatch pattern), pickRandomAgent resolves its input array BEFORE the RNG advance (chained-RNG-producer order), getConstant orientation-clamp/faceLabel parity. WebGPU agent mirrors: `between` + `%` + options-mode Get Random now EMIT (their gate rejects removed; `compareType === 'neighborIndex'` is the only Compare reject left).
- **setCellLooks viewer guard on the WASM agent behaviour**: a 16th `activeViewerIdx` i32 param + the compile-time `viewerGuardIds` table (compile result → init/recompile msg `agentWasmViewerGuardIds` → the worker passes `indexOf(activeViewer)`) reproduces JS's `_isV_` guard exactly (was an unconditional write — cross-target visual divergence on multi-viewer models).
- **CSE/loop-invariance hardening** ([accessorCSE.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/accessorCSE.ts) / [loopInvariant.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/loopInvariant.ts)): any node with a FLOW port is never a CSE source (two bare For Each Bonds shared a purity key → out-of-scope-const ReferenceError; two Create Agents merged handles → silent population loss); the agent engine-buffer readers (velocity/position/radius/nearby/curvature/field reads) + `createAgent` + the agent roots joined `NEVER_PURE_TYPES`; `createAgent`/`agentInit`/`agentOutputMapping` joined `NEVER_INVARIANT` (handle-consumer TDZ crash in the Init Event).
- **By-id reader guards on ALL THREE targets** (Get Agent Position abs+rel / Offset / Radius / Attribute + wired Get Velocity): `-1` (the empty sentinel — Pick Random Agent on an empty set) / out-of-range ids yield 0 instead of `undefined→NaN` position math (JS) or adjacent-memory reads (WASM); unwired reader ids default to `-1`. WASM = `emitAgentIdGuard` (ok flag + clamped safe id + `OP_SELECT`, eager-load-safe); WebGPU = `emitAgentIdGuarded` + `select()`. `forEachBond.currentLength` is torus-folded + 3D-aware on all targets (a seam bond read ≈ W − actual and "break over-strained bonds" severed every seam bond; WebGPU already folded 2D — JS/WASM caught up, all three gained the z arm).
- **Worker lifecycle fixes** ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)): the cell Init Event runs BEFORE the agent Init Event on load AND Reset (D-FIELD ordering — a Create-Agent rule that reads the field sees the seeded substrate); `runAgentStepWebGPU` resets `forceZ` (a JS fallback step left a permanent phantom z-force in 3D) and DEFERS mutation messages arriving during its awaited readback (`AGENT_GPU_DEFER_TYPES` + `flushDeferredAgentGpuMsgs` — a mid-step move/seed/paint was clobbered by the readback); `agentAddToWorld` only commits ids staged by THIS Init Event's Create Agent (ghost slots / free-list double-alloc); `currentAgentHash` is invalidated in `initAgents` + after the auto-bond rebuild (it reuses the same per-store scratch arrays); a throwing agent OM colour pass removes itself (was one error post per step); the `createAgent` msg carries `z`; daughter B inherits sprite state in `divideAgent`; the recompile message now ships `agentLayoutExtras`/`agentWebgpuLayout`/`agentWebgpuUsesI32Write`/usage flags (a live target flip to WASM rebuilt the store WITHOUT the extras the module was compiled against — the baked-offset corruption class); the agent Init ABI carries a trailing `_agentZ` in 3D (Set Agent Position's z write threw `_agentZ is not defined` — the canonical 3D spawn recipe crashed) + `zNext` syncs after init; the division ABI carries `_bondRestLength`/`_bondPartnerEpoch` (For Each Bond in a division event crashed on the missing param).
- **3D fixes**: `coords3d` is seeded (`coords` with dl=0) on the dimension flip to 3D AND on LOAD_MODEL, and the JS NI pre-pass `packCoord` falls back to `coords`+dl=0 like the worker (a 2D-authored neighbourhood in a 3D model packed all-INVALID_NI while the sim kept running it, and the slice editor showed 0 cells + clobbered on first click); the agent sphere pick maps back through the alpha-sort permutation (`agentInstOrder` in [gl3d.ts](src/simulator/render/gl3d.ts) — with Alpha blend on, kill/move/inspect hit the WRONG agent); `pick()`/`pickAgent()` respect the Layers Show toggles (no killing invisible agents); Ctrl+C/V/X is gated OFF in 3D (it silently corrupted layer-0 cells at a bogus 2D-projected position — a layer-aware region copy anchored on the brush plane is the follow-up); the 2D `handleMouseMove` bails in 3D (the hover-coords chip showed garbage); the idle agent-hover pick is rAF-throttled + canvas-rect-gated (was a full pick-FBO render + synchronous readPixels stall per raw window-wide pointermove).
- **Model lifecycle / UX**: `REMOVE_AGENT_MAPPING` cascades `clearDeletedId('mappingId')` (dead compiled pass + dangling picker); `UPDATE_AGENT_ATTRIBUTE` gained the Compare-tag `_port_x/y/y2` remap + a type-change linked-agent-mapping palette reset + `agentVariables` initialValue remap / tag-detach (REMOVE detaches too); `REORDER_AGENT_ATTRIBUTES` reducer + panel wiring (the drag handle was a silent no-op; a reorder forces the full reinit via `attrsStructurallyEqual`); `ADD_AGENT_MAPPING` seeds Standalone when no eligible agent attribute exists; the agent seed-config merge iterates `model.agentAttributes` keyed on `agentAttrSig` (it iterated CELL attrs — persisted seed config never survived a reload and cell edits wiped it); agent-graph compile errors surface in the compile-error banner (`[agents] …` — "No Behaviour Step node" was console-only) and agent OM compile errors are collected into `compileAgentGraph().error` instead of swallowed; the panel-drag drop menu applies `isNodeAvailable` (it offered lattice-only/async-only nodes the other menus hide); `behaviourStep`/`divisionEvent`/`agentInit` are singleton-enforced like Step/Init (`SINGLETON_NODE_TYPES` covers add/connect/paste/quick-add — a duplicate root was silently dead); a cross-graph clipboard renders a DISABLED "Paste (from X graph)" item instead of a silent no-op; Seed Pattern (compact/scatter) + Bond Rest Length got Properties rows; agent views got a Description field (feeds the viewer-tab tooltip) + "+ Add Agent View" works with zero agent attrs (Standalone); the Variables panel no longer spuriously auto-selects on a Cells↔Agents swap and agent tag variables bind AGENT tag attributes; `neighborIndex` is hidden from the agent-attribute type dropdown; `Neighborhood3DEditor` remounts per selection (`key={selected.id}` — stale form state overwrote neighbourhood B with A's spec); a glyph setCellLooks in a 3D model gets a validation badge (glyphs aren't rendered by the voxel view).
- **Parity-harness discipline**: [scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs](scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs)'s `buildArgs` mirror was missing the 3 sprite args (every trailing arg shifted — `_field_*` landed undefined), masking real-looking failures. The agent-loop ABI now has THREE mirrors: `buildAgentLoopParams` (compile.ts) ↔ `buildAgentLoopArgs` (sim.worker.ts) ↔ the harness `buildArgs` — edit ALL THREE together.
- **Agent `sequence` emitters walked NONEXISTENT ports** (found via a user gravitation model): both agent compilers' `case 'sequence'` walked `then0`/`then1` keyed on a nonexistent `sequenceCount` — the real ports are **`first`/`then`/`then_2`…** keyed on `extraCount` — so ANY Sequence in an agent behaviour silently dropped its ENTIRE downstream chain on WASM AND WebGPU (JS was fine → "works on JS only"). Sneaky because the gate/reachability walk the EDGE MAP (prefix scan), so every node still gate-passed + compiled — only the emitter's flow walk missed. Fixed in both; the parity harness's synthetic model now routes its agent chain through a Sequence for permanent regression coverage. **When adding a flow construct to the agent compilers, copy the port ids from the NODE DEF / asyncWriteHazard's walk — never guess.** No shipped sample used an agent-graph Sequence, which is why 9/9 parity passed while user models broke — sample coverage ≠ catalogue coverage for FLOW shapes.
- **WebGPU-agent perf posture surfaced in the UI**: the Properties WebGPU agent radio hint now states the per-step CPU↔GPU upload/readback cost (agents + cell fields) — below ~10k agents, and especially for field models (Ant Necrophoresis / Chemotaxis), JS/WASM is usually FASTER; WebGPU wins only at very large populations / very heavy per-agent math. This is the documented benchmark reality, not a bug.
- **Agents-only world resize at scale (the 600×600×400 hang)** — an agents-only (Grid Cells OFF) 3D model froze on resize to large volumes and never concluded, on every target. FOUR compounding worker causes, all fixed in [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts):
  1. **The nbr-table reservation** — `computeMemoryLayout` reserved per-cell neighbour index tables (`total × nSz × 4` bytes; a Moore-3D nbh at 600×600×400 = 144M cells ≈ 15 GB, past the wasm32 4 GiB address space) even though `gridCellsEnabled === false` means `buildNeighborIndices` + the cell step never run. `initGrid` now passes `gridCellsEnabled ? neighborhoods : []` into the layout, so an agents-only world allocates only attrs/colors/scratch. Also wrapped `initGrid()` in the init handler with try/catch → a genuinely-too-big world posts a clear `Grid allocation failed for W×H×D…` error instead of a silent dead worker.
  2. **Static colours shipped every step** — with no colour pass, the (huge) unchanged `colors` buffer (144M cells × 4 B ≈ 576 MB/step) was copied + transferred on every `stepped`. New module flag `colorsDirty`: set by `runStep`, `writeDefaultColors`, a mapping fn actually running in `runColorPass`, the WebGPU colours readback, and the loadState restore; when `!gridCellsEnabled && !colorsDirty`, `sendColors` posts the stepped message WITHOUT `colors` (agents payload + indicators still ride). SimulatorView's stepped handler keeps the last buffer when `msg.colors === undefined`. Grid-ON models are untouched (the skip requires `!gridCellsEnabled`). The post-batch `runColorPass()` is also gated on `gridCellsEnabled` (2 sites) — the cell graph of an agents-only model still has an (empty) Step root, so `stepFn` exists and used to re-dirty the colours every batch.
  3. **`generation` never advanced** — the batch loops only incremented via `runStep`, so an agents-only model was stuck at gen 0 (end conditions, the transport display, and "is it even running?" all broken). All 3 batch loops (sync, webgpu-agents, webgpu-grid) now `generation++` when agents stepped but the cell step was skipped.
  4. **Lattice-target instantiation ran with a mismatched layout** — the lattice WASM module / WebGPU runtime bake nbr offsets the worker layout no longer reserves. `init` + `recompile` + `setUseWasm`/`setUseWebGPU` now gate lattice-target bring-up on `gridCellsEnabled` (agents-only runs the agent target only; the AGENT WASM/WebGPU targets are unaffected — their layout is separate).
  Verified live at 600×600×400 gridCells:false: JS agents gen 0→20 at **6.75 ms/step**, WASM agent target gen 0→20, 0 per-step colours ships, 0 errors (was: frozen forever); grid-ON regression checks — WASM GoL ships colours 5/5 steps gens 1..5, WebGPU GoL direct-render unchanged.
- **Agents-only per-cell layout eliminated entirely (round 2 — the 500³ / 5000×500×500 failure).** Even after the nbr-table skip, an agents-only world still reserved **9 bytes/cell of inert engine regions** — colors (4) + the always-reserved async orderArray (4) + the markCellUpdated skip flag (1) — so 5000×500×500 (1.25e9 cells ≈ 11.25 GB) threw `WebAssembly.Memory(): value 171662 is above the upper bound 65536`. (The error names WebAssembly on ALL targets because **`wasmMemory` is the single backing store every target's JS views sit on** — it's the container allocation, not the WASM compile target.) Fix: `computeMemoryLayout` ([wasm/layout.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/wasm/layout.ts)) gained a trailing `gridCells = true` param — when false, the colors / glyph / order / skipped regions are 0 bytes, the sync attr-WRITE region aliases the read region, and initGrid also drops the variegated orientation inputs; **per-cell memory for an agents-only world = its CELL ATTRIBUTES only** (kept — the agent field bridge reads/deposits through `readAttrs`; the shipped agent samples have zero cell attrs post-migration, so cost ≈ 0). Compile-side callers (`computeLayoutFromModel`) never pass the param → lattice layouts byte-identical. Worker guards: `writeDefaultColors`/`runStep`/`runColorPass` early-return when `!gridCellsEnabled` (mutation-handler tails could otherwise loop a huge `total`); the order/skipped views + `colorsDirty` are grid-gated; `sendColors` also skips on `colors.length === 0`; the allocation-failure message now explains the shared backing store + the 4 GiB cap + (grid-off) "per-cell storage is the cell attributes only". **SimulatorView renders agents WITHOUT a colors buffer**: the 3D draw no longer early-returns on `colorsRef === null` (voxel upload skipped, stale instances zeroed via `r.instanceCount = 0`), and the 2D draw's `!colors` early-return is waived for agent models (blit + glyph-fallback ImageData guarded by `colors.length >= w*h*4`). Verified live: 500³ AND **5000×500×500 (1.25 BILLION cells)** grid-off run gen 0→20, agents render (5 sphere instances, 0 voxels), 0 errors; grid-ON regressions clean (WASM GoL colours 5/5, async Amphiphile 5/5, Ant Necrophoresis grid+agents+field 5/5); parity harness 0 mismatches.

### Agent Stop Event (all 3 agent targets — branch `absorb_old_automatosgt`)
A **Stop Event** node in the agent BEHAVIOUR graph now pauses the simulation on every agent target (JS + WASM + WebGPU); it was a JS-only carve-out. The `_stopIdx` (1-based, offset by the cell stop count via `stopIdxBase`) is baked by the JS `compileAgentGraph` (runs first in SimulatorView) on the original nodes — `expandMacros` passes top-level nodes by reference, so the WASM/WebGPU agent compilers read the same baked config (exactly like the cell grid).
- **JS**: unchanged — the behaviour writes the shared `_stopFlag[0]` via the ABI's `_stopFlag` param.
- **WASM**: `computeAgentMemoryLayout` ([agentEngine.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentEngine.ts)) **appends** a 4-byte `stopFlagOffset` cell (always reserved → existing offsets byte-stable, verified: parity + gate + ABI audit unchanged). `stopEvent` ∈ `AGENT_WASM_SUPPORTED_TYPES` with an emitter mirroring the cell WASM (`if i32.load(stopFlagOffset)==0 { i32.store(stopFlagOffset, stopIdx) }`).
- **WebGPU**: a dedicated `stopFlag` atomic<u32> storage buffer (**binding 13**, gated on the `usesStop` flag like `usesSpawn`/binding 12), `atomicCompareExchangeWeak(&stopFlag, 0u, stopIdxU)` in the shader; `resetAgentStopFlag` seeds it to 0 before each dispatch, `readbackAgentStep` returns it, `runAgentStepWebGPU` merges it into `stopFlag[0]`.
- **The ordering fix (also fixes a pre-existing JS-agent bug)**: the agent step runs BEFORE the cell step in every batch loop, and `runStep`/`runStepWebGPU` reset the shared `stopFlag` at their top (finalizeStepWebGPU even OVERWRITES it from the GPU) — which clobbered an agent stop in a grid+agents model. New `drainAgentStop()` ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)) reads the agent stop source (JS/WebGPU → `stopFlag[0]`; WASM → the memory cell) + clears both; each batch loop **captures** it before the cell step and **surfaces** it after `generation++` (so the paused generation matches the cell-stop semantics). Gated on `agentStore && simulateAgents` → cell-only models are byte-identical.
- **Verified** (real worker, all 3 targets): an agents-only `behaviourStep → stopEvent` fires "AGENT STOP FIRED" at gen 1 on JS / WASM (gate accepts, module emits it) / WebGPU (real-GPU shader compiles + the atomic + readback merge, 0 errors); a **cell-only** stopEvent still fires (unregressed); a **grid+agents** agent stop fires (the clobber fix). tsc + build + parity (11 samples) + WASM gate + ABI audit all green.

### Cross-Agent Write Semantics (branch `sim_agent_fixes`) — sync-overwrite gate + Apply Force To Agent
The agent form of the CA grid's **Fundamental #4 (Writability)**, plus the commutative force sibling it enables. **The throughline: OVERWRITE writes race across agents; ACCUMULATE writes don't** — so the two get opposite rules. Plan + Impact Map + mockup: [docs/PLAN_CROSS_AGENT_WRITES.md](docs/PLAN_CROSS_AGENT_WRITES.md) / [IMPACT_MAP_CROSS_AGENT_WRITES.md](docs/IMPACT_MAP_CROSS_AGENT_WRITES.md) / `.html`. All additive/gated — lattice + every non-touched agent model byte-identical (JS↔WASM parity re-verified on all samples).
- **Part A — cross-agent OVERWRITE writes are async-only.** `setAgentAttribute` / `setAgentsAttribute` / `setAgentPosition` / `setAgentRadius` write ANOTHER agent's slot; in **sync** agent mode (double-buffered attrs) that collides with the target's own self-update (lost update, order-dependent, a genuine data race on parallel targets). A single **compile-time gate** in `compileAgentGraph` ([compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/compile.ts), right after `buildAdjacency`) rejects them when `centerBased.agentUpdateMode === 'sync'` AND the node is reachable from the **behaviour** root (a BFS over `flowOutputToTargets` — init/division roots are sequential one-time / structural-phase, never the parallel self-update loop, so NOT gated) AND the target isn't a one-hop **`createAgent` handle** (spawn configuration: the staged newborn isn't concurrently written — the exemption that keeps unified spawning working in sync mode). The JS compile always runs first in SimulatorView, so its error short-circuits ALL targets (no per-target copy needed). **In ASYNC mode the cross-agent overwrites are legal but SEQUENTIAL-order-dependent, which the parallel GPU cannot honour** — so `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported` ([agentWebgpu/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/compile.ts)) rejects any behaviour-reachable member of the same 4-type set whose id input is WIRED to a non-`createAgent` source (unwired = `-1` = guarded no-op; a spawn handle is thread-owned — both race-free): such a model clamps to JS/WASM, where the sequential agent loop gives a well-defined write order. This closed a real hole — before this gate an async cross-agent-overwrite model silently EMITTED on WebGPU with racy last-writer-wins visibility. (NB the earlier "WebGPU is sync-only" phrasing here was WRONG — the WebGPU agent target runs `agentUpdateMode:'async'` models fine [Tissue is async]; on the GPU the mode only governs the CPU-side buffering + residency eligibility, since a parallel dispatch always gives snapshot-reads + thread-own-writes semantics regardless.) `setVelocity` is self-only (no id input) → untouched.
- **Part B — `applyForceToAgent`** ([ApplyForceToAgentNode.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/ApplyForceToAgentNode.ts)): add a force vector to another agent by id (`force[target] += f`), the cross-agent counterpart to Apply Force. **Commutative** (`+=` onto the per-step-zeroed force buffer the integrator consumes AFTER the whole behaviour pass) → **race-free in BOTH sync and async** (order doesn't matter for a sum; no collision with the target's own Apply Force). Newton's 3rd law, custom pairwise / Coulomb laws, action-at-a-distance. Ports mirror Apply Force (`fx/fy/fz`, `fz` 3D-only) + an `agentId`; range+alive guarded (live-agent guard in behaviour/division, `_agentMaxAgents` range-only in init); `AGENT_NODE_REQUIREMENT` → `motion: 'force'`. **(Note the corrected Apply Force semantics: it ACCUMULATES `+=` onto a buffer zeroed each step — `s.forceX.fill(0,0,hw)` — then the fused pass seeds from it and the engine adds its own soft-sphere/springs on top; a single call ≈ "set from 0", N calls sum. Not "set the force".)**
- **All three targets** (the ALL-TARGET rule — no JS-only clamp): **JS/WASM** — a plain guarded `+=` (the behaviour loop is SEQUENTIAL, no atomics; WASM `forceAddAt` = the arbitrary-target sibling of `forceAdd`, in `AGENT_WASM_SUPPORTED_TYPES`). **WebGPU** — the parallel scatter CONTENDS (many threads add to the same target → lost-update race on the read-modify-write), so it goes through an **f32-bitcast atomic-CAS** (`forceScatterAdd`, the EXACT `fieldDepositCell` pattern) into a dedicated `forceScatter : array<atomic<u32>>` buffer (**binding 14**, gated on the new `usesForceScatter` flag like `usesSpawn`/`usesStop`), X/Y[/Z] regions strided by `maxAgents`; the runtime `clearBuffer`s it each step ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts)), and the **force pass** reads its own slot (no contention → plain `bitcast<f32>(forceScatter[i])`, **binding 4**, gated) and folds it into the self-force seed. Self Apply Force stays a plain non-atomic write (each thread owns its slot). `usesForceScatter` threads compile-result → SimulatorView (`emitAgentForcePassWGSL(layout, usesForceScatter)`) → init/recompile msg → `createAgentWebGPURuntime` usage — same plumbing as `usesSpawn`. A no-scatter (Boids) shader is byte-identical (no binding 14/4, no helper).
- **Array broadcast — `applyForceToAgents`** ([ApplyForceToAgentsNode.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/ApplyForceToAgentsNode.ts)): add the SAME force to EVERY agent in an id array (feed Get Nearby / Bonded / Filter Agents). **Pure editor sugar with ZERO new per-target emit** — a shared pre-compile transform **`expandForceToAgents`** ([forceToAgentsExpand.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/forceToAgentsExpand.ts)) lowers it to **`For Each In Array → applyForceToAgent`** (the sanctioned "lower to primitives" pattern — `expandComposites`/`expandMultiAttrs`/`lowerVectorAttrs`), so it reuses the single node's JS/WASM/WebGPU emitters ENTIRELY. Wired into all three agent front-ends right after `collapseReroutes` (JS `compileAgentGraph` + both `flattenAgentGraph`s), so the agent-target GATE inspects the flattened graph (`forEachInArray` + `applyForceToAgent`, both supported) — `applyForceToAgents` needs NO supported-types entry. Bit-parity inherited from the two verified primitives; semantically identical to the hand-built For-Each pattern. Hot-path no-op when unused.
- **Verified**: tsc + `npm run build` clean; Part A gate (real compiler — sync→error, async→compiles, `createAgent`-handle→exempt); Part B **JS↔WASM bit-parity** ([scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs](scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs) `buildApplyForceToAgentModel` pairwise scatter + `buildApplyForceToAgentsModel` array broadcast, 30 steps, 0 mismatches) + WGSL generation + the lowering ([scripts/test-cross-agent-writes.mjs](scripts/test-cross-agent-writes.mjs), Parts A/B/C, 2D+3D); **real-GPU** (in-browser) — both behaviour + force shaders `createShaderModule` with **0 errors** (2D+3D, single node AND the array node's scatter-CAS-inside-forEach), the runtime BUILDS (bind groups match pipeline layouts) and a real `dispatchAgentStep` runs with **0 validation errors**. Catalogue → **143 selectable / 47 agent**.

### Agent-engine performance review round (branch `optimize`, 2026-07-22)
A measured, phase-profiled review of "Particle Life struggles before 50k" — full findings + prioritized fix list in **[docs/PERF_REVIEW_AGENT_ENGINE.md](docs/PERF_REVIEW_AGENT_ENGINE.md)**. **The FOLLOW-ON project (GPU-resident agent RENDER + residency widening, phases A1→F) is planned and session-orchestrated via [docs/HANDOFF_GPU_AGENT_RENDER.md](docs/HANDOFF_GPU_AGENT_RENDER.md)** (master runbook: invariants, phase sequence, per-phase handoffs, status board) — new sessions execute one phase each; read the master's §0/§3 before touching this area. What SHIPPED in the round:
- **Resize → agent-compiler dims desync FIXED** (the `[agents] spatial hash (N bins) exceeds the WebGPU reserve (M)` error after a simulator Resize): `compileAgentModel` now takes the SAME dims-overridden **`dimsModel`** the grid compilers get (both call sites — init + soft recompile). Before, the agent WASM/WebGPU layouts baked the MODEL dims while the worker ran LIVE dims: WebGPU → permanent silent per-step JS demotion after any resize-to-larger; WASM → a store↔module offset desync of every region after the hash reserve (nearby-scratch/model-attrs/**lookup-tables**/fields — silent wrong-offset reads, the baked-offset corruption class). Defence-in-depth: the init/recompile messages ship **`agentWasmLayoutSig`** (`{maxHashBins, totalBytes}` of the compiled layout) and `instantiateAgentWasmIfNeeded` REFUSES to instantiate on a mismatch (loud error + safe JS-on-views fallback). Browser-verified: WASM + WebGPU targets at 600×600 run clean post-resize.
- **WebGPU agent scratch arrays capped** (`AGENT_GPU_ARRAY_CAP = 2048` in [agentWebgpu/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/compile.ts)): the per-thread `var<function>` array-producer slots were sized at **maxAgents** — WGSL zero-inits function-address-space vars, so 50k maxAgents = 200 KB of private memory zeroed PER THREAD per dispatch (measured 415 ms/gen on a Pascal → 190 ms with the cap). Slots are now `min(maxAgents, 2048)` (+ fill guards in nearby/join); maxAgents ≤ 2048 models emit byte-identical shaders; a >2048-member query TRUNCATES (documented GPU capacity bound). **Never size a per-thread WGSL array by maxAgents.**
- **[scripts/bench-agent-engine.mjs](scripts/bench-agent-engine.mjs)** — the per-phase agent-engine profiler (real `.gcaproj` + real instantiated WASM; phases: hash/behaviour/force/snapshot/clone at N ∈ {2k,10k,50k} at shipped + user densities; `MODEL=` env override). THE measurement tool for agent perf work — rerun it after any engine change.
- **Measured truths to keep in mind**: the per-pair physics is >99% of an agent generation (hash/snapshot/args are noise) — cost ∝ N × density × queryRadius², so the pair count (not the compile target) is the lever; WASM ≈ 1.1× JS on gather-heavy workloads. (The Help tab's Bond-Graph Agents § Performance now carries the user-facing version of this guidance.)
- **The "WebGPU wrong dynamics" bug — RESOLVED (async step-batch re-entrancy, commit 8173f25).** Boids plateaued at ~0.03–0.45 polarization on the WebGPU agent target with zero errors; an exhaustive primitive-probe session found nothing because **the GPU physics was correct all along** — the state-roundtrip experiment (loadState the SAME agent state into both targets) showed a single GPU step matches JS to 1.4e-5 (pure f32) and strictly-SEQUENTIAL batches flock 0.378→0.998. The corruption trigger was **queuing multiple `step` messages at once**: the async WebGPU batch loops yield to onmessage at every `await`, so a queued `step` started a CONCURRENT batch interleaved with the running one (stale CPU uploads racing fresh GPU results). The sync JS/WASM loop can't be interleaved — which is why only the GPU paths broke. **Fix** ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)): while an async step batch is in flight the worker **defers EVERY incoming message** and replays them in order when the batch settles (`asyncStepBatchInFlight` + `deferredDuringAsyncBatch` + `endAsyncStepBatch`), restoring the synchronous path's semantics; plus permanent GPU diagnostics (`device.onuncapturederror`, `device.lost`, `pushErrorScope('validation')` around the dispatch). **Any future async worker batch loop must set/clear this flag.** Verified: the previously-failing overlapped-burst pattern flocks 0.223→0.998 on the GPU.
- **Density-scan skip (commit 50e6f96, all 3 targets)**: the fused force pass ran its full neighbour scan UNCONDITIONALLY just to count `density`, even when engine physics is off and nothing reads it — ~70% of the behaviour's cost wasted on custom-force models. Now compile-side **`agentUsesDensity`** (a macro-aware scan for `neighbourDensity`/`divideAgent` in [SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx), shipped in init/recompile) gates the scan: `doScan = engineForces || usesDensity` in the JS force loop (both 2D+3D arms), a **`doDensity`** WASM forcePass param (index 25 — the ABI is now 26 params; [agentWasm/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWasm/compile.ts) wraps the scan in an `ifThen` on `bonding|doCollision|doDensity`), and a WGSL `ForceControl.doDensity` field (u[24], `FORCE_CONTROL_BYTES` 96→112). Measured: 50k@600² force 449→**2.8 ms**, total 1123/994→**589/544 ms/gen** (~1.9×). [scripts/parity-agent-force.mjs](scripts/parity-agent-force.mjs) gained density-skip + density-only combos (6 total, bit-exact).
- **PR7c GPU RESIDENCY — SHIPPED (commit 614bbee).** For eligible models the WebGPU agent target now runs **whole frames resident on the GPU**: the spatial hash is built ON-GPU (clearBuffer → atomic count → a single-workgroup two-level exclusive scan [256 threads × ≤256-bin chunks] → atomic scatter with cursors), a whole gens/frame batch is encoded in **ONE queue submit** (per gen: hash passes → behaviour → force → **posCommit** — a compute pass that commits xNext→x AND zeroes the force accumulators GPU-side; a same-buffer copyBufferToBuffer is a validation error, hence compute), and the CPU reads back once per FRAME (`readbackAgentFrame`). `agentGpuUploadPending` (set by every mutation message — the AGENT_GPU_DEFER_TYPES set ∪ loadState/reset/setRngSeed/importImage — and initAgents) re-uploads the SoA only when the CPU touched it (CPU-side force zero before that upload — **per-step force accumulators MUST be zeroed each generation**; the resident path does it in posCommit). **Eligibility** (`agentResidentEligible` in [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)): behaviour-scoped compiler flags **`usesStructural`/`usesRadiusWrite`** (set by the GPU emitters in [agentWebgpu/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/compile.ts) — behaviour-scoped so an Init-Event spawn like Particle Life stays eligible) + agents-only + async attrs + no field/springs/growth/spawn/stop/indicators/positional; anything else falls back to the per-gen GPU path unchanged. Runtime lives in [agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts) (`ensureAgentResident` — 4 extra pipelines with explicit bind-group layouts, `computeResidentHashParams` coarsens the bin edge ×1.3 to fit maxBins, `dispatchResidentBatch`). Measured: Boids **~0.4 ms/gen** (flocks 0.105→0.998); 50k Particle Life @600² **~20 ms/gen** (29× the 1030 ms pre-review WASM baseline, ~10× the per-gen GPU path); mutation round-trip exact (moveAgents lands + integrates).
- **P2 render + snapshot (commit 463077a)**: `stampBatchedTile` draws sub-2px batched opaque discs as `rect()` splats (`SPLAT_MAX_RAD = 2`) instead of moveTo+arc subpaths; `snapshotAgentsForRender` ships **Float32** copies (render precision — the store stays f64) and gates **vx/vy on `includeSprites`** (the sprite orientToVelocity heading is their only consumer; measured 45→17 B/agent, per-frame snapshot+clone 5.4→2.2 ms at 50k). The worker transfer list gates the length-0 placeholders on `length > 0` — they SHARE one ArrayBuffer, and listing a duplicate in a transfer list is a DataCloneError. gl3d's `AgentSnapshot3D` types follow. Measured: 50k stepped-handler (snapshot consume + draw) median **25 → 9.6 ms**.

### Direct agent render — GPU-side, free-when-idle (A1 + A2 + A1.5 + C + D; branch `optimize`)
The **WORKER renders agents into a transferred OffscreenCanvas**; the main thread just blits it 1:1 (`ctx.drawImage(agentRenderCanvasRef, 0, 0)`, the grid direct-render seam). **A1** feeds the render from the resident/per-gen GPU SoA (WebGPU-target models). **A2** widens this to CPU (JS/WASM) targets: the worker builds a lightweight **render-only** WebGPU surface and uploads the CPU store's positions/colours each frame, then presents on the SAME pipeline — the sim keeps running on the CPU, only the DRAW moves to the GPU (see the A2 subsection below). The per-frame CPU pipeline (readback → snapshot → postMessage → Canvas2D `drawAgentsOverlay`) runs **ONLY while a feature needs it** — free-running cost ≈ one `drawImage`. Additive/gated — every non-eligible model keeps today's CPU-overlay path byte-for-byte. Handoff: [docs/HANDOFF_GPU_AGENT_RENDER_A1.md](docs/HANDOFF_GPU_AGENT_RENDER_A1.md) (+ the master [HANDOFF_GPU_AGENT_RENDER.md](docs/HANDOFF_GPU_AGENT_RENDER.md) / [IMPACT_MAP](docs/IMPACT_MAP_GPU_AGENT_RENDER.md) / [PLAN](docs/PLAN_GPU_AGENT_RENDER.md)). Touches ONLY [agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts) + [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts) + [SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx) — **NO compilers / gl3d / agentEngine / JS-WASM paths** (`git diff --stat` = 3 files), so byte-identity holds by construction.
- **FP-1 — the render is a VIEW of engine-owned state.** `AGENT_RENDER_WGSL` reads ONLY `agentF32[x/y/radius]` + `agentAlive` + the packed `agentColors` (r|g<<8|b<<16|a<<24) — fields every agent model has, so **zero compiler changes**, graph-agnostic. Instanced disc quads (`draw(4, highWater × copiesX × copiesY)`, triangle-strip): the VS decomposes the instance index into `(agent = inst % highWater, copy = inst / highWater)` → torus tile offset from the RenderView, world→screen via `scalePx/oxPx/oyPx`, degenerate off-screen quad for dead/alpha-0 agents. FS = disc SDF (premultiplied) + optional outline rim (matches the 2D `stampBatchedTile` ×0.60 band) + an **additive Glow** pipeline (radial `intensity·pow(1-d, steepness)`). **The drawn radius is floored at 1.2 px** (`max(ar*scalePx, 1.2)` — audit M2) to match the CPU overlay's `Math.max(1.2, r*scale)` at all three draw sites; without it a sub-pixel agent (zoomed out / large world) vanished on the fast path while the CPU path still drew a dot. Two pipelines (plain premultiplied-over / glow additive) selected per frame by `glowOn`. `presentAgentsEncode` patches the uniform's `highWater` from the caller's value each present (the camera message can't know the free-mode highWater), so the instance decomposition always matches the draw.
- **FP-2 — the CPU never NEEDS per-frame agent state; FEATURES do.** Worker readback POLICY: `agentUiSync` (default ON) — when ON the resident batch reads the GPU state back each frame and `sendColors` ships the render snapshot (picks/hover/inspector/recording read it, `drawAgentsOverlay` is STILL skipped — only the DATA flows); when OFF the resident batch SKIPS `readbackAgentFrame`, sets `agentStoreStale`, and `sendColors` ships **no `agents` payload** — just an `agentLiveCount` scalar (the stats chip). The batch presents INSIDE `dispatchResidentBatch`'s single submit (GPU-authoritative — posCommit ran, so `agentF32[x]` is current); the per-gen path + mutations present via `presentAgentsFromStore` (uploadAgentSoA + **uploadAgentColors** [syncs the CPU-set colours for a fresh/mutated agent] + presentAgentsOnce). `agentBatchPresented` flags the internal present so `sendColors` doesn't double-present.
- **`uploadAgentSoA` MUST seed `agentColors` (the transparent-agents trap, fixed 2026-07-23).** The per-gen + resident SoA-upload path uploads positions/attrs but NOT colours; `readbackAgentStep` then OVERWRITES `s.colors` from the GPU `agentColors` buffer. A model whose agent behaviour has **NO colour node** (no Set Cell Looks, no Agent Output Mapping — Boids/Chemotaxis rely on the per-slot `DEFAULT_AGENT_COLOR`) never writes `agentColors` in the shader, so without a seed the readback pulls the buffer's uninitialized ZEROS back → fully-transparent, INVISIBLE agents on the CPU overlay AND the composite. So `uploadAgentSoA` now calls `uploadAgentColors(rt, s)` at its end (idempotent — a colour-writing shader overwrites the seed GPU-side; only supplies the default when nothing writes). This was the symptom-1 root cause of the WASM-grid+WebGPU-agent Chemotaxis "no agents" report — a field-coupled WebGPU-agent model is NOT render-eligible (CPU overlay), so it read the never-seeded buffer.
- **E2 single-canvas composite is RE-ENABLED, redesigned at DISPLAY resolution (2026-07-25).** The original E2 rendered both layers into ONE grid-sized (`W×H`) canvas with the agent camera at world resolution (`scalePx=1`), letting the main-thread blit scale it — so a radius-1 agent was a ~1px disc that became a CELL-SIZED BLOCK once scaled to the display (the user's "agents become a blob of cells" on WebGPU/WebGPU). The **display-res redesign** renders BOTH layers at DISPLAY resolution through the camera into a **display-sized** canvas: the grid layer is a fullscreen triangle whose FS **inverts the camera** (display pixel → world coord `(fragCoord−oxPx)/scalePx` → cell `floor(wx)/floor(wy)` → grid `colorsBuf`, NEAREST = crisp CA cells), and the agent discs render through the **SAME display-res camera** as the A1 render — so agents stay CRISP DISCS at any zoom. See the "E2 — single-canvas composite render" subsection for the full design; the `agentComposite` gate is live again.
- **The one-shot rule — ONE place** (the load-bearing correctness): at the TOP of `self.onmessage`, if `agentStoreStale` and the message reads/mutates agents (`AGENT_GPU_DEFER_TYPES ∪ {getAgentState, getState}`) → block via the existing `asyncStepBatchInFlight` deferral, `await readbackAgentFrame`, clear the flag, then REPLAY the (now-fresh) message. So **no CPU consumer ever sees stale state** — it pays a one-shot readback (~2 ms @50k). `getState` (save) is fresh for free (also fixes the pre-existing agent save-freshness gap). Verified with a hostile test: `moveAgents` / `getAgentState` fired mid-free-run reflect post-readback state exactly.
- **The DUAL of the one-shot rule — no PRESENT may ever WRITE a stale CPU store back to the GPU (`42a44a0`).** `presentAgentsFromStore` `uploadAgentSoA`s the CPU store before presenting, so a present fired while `agentStoreStale` OVERWRITES the live diffused GPU state with the stale ≈last-synced frame — reverting the sim (the Particle-Life "brush-remove a hole, play, then a re-attach/refocus jumps back to the post-brush frame and freezes" report; the audit-B1 hazard realised on a present path). The one-shot rule keeps CONSUMERS fresh; this keeps PRODUCERS from clobbering. **`presentAgentsIfActive()` is now present-only (`presentAgentsOnce`, no upload) whenever `agentStoreStale`** — so EVERY present site is safe by construction (the `setAgentCamera` handler's explicit stale branch is now redundant-but-harmless; `refreshAgentDisplay` [tab-refocus] no longer reverts). Companion: `attachAgentCanvas` (display resize / re-attach) previously force-cleared `agentStoreStale = false`, LYING that the CPU was fresh on a re-attach to the still-authoritative GPU compute runtime → its present then uploaded the stale store; it now `await ensureAgentStoreFresh()` instead (read the GPU down if stale — a no-op on a fresh/rebuilt runtime or a CPU target), so the present's upload is an identity. **Rule for any new agent present path: never upload when `agentStoreStale`; present-only or `ensureAgentStoreFresh()` first.** Verified in-browser (WebGPU agent target, 1800 particles, free mode): mean per-agent advance over a 2.5 s window was 59.1 with no refresh vs 54.1 while spamming `refreshAgentDisplay` (ratio 0.92 — a revert would have collapsed the refresh run toward zero).
- **The gate** (all GENERAL properties, evaluated in SimulatorView at init/recompile; mirrors the grid's placement): `topologyMode.agents && gridCells === false && !is3D && agentResult.agentTarget === 'webgpu' && transferControlToOffscreen supported && (model.sprites?.length ?? 0) === 0 && !agentMetaballs.enabled && (model.agentMappings?.length ?? 0) === 0`. (**D relaxes the `gridCells === false` term to `gridCells === false || (agentDecoupled && !is3D)`** — see the D subsection; a 2D field-decoupled grid+agents model qualifies.) **NOT coupled to `agentResidentEligible`** — the per-gen GPU path renders too (the SoA is uploaded there). **The Agent-Output-Mapping exclusion is a deliberate ADDITION to the handoff gate** (see the A1 Completion Report): OM colours are computed CPU-side by `runAgentColorPass` into `s.colors`, NOT into the GPU `agentColors` the render reads, so an OM model keeps the CPU-overlay path where its colours stay correct. **A1.5 LIFTS this for a WebGPU-target model whose OM graph compiles to GPU colour passes** — the OM term becomes `agentMappings.length === 0 || agentOmGpuSupported` (see the A1.5 subsection). Sprites / metaballs / 3D / a non-webgpu target likewise fall back to today's path (function first). **BONDS are a shared exclusion in BOTH dimensions** (`resolveMaxBonds(model.centerBased) === 0` — audit H1, originally a 3D-only term): the GPU pass draws discs/spheres only and `draw()` skips `drawAgentsOverlay()` under direct render, which is the SOLE bond renderer — a bonded model would silently lose its bond lines (the shipped 2D Tissue samples did) and the `showBonds` Layers toggle would become a no-op. Emitting bond lines in the GPU pass (a line pipeline + the bond-pair buffer) is a real render feature, not a wiring repair — recorded as a follow-up.
- **Attach lifecycle** (clones the grid seam): the worker posts `agentRuntimeReady` when `buildAgentWebGPUIfNeeded` installs the runtime → SimulatorView `maybeAttachAgentCanvas` (transfer a DISPLAY-pixel-sized OffscreenCanvas, post `attachAgentCanvas`) → worker `setupAgentDirectRender` (validates the WGSL module async, builds pipelines, presents once) acks `agentRenderStatus{active}` → SimulatorView commits the placeholder as the blit source + sends the initial camera. `agentRuntimeReady` resets the active flag first (covers soft-recompile re-attach, since the runtime rebuild drops the old render pipeline); a display RESIZE re-attaches with a fresh canvas (transferred dims are fixed); tab-refocus posts `refreshAgentDisplay`. `agentRenderActive`/`agentStoreStale` reset on every runtime teardown (initAgents / buildAgentWebGPUIfNeeded / step-fail). **A re-attach REUSES a layout-matching render-only surface** (audit H3): every real display-size change re-attaches (once per frame while a splitter is dragged), and building a fresh surface there orphaned three `maxAgents`-sized buffers AND a shared-device reference that could never be released — so the attach handler reuses `agentRenderRuntime` when `maxAgents`/`gridDepth`/`f32Len` match and destroys a stale-layout one first (`buildAgentDiscPipelines`/`setupAgentSphereRender` likewise destroy the previous render-view uniform). **The gate's two MODEL-dependent terms are re-evaluated on a SOFT recompile** (audit M1): `sprites` + `agentMappings` are deliberately NOT in `needsFullInit` (a reinit would reset the grid + re-seed the population), so the once-computed gate went stale — adding a sprite kept direct render on (sprites never drawn) and adding a GPU-unsupported agent OM kept it presenting behaviour colours. `agentRenderModelTermsOk()` (module-scope, ONE source) feeds both the init gate and a soft-recompile refresh of `agentRenderModelTermsOkRef`, which `maybeAttachAgentCanvas` consults exactly like the metaballs suppression (detach/re-attach, no worker teardown). **A `recompile` is in the one-shot staleness set** (audit M3): the rebuild drops every GPU buffer, so a free-mode soft recompile used to REWIND to the last synced frame — the dispatcher now reads the GPU state down first, and the rebuild's `agentGpuUploadPending` re-seeds the new runtime from it (lossless).
- **Camera** (`setAgentCamera`, present-only): SimulatorView `computeAgentRenderView` reuses the exact 2D draw math (scale/pan/infinity tile range, cap 256 copies) + `agentOutlines` + `agentGlow` + `bg2d` (the shader clears with the bg, so `draw()` skips the CPU bg fill under direct render). rAF-coalesced + deduped (a plain step doesn't move the camera, and each `setAgentCamera` triggers a present — a per-frame post would double the render cost).
- **The UI-sync driver** (`setAgentUiSync`): ON iff a feature is (or may be) reading agent state — paused (`!playing`), recording, a pinned/sweep inspector, an edit target, the agent brush armed + hovering, or metaballs suppressing direct render. Debounced OFF ~300 ms (brush strokes don't thrash). Re-evaluated per-frame in the stepped handler (catches hover-during-play) + on the state-signal effect.
- **Glow** is a new simulator Graphics option (`genesisca_sim_settings.agentGlow {on,size,intensity,steepness}`, sanitized on load) — renders ONLY on the direct path (the CPU overlay ignores it; the checkbox tooltip says "WebGPU direct render"). A checkbox + Size/Intensity/Falloff sliders in the 2D agent common-controls.
- **Verified in-browser** (the pane reports hidden, so protocol + worker state + DOM, NOT composited pixels — the compositor is suspended while occluded, so `drawImage(transferredCanvas)` / screenshots can't be read; the render pipeline is proven by `agentRenderStatus:true` [WGSL compiled 0 errors, both pipelines built, the present executed] + 0 GPU errors over 900 gens + the identical battle-tested grid blit): a WebGPU-agent Boids ENGAGES direct render; free mode ships `hasAgents:false`+`agentLiveCount` while gen advances; UI-sync ON re-ships the snapshot; the hostile staleness test (`moveAgents`/`getAgentState` mid-free-run) reflects post-readback state; `getState` is fresh; Boids flocks to **polarization 0.9991**; the stats chip reads the free-mode count; a JS-target model does NOT engage (CPU path unchanged); no re-attach storm. tsc + `npm run build` + `parity-agent-wasm` (18) + `parity-agent-force` (7) all green.

#### A2 — the same renderer, snapshot-fed (JS / WASM / per-gen-WebGPU targets)
A CPU-target (or non-resident WebGPU) agent model loses the ~10 ms main-thread Canvas2D `drawAgentsOverlay`: the worker uploads the render fields to the A1 render pipeline and presents on the same canvas. The CPU keeps simulating; only the DRAW moves to the GPU. Touches the SAME three files (no compilers / gl3d / agentEngine / JS-WASM paths — `git diff --stat` = 3 files), reusing `AGENT_RENDER_WGSL` + the camera/RenderView + attach-handshake + UI-sync driver **unchanged**.
- **The render-only surface** ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts)): `AgentRenderSurface` is the narrow render surface (device + layout + the three render buffers `agentF32`/`agentAlive`/`agentColors` + the render-pipeline state); `AgentWebGPURuntime` is structurally a superset, so every render helper (`setupAgentDirectRender` / `uploadAgentColors` / `uploadAgentRenderView` / `presentAgentsEncode` / `presentAgentsOnce`) is retyped to `AgentRenderSurface` and takes BOTH the full webgpu runtime AND the render-only surface — **parameterize over copy**. **`createAgentRenderOnlyRuntime(layout)`** acquires its OWN device + allocates only the three buffers (no compute pipelines), returns null on WebGPU-unavailable → the worker keeps the CPU overlay. **`uploadAgentRenderFields`** is the tight per-frame upload (ONLY x/y/radius runs [0,hw) + full alive + colours ≈ 1 MB at 50k, reusing persistent scratch — dead/beyond-hw slots are culled by the alive mask, so the other SoA runs need no zeroing). **`presentAgentRenderFromStore`** = tight upload + present. **`destroyAgentRenderSurface`** tears down the three buffers + view + device.
- **The worker** ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)): `agentRenderRuntime: AgentRenderSurface | null` (the CPU render-only surface, mutually exclusive with `agentWebgpuRuntime`); `activeRenderSurface() = agentWebgpuRuntime ?? agentRenderRuntime`. `presentAgentsIfActive()` dispatches — webgpu → `presentAgentsFromStore` (full SoA, its compute step reads it next), CPU → `presentAgentRenderFromStore` (tight). The A2 present rides the EXISTING `sendColors` path (the per-batch present at `if (agentRenderActive && !agentBatchPresented && !agentStoreStale) presentAgentsIfActive()`), so every CPU step batch + every mutation presents for free; free mode (`agentUiSync` OFF) ships no snapshot + `agentLiveCount` exactly like A1. `buildAgentWebGPUIfNeeded` posts `agentRuntimeReady` for a CPU target too (when a render layout is pending), so the main-thread attach handshake is IDENTICAL; `attachAgentCanvas` builds the render-only surface lazily when there's no webgpu runtime. `initAgents` / a re-init drop the render-only surface (re-attach on the next `agentRuntimeReady`). `setAgentCamera` / `refreshAgentDisplay` route through `activeRenderSurface()`.
- **The gate** ([SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx)): DROPS A1's `agentTarget === 'webgpu'` term (so JS/WASM/per-gen-WebGPU targets all qualify) and RELAXES A1's OM-exclusion to `agentTarget !== 'webgpu' || agentMappings.length === 0`. **A CPU present ALWAYS uploads `s.colors` = CPU-computed colours INCLUDING Agent Output Mappings** (`runAgentColorPass` writes them before every present), so OM-coloured CPU-target models (Particle Life itself, `agentTarget: 'wasm'`) ARE render-eligible. The OM exclusion is KEPT for the **WebGPU target** (a resident WebGPU batch presents the GPU `agentColors` the behaviour wrote, NOT the CPU OM `s.colors`, so a WebGPU+OM model must stay on the CPU overlay until A1.5 compiles the OM into a GPU colour pass). Kept: agents-only + 2D + OffscreenCanvas + no sprites + metaballs off. `compileAgentModel` computes a minimal render-only layout (`computeAgentWebGPULayout(maxAgents, 0, undefined, [], { gridDepth: 1 })`) for CPU targets and ships it as `agentRenderLayout` in the init/recompile messages (x/y/radius are the leading static SoA fields, so a minimal layout gives the right bases). The staleness one-shot rule does NOT apply to A2 (the CPU store is always fresh — one-shots serve directly).
- **Verified in-browser** (hidden pane → worker protocol + snapshot values, NOT composited pixels — the occlusion trap): **Particle Life on WASM (+ 1 Agent Output Mapping) ENGAGES A2** (`agentRenderStatus:true`, `runtimeReady:1`, 0 errors) — the model A1 EXCLUDED; its snapshot carries **6 distinct OM species colours** (~300 agents each), the exact `s.colors` the render uploads. **Boids on the JS target ENGAGES** (`active:true`) and flocks to **polarization 0.998** under A2. **Free mode ships no snapshot + `agentLiveCount`** (verified at 1800 and 50000 agents); UI-sync ON resumes snapshots. **Fallbacks keep the CPU overlay** (no attach — `renderStatus` never active): a 3D model (Particle Life 3D), a grid+agents model (Ant Necrophoresis), a sprites model. **0 console/worker errors** throughout at 50k. Handler-ms at 50k (sync ≈ 126 / free ≈ 114, ~12 ms delta) is INDICATIVE not authoritative — the hidden pane's 0-px display canvas makes `drawAgentsOverlay` a partial no-op, so the real ~10 ms draw savings need a visible pane (the same occlusion limit A1 hit; A1's pixels were user-confirmed separately). **The re-attach storm** seen while occluded (0-width parent → the `draw()` resize-check detaches/re-attaches) is PRE-EXISTING A1 SHARED behaviour (Boids-webgpu storms identically, 652/1.5s) — a hidden-pane-only artifact (a real width → `dims === parentW` → no re-attach), NOT an A2 regression, and NOT in A2's scope to fix.
