Gates: tsc + `npm run build` + `parity-agent-wasm` (18) + `parity-agent-force` (7) all green.

#### A1.5 — GPU Agent-Output-Mapping colour pass (resident fast path for OM-coloured WebGPU models)
A WebGPU-target model with **agent Output Mappings** (Particle Life's species colour) was EXCLUDED from A1's resident direct render because OM colours are computed CPU-side (`runAgentColorPass` → `s.colors`), NOT into the GPU `agentColors` the render reads. A1.5 compiles each agent OM graph into a **per-agent WGSL COMPUTE pass writing the GPU `agentColors` buffer**, dispatched GPU-side each resident batch — so OM-coloured models run FULLY RESIDENT. Touches ONLY [agentWebgpu/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/compile.ts) + [agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts) + [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts) + [SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx) (+ the DEV harness) — **NO `agentWasm`, NO JS-agent OM path, NO gl3d / agentEngine / lattice compilers**. The behaviour shader is **byte-identical** (`check-compile-identity`: only the new `omShaders` surface appears).
- **Compiler** ([agentWebgpu/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/compile.ts)): `compileAgentOutputMappingsWebGPU` flattens the agent graph, runs `injectAgentLinkedOutputMappings` (the SAME injector the JS `compileAgentGraph` uses — synthesizes the LINKED `getCellAttribute → colorScale|categorical → setCellLooks` chain + sequences it with any user `agentOutputMapping` root), then compiles EACH `agentOutputMapping` root into its own WGSL module. The behaviour-shader emit was factored into a shared **`emitAgentRootModule(rootNode, rootPortId, …, entryName)`** that BOTH the behaviour root AND every OM root call (one source of truth — the module assembly, ctx-setup, emitters), so a byte-drift on the behaviour path is a `check-compile-identity` red. The reject policy is factored into **`agentSubsetSupported`** (shared by the behaviour gate `isAgentGraphWebGPUSupported` AND the OM gate — same rejects). Result gains **`omShaders: {mappingId, code, usesBondStore/Indicators/Aux}[]` + `omSupported: boolean`** — a SEPARATE verdict: a model with a GPU behaviour but an unsupported OM keeps its behaviour on the GPU + the CPU overlay (`omSupported false`), NEVER changing the behaviour verdict. `setCellLooks` in an OM module writes packed `agentColors[idx]` (the same emitter the behaviour uses); each OM pass is dispatched only for its own mapping, so the viewer guard is a no-op. **`agentOutputMapping` was added to `AGENT_WEBGPU_SUPPORTED_TYPES`** (an entry-point root — never emitted as a value/flow node, only walked from its `do` output; the behaviour cone never contains it, so the behaviour verdict is unaffected).
- **Runtime** ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts)): `createAgentWebGPURuntime` takes `omShaders: AgentOMShaderInput[]` and builds one compute pipeline + bind group per OM (each declares bindings 0-6 always + the layout-driven field bindings 7/8 + the read-only aux/indicators/bondStore per THAT OM's usage — the Naga stripped-binding discipline; an OM never spawns / writes i32 / scatters force, so agentI32/agentAlive are always read-only-storage). All OM pipelines SHARE the runtime's SoA/control buffers. **The read-only aux/indicators/bond buffers are created for the UNION of behaviour + OM usage** (an OM may read a model attr / indicator / bond the behaviour doesn't); the behaviour bind group binds ONLY the behaviour's own declared bindings (`hasAux`/…), each OM binds its own — so buffer-existence is the union while each bind group matches its shader exactly (`bufAux`/`bufIndicators`/`bufBondStore` for creation vs `hasAux`/… for the behaviour group). `rt.omPipelines: Map<mappingId,…>` + `rt.activeOmMappingId` (the active agent viewer). **`dispatchAgentOMEncode(rt, enc, hw)`** appends the active OM pass (default to the first OM when the id isn't found, mirroring `runAgentColorPass`'s `?? agentOutputMappingFns[0]`); it runs INSIDE `dispatchResidentBatch` AFTER the gen loop (reads the committed final-state attrs) and BEFORE `presentAgentsEncode`, so the presented frame carries the OM colours.
- **Worker** ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)): `pendingAgentWebgpuOmShaders` (shipped in init/recompile as `agentWebgpuOmShaders`); `buildAgentWebGPUIfNeeded` passes them to `createAgentWebGPURuntime` + sets `rt.activeOmMappingId = agentColorViewer`. `runAgentBatchResident` re-sets `activeOmMappingId` before each batch (a viewer switch mid-play); the `colorPass` handler sets it on a viewer switch. **The GPU OM dispatch lives ONLY in `dispatchResidentBatch`** (the one path where the CPU never computes `s.colors` — free-mode resident presents from GPU `agentColors`); every OTHER present (paused / mutation / non-resident per-gen / attach) goes through `presentAgentsFromStore` which uploads the CPU `s.colors` (= `runAgentColorPass`'s OM colours) — a JUSTIFIED DEVIATION from the handoff's "OM dispatch in presentAgentsOnce paths": the CPU present already carries the correct OM colours, the OM colour math (categorical/linear → integer RGB) is integer-exact so there's NO GPU-vs-CPU flicker, and it avoids a control-uniform-staleness problem at attach (control.highWater is only fresh after a resident batch). The CPU `runAgentColorPass` is KEPT exactly as-is (it serves snapshot consumers under UI-sync).
- **SimulatorView** ([SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx)): the A1/A2 gate's OM term becomes `(model.agentMappings?.length ?? 0) === 0 || !!agentResult.agentWebgpuOmSupported` (only the WebGPU-target arm — CPU targets already render OM colours via `s.colors`). Ships `agentWebgpuOmShaders` in init/recompile. Everything else (attach / camera / UI-sync) is A1/A2 shared code.
- **Verified in-browser (real GPU, hidden pane → worker protocol + GPU-buffer readback, not composited pixels)**: Particle Life on the WebGPU target ENGAGES direct render (the A1 exclusion lifted) + free-runs resident (`hasAgents:false` + `agentLiveCount:1800`), **0 GPU errors**. **The GPU `agentColors` buffer (read via a free-mode `getState` → the one-shot readback, NO CPU recolour) contains EXACTLY the 6 PL species colours, BUCKET-IDENTICAL to the CPU OM path** (same counts per species) — the definitive colour-correctness proof (the handoff's frozen-state CPU-vs-GPU comparison). A **two-view** test (a 2nd linked view with a grayscale palette): switching the active agent viewer re-colours the GPU buffer to the grayscale palette and back to species, bidirectionally, 0 errors — the `activeOmMappingId` selection works. **Boids (no OMs) unregressed** — the OM dispatch is a no-op (empty `omPipelines`), the behaviour shader is byte-identical, and it flocks to **polarization 0.9992** (sampled via `getAgentState`; NB `getState`'s vx/vy field has a PRE-EXISTING readback artifact — garbage in some slots — unrelated to A1.5, which touches no velocity path). An **unsupported OM** (a standalone `agentOutputMapping` with a `neighborIndex` Compare) → `omSupported:false` while the behaviour verdict stays `true` → the model keeps its GPU behaviour + the CPU overlay (no crash). Gates: tsc + `npm run build` + `parity-agent-wasm` (18) + `parity-agent-force` (7) + `check-compile-identity` (25 models, only the new `agent.webgpu.om`/`omSupported` surface added) all green.

#### B1 — bin-sorted mirror for the ENGINE force pass (branch `optimize`)
The resident hash build additionally scatters a **bin-SORTED, field-major MIRROR** of the fields the engine force pass reads for NEIGHBOURS, and the resident force pass reads them COALESCED from the mirror at the CSR slot instead of the random-access indirection `binAgents[p] → agentF32[base + j]`. Purely a resident-path optimization; the behaviour shader + the per-gen GPU path + every CPU/WASM path are UNTOUCHED. **NB B1's diff spans TWO commits**: most of the implementation was accidentally swept into hotfix `380b00b` by an orchestrator `git add -A`, and the follow-up commit removes a stray temp-debug line + adds these docs.
- **The mirror** (built ONLY when `needScan = usesBondingPhysics || usesSoftCollision || agentUsesDensity` — i.e. the force scan actually runs): a `sortedBuf` (field-major, one contiguous `maxAgents` f32 run per **`agentMirrorFields(is3d)`** field = `['x','y','radius','vx','vy']` 2D / `['x','y','z','radius','vx','vy','vz']` 3D; a field's slot `s` is `sorted[k*maxAgents + s]`) + a `sortedId` u32 array (each CSR slot's canonical agent id). Both live in `AgentResidentRuntime` ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts)), allocated only under `needScan`. Pure-custom-force models (**Boids / Particle Life** — `customForcesOnly`, no collision/density) skip the scan ⇒ `needScan` false ⇒ **NO mirror buffers, NO mirror scatter, NO mirror force** (zero cost; the resident batch uses the shared per-gen `rt.forcePipeline`). `agentMirrorFields` (exported from [forcePass.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/forcePass.ts)) is the SINGLE source shared by the mirror scatter emit + the mirror force scan emit.
- **The mirror scatter** (`emitHashScatterWGSL(layout, withMirror)`): the existing GPU scatter EXTENDS to also write `sorted[k*MA + slot] = agentF32[fieldBase + i]` (one extra store per mirror field) + `sortedId[slot] = i` at the SAME CSR `slot` it writes `binAgents[slot] = i` (same atomic cursor) — so `binAgents`/`sortedId`/the mirror stay index-consistent, and the mirror is a strict SUPERSET of the plain scatter (the behaviour shader still reads `binAgents` for `getNearbyAgents`). Runs only in `dispatchResidentBatch`'s hash build (`if hashValid`) — when the world is too small to tile (`hashValid 0`), no scatter runs and the mirror force falls back to the CANONICAL all-pairs branch.
- **The mirror force pass** (`emitAgentForcePassWGSL(layout, usesForceScatter, mirror)` — a **resident-only variant flag**, default `false`): when `mirror`, the hash-stencil branch reads each neighbour's fields COALESCED from `sorted[k*MA + p]` (+ `j = sortedId[p]` for the self-skip; no `agentAlive[j]` check — the mirror holds only alive agents), while the SELF agent + the all-pairs fallback stay canonical (own slot / `agentF32`). **`mirror` false ⇒ the emitted WGSL is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the pre-B1 force pass** (verified: 2D/3D × scatter on/off all byte-identical), so the per-gen `rt.forcePipeline` + `check-compile-identity` are unaffected. `ensureAgentResident(rt, needScan)` builds a SEPARATE `res.forceMirrorPipeline` (bindings 5=sorted / 6=sortedId added) under `needScan`; `dispatchResidentBatch` routes to it when `res.forceMirrorPipeline` is set, else the shared `rt.forcePipeline`. The mirror buffers are added to the destroy path.
- **Verified on the real GPU** (in-browser, occlusion-safe compute + message-driven waits): (1) a **standalone** page-context test — the mirror force fed a matched-order hash is **BIT-IDENTICAL** to the canonical force (0 density-count mismatch, 0 position delta) AND matches a CPU brute-force neighbour count (2D+3D fields, 0 mismatches); (2) a **soft-collision gas** (a general-property clone of Boids: `agentCapabilities.collision:'soft'` + webgpu target) ENGAGES the mirror (`needScan=true`, `forceMirrorPipeline` set) and a direct GPU-scatter readback confirms **every alive agent appears exactly once** in the mirror with correct field values (`radiusMismatch=0`, `idOutOfRange=0`, `aliveSeenOnce=N/N` at 450 & 50000 agents) — a correct CSR permutation; the gas produces physically-correct collision (0.35% overlapping pairs, median-nn 1.45); (3) **Boids** (custom force) builds NO mirror + flocks (polarization 0.998, no regression); tsc + build + `parity-agent-force` (7) + `parity-agent-wasm` + `check-compile-identity` (25 models unchanged) all green.
- **HONEST perf finding (a wash — like W1).** Whole-batch timing (the only GPU timing this path exposes — the force pass is 1 of ~6 passes/gen + the readback): 10k dense collision mirror **3.73** vs canonical **3.46** ms/gen; 50k mirror **46.83** vs canonical **47.03** ms/gen — a WASH within measurement noise (the force-pass-specific coalescing improvement is below worker-round-trip resolution; the extra scatter stores + `sortedId` read offset the coalescing gain at these scales). B1 is **correctness-neutral, zero-cost-for-custom-force infrastructure for B2** (the compiler fused gather, where the behaviour graph's `getNearbyAgents` loops read neighbour FIELDS from the same sorted storage — the intended coalescing win), NOT a standalone speedup at the measured scales. Mirrors the W1 conclusion for the WASM force pass.

#### C — 3D free-mode WGSL sphere render (gl3d keeps frame mode; branch `optimize`)
3D agents-only models get the free-mode fast path too: in **free mode** the WORKER renders the agents as **sphere impostors in WGSL from its buffers** into a canvas layered UNDER the gl3d canvas (browser-composited, NO blit); gl3d renders ONLY the overlays (axes/grid/bounds/gizmo/brush plane + outline) over a **transparent clear**. Any feature that needs CPU state flips UI-sync → **frame mode** → gl3d renders EVERYTHING from the snapshot exactly as today. RENDER-ONLY (**zero compiler changes** — `git diff --stat` = [gl3d.ts](src/simulator/render/gl3d.ts) + [agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts) + [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts) + [SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx), no `compiler/` file / agentEngine / lattice); the 2D disc path + every non-3D-eligible model are byte-behaviour-identical. Both feeds work: resident (WebGPU target) and snapshot-fed (CPU targets, the A2 render-only surface).
- **The gate** (extends the 2D gate, all GENERAL): agents-only AND `is3D` AND no sprites AND metaballs OFF AND **`resolveMaxBonds(model.centerBased) === 0`** (no bond LINES to draw — resident models satisfy this; it only bites snapshot-fed 3D bonded models) AND **3D alpha-blend OFF** (translucent spheres need back-to-front sorting = gl3d's job; opaque impostors + depth-write only) AND OffscreenCanvas. Re-evaluated on every model/settings change: **flipping Alpha blend ON detaches** (→ frame mode) and OFF re-attaches (an `alpha3d` effect, mirroring the metaballs detach/re-attach). The 2D/3D adds are behind `!is3D ||` so 2D is untouched.
- **The WGSL sphere pass** ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts) `agentRenderSphereWGSL`): a camera-facing billboard quad per agent (VS pulls x/y/z/radius from `agentF32` + packed `agentColors` + `agentAlive`, **same Z-up remap as gl3d SPHERE_VS: col→+X, row→−Y, layer→−Z**); FS ray-casts the unit sphere, discards on miss/dead/alpha-0, writes `@builtin(frag_depth)` so spheres depth-sort, and shades with the **SAME formula gl3d's SPHERE_FS uses** (`ambient + diffuse·max(0,n·L)` + Blinn-Phong `specular·pow(max(0,n·H),32)`; shadows/AO NOT replicated — frame mode covers those). Opaque (no blend) + a `depth24plus` attachment (`ensureAgentDepthTex`, recreated on resize). `setupAgentDirectRender` branches on `layout.gridDepth > 1` (sphere pipeline) vs 2D (disc); `presentAgentsEncode` routes to `presentAgentSpheresEncode` when `rt.render3D` — so the resident batch present + camera/mutation presents all work 3D with one branch.
- **The MVP is main-thread-computed from the SAME math gl3d uses** — `sceneCameraMatrices(cam, aspect, W, H, D)` + `lightWorldDirFor(light, dir, right, up)` are **EXPORTED from gl3d.ts** and gl3d's `setCamera`/`lightWorldDir` now DELEGATE to them (single source → the two renderers can't disagree on projection/lighting). SimulatorView's `computeAgentRenderView3D` ships the MVP (16 f32) + camera basis + world light dir + uHalf + bg in a `mode:'3d'` `AgentRenderView3D` via the SAME `setAgentCamera` message (`AgentRenderView | AgentRenderView3D` union; the worker's `applyAgentRenderView` routes by `.mode`). The 3D uniform is a 176-byte struct (`RenderView3D`, mirrored in `uploadAgentRenderView3D`).
- **Layering** ([SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx)): a stable `<div>` sphere layer (`position:absolute; z-index:1; pointer-events:none`) sits UNDER the gl canvas (given `z-index:2` in 3D). `maybeAttachAgentCanvas` (3D branch) imperatively appends a FRESH `<canvas>` into the layer each attach (handles transfer-once + resize + recompile), transfers its control, and the browser composites it (no blit). gl3d gets `setOverlaysOnly(on)` — a small additive flag; `render()` skips voxels/agents/bonds/metaballs/shadows + clears TRANSPARENT when set, keeping only the interaction overlays.
- **The free/frame flip** (`draw()` 3D branch): `agent3dFrame = active && agentUiSyncPostedRef && agentsRef != null` (frame mode requires a snapshot IN HAND — arming a feature posts UI-sync ON but the snapshot arrives ~1 frame later, so keep the spheres visible until then → seamless, no blank frame). Free = active && !frame → `setOverlaysOnly(true)` + show the sphere canvas + `postAgentCamera()` (deduped). The worker keeps the sphere canvas fresh in BOTH modes (the resident batch presents inside its submit regardless), so flipping visibility back is instant. **The paused-render fix** (worker `setAgentUiSync`): OFF→ON ALSO ships ONE snapshot (`sendColors()` after `ensureAgentStoreFresh`) so a PAUSED frame-mode gl3d — which draws/picks agents from the snapshot, not the sphere canvas — has fresh agents immediately (a paused sim ships no step). A dedicated `glPointerOverRef` (gl-canvas enter/leave) drives the 3D agent-brush frame-mode flip so the gl3d pick FBO resolves.
- **Recording/screenshot** need no new code: recording forces UI-sync ON ⇒ frame mode ⇒ gl3d full render ⇒ `readPixels` captures the spheres.
- **Known accepted losses in free mode** (documented): overlay↔sphere depth interaction (the brush plane won't occlude spheres — "Draw agents in front" collapses to always-front for overlays) and shadows/AO/alpha-blend (gated off or frame-mode-only). Models wanting those use frame mode (interact/pause/record).
- **Verified on the real GPU** (in-browser, occlusion-safe: worker protocol + DOM + message-driven `performance.now()`-bounded waits — hidden pages throttle `setTimeout`/`setInterval`, NOT worker messages, so timer-poll loops hang [a trap hit + avoided]; composited PIXELS + the lighting-parity eyeball need a VISIBLE pane): **PL3D on the WebGPU target** engages 3D direct render (`agentRenderStatus:true` → the sphere WGSL compiled 0 errors, pipelines built, present ran), free-runs resident (stepped ships `hasAgents:false` + `agentLiveCount:1200`), 0 GPU errors over many batches, agents move in 3D (getAgentState z 4.11→5.92). **CPU-target (wasm) PL3D** engages the snapshot-fed render-only 3D surface (renderStatus true, free liveCount 1200). **Frame-mode flip**: OFF→ON ships a snapshot even PAUSED (the fix); Alpha-blend ON detaches → frame-mode snapshot (has:1, no:0), OFF re-attaches (renderStatus true). **Fallbacks**: 3D Tissue (maxBonds 12) stays on the CPU path (no attach); 2D Boids-webgpu still uses the disc render (sphere layer empty). **Layering**: sphere layer z-index 1 comes BEFORE the gl canvas (z-index 2) in DOM (correct under/over). No sphere-canvas leak (exactly 1 element across all re-attaches), 0 errors across the whole session. tsc + `npm run build` + `parity-agent-wasm` (18) + `parity-agent-force` (7) green.

#### D — field-decoupled grid+agents (residency + render relax; branch `optimize`)
The A1/A2/A1.5/C fast paths required an **agents-only** model (`gridCells === false`). D replaces that proxy with the TRUE first-principles term — **field DECOUPLING**. A grid+agents model whose agent layer never touches a cell field (the agent↔grid morphogen round-trip) is two INDEPENDENT sims sharing a viewport: the agent layer runs resident + direct-renders while the grid steps + renders by its own (any-target) path, composited as layers (grid below, transparent agent canvas above — the 2D z-order agents already use). RENDER + worker only — **zero compiler changes** (`git diff --stat` = [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts) + [SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx); the `agentUsesField`/`fieldSpecs` flags already exist). Every field-coupled / agents-only / non-eligible model is byte-behaviour-identical.
- **The decoupling predicate** (general; each side computes its own — the worker from its state, the main thread from the model): `agentUsesField === false` (no field node — sampleField/fieldGradient/readCellsUnder/affectCellsUnder/secreteToField — reachable in the agent graph) AND `fieldSpecs.length === 0` (no cell attribute has `agentAccess !== 'none'`; belt-and-braces — with no field nodes the ABI threads nothing). Replaces the agents-only proxy in BOTH gates.
- **Worker residency** (`agentResidentEligible`): `!gridCellsEnabled` → `!agentUsesField && fieldSpecs.length === 0`. NO 3D restriction on residency (a 3D decoupled grid+agents model can still run its SIM resident + snapshot-render via gl3d — only the DIRECT render gate excludes 3D grid+agents, see below). Every other eligibility term (async attrs / no springs / no growth / no spawn/stop / no indicator accumulation / `stopMessages.length === 0`) is unchanged.
- **The resident step-batch** (the `agentStore && agentTarget === 'webgpu'` branch — JS/WASM grid + WebGPU agents): when eligible AND `gridCells && simulateCells && stepFn`, run the resident agent batch (`runAgentBatchResident(count, /*bump*/false)`) FIRST, then — only on success — the grid's N `runStep(true)` (its own JS/WASM per-gen path) + the batch-tail deferred indicator scan + a sparse-safe `runColorPass(true)`, then `sendColors()`. Order is free (decoupled = no shared state); running the resident batch first (it advances nothing on failure) keeps the fallback CLEAN — a bailout falls through to the existing per-gen loop (which already steps both layers) with no double-stepped grid. **Generation counts ONCE per gen**: `runAgentBatchResident` gained a `bumpGeneration` param (default true = agents-only owns the count) — a decoupled batch passes `false` and the cell-step loop does the `generation++`. The per-gen fallback loop already stepped both layers + counted once (its grid `runStep` increments; the agent step doesn't), so it needed NO change. The one-shot / readback / free-vs-frame policy is agent-store-only, unchanged.
- **Render gate + composite** ([SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx)): `gridCells === false` → `(gridCells === false || (agentDecoupled && !is3D))`. **3D grid+agents deliberately keeps the CPU path in D** (the `&& !is3D` on the decoupled arm) — voxels-vs-spheres depth compositing across two canvases is Phase E (one device, one canvas); 3D agents-only stays eligible via `gridCells === false`. `draw()` needed NO change — the 2D structure already blits the grid (`showGrid2d` → srcCanvas/blitSource) THEN the agent canvas above, and suppresses the CPU bg fill when the grid shows. The ONE render tweak: `computeAgentRenderView` clears the agent canvas **TRANSPARENT** (bgA=0) when `showGrid2d` (the grid IS the background — the grid shows through, discs composite over it), applying `bg2d` only when the grid layer is hidden (agents-only / "Show CA grid" off) — mirroring the existing `draw()` bg-fill suppression. The grid itself renders via its normal `colors` → srcCanvas → `putImageData` path (the grid direct-render attach is gated `!agentModel`, so an agent model never GPU-direct-renders the grid).
- **Verified in-browser** (real WebGPU, hidden pane → worker protocol + `getState` cell attrs + a TEMP `__dresident` probe on the resident batch, since removed; composited PIXELS / z-order visual / recording-both-layers need a VISIBLE pane, flagged for the eyeball): a **SYNTHETIC decoupled model** (Boids agent side + Game of Life cell side [attrs/nbhd/graph/mappings], WASM grid, no `agentAccess` attrs): on the **WebGPU agent target** it ENGAGES residency (the resident batch ran with `bump:false, grid:true` — ONE submit for all N gens), free mode ships `hasAgents:false` + `agentLiveCount:260` + the GRID colours (57600 = 120²×4), the grid **evolves** (a seeded 20×20 block: aliveSum 400→328 over 4 gens = GoL ran), generation counts ONCE per gen (0→8→12 over 8+4 steps), 0 errors. On a **CPU (js) agent target** the same model engages the A2 snapshot-fed render (free mode `hasAgents:false` + liveCount) with the grid stepping below (colours ship), 0 errors. **Field-coupled fallbacks**: Ant Necrophoresis + Chemotaxis (both `agentUsesField` true) → NO resident batch, per-gen CPU path ships agents + grid colours as today, 0 errors. **Agents-only Boids (WebGPU)**: unchanged (resident batch `bump:true, grid:false`, free mode, no colours). tsc + `npm run build` + `parity-agent-wasm` (18) + `parity-agent-force` (7) green.

#### E1 — unified GPUDevice singleton (branch `optimize`)
Before E1 the grid runtime ([webgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/webgpuRuntime.ts)) and each agent runtime ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts) — full compute runtime + the A2 render-only surface) each requested their OWN `GPUDevice`. A WebGPU-grid + WebGPU-agent model held TWO devices (double driver pressure; the two runtimes can't share a buffer, so a field-coupled model still round-trips the field CPU-side); a re-attach that built a fresh render-only device without releasing the prior one leaked a whole device (the C-report note). **E1 makes ONE worker-owned device serve every WebGPU runtime**, via a small refcounted singleton [sharedGpuDevice.ts](src/simulator/engine/sharedGpuDevice.ts): `acquireSharedGpuDevice()` (adapter + device requested ONCE with the UNION of both runtimes' `requiredLimits` = the grid's superset — the retry-with-defaults fallback preserved) / `releaseSharedGpuDevice(device)` (destroys the device only when the LAST reference is released). Every `createX` runtime factory takes the device from the singleton (each acquire is released on throw so a mid-build failure doesn't leak); every `destroyX` releases its reference instead of destroying the device outright (which would have killed a still-live sibling runtime). `uncapturederror` + `device.lost` (filtering reason `'destroyed'` = our own teardown) are registered ONCE at the singleton — the per-runtime hooks removed (the worker's per-agent-runtime `onuncapturederror`/`lost` block, `createWebGPURuntime`/`createAgentWebGPURuntime` `addEventListener`), since on a shared device they'd mislabel grid errors as agent errors and accumulate a lost handler per rebuild; real dispatch failures stay user-visible via the existing `pushErrorScope('validation')` around each dispatch. **Failure isolation**: either runtime's pipeline build failing degrades that runtime only (falls back exactly as today); the device survives for the other. RENDER/runtime-only, **ZERO compiler changes** (`git diff --stat` = the new `sharedGpuDevice.ts` + the two runtime files + a small worker hunk); every non-WebGPU / single-runtime model is byte-behaviour-identical.
- **The device-leak METRIC** (DEV-only `sharedGpuAdapterRequestCount()` / `sharedGpuRefCount()` in the module): the adapter is requested exactly ONCE per worker and stays 1 across all in-session rebuilds (recompile / reset / target flips) because the singleton is reused. **Verified in-browser** (real WebGPU): Chemotaxis on **WebGPU grid + WebGPU agents** → **adapterRequests = 1, refCount = 2** (BOTH runtimes on ONE device — pre-E1 this was two devices), 0 errors; after **2 recompile cycles** (each rebuilds both runtimes) adapterRequests **stays 1** + refCount balanced at 2 (rebuilds reuse the device, no leak, no premature destroy); the field model steps to gen 20 + post-recompile on the shared device, fresh `getState`, 0 errors; agents-only **Boids-webgpu** flocks to **polarization 0.9987** on the shared device (adapterRequests 1, refCount 1). tsc + `npm run build` + `parity-agent-wasm` (18) + `parity-agent-force` (7) green.
- **E1 SCOPE — device unification ONLY (this session); the zero-copy field bridge is NOT shipped.** The E1 handoff's item 2 ("bind the agent `fieldRead` binding to the GRID's `attrsRead` buffer DIRECTLY") is a **confirmed-false assumption**: the agent field layout ([agentWebgpu/layout.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/layout.ts)) is a COMPACTED `array<f32>` (one `fieldTotal`-run per field attr, `fieldReadBase + cellIdx`, no sentinel) while the grid attrs buffer ([webgpu/layout.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/webgpu/layout.ts)) is per-attr bitcast WORDS (`attr.wordOffset + cellIdx`, `cellsPerAttr = total` OR `total+1` with a constant-boundary sentinel, in the grid's attr order) — a raw bind reads the wrong offsets, and int/bool/tag fields differ in bit pattern (grid `bitcast<u32>(i32)` vs agent `f32`). A direct bind needs a compiler change (forbidden). Also, a WebGPU-grid + WebGPU-agent FIELD model currently runs its **agents on JS** (the `webgpuActive` branch's `runAgentStep()`), so field-coupled residency additionally needs a branch restructure to route those agents onto the GPU runtime. Per the phase's "STOP on a false assumption, don't redesign" rule, the field bridge is deferred with a concrete GPU-copy re-plan (copyBufferToBuffer grid↔agent field runs on the shared device, float-only gate) — see the E1 Completion Report in [docs/HANDOFF_GPU_AGENT_RENDER_E.md](docs/HANDOFF_GPU_AGENT_RENDER_E.md). E2 (single-canvas composite) can build on the shared device this session delivers.

#### E1b — GPU field bridge via buffer copies (field-coupled residency; branch `optimize`)
On the E1 shared device, a **float-field** model on **WebGPU grid + WebGPU agents** now runs the per-generation field round-trip as **GPU `copyBufferToBuffer` passes** instead of the CPU `uploadAgentField`/`readbackAgentField` round-trip — so Chemotaxis/Ant-class models run their whole generation GPU-side (grid step + agent step + field bridge on one device). NOT a direct bind (E1 proved that needs a compiler change) — the float-only gate makes a plain byte copy sufficient (grid `bitcast<u32>(f32)` word == agent `f32`). RENDER/worker only, **ZERO compiler changes** (`git diff --stat` = [agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts) + [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts) + the Properties hint in [PropertiesPanelContent.tsx](src/modeler/panels/PropertiesPanelContent.tsx)); the layouts are untouched (the whole point). Every JS/WASM-grid field model + every int/bool/tag-field model keeps today's CPU bridge byte-for-byte.
- **The gate** (`agentFieldBridgeGpuEligible()`, all GENERAL): WebGPU grid active (`useWebGPU && webgpuRuntime.stepReady`) + WebGPU agent target (`agentTarget === 'webgpu'`, agent runtime ready) + the **shared device** (`agentWebgpuRuntime.device === webgpuRuntime.device`) + a field bridge present (`fieldReadLen>0 || fieldWriteLen>0`) + **every `fieldSpecs` attr is type `float`**. Any int/bool/tag field (bit-pattern mismatch), a non-shared device, or a JS/WASM grid → false → the CPU bridge (unchanged).
- **The copies** ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts) `primeAgentFieldFromGrid` / `foldAgentFieldToGrid`): **prime** = per read-attr `copyBufferToBuffer(gridAttrsReadBuf @ attr.byteOffset → agentFieldReadBuf @ fieldReadBase[id]*4, total*4)` + per write-attr the same into `agentFieldDepositBuf` (mirrors `uploadAgentField`'s priming of the atomic accumulator with the current field; `total*4` bytes copy cells 0..total-1, so a constant-boundary sentinel at index `total` is excluded automatically). **fold** = per write-attr `copyBufferToBuffer(agentFieldDepositBuf @ fieldWriteBase[id]*4 → gridAttrsReadBuf @ attr.byteOffset, total*4)` — the deposit words ARE the final f32 field (`readbackAgentField` is a plain copy today), so the fold is a plain copy, no decode. Both agent field buffers already carry the needed usage (`fieldReadBuf` COPY_DST, `fieldDepositBuf` COPY_SRC|COPY_DST) and grid `attrsBufA/B` already carry COPY_SRC|COPY_DST — no usage-flag change needed.
- **Routing** ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts) `webgpuActive` branch): the field-agent block became `if (…&& agentFieldBridgeGpuEligible())` (GPU bridge) `else if (…)` (the unchanged CPU bridge). Per gen: guard `if (!gpuOwnsAttrs) uploadAttrs` (make the grid GPU-side fresh if CPU was authoritative), **re-resolve the LIVE `webgpuRuntime.attrsReadBuf` each gen** (`buildGpuFieldBridge()` — it ping-pongs per step), then `runAgentStepWebGPU(bridge)`. `runAgentStepWebGPUInner` takes an optional `gpuFieldBridge`: when set, the `uploadAgentField` prime becomes `primeAgentFieldFromGrid` and the `readbackAgentField` fold becomes `foldAgentFieldToGrid` (the CPU `readArrays`/`writeArrays` build is skipped). Queue order is the barrier (separate submits: prime → dispatchAgentStep → fold → grid `runStepWebGPU`); the grid step reads the folded `attrsReadBuf`, its copy line carries the deposit into the next gen. **`gpuOwnsAttrs` stays TRUE** (the CPU `readAttrs` is never touched) — a `getState` mid-run pulls fresh grid attrs via `ensureCpuAttrsFresh`, and the per-gen path commits the agent SoA each gen (readbackAgentStep) so agents are fresh too. On any GPU bail (hash overflow / device failure → `runAgentStepWebGPU` returns false) the gen falls back to the CPU bridge inline (the prime touched no grid state; the fold never ran), loudly-but-safely.
- **RNG note**: routing agents JS→GPU changes the RNG family (shared xorshift32 → per-agent PCG) — equivalence is STATISTICAL (the documented WebGPU stance), never bitwise.
- **DEV probe** (verification only, mirrors E1's `sharedGpuAdapterRequestCount`): module counters `e1bGpuBridgeGenCount`/`e1bCpuBridgeFallbackCount` + a `__e1bCounters` worker message (the app never sends it) report the GPU-vs-CPU gen counts + live eligibility.
- **Verified on the real GPU** (in-browser, hidden pane → worker protocol + `getState` field/agent metrics): **Chemotaxis on WebGPU grid + WebGPU agents** ENGAGES the bridge (`eligible:true`, `sharedDevice:true`, `fieldSpecTypes:['float']`; over 420 gens **gpuBridge=420, cpuFallback=0**, 0 errors), the field BUILDS (chemical max 0→34, all 10000 cells nonzero) and agents AGGREGATE (occupied bins 89→69, clusterSq 712→1016) — **statistically equivalent to the CPU-bridge run** (WASM grid + JS agents: clusterSq 1000, occupied 68, chemMax 31 — the only difference is the documented RNG family). **Ant Necrophoresis** likewise (piles form: corpse max 1→8, cells≥3 0→44 over 600 gens, gpuBridge=600, 0 errors). **Defaults untouched**: shipped Chemotaxis (WASM grid) → `eligible:false` → CPU bridge. **Int-field synthetic** (chemical type flipped to integer) → `eligible:false` → CPU bridge, 0 errors. **Agents-only Boids-webgpu** unchanged (gpuFieldBridge 0, polarization 0.999). **Perf** (warm step-batch, WebGPU grid, GPU bridge vs CPU bridge [= WebGPU grid + JS agents]): 100×100 field **3.34 vs 6.67 ms/gen (~2×)**; 200×200 (4× field) **4.04 vs 8.00 ms/gen (~2×, scales better)**. tsc + `npm run build` + `parity-agent-wasm` (18) + `parity-agent-force` (7) green.

#### E2 — single-canvas composite render (2D grid+agents on a WebGPU grid; branch `optimize`)
A **2D grid+agents model with a WebGPU GRID + a WebGPU AGENT target** presents BOTH layers into **ONE DISPLAY-sized canvas per frame, one encoder** at DISPLAY resolution through the camera — the grid layer first (a fullscreen triangle whose FS inverts the camera to sample the grid runtime's `colorsBuf`), then the agent disc pass with `loadOp:'load'` over it. This **removes the grid's per-gen colors READBACK for agent models** AND the two-canvas composite, WHILE keeping agents crisp discs at any zoom. Covers BOTH **decoupled** (D — GoL grid + Boids agents) and **field-coupled** (E1b — Chemotaxis/Ant Necrophoresis); the field-bridge mechanism is orthogonal to the render (the composite only READS `colorsBuf` for display — the E1b GPU field bridge is untouched). RENDER/worker only, **ZERO compiler changes** (`git diff --stat` = [agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts) + [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts) + [SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx) + the uniform-layout harness — NO gl3d / agentEngine / lattice / **even `webgpuRuntime.ts`** untouched: the grid layer is a self-contained render-pass in the agent runtime, binding the grid `colorsBuf` on the shared E1 device). Every non-eligible config keeps the D two-canvas / A1 / CPU-overlay path byte-for-byte.
- **The DISPLAY-res arrangement (the load-bearing redesign — the world-res original was rejected for the "blob of cells").** The composite canvas is **DISPLAY-sized** (the A1 sizing, NOT grid `W×H`). The grid layer is a fullscreen triangle whose FS **INVERTS the camera** — `wx = (fragCoord.x − oxPx) / scalePx`, wrap (torus/infinity) or bounds-discard, then the cell is `min(gridW−1, u32(wx))` / `min(gridH−1, u32(wy))` (integer floor of the world coord = NEAREST = hard cell edges = crisp CA-block cells; NOT linear) → `colorsIn[row*gridW+col]`. This is **DISPLAY-PIXEL-BOUND** (one cell lookup per covered display pixel), NOT cell-count-bound — a 5000² field costs the same to present as a 300² one (the single sampled plane is the efficient realization of "a plane of cells"; do NOT render W×H instanced quads). The agent discs render through the **SAME display-res camera** as the A1 render (`scalePx=zoom·baseScale, ox/oy=pan, canvas=display, copies/tiling`) — so agents stay CRISP DISCS at any zoom (the fix for the world-res cell-sized blobs).
- **The combined present** ([agentWebgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/agentWebgpuRuntime.ts) `setupAgentCompositeRender` / `presentCompositeEncode` / `presentAgentCompositeFromStore`): `GRID_PRESENT_WGSL` (the FS-inverse fullscreen triangle above) + a small **`GridPlaneView`** uniform (32 B, all scalars — `gridW,gridH,torus,scalePx,oxPx,oyPx` — registered in [scripts/verify-render-uniform-layouts.mjs](scripts/verify-render-uniform-layouts.mjs) via `writeGridPlaneView`; no vec3 trap). The camera the grid uniform carries (`scalePx/oxPx/oyPx/torus`) is the SAME the disc pass reads from `renderViewBuf`, so grid + agents can't disagree on the transform. The disc pipeline is REUSED via a factored **`buildAgentDiscPipelines`** (shared with `setupAgentDiscRender`). One encoder: grid pass (`loadOp:'clear'` transparent + write) → agent pass (`loadOp:'load'`, display-res camera). **Per-layer Show toggles**: `showGrid` false → skip the grid pass + the agent pass clears to the agent `bg2d`; `showAgents` false → skip the disc draw (both threaded through the `AgentRenderView.showGrid/showAgents` CPU-only fields, NOT the RENDER_VIEW byte layout). The canvas stays `RENDER_ATTACHMENT`-only (a render pass, not compute-present).
- **The gate** ([SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx), `agentComposite`, all GENERAL): `agentModel && gridCells !== false && !is3D && useWebGPU(grid) && !webgpuResult.error && agentResult.agentTarget === 'webgpu' && offscreenSupported && agentRenderModelTermsOk(sprites/OM) && !metaballs`. The **union** `agentRenderEligible || agentComposite` drives the attach machinery (a field-coupled composite model is NOT `agentRenderEligible` — `agentDecoupled` is false — but IS composite-eligible). The worker ASSERTS the shared device (`rt.device === webgpuRuntime.device`) before enabling composite; if it refuses, it falls back to the disc render + acks `composite:false` — and since the canvas is DISPLAY-sized in both cases now, the main thread simply treats it as a plain A1 direct render (NO re-attach; post-E1 the device is always shared so this never fires). A **CPU agent target keeps the D two-canvas / A2 path** (composite-for-CPU-agents is a follow-up — it needs the A2 render-only surface). The worker `agentCompositeActive` flag skips the grid colors readback + routes `presentAgentsIfActive` to the composite present.
- **Worker orchestration** ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)): the composite present rides the EXISTING agent present points (batch tail via `sendColors`'s `presentAgentsIfActive`, camera, mutations, refresh — all composite-aware). `presentAgentsIfActive`'s composite branch reads the stashed `agentRenderView` for `scalePx/oxPx/oyPx/torus` + `showGrid/showAgents` and passes them to `presentAgentCompositeFromStore`. The branch-1 (`webgpuActive`) batch tail runs `runColorPassWebGPU()` (grid `colorsBuf` fresh on GPU) then `finalizeStepWebGPU({ needColors: !agentCompositeActive })` — **skipping the grid colors readback** — and `sendColors` ships NO `colors` (the composite canvas carries them; the main thread blits it). `attachAgentCanvas` gained a `composite` flag → `setupAgentCompositeRender` (on a DISPLAY-sized canvas). Recording captures the DISPLAY canvas (which holds the composite blit), so no colors readback is needed even under recording.
- **`draw()`** ([SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx)): when `agentCompositeActiveRef`, the DISPLAY-sized composite canvas is blitted **1:1** (like the A1 direct render) at the grid-blit position, with resize-reattach on a real display-size change (transferred canvas dims are fixed); the grid srcCanvas blit / glyph overlay / CPU bg fill / `drawAgentsOverlay` are ALL skipped (grid+agents are in the composite); gridlines + brush cursor overlay on top. `computeAgentRenderView` computes the STANDARD display-res camera (unified with the non-composite A1 branch) and, when composite, ADDS the `showGrid/showAgents/torus` flags. `postAgentCamera` posts on pan/zoom/resize (the display-res camera DOES change now — unlike the world-res original).
- **Verified on the real GPU (VISIBLE pane, 2026-07-25 — screenshots)**: **Chemotaxis on WebGPU/WebGPU (field-coupled)** ENGAGES composite (`composite:true`), 250 steps with **0 grid-colours readbacks** (`withColors:0`); at extreme zoom the agents are **CRISP SMOOTH ROUND CYAN DISCS over crisp SQUARE grid cells** (the mandatory pass/fail — the world-res bug rendered them as cell-sized square blocks; the DISPLAY-res redesign renders smooth circles), the Viridis chemical FIELD builds up and agents AGGREGATE into clusters (the field bridge works). **Decoupled (GoL grid + Boids agents synthetic, WebGPU/WebGPU)**: `composite:true`, 0 readbacks, generation advancing (both layers), 260 agents flock, the **Show-Agents toggle** hides the agent layer. **Ant Necrophoresis (field-coupled, WebGPU/WebGPU)**: `composite:true`, 0 readbacks, the **E1b GPU field bridge is UNCHANGED** (`gpuBridge:5, cpuFallback:0, sharedDevice:true, fieldSpecTypes:['float']`) — corpse field + ant discs on one canvas, piles forming. **Fallbacks unchanged**: agents-only Boids-webgpu → `composite:false` (A1 disc render); grid-only GoL-webgpu → its own grid direct render (`composite:false`, evolving); JS/WASM grid / 3D / sprites / metaballs → gate false (structural). **0 console/GPU errors** throughout. tsc + `npm run build` + `parity-agent-wasm` (18) + `parity-agent-force` (7) + `verify-agent-render` + `verify-render-uniform-layouts` (incl. the new GridPlaneView) all green; `git diff --stat` = the 3 render/worker files + the harness. **The GRID-ONLY follow-up was BUILT (`7fd8f37`) and then WITHDRAWN (`b2e544b`, 2026-07-27) — do NOT rebuild it.** Porting this display-res present to grid-only 2D WebGPU models regressed the user's throughput at high gens/frame (G/F=50: 2300→1600 g/s on their stable A/B vs v1.28.0) and added a structural ~5-frame camera round-trip lag (zoom/pan/grid-toggle), because grid-only has NO agent residency to ride: its present sat in the per-batch backpressure fence, and moving the 2D camera into the worker forfeited the instant main-thread transform the JS/WASM targets share. The "~330 ms/frame main-thread scale at 2000²" premise was measured on the since-reverted tip and is FALSE on the base (~0.002 ms at 1000²). Grid-only keeps: compute present into a grid-res OffscreenCanvas + instant main-thread camera. **E2 itself STAYS** (it rides the agent-residency present; the revert moved `GRID_PRESENT_WGSL`/`GridPlaneView` back inline into agentWebgpuRuntime.ts). Two follow-up decoupling attempts (present-after-fence; one-deep pipelined bare fence) also measured NO improvement — full record + retry preconditions in [docs/HANDOFF_LATTICE_DISPLAY_RES_PRESENT.md](docs/HANDOFF_LATTICE_DISPLAY_RES_PRESENT.md). The 3D voxel-composite stretch is still out of scope. See [docs/HANDOFF_E2_DISPLAY_RES_COMPOSITE.md](docs/HANDOFF_E2_DISPLAY_RES_COMPOSITE.md).

#### M4 — a DECOUPLED grid+agents model on a WebGPU grid runs its agents on the GPU (branch `optimize`)
**The routing rule: which agent step runs is decided by the resolved AGENT TARGET, never by which GRID branch you happen to be in.** The `webgpuActive` step branch ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts) `case 'step'`) used to dispatch the CPU `runAgentStep()` for every non-field-bridge model **regardless of `agentTarget`** — E1b had routed only the field-COUPLED sub-case onto the GPU — so a field-DECOUPLED grid+agents model configured for a **WebGPU grid AND WebGPU agents** silently ran its agents on JS (the same model with a JS/WASM grid already dispatched them on the GPU, so the old behaviour was the inconsistency, not the fix). A new arm between the E1b field arm and the CPU arm, gated on **`agentDecoupledGpuAgents()`** (general properties only: resolved target `webgpu` + runtime ready + `!agentUsesField` + `fieldSpecs.length === 0` + the compiled GPU agent layout reserved no field regions), calls the ordinary per-gen **`runAgentStepWebGPU()` with NO bridge** — `runAgentStepWebGPUInner` gates every field operation on `rt.layout.fieldReadLen/fieldWriteLen`, so the bridge parameter is genuinely optional — and falls back to `runAgentStep()` for that generation on any GPU failure (the same bail-out contract as every other GPU agent dispatch site). Sprites are advanced explicitly (the GPU path does not). **The user-visible change is the documented statistical-not-bitwise difference** (shared xorshift32 → per-agent PCG, f32 agent math), which the user opted into by selecting the WebGPU agent target. **Still deferred**: reaching the RESIDENT batch from this branch (it restructures the batch loop that produced the P0 concurrency bug and needs its own measurement). DEV probe: `m4Gpu` / `m4Cpu` / `m4Eligible` (+ `agentRuntimeReady`, `residentEligible`, `agentGpuUploadPending`) on the existing `__e1bCounters` message. **Verified on the real GPU** (synthetic decoupled model — GoL grid on WebGPU + Boids agents on WebGPU, shared device): 271 generations with `m4Gpu 271 / m4Cpu 0`, 260 agents flocking at polarization **0.9964**, **every agent position exactly f32-representable** (`Math.fround(x) === x` — the proof the state came through the GPU pipeline rather than the f64 CPU step, stronger than the counter alone), the grid layer evolving alongside (seeded soup 530 → 492 → 327 alive), generation counted exactly once per generation, 0 errors; Chemotaxis on WebGPU/WebGPU keeps the E1b bridge (`gpuBridge 90 / cpuFallback 0`, `m4Eligible false`) and agents-only Boids is untouched.

#### Render-layer regression harness ([scripts/verify-agent-render.mjs](scripts/verify-agent-render.mjs))
**The FIRST automated coverage of the render / gate / attach / upload layer** — `parity-agent-wasm` / `parity-agent-force` / `check-compile-identity` cover the COMPILERS and the ENGINE only, which is exactly why the audit's BLOCKER + HIGH findings and both user-reported render bugs (invisible agents, missing bond lines) sat outside every automated check. Run it after ANY change to the render gate, the attach lifecycle, the upload/readback policy, or the agent render runtime. Three tiers, and the split is deliberate about what each can prove:
- **Tier A — COMPUTED**: imports the REAL modules (`resolveMaxBonds`, `agentTargetOf`, `cellFieldAttrsOf`, `isAgentGraph*Supported`, `migrateForHarness`) and evaluates the gate's discriminating INPUTS over **every shipped library model** — no gate logic is replicated. E.g. a model whose agent graph forms bonds MUST resolve `resolveMaxBonds > 0` (if it ever reported 0 the gate silently admits a bonded model to a disc-only renderer and the bond lines vanish — audit H1); a model with a field node or an agent-accessible cell attr must never classify as decoupled; the resolved agent target must never name an unsupported backend.
- **Tier B — SOURCE INVARIANTS**: the render layer needs a real GPUDevice + an OffscreenCanvas + a React tree, so it cannot run headlessly. Instead each line whose ABSENCE caused a shipped bug is pinned by an assertion **anchored to the enclosing function body** (a balanced-brace `blockAfter` scan — never a whole-file grep, so an unrelated occurrence elsewhere can't satisfy one): the rebuild upload flag + its conditional consumer (BLOCKER), the colour seed in `uploadAgentSoA` (invisible agents), the gate's bonds / sprites / metaballs / decoupling terms (H1, M1), the attach reuse-or-destroy pair (H3), `agentRenderLayout` on BOTH the init and recompile messages, every `asyncStepBatchInFlight` setter clearing from a `finally` (H2 — counts BOTH `.finally(endAsyncStepBatch)` and `finally { endAsyncStepBatch(); }`), the M4 routing arm, and that the render shader stays graph-agnostic (FP-1). Structural, not behavioural: they catch deletion/rewiring, not new logic errors. Tier B also pins the gate TERMS that tier A feeds, so the two cannot drift.
- **Tier C — `--probes`**: prints the invariants only reachable with a live device (routing, no-leak re-attach, the gate matrix, the rebuild seed, colours with no colour node) as a copy-pasteable in-browser probe list.
**Proven live by a negative control** (the discipline any structural harness needs — a harness that only ever passes is worthless): seven targeted mutations were applied byte-exactly (latin1 read/write so `sim.worker.ts`'s mojibake comment bytes survive; CRLF-agnostic anchors) and reverted — drop the rebuild flag / drop the colour seed / remove the bonds term / stop reusing the render surface / drop `agentRenderLayout` from the recompile / remove the M4 GPU dispatch / make `resolveMaxBonds` return 0 — and the harness caught **all seven**. Two initial FAILs were harness bugs, not findings, and both are worth remembering: `buildAgentWebGPUIfNeeded` is a plain `function` (not `async`), and `endAsyncStepBatch` is cleared through TWO sanctioned shapes.

### Out of scope / deferred
Phase E (the 3D agent RENDERER) is **DONE** — [gl3d.ts](src/simulator/render/gl3d.ts) draws 3D agents as instanced sphere impostors + bond lines + **textured sprite billboards** (`uploadAgents`/`sphereProg`/`spriteProg`/bond line list, wired in SimulatorView); the 3D agent COMPUTE runs on JS/WASM/WebGPU. 3D agent SPRITES are now DONE (the WebGL2 billboard pass — see "3D agent sprites" above); the 3D renderer has no remaining gap. The WebGPU agent **3D field bridge** + **glyph setCellLooks** + **Stop Event** are DONE (see "PR7 FULL-COVERAGE CLOSE-OUT" + "Agent Stop Event"); the remaining WebGPU agent rejects are the genuine fundamentals — `aggregate`/`groupOperator` median+uniform-random + `updateIndicator` toggle/next/prev (the same WebGPU fundamentals as the lattice grid — all of which DO run on the WASM agent target, which is f64+sequential). The **WASM-agent 3D field bridge** (trilinear) is now DONE — a 3D-field WASM-agent model runs on WASM with JS bit-parity (see "Phase F — WASM AGENT 3D FIELD BRIDGE"). The WASM-agent field bridge (2D + 3D) + division-event[JS-on-CPU] + array vars + the whole catalogue are DONE (Tissue/Chemotaxis/everything run on WASM with JS bit-parity — see "Phase F — WASM AGENT WHOLE-TARGET PORT"). ~~PR7c zero-copy/atomics~~ — SHIPPED as GPU residency in the perf round on `optimize` (eligible models run whole frames on-GPU; see "Agent-engine performance review round"). ~~The `.gcastate` FILE-format agent persistence~~ — DONE in the production-hardening pass above. A layer-aware 3D region copy/paste (Ctrl+C/V/X is 2D-only for now). A graph-authorable per-PAIR force LAW/curve (Apply Force authors the net per-agent force, and **Apply Force To Agent** now authors a cross-agent force by id — see "Cross-Agent Write Semantics" — but neither is a declarative pairwise-law abstraction — the **array broadcast `applyForceToAgents` IS shipped**). A strict deposit-snapshot for the closed feedback (the fused pass mixes same-step deposits intra-loop — a small order-dependence, harmless for diffusion-driven models).
