## Grid Init Event (global procedural seeding — all 3 targets)

A SECOND cell-grid init entry-point, the free-form GLOBAL counterpart to the per-cell Init Event — the exact analogue of the **Agent Init Event**. The per-cell `initEvent` runs once for EVERY lattice cell (the engine loop is the outer loop, so it can only answer "should THIS cell be seeded?"); the new **Grid Init Event** (`gridInit`) runs **EXACTLY ONCE**, so the user wires a Loop (or nested Loops) inside its DO chain and writes ARBITRARY cells with **Set Cell (at Position)** — imperative seeding the per-cell init can't express (place N random seeds, draw a line/shape, seed the middle box). Additive/opt-in: a model without a Grid Init Event is byte-identical.

### The two nodes ([GridInitEventNode.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/GridInitEventNode.ts) + [SetCellAtPositionNode.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/SetCellAtPositionNode.ts))
- **`gridInit`** ("Grid Init Event") — a white `event` root, SINGLETON (in `SINGLETON_NODE_TYPES`), in `MULTI_OUTPUT_TYPES` (its value-outs resolve via `_v<id>_<port>`). Value-outs: `width` (W), `height` (H), and `depth` (D, hidden in 2D via `hiddenPorts`/`is3dModelLike`) — so seeding is grid-size-independent (seed the middle at `width/2`). `compile()` returns `''` (root; the compiler emits the once-only function). It has NO "current cell", so per-cell reads (Get Cell Attribute / Get Cell Position — which need `idx`) don't apply here — a runtime `idx is not defined` surfaces via the worker if misused (same caveat as the Agent Init Event's per-agent readers).
- **`setCellAtPosition`** ("Set Cell (at Position)") — an `output` flow node: `x`/`y`/`z` (z hidden in 2D) + `value` (the type-adaptive widget — bool/tag/number by the chosen attribute's type, same `effectiveWidget` swap + attribute dropdown as Set Attribute) + config `attributeId`. Its `compile()` evaluates each coordinate ONCE into an int local (a wired getRandom source would else advance the RNG twice), bounds-checks against the grid, and writes `w_<attr>[idx]` — 2D `idx = _cy*W + _cx`, 3D `idx = _cz*W*H + _cy*W + _cx` (picked by `ctx.is3d`). Out-of-range positions are skipped. **JS-only emit** (see below).

### The design — JS-executed once, on EVERY compile target (the Agent Init Event precedent)
`gridInit` compiles to **ONE JS function executed once in the worker (`runGridInit`), regardless of the model's compile target** — exactly like `agentInit`/`divisionEvent` (JS-on-CPU while the behaviour runs on WASM/WebGPU). So there is **NO WASM/WebGPU emitter** for these two nodes and no `expandX`-style lowering, yet the feature works IDENTICALLY on JS/WASM/WebGPU because the WASM/WebGPU **step** compilers only walk the `step`/`initEvent`/mapping roots — they NEVER see `gridInit` (not a root they look for) or `setCellAtPosition` (only reachable from `gridInit`). The seeding is a one-time pass, so nothing is lost by not using WASM/WebGPU. **This satisfies the ALL-TARGET rule** — the feature runs on all three targets; it is not a JS-only clamp of a step-hot feature.
- **Compiler** ([compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/compile.ts)): emits `gridInitCode` (the `CompileResult.gridInitCode` field) right after the per-cell `initCode`, using the SAME `omParams` signature (called with `buildLoopArgs()`, like `initFn`) — no loop wrapper. Order: scratch decls → Local Variables (`buildVariableJS(model.variables)` — the scalar `let`s + array fills run ONCE, so a counter persists across the user's Loop) → the `_v<id>_width/height/depth = W/H/D` preamble (emitted BEFORE `preLoopValueLines` so a loop-invariant `width/2` sees them) → `_rs` → value/flow lines.
- **Worker** ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)): `gridInitFn` (eval'd in `compileFns`, alongside `initFn`; `gridInitCode` rides `InitMsg`/`RecompileMsg`). `runGridInit()` runs on Reset AND first load, AFTER `runInit()` (so a global seed is the final word), gated on `gridCellsEnabled`. Buffer discipline mirrors `runInit`: it runs right after `resetGrid`/`initGrid` (so `readAttrs === attrsA`), sync mode seeds the write buffer from the read buffer, runs the fn (which writes specific `w_<attr>` cells), then copies write→read; async mode shares one buffer. Under WASM the `r_`/`w_` params are views over `wasmMemory`, so writes land in the right region and the WASM step reads them; under WebGPU **a gridInit model always takes the CPU init path**: `runInit`'s GPU-init shortcut AND the reset handler's `useGPUInit` are both gated on `gridInitFn === null` — the Grid Init's seed is CPU-written, so the CPU must be the init authority end-to-end (per-cell init runs via the JS `initFn`, gridInit seeds on top, then the reset handler's `!useGPUInit` branch uploads the COMPLETE state; the WebGPU-ready handler's `uploadAttrs` covers first load). Without that gate, a model with BOTH events (the Accretor: per-cell `border` + gridInit seed) dispatched the GPU init, skipped the upload, and the seed silently never reached the GPU — Reset showed nothing (a user-reported bug, fixed + verified: Accretor-on-WebGPU Reset seeds asym/sym + grows; Life3D's initEvent-only GPU-init path unregressed). `hadInit`/`loadHadInit` include `gridInitFn` so the post-init colour refresh fires.
- **Gating**: both nodes are in `LATTICE_ONLY_TYPES` (hidden on the Agents graph); `detectMissingConfig` badges `setCellAtPosition` without an attribute; Show Code adds a "Grid Init Event (global, runs once on Reset)" section.
- **Verified** end-to-end through the REAL worker (`getState`): a model seeding the middle (`width/2`,`height/2`[,`depth/2`]) + a Loop-drawn line via a Local-Variable counter produces the EXACT same cells on **JS / WASM / WebGPU × 2D / 3D** (all 6 combinations identical: 2D `[[0,0]..[4,0],[7,7]]`, 3D `[[0,0,0]..[0,0,4],[4,4,4]]`); Reset re-runs it (a painted stray cell is cleared, the seeds return); both nodes render in the Modeler; hidden on the Agents graph; tsc + `npm run build` clean.

---

## PWA / Offline Support (installable app + Tauri scaffold)

GenesisCA ships as an installable, offline-capable Progressive Web App (the main install path), with a Tauri v2 shell that builds a standalone portable native exe (no installer — for ad-hoc / USB sharing). Decisions + full subsystem analysis: [docs/IMPACT_MAP_PWA_INSTALL.md](docs/IMPACT_MAP_PWA_INSTALL.md) + [docs/PLAN_PWA_INSTALL.md](docs/PLAN_PWA_INSTALL.md) (illustrated mockup: `docs/PLAN_PWA_INSTALL.html`).

### Build wiring ([vite.config.ts](vite.config.ts))
- `vite-plugin-pwa@1.3.0` (Workbox 7), **`generateSW`** strategy — NOT a hand-written service worker (Workbox revisions the precache from the real build output, so Vite's content hashing can't desync it; a hand-written SW would break on every deploy). Registered LAST in the plugins array so the library plugins' `closeBundle` (`models`/`macros` `index.json` + `*.thumb.*`) runs before the SW precache glob.
- **Base-path is the load-bearing detail** (§B of the impact map). `const base = process.env.GHPAGES ? '/GenesisCA/' : '/'`. The production site is served at the ROOT of the custom domain **genesisca.online** (pinned by [public/CNAME](public/CNAME), which GitHub Pages serves at the apex root), so the deploy now builds with base `/` — [deploy.yml](.github/workflows/deploy.yml) NO LONGER sets `GHPAGES`. `GHPAGES` is an opt-in fallback that rebuilds under the `/GenesisCA/` subpath, needed ONLY if the custom domain is removed and the bare `rff255.github.io/GenesisCA/` project URL is used directly (with the domain set, that URL 301-redirects to genesisca.online). Everything else already used `/`: `npm run dev`, a local `npm run build` + `npm run preview` (both serve at `http://localhost:<port>/`), and the Tauri native shell (its webview loads `dist/` from `tauri://localhost/` at root; release.yml does NOT set GHPAGES). The manifest `id`/`start_url`/`scope` + every icon `src` are built from `base` (`buildManifest(base)`); `index.html` icon links use `%BASE_URL%`. (The `GHPAGES` flag itself replaced an earlier `command === 'build'` + `TAURI_ENV_PLATFORM` heuristic — it's explicit and keeps local preview painless.)
- **Precache vs runtime cache — keep the precache LEAN.** A couple of library `.gcaproj` embed large saved sim-states (Elementary CA 1D ≈ 10.8 MB, Gray-Scott ≈ 5 MB), so `globPatterns` precaches only the app shell + `sim.worker` + icons + `models/index.json` + `macros/index.json` + macros + thumbnails (~33 entries, ~8.6 MB — the animated-GIF thumbnails dominate). Model `.gcaproj` files are NOT precached; they're runtime-cached `StaleWhileRevalidate` on first open (offline thereafter). **Overriding `globPatterns` REPLACES Workbox's default `**/*.{js,css,html}`** — the shell MUST be re-listed. `maximumFileSizeToCacheInBytes` is a guard: the build ERRORS if a glob-matched file exceeds it rather than silently dropping it (this is how the oversized models were caught). The `models/`+`macros/` globs are gated on `command === 'build'` — they only exist in `dist/`, not `dev-dist/`, so including them under `vite dev` makes Workbox warn "pattern doesn't match any files". (The shell glob `**/*.{js,css,html,…}` is always present and doesn't warn in dev.)
- `devOptions: { enabled: true }`: the SW runs under `vite dev` too, so install / offline are testable straight from `npm run dev` at `http://localhost:5173/` — no `npm run preview` needed. (Combined with the build-only library globs above, dev is warning-free.)
- `registerType: 'prompt'` (NOT `autoUpdate`): a new deploy's SW installs but WAITS — it applies on the next natural launch (all tabs closed → the waiting SW activates), and **never force-reloads a live session**. `autoUpdate` was the earlier setting; it does `skipWaiting`+`clientsClaim`+a `controlling`→`window.location.reload()`, so every open tab self-refreshed a few seconds after load when a background deploy activated — wiping the user's open model mid-session (Pages redeploys on every master push, so users hit it constantly). App.tsx registers with `registerSW({ immediate: true })` and passes NO `onNeedRefresh`/`onOfflineReady` handlers, so there's no "update available" prompt (there's no in-app update channel and the app does no online processing) and no "offline ready" toast — the update is silent + deferred, matching the original PWA plan's `'prompt'` recommendation.

### App wiring ([src/App.tsx](src/App.tsx))
- `registerSW({ immediate: true })` from `virtual:pwa-register` (types via `/// <reference types="vite-plugin-pwa/client" />` in [src/vite-env.d.ts](src/vite-env.d.ts)) — **registered on the WEB ONLY**, NO callbacks: the offline-ready and update toasts were removed as noise (the app always works offline; updates auto-apply). The only remaining toast is the load-confirmation one (`showToast` in App.tsx, plain `string`, 3.5 s auto-dismiss).
- **CRITICAL — never run the service worker in the Tauri native shell.** In WebView2 the SW caches the whole app into the webview's PERSISTENT profile and then serves that STALE copy on every launch, so a freshly-built exe shows the OLD UI / models / version no matter what it actually embeds (this caused the v1.19.x "stale exe" saga — the exe was always correct; the cached SW was overriding it; the web PWA was unaffected because browsers refresh SWs). So App.tsx's mount effect branches on `'__TAURI_INTERNALS__' in window`: **web** → `registerSW()` + `navigator.storage.persist()`; **native** → unregister any SW (`serviceWorker.getRegistrations().then(r=>r.unregister())`) + clear `caches`, and do NOT register. The native build embeds + serves its own assets, so it never needs a SW. **Debug aid:** if a machine already has the stale SW cached, the new exe alone can't always clear it (the old SW may serve the old bundle before the new code runs) — delete `%LOCALAPPDATA%\io.github.rff255.genesisca\EBWebView\Default\Service Worker` (kill lingering `msedgewebview2.exe` processes for that profile first, or the folder is locked) to break the loop once.
- `navigator.storage?.persist?.()` on mount — auto-granted once installed; protects the offline cache from LRU eviction. **Storage durability only — does NOT raise the memory ceiling** for large grids (renderer heap / WASM32 4 GiB address space). That ceiling is identical in the current Tauri shell (it wraps the same Chromium/WebView2 engine) — only moving the simulation to native Rust would raise it; the native build's real wins are storage/filesystem + a clean window, not compute.
- **Boot splash** ([index.html](index.html) → removed in [App.tsx](src/App.tsx)): a static `#splash` overlay (inline `<style>` + two-cell mark + wordmark + shimmer) painted the instant the page parses so there's no white-screen gap before React mounts; `html`/`body` are set to `#0c0d10` for the same reason (manifest `background_color` covers the OS-launch frame). App.tsx's mount effect adds `.splash-hide` then removes `#splash` after a ~350 ms fade. Pure static HTML/CSS (no bundle dep) — don't let the theme boot `<script>` clobber it. It self-removes, so to screenshot it, re-inject the markup via `preview_eval`.
- [InstallButton.tsx](src/components/InstallButton.tsx) (rendered last in the navbar RIGHT group): captures `beforeinstallprompt` (`preventDefault` + stash), renders the amber "⤓ Install" pill ONLY while installable, fires the native prompt on click, and self-hides on `appinstalled` (no toast — the button vanishing IS the feedback). The shared navbar icon-button style was lifted out of App.tsx to [navStyles.ts](src/components/navStyles.ts) (also used by the keyboard-shortcuts button).
- **No window-controls-overlay (evaluated + dropped).** WCO can't auto-hide the installed PWA's title bar — Chromium shows it by default and only adds a per-app "hide title bar" toggle the user must click (sticky per app). That fails the zero-effort bar, so the PWA stays plain `display: 'standalone'` (own window + icon, no tabs/address bar, but a thin browser title bar remains). The toggle-free clean native header is the **Tauri** build instead (its own OS window). Don't re-add `display_override: ['window-controls-overlay', …]` expecting an automatic clean header — it only surfaces that manual toggle. (The matching `@media (display-mode: window-controls-overlay)` CSS block was removed too.)

### Icons ([public/](public/))
- Source mark: [public/icon.svg](public/icon.svg) — the two-cell (first-cleavage) mark on a full-bleed Nocturne `#0c0d10` tile, cells sized to sit inside the maskable safe circle. Doubles as the scalable SVG favicon.
- `@vite-pwa/assets-generator` ([pwa-assets.config.ts](pwa-assets.config.ts), `npm run generate-pwa-assets`) emits `pwa-{64,192,512}x*.png`, `maskable-icon-512x512.png`, `apple-touch-icon-180x180.png`, `favicon.ico` (maskable/apple backgrounds overridden to `#0c0d10` so no white halo). **The generated icons are COMMITTED** — CI's `npm run build` does not regenerate them; rerun the script if `icon.svg` changes.
- `npx tauri icon public/icon.svg` emits the native set into `src-tauri/icons/` (`.ico`/`.icns`/PNGs + Windows-store/Android/iOS).

### Native shell ([src-tauri/](src-tauri/), Tauri v2)
- Wraps the same `dist/` in the OS webview; `npm run tauri build -- --no-bundle` → a **standalone portable `genesisca.exe`** (the Cargo package name, in `target/release/`) — NO installer. `bundle.active:false` in `tauri.conf.json` (installer bundling intentionally dropped — the PWA is the main install path; the exe is just for ad-hoc/USB sharing); `bundle.icon` is kept so the exe still embeds the GenesisCA icon. Also in `tauri.conf.json`: `frontendDist:'../dist'`, `beforeBuildCommand:'npm run build'`, identifier `io.github.rff255.genesisca`, and **`version:'../package.json'`** so the exe version always matches the web app — package.json is the single source of truth; do NOT hardcode a version in tauri.conf.json (it's no longer one of the version-bump touch points).
- **Release CI** ([.github/workflows/release.yml](.github/workflows/release.yml)): on every `v*` tag (or manual dispatch), `windows-latest` + Rust runs `npm run tauri build -- --no-bundle`, renames `target/release/*.exe` → `GenesisCA-<tag>.exe`, and publishes it to a GitHub Release via `softprops/action-gh-release` (NOT `tauri-action` — that uploads installer *bundles*, and we ship a raw exe). **The job runs a CLEAN build — NO `swatinem/rust-cache`** — on purpose: Tauri embeds the web frontend INTO the binary at compile time (`generate_context!`), so a restored `target/` cache can let cargo skip the recompile that re-embeds it and ship a STALE UI in the exe even when the source is current (the v1.19.0 build hit exactly this — correct source, stale embedded frontend; Pages is immune since it rebuilds `dist/` fresh). Releases are infrequent, so the extra compile time is worth the guarantee. Pages (`deploy.yml`) still deploys on every master push; since the version only bumps at release, the online app and the latest exe always report the same version. Download link for users: `https://github.com/rff255/GenesisCA/releases/latest` (unsigned → first run shows a SmartScreen "More info → Run anyway" prompt). The job needs repo **Settings → Actions → General → Workflow permissions = "Read and write"** so its `GITHUB_TOKEN` can create the Release (else it 403s). **Building needs the Rust toolchain (`rustup`)** + WebView2 (preinstalled Win10/11); the committed `src-tauri/Cargo.lock` carries the `alloc-stdlib` 0.2.2 pin that fixes a transient brotli build break (keep it). `src-tauri/target/` + `gen/` are gitignored; the rest is committed.
- **Native Save As (implemented):** in the Tauri shell the browser blob-download (`<a download>`) is silently dropped by WebView2, so [fileOperations.ts](src/model/fileOperations.ts)'s `saveTextFile` branches on `'__TAURI_INTERNALS__' in window` → `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog` `save()` (real OS Save As dialog) + a custom `save_text_file` Rust command (`std::fs::write`, in [src-tauri/src/lib.rs](src-tauri/src/lib.rs)) to write the chosen path. App-defined commands need no ACL; the dialog plugin needs `dialog:default` in `capabilities/default.json` (+ `tauri-plugin-dialog` in Cargo.toml, `@tauri-apps/plugin-dialog` in package.json). `downloadJSON`/`downloadStateFile` are now `async → Promise<boolean>` (false = user cancelled → FileMenu skips `markSaved`). Load still uses the browser `<input type=file>` (WebView2 opens the native picker natively). The browser/PWA path is unchanged (blob download to the Downloads folder).
- Remaining future escape from browser storage limits: move bulk `.gcaproj`/`.gcastate` storage onto `tauri-plugin-fs` (no quota). WebGPU is only guaranteed on the Windows WebView2/Chromium build; macOS/Linux Tauri use WebKit → the simulator falls back to the WASM/JS targets.

### Manifest is intentionally version-less
PWA install identity is `id`/`start_url`, and the SW precache is content-hash-revisioned by Workbox — so a version bump drives neither. The `/updateversion` four-file sweep is unchanged; do NOT add a `version` field to the manifest.

---

## UX/feature batch (branch `tasks_batch_2026_07`, off `polish_agents`)

A batch of general + agent + CA-grid improvements. All additive/gated; the lattice (2D+3D, all 3 targets) + every non-touched model are byte-identical. Verify with `npm run build` (tsc -b + vite).

### Presets — reorder (simulator)
Simulator presets (`CAModel.presets`) are drag-to-reorderable via a `⋮⋮` handle, mirroring the attribute/neighborhood/indicator reorder UX. `REORDER_PRESETS` reducer (clone of `REORDER_INDICATORS`, reuses `reorderById`) + `reorderPresets` context callback; [SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx) wires `useListReorder(model.presets, reorderPresets)` into the Presets block; the four reorder CSS classes were copied into [SimulatorView.module.css](src/simulator/SimulatorView.module.css). **Order round-trips in `.gcaproj` for free** (array order via `stringifyCompact`; no fileOperations change). Presets persist ONLY via explicit `.gcaproj` save gated by the SaveProjectDialog "Include model presets" checkbox — there is NO active model autosave (the older CLAUDE.md note about presets being "stripped from localStorage autosave" was stale; `ModelContext` only one-shot removes a legacy `genesisca_autosave` key).

### Duplicate buttons (attributes / variables / mappings / indicators / presets, cell + agent)
`DUPLICATE_*` reducers + callbacks for `DUPLICATE_ATTRIBUTE` / `DUPLICATE_AGENT_ATTRIBUTE` / `DUPLICATE_MAPPING` / `DUPLICATE_AGENT_MAPPING` / `DUPLICATE_VARIABLE`(target cell|agent) / **`DUPLICATE_INDICATOR`** / **`DUPLICATE_PRESET`**, mirroring `DUPLICATE_NEIGHBORHOOD`. Each deep-clones the source via `JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(...))` (the graphHistory.ts convention) with a fresh `generateId()` + " (copy)" name and **APPENDS** (so the panels' list-grew auto-select lands on the copy). A sub-attribute copy keeps its `parentAttributeId`; a linked mapping/variable/indicator keeps its attribute reference; a **preset** copy also gets a fresh `createdAt` and deep-clones its embedded `SimulationState` (grid snapshot). UI "Duplicate" buttons: [AttributesPanelContent.tsx](src/modeler/panels/AttributesPanelContent.tsx) (cell/agent + model button rows; `handleDuplicate` routes on `selectedIsAgent`), [VariablesPanelSection.tsx](src/modeler/panels/VariablesPanelSection.tsx), [MappingsPanelContent.tsx](src/modeler/panels/MappingsPanelContent.tsx) (A→C, C→A, + an inline Duplicate per agent view), **[IndicatorsPanelSection.tsx](src/modeler/panels/IndicatorsPanelSection.tsx)** (a `buttonRow` Duplicate, disabled until an indicator is selected — Properties → Indicators), and **[SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx)** (a `⧉` icon button per preset row in the simulator Presets list). **Standalone-mapping/indicator duplicate is DEFINITION-ONLY** — a LINKED mapping/indicator's copy regenerates its colour pass / aggregates immediately, but a hand-built standalone mapping's copy (and a standalone indicator's copy) has no graph nodes pointing at its new id (renders an empty pass / stays unwired until the user wires it); this matches the "duplicate the definition" scope of the neighborhood duplicate.

### Indicators — Clear chart + selectable time-axis Window
- **Clear button** (`⌫` in each indicator's header, for scalar + freq charts): `onClearHistory(id)` → SimulatorView `clearIndicatorHistory` deletes `indicatorHistoryRef.current[id]` and bumps a `useReducer` counter (`bumpIndicatorRender`) so the display repaints the now-empty history even while PAUSED (history is a ref, no auto re-render).
- **Window** = `IndicatorChartSettings.window` (number of most-recent generations shown on the time X-axis). TWO layers like the colour/axis settings: model DEFAULT (the Indicators panel's `ChartDefaultsEditor`) + simulator OVERRIDE (the ⚙ gear popover), merged in `mergeChartSettings`. [indicatorChartSettings.ts](src/simulator/indicatorChartSettings.ts) adds `historyWindow(s)` (clamp ≥2, ≤`INDICATOR_HISTORY_HARD_CAP`=5000) + `sliceWindow`. [IndicatorDisplay.tsx](src/simulator/IndicatorDisplay.tsx) slices the scalar sparkline + freq multiline/stacked data to the window before drawing (the charts derive `genStart` from the visible length, so X-axis labels stay correct); **spatial (rows/cols/layers) charts are EXCLUDED** (position-binned, no time-history). SimulatorView keeps an adaptive `indicatorHistoryCapRef` = max(500, largest configured window) so a window >500 truly shows that many generations (the two collection-loop `500` caps now read the ref).
- **"All" (blank window) is ALWAYS BOUNDED — no unbounded growth.** Stored history is trimmed EVERY step (the `.shift()` at the two collection sites) to `indicatorHistoryCapRef.current` = `max(INDICATOR_HISTORY_DEFAULT_CAP=500, largest configured window)`, and `historyWindow` clamps ANY configured window to `≤ INDICATOR_HISTORY_HARD_CAP=5000`. So the per-series sample count can never exceed 5000 (500 by default when every indicator is on "all"); blank/"all" = *show all stored history*, itself capped. THREE layers enforce the ceiling: the `NumberField max={INDICATOR_HISTORY_HARD_CAP}` blur-clamp (both editors), `historyWindow`'s `Math.min(n, 5000)` at read time, and the unconditional collection `.shift()`. The window field tooltips say so explicitly ("Blank = show all stored history (always bounded: history is capped at 5000 samples per series)"). No "very large limit" (999999999) is needed — 5000 IS the hard guarantee.

### Recording — agents/3D "stale trails" fix (`forceFrameOpaque`)
GIF (and defensively WebM) recordings of transparent-background content (2D agents-only cleared to transparent black; 3D's transparent GL clear when no environment background is set) accumulated **stale trails** as the camera orbited / an agent moved — the classic GIF frame-disposal-over-transparency bug. Fix: `forceFrameOpaque(data)` sets every captured frame's alpha to 255. The RGB already equals the straight-alpha composite over black (SRC_ALPHA blend / `0,0,0,0` clear), so opacifying preserves the visible look; a SET environment background is already baked opaque (no-op). Both captured buffers are fresh copies (safe to mutate). Screenshots (PNG, single-frame, alpha OK) are untouched.

### Recording + Screenshot — capture-scope selector (view vs simulation) (branch `improvements`)
Recording (WebM/GIF) and screenshot (PNG) each expose a **capture scope**, folded into the format dropdown so there's no new UI element. The two scopes are fundamentally different renders (NOT a crop of one another):
- **`RecordScope = 'view' | 'simulation'`** (`recordScope` + `screenshotScope`, both default **`'simulation'`**, persisted independently in `genesisca_sim_settings`; refs `recordScopeRef` / `screenshotScopeRef`). The dropdown value is `"<fmt>:<scope>"` (`webm:view` / `webm:simulation` / `gif:view` / `gif:simulation`; screenshot `png:view` / `png:simulation`), parsed in `onChange`.
- **`'view'` (current view)** = the DISPLAY canvas exactly as shown — WYSIWYG, respects zoom/pan/margins. 2D reads `canvasRef` (the FINAL composited surface for every 2D path) via the CPU-backed `recordScratchRef` (drawImage → scratch = texture READ, safe; getImageData the willReadFrequently scratch — NEVER the live display, the ~6× de-opt rule); recording locks only the OUTPUT dims on the first frame (`recordCropRef`, {outW,outH}, RECORD_MAX=960) so a panel resize can't change frame size. Screenshot copies the display to a throwaway offscreen (full display res).
- **`'simulation'` (the fix — the load-bearing correction)** = the WHOLE grid/world at a FIT framing, **INDEPENDENT of the current zoom/pan**, so it captures the entire simulation no matter how far you've zoomed in to inspect a part. Rendered on the MAIN THREAD from data that's always available while recording — **`renderSimulationFrame(maxSize, target?)`** ([SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx)): the **colours buffer** (grid, `colorsRef` → a W×H temp → drawImage-upscaled nearest-neighbour into a grid-aspect offscreen) + the **agent snapshot** (circles + bonds at the fit transform). Output is grid-aspect (W:H) → NO letterbox margins by construction, and needs no lock (deterministic dims). Works on EVERY compile target incl. WebGPU direct render / E2 composite / A1 agent direct (which otherwise only expose the current-view framing via the worker's transferred canvas — the main-thread data-render sidesteps the worker camera entirely). Recording reuses a persistent `simCaptureRef` offscreen (getImageData on a never-displayed canvas is de-opt-safe); screenshot uses a fresh offscreen (toBlob is async), sized `min(2048, max(720, max(w,h)))`.
  - **KNOWN LIMITATION (documented + surfaced in Help):** the simulation-scope agent render is CIRCLES + BONDS only — agent **sprites / metaballs / glow** are NOT reproduced (use the current-view scope for a WYSIWYG capture of those). Full-fidelity agents in simulation scope would need `drawAgentsOverlay` extracted to a transform-parameterised function — a follow-up. The GRID layer is full-fidelity (the colours buffer).
  - **Why NOT the earlier crop-the-current-view approach:** the first implementation cropped the display to the drawn world rect (`simCropRect`, deleted). That only removes letterbox margins at FIT-view; **when zoomed in the sim fills the canvas so there's nothing to crop → "simulation" == "current view"** (the user's reported bug — "both save the current view"). The fit-render is zoom-independent, so it's correct at any zoom AND removes margins at fit-view (superset of the old behaviour). `viewXformRef` is no longer read by capture (still used by the cursor overlay).
- **Every 2D frame is `forceFrameOpaque`'d** (kills margin / translucent-cell GIF-disposal trails; RGB kept = composite over the dark canvas). **3D** reads the WebGL2 buffer (`capture3dPixels() ?? readPixels()`; the scene fills the frame, no letterbox → both scopes = the full frame).
- **3D UI:** the recording `<select>` shows only the two FORMATS (`WebM`/`GIF`, no scope suffix) and the screenshot scope `<select>` is HIDDEN. `is3D` gates the option rendering; picking a format in 3D does NOT overwrite the persisted 2D scope (`if (!is3D) set…Scope`), and the select value is coerced to `…:view` in 3D so it matches a rendered option.
- **Option readability**: the transport-bar `<select>`s carry `.transportBtn` styling that leaves the expanded native `<option>` popup unreadable (light text on a white OS popup); each option gets a concrete dark-bg/light-text `PICK_OPT_STYLE` + the select's own `color:'#eaeaea'`.
- **`screenshotPendingRef` / `requestColorsSnapshot('screenshot')`** is now dead-but-harmless (the worker `stepped` consumer stays; nothing sets it). Verify a recording via the actual Record button + inspecting the downloaded file's dims (GIF header bytes 6–9), not synthetic capture — and note zoomed-in is the case that distinguishes the two scopes.

### Agents — no longer leak from a previously-loaded model
Loading a new model left the previous model's agents rendered — seen in 3D (the gl3d agent instance buffer is only refreshed inside `if (isAgentModelRef.current)`, and the 3D viz flag was `agents: !isAgentModelRef.current || …` = **true** for a non-agent model → it kept drawing stale spheres). Three-part fix in [SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx): (1) the full-reinit path resets `agentsRef.current = null` + `lastUploadedAgentSnapRef.current = null` (a `.gcaproj`/library load always full-reinits — nbr/mapping array refs change); (2) the 3D viz flag is now `agents: isAgentModelRef.current && showAgentsRef.current`; (3) an `else` on the agent-draw block zeroes `r.agentInstanceCount` for a non-agent model. `drawAgentsOverlay` already null-guards the snapshot, so a null `agentsRef` is safe (2D + 3D). **The REVERSE direction (voxels leaking under a newly-loaded AGENTS-ONLY model) is fixed in the `sim_agent_fixes` batch**: the reinit clears BOTH `colorsRef` and `lastUploadedColors3dRef`, so the draw-path guard that zeroed stale voxel instances (keyed on `lastUploadedColors3dRef`) never fired and an agents-only model loaded after a voxel model (Life3D → Morphogenesis 3D Tissue) kept rendering the previous grid — the agents-only worker never ships a colours buffer to overwrite it. The guard now keys on the RENDERER's live `instanceCount` (the no-colours `else` branch zeroes it directly).

### Lookup Tables — user-editable rows/cols + selectable value type
- **Custom axis** — a new `LookupKeySource` kind `{ kind: 'custom'; labels: string[] }` lets the user define an arbitrary ordered set of row/column labels directly on the definition page (alongside face palette / tag attribute / single-value). It flows through **`resolveKeyLabels`** (the single source of truth) — verified the JS/WASM/WebGPU compilers + the worker (`normalizeLookupTable`) all derive `rowCount/colCount` from `resolveKeyLabels(...).length`, so custom needs NO per-target code (a custom N-label axis compiles identically to a tag axis with N options). `attrsStructurallyEqual` already JSON-compares `rowKeySource`/`colKeySource`, so adding/removing a custom label triggers the correct worker reinit. UI: `KeySourceField` ([AttributesPanelContent.tsx](src/modeler/panels/AttributesPanelContent.tsx)) offers "Custom labels…" + an inline add/remove/rename label editor. **Labels are kept UNIQUE** — `tableValues` is keyed by label NAME, so two columns with the same name would share one cell (editing one edits both) and leave a "ghost" empty column after a rename. `dedupeCustomLabels` (in [variegation.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/variegation.ts)) suffixes later duplicates ` (2)`/` (3)` (empty → `label`); it runs BOTH on every editor commit (so duplicates never enter the model) AND inside `resolveKeyLabels` for the `custom` kind (defensive — a hand-edited dup file renders distinct columns and self-heals on the next edit). The label inputs use a draft+blur `CustomLabelInput` so the de-dup doesn't fight typing keystroke-by-keystroke (commit on blur/Enter; the field then shows the final unique name). **Rename preserves data:** `UPDATE_ATTRIBUTE` remaps `tableValues` keys on a custom-axis rename via the SAME index-paired name heuristic used for tag-attribute axes (`applyCustomAxisRemap` in [ModelContext.tsx](src/model/ModelContext.tsx)) — without it, renaming a custom label orphaned that row/column's values (they read back as 0 on every target). Added labels start empty; removed labels' values drop.
- **Value type** — `Attribute.valueType?` (lookup table only; default `'float'`), restricted to **bool / integer / float / tag** — the scalar-numeric types that fit ONE stored number exactly on all targets. **DESIGN DECISION (documented per request):** the value is stored + emitted as a single NUMBER that is exactly correct for the type (bool → 0/1, tag → index, integer → whole, float → the number). This is intentionally **UI + semantic + authoring** only, with **zero compiler emit change**: `tableValues` stays `Record<string, Record<string, number>>`, and `lookupInteraction`/`interactionTableMap` still read a float from the table region. Rationale for NOT changing the byte-level storage/emit per type (even though it was on the table): (a) GenesisCA's value ports coerce freely — a Binary table's `0/1` output already works wherever a bool is expected (the user's Golly Born/Survive table wires the lookup straight into a bool `Set Attribute`), so the type IS respected end-to-end; (b) the value fits a float EXACTLY for bool/int/tag, so a per-type typed-array storage would save only bytes on tiny tables (negligible) at real 3-target risk — the **WebGPU f32-bitcast table region genuinely cannot hold an exact i32**, which is precisely why `color` (needs a multi-output read) and `neighborIndex` (needs exact-int f32) are the two value types NOT offered (a scope boundary, not a JS-only clamp). [LookupTableEditor.tsx](src/modeler/panels/LookupTableEditor.tsx) renders a per-cell widget by value type (bool → a **checkbox** [not a true/false dropdown], tag → InlineTagSelect over the resolved tag values, int/float → NumberField). **Tag value labels** come from `resolveValueTagOptions(attr, model)` ([variegation.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/variegation.ts)): either a MANUAL `valueTagOptions` list OR (when `valueTagAttributeId` is set) an EXISTING tag attribute's `tagOptions` (a "Custom values… / from tag attribute" dropdown in the value-type row, mirroring how an axis can be keyed by a tag attribute). The RESOLVED options are passed as a `valueTagOptions` prop to LookupTableEditor (both the design-time + simulator call sites resolve). Cascade ([ModelContext.tsx](src/model/ModelContext.tsx)): `REMOVE_ATTRIBUTE` detaches a dangling `valueTagAttributeId` (→ manual fallback); a tagOptions reorder/removal on the referenced attr remaps the stored value INDICES via the same indexMap (a rename is index-stable). No compile impact (the value is still a float index). **Golly use case:** custom labels (rows = neighbour counts 0-8, cols = Born/Survive) + `bool` value type = a Golly-style Born/Survive rule's on/off table with NO internal tag model attributes and NO decimal placeholders — see the `General 2D CA (Golly style)` library sample. Additive schema; old files load unchanged (no migration).

### Agent Sprites — rotation, chroma-key, image sequence, sprite sheet
Extends the sprite exhibition layer ([spriteRegistry.ts](src/simulator/spriteRegistry.ts), `SpriteAsset`). All additive; non-sprite models byte-identical.
- **Rotation:** three layers. Per-SPRITE `SpriteAsset.defaultDirection` (compass degrees, 0=up, edited via a `CompassDial` widget in [MappingsPanelContent.tsx](src/modeler/panels/MappingsPanelContent.tsx)) = which way the ART faces; `orientToVelocity` (auto-rotate the art to the agent's heading) + `rotationOffset` (fixed extra degrees). PER-AGENT `spriteRotations` (compass degrees, an engine Float64Array) set by the **Set Agent Sprite node's Set-rotation facet** — either an ANGLE input or a `Dir X`/`Dir Y` VECTOR the art aligns to via `atan2(dx,-dy)` (so a STATIC agent can "look at" a target). The render (`drawAgentsOverlay`) computes `facingDeg = orientToVelocity&&moving ? velHeading : spriteRotations[i]` then `rot = (facingDeg − defaultDirection) + rotationOffset`. `spriteRotations` rides the agent-loop ABI (see below).
- **Scale:** per-AGENT `spriteScales` (engine Float64Array; 0 = use the sprite asset's `scale`, >0 = override) set by the Set Agent Sprite node's **Set-scale** facet. The render uses the per-agent override when >0.
- **Set Agent Sprite is now a BY-ID setter** ([SetAgentSpriteNode.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/SetAgentSpriteNode.ts)): an optional `agentId` input targets a specific agent (a **Create Agent handle** — so a sprite CAN be set at spawn time inside the **Agent Init Event**, with an `_agentMaxAgents` range guard like `setAgentPosition`); left unwired it targets the current-loop agent `idx` (the typical Output-Mapping / Behaviour use). **Init-event fix:** an unwired Set Agent Sprite in the Agent Init Event is now a safe **no-op** (`ctx.agentRoot==='init' && !wired → return ''`) instead of emitting `spriteIds[idx]` — the init event is NOT a per-agent loop, so `idx` was undefined (the "`[agents] init event failed: idx is not defined`" bug). For agents that are seed-painted (not created in init), place Set Agent Sprite in an **Agent Output Mapping** (or Behaviour) graph — it runs per-agent.
- **`spriteRotations` + `spriteScales` ride the agent-loop ABI** (the THREE-mirror discipline): `buildAgentLoopParams`/`buildDivisionParams`/`buildAgentInitParams` push them after `spriteSpeeds`; the worker's `buildAgentLoopArgs`/`buildDivisionArgs`/`buildAgentInitArgs` + the empty-store snapshot + `scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs` `buildArgs` mirror them; `AgentStore` allocates them, `initAgentSlot` zeroes them, `divideAgent` inherits them, the render snapshot ships them when `hasAgentSprites`, and `serializeAgentStore`/deserialize round-trip them. JS-only param list (WASM/WebGPU behaviour ABIs untouched — Set Agent Sprite has no WASM/WebGPU emit). Verified: JS↔WASM bit-parity on all 9 agent samples unchanged.
- **Chroma key:** `removeBgColor` + `removeBgTolerance` — matching pixels are made transparent at decode time via an offscreen-canvas pass (`applyChromaKey`). The Sprites panel adds a **`SpriteBgPicker`** — a small canvas of the sprite's first frame; **clicking a pixel sets `removeBgColor`** to that pixel's hex (the native colour picker covers the sprite, so this lets the user pick the background off the image directly).
- **Image sequence:** `SpriteAsset.frames?: string[]` — several imported images become one animated sprite (filename order).
- **Sprite sheet:** `SpriteAsset.sheet?: SpriteSheetSpec` (cols/rows/count + margin/spacing) — one grid image sliced row-major into frames (`sliceSheet` via `createImageBitmap` crop).
- Decode is restructured into **`decodeSpriteAsset`** (sequence → sheet → animated → static, then chroma-key); the `SpriteRegistry` re-decodes on a **decode-signature** change (dataUrl/frames/sheet/chroma), not just dataUrl. Import UI adds "+ Frame sequence" (multi-file) and "+ Sprite sheet" buttons.

### "Map Image to Cells" dialog (replaces the 2D Open-Image import)
A [ImageMappingDialog.tsx](src/simulator/ImageMappingDialog.tsx) modal: source image (left, with a draggable/resizable region box + a cell-grid overlay) + gridified preview (right). Setup: Input-Mapping picklist, average-pixels, invert, binarize+threshold, **resize-grid vs paste-centered**, and **"use manual input mapping"** (binarize-true cells painted with the manual-brush values, embeds `ManualBrushPanel`). Also opens on **Ctrl+V** of a clipboard image (a window `paste` listener, latest-ref, `visibleRef`-gated). The pure sampler is [imageMapping.ts](src/simulator/imageMapping.ts) (`gridifyImage` → `{cols, rows, pixels, mask}`). Apply covers 4 combos: resize (reinit to cols×rows, then `importImage` or a pending `paintManual`) × center (keep grid; `importImage` with a region, or `paintManual` on centred cells). The worker's **`importImage` gained an optional `region`** — paste-centered writes ONLY the sub-region (cells outside preserved); under WebGPU with `gpuOwnsAttrs` (post-Play) it uses **`patchWebGPUCells(regionIdxs)`** instead of a full `uploadAttrs` so evolved cells outside the region aren't clobbered by the stale CPU mirror. **2D-only** (the worker's per-cell `importImage` is 2D-linear); a 3D model keeps the classic 1px=1cell resize import.

**Dialog rework (usability round):** (1) The **Open Image button is never disabled** — the dialog covers the no-mapping case itself. It takes `initialUseManual` (= the brush being on the Manual tab); `useManual` defaults to `initialUseManual || colorToAttrMappings.length === 0`, so opening from Manual (or a model with no C→A mapping) pre-selects the manual path (Apply is never dead), while opening from a colour tab pre-selects that mapping. (2) A second **"cell reference" square** (orange, alongside the blue included-area box) sets BOTH the sampling **cell size** and the **grid phase**: the sampler's new **`gridLayout(region, cellSize, cellOriginX?, cellOriginY?)`** returns the phase-anchored origin `gx/gy` (the first lattice boundary `ax + k·cs` inside the region) + `cols/rows`; `gridifyImage` samples from `gx/gy`. **Backward-compatible** — absent anchor (or anchor == region origin) ⇒ `gx=region.x`, `cols=floor(region.w/cs)`, byte-identical to before (the output pixels/mask shape is unchanged, so the worker consumers are untouched). An **"Align to area"** button resets the anchor to the region's top-left. (3) The left viewport is now **pan/zoom** (affine `canvasX = srcX·scale + ox`; wheel = zoom-at-cursor via a native non-passive listener, middle/right-drag = pan, −/+/Fit buttons) so you needn't see the whole image. (4) The **dialog is fixed-size + top-anchored** (`width: min(1000px, 96vw)`, `alignItems: flex-start`) with **fixed-size canvas viewports** (`VIEWPORT=460`, `PREVIEW_BOX=280` container) — resizing the region no longer resizes the card or shifts the canvas mid-drag (the old **feedback loop**: variable-size canvases + a vertically-centred card meant a region resize reflowed the card, moving the canvas under the cursor). onDown hit-priority: cell-handle → cell-body → region-handle → region-body → draw-new. Pointer-drag can't be driven by synthetic events (`setPointerCapture` needs a live pointer) — verify the pure `gridLayout` math + the fixed-layout invariants instead.


### Modeler — topology-aware panels (CA Grid vs Agents) + white event roots
Better separates the two topologies in the modeler UI. All additive/gated; grid-only and agents-only models were already handled elsewhere, this just tidies the panels.
- **Neighborhoods tab elided for an agents-only model** ([ActivityBar.tsx](src/modeler/ActivityBar.tsx)): neighborhoods only apply to the lattice CA, so the tab is hidden when `topologyMode.gridCells === false` (mirrors the V-tab elision for `variegatedCells.enabled`). [ModelerView.tsx](src/modeler/ModelerView.tsx) auto-switches the active panel to Properties if `gridCells` flips off while Neighborhoods is open (same one-line guard as the variegated case; the `gridCellsOn` predicate is `topologyMode?.gridCells !== false`).
- **Mappings panel splits by topology** ([MappingsPanelContent.tsx](src/modeler/panels/MappingsPanelContent.tsx)): the two CA-grid mapping sections (Attribute→Color / Color→Attribute) are gated on `gridCellsOn` — hidden entirely for an agents-only model. When a model has BOTH topologies (`showGroupHeaders = gridCellsOn && agentsOn`), a **`.groupTitle`** header (new class in [PanelContent.module.css](src/modeler/panels/PanelContent.module.css) — bolder than `.sectionTitle`, `--color-accent-strong` underline) prefixes each layer: **"CA Grid"** above the two grid sections, **"Agents"** above the Agent Output Mappings + Sprites sections. A single-topology model shows no group header (no redundancy). The `mode === 'detail'` editor is unaffected (it only edits `model.mappings` = CA-grid mappings; agent mappings are inline-edited).
- **All event-root nodes are white** (the CA-grid standard): `BehaviourStep` / `DivisionEvent` / `AgentInit` were recoloured `#ffffff` (were `#7e57c2` / `#ad1457`) to match the already-white `Step` / `InitEvent` / `InputColor` / `OutputMapping` / `StopEvent` / `AgentOutputMapping` — every `category: 'event'` node is now white. CaNode's `textColorForBg`/`borderColorFor`/`isLightHeaderBg` already give a light header dark text (`#1e2a3a`) + a visible border, so they render like the other event roots. The Cells/Agents graph-tab identity dots ([GraphEditor.tsx](src/modeler/vpl/GraphEditor.tsx), cyan `#4cc9f0` / purple `#7e57c2`) are unchanged — those are sub-tab markers, not node colours.
- **Library agent models corrected to agents-only where the grid was dead weight**: 6 of the 8 shipped agent `.gcaproj` had `topologyMode.gridCells:true` but never used the lattice (empty cell graph, no field-bridge nodes, no `agentAccess` cell attrs) — flipped to `gridCells:false`: **Agent WASM Drift Test / Boids - Flocking / Game of Life on Agents / Morphogenesis - {3D, Differential, Growing} Tissue**. The two that DO use the grid as a morphogen field keep `gridCells:true`: **Ant Necrophoresis** (`corpse` field, `readCellsUnder`/`affectCellsUnder`) and **Chemotaxis - Aggregation** (`chemical` field, `secreteToField`/`fieldGradient` + a cell diffusion rule). The determinant of "uses the grid" is field-bridge node usage OR a cell attr with `agentAccess !== 'none'` OR a non-trivial cell graph. (Each flip is a one-line `gridCells` change, formatting preserved; the vestigial dead cell attr in each Tissue model + a dead A→C cell mapping in GoL-on-agents were left in place — invisible with the grid disabled, harmless.) Verified in-browser agents-only: Boids flocks (260 agents), Growing Tissue grows 12→24→48, GoL-on-agents steps to gen 653 (1024 agents), 0 errors.

### Graph-aware tag/own attribute resolution for universal nodes (bug fix)
Universal nodes (available on BOTH the Cells and Agents graphs) that resolve an OWN attribute (`attributeId`) or a TAG attribute (`tagAttributeId`) were resolving against raw `model.attributes` (cell attrs only), so on the **Agents graph** they couldn't see agent attributes — the Compare (`statement`) tag-attribute dropdown listed nothing usable, tag options came back empty, and Set/Update Attribute's inline value widget didn't even render. Two graph-aware scopes are the fix (both keyed on `getActiveGraphKind()`):
- **`ownAttrList`** (already existed for the config-UI attribute dropdowns) = `agents ? (model.agentAttributes ?? []) : cellAttrs`. Now ALSO used by the collapsed-label resolution for `getCellAttribute`/`setAttribute`/`updateAttribute` and by the **inline value-widget resolution** (`setAttr`) of the whole attribute-writer family — `setAttribute`/`updateAttribute`/`setNeighborhoodAttribute`/`setNeighborAttributeByIndex` **plus the by-id agent writers `setAgentAttribute`/`setAgentsAttribute`** (the functional bug: an unresolved attr gave `effectiveWidget = undefined` → no widget). `setNeighborhood*`/`setNeighbor*` are lattice-only and the `setAgent*` writers are bondGraph-only (render only on the Agents graph, where `ownAttrList` = `model.agentAttributes`), so the SAME graph-aware `ownAttrList` resolves the right attribute for every node type on whichever graph it renders — the `setAgent*` widget adapts to the AGENT attribute's type (bool→True/False, tag→named options, int/float→number, neighborIndex/color/vector→wired-only) with no compiler change (all three targets already coerce the stored `'true'`/`'false'`/tag-index).
- **NEW `tagAttrScope`** (a local next to `ownAttrList` in [CaNode.tsx](src/modeler/vpl/CaNode.tsx)) = `agents ? [...agentAttrs, ...cellFieldAttrsOf(model), ...modelAttrs] : model.attributes`. Tag pickers reference a tag attribute purely for its OPTION NAMES; the scope is every attribute whose DISCRETE value the active graph can read/compare. On the Cells graph this equals `model.attributes` (cell + model) so the `(model)`-suffixed dropdowns stay **byte-identical**. On the Agents graph it's the agent attrs + **agent-accessible CELL FIELD attrs** (`cellFieldAttrsOf` = `agentAccess` read|readWrite) + model attrs — the cell-field arm is load-bearing: when an agent samples/deposits a discrete cell field (`sampleField`/`readCellsUnder`/`affectCellsUnder`/`secreteToField`) it must be able to compare or produce that CELL attribute's tag value on the Agents graph. A cell attr with `agentAccess:'none'` is correctly EXCLUDED (the agent can't read it). Applied to the tag-attribute dropdown + option-resolution + collapsed label of `getConstant` (tag), `statement` (Compare tag), and `switch` (value+tag). Compilers are UNAFFECTED (they emit tag INDICES directly / read `_tagLen` from config — the agent compilers too), so this is a pure UI-resolution fix.
- **[nodeValidation.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/nodeValidation.ts)** had the matching gap: `getConstant`/`statement` tag validation used `hasAnyAttr` (cell+model, no agent attrs) → a false "Select a tag attribute" badge for a valid agent tag attr. Replaced with a graph-aware `hasTagAttr`/`findTagAttr` over the same `tagAttrScope`; the now-unused `hasAnyAttr` was removed.
- Correctly LEFT as `model.attributes` (confirmed OK by the audit): `getModelAttribute` (shared model attr), `lookupInteraction`/`interactionTableMap` (lookupTable = model attr), and all lattice-only nodes (neighbour family, neighbourhood writes, `getNeighborAttributeByTag`, `filterNeighbors`, `moveSelfToNeighbor` — never on the Agents graph). Indicators/sprites/mappings/variables were already graph-correct.
- The audit (a fanned-out workflow) swept CaNode + nodeValidation + all 5 compilers + other UI; 13 CaNode sites + 2 nodeValidation sites fixed, everything else confirmed OK. Verified in-browser on a both-topology model: Cells-graph Compare lists `CellMode`+`A/B/C` (unchanged); Agents-graph Compare / Set Attribute / Get Constant list `AgentState`+`idle/active/dead`, the Set Attribute value widget renders, and no false validation badges appear. The **field scenario** was verified separately: a `Terrain` CELL tag field (`agentAccess:'read'`) + a `Hidden` cell tag (`agentAccess:'none'`) + an `AgState` agent tag — an Agents-graph Compare shows `AgState`+`Terrain` (NOT `Hidden`) and resolves `Terrain`'s `grass/water/rock` options; the Cells-graph Compare shows both cell tags.

### Field-bridge nodes gained their config UI (bug fix)
The 5 field-bridge nodes (`sampleField` / `fieldGradient` / `readCellsUnder` / `affectCellsUnder` / `secreteToField`) had **NO config UI in CaNode** (empty node body) and were **not** in `RELATED_NODES['cell-attribute']` — so a user could NOT pick the field's cell attribute (or the `reduce`/`op` mode) at all; the shipped Chemotaxis / Ant Necrophoresis models only worked because their generator scripts set `attributeId` directly. Since these nodes live on the Agents graph (where the Attributes panel lists AGENT attrs, not cell attrs), the field attribute MUST be picked in-node. Added a CaNode config block for the 5 types: a **"Field (cell) attribute…"** dropdown over `cellAttrsOf(model)` (ALL cell attrs — the nodeValidation badge then guides granting Agent access if the chosen attr isn't yet agent-accessible, matching the existing `hasCellAttr` → `hasFieldReadAttr`/`hasFieldWriteAttr` two-tier check) + a **reduce** dropdown (Mean/Sum/Max/Min) for `readCellsUnder` + an **op** dropdown (Set/Add/Subtract/Max/Min) for `affectCellsUnder`; plus a collapsed-label (`<Label> · <attr>`). `cellAttrsOf` (NOT graph-aware — field nodes always target cell attrs) is imported from [attributeScope.ts](src/model/attributeScope.ts). Verified in-browser: Chemotaxis' `fieldGradient`/`secreteToField` + Ant Necrophoresis' `readCellsUnder`(Mean)/`affectCellsUnder`(Add/Subtract) all show the right dropdowns with the shipped values, and a Mean→Sum edit round-trips through `updateConfig`. This complements the tag-scope fix above: together, an agent can now BUILD the whole field-interaction chain in the UI (pick the field attr on the read/write nodes, then Compare/Get-Constant its discrete cell value).

## N-Dimensional Lookup Tables + Seeded Randomization (Accretor) — branch `sim_agent_fixes`

Generalizes the 2-axis `lookupTable` attribute to **N axes** (1..6) + a seeded random table fill, so rule-table CA families are authorable — flagship: the **Accretor** CA (Driessens & Verstappen), a 3D accretion automaton whose rule is `Rule[state, faceCount, edgeCount, cornerCount] → newState` (a 4-axis table). Impact map + plan: [docs/IMPACT_MAP_ND_LOOKUP_TABLES.md](docs/IMPACT_MAP_ND_LOOKUP_TABLES.md) / [PLAN_ND_LOOKUP_TABLES.md](docs/PLAN_ND_LOOKUP_TABLES.md) (+ `.html` mockup). **All additive** — the legacy 2-axis path is byte-identical (proven by [scripts/check-compile-identity.mjs](scripts/check-compile-identity.mjs): 23 library models, every compile surface unchanged), no `.gcaproj` migration.

### The design — lower to a flat dense read, one emit story on all six surfaces
A 2-axis table already compiles to a flat array read `row*colCount+col` everywhere; N-D is the same flat read with `Σ idxₖ·strideₖ` — **zero new runtime machinery** (the table regions in WASM memory / varAux / auxF32 are already flat + size-agnostic). Emit branches on mode inside each emitter, so a legacy table emits its exact pre-N-D code (byte-identical) and only axes-mode gets the new path.
- **Schema** ([types.ts](src/model/types.ts), all optional): `LookupKeySource += { kind:'intRange', min, max }` (integer-range axis — labels `String(min)..String(max)`, the natural kind for neighbour-count axes); `LookupAxis = { name?, source }`; on a lookupTable `Attribute`: `axes?: LookupAxis[]` (present ⇒ axes mode, supersedes rowKeySource/colKeySource), `tableData?: number[]` (dense row-major over `axes`, length = Π dims), `tableRoll?: { seed, density, max? }` (informational — seeds the editor Randomize fields; `max` = the 1..max integer value range, stored only for integer-valued tables and also consumed by the Overseer `randomizeTable` dep so a runtime re-roll of an integer table reproduces the editor's value policy). Preset capture: `SimulationState.lookupTableData?: Record<attrId, number[]>` (the axes-mode sibling of `interactionTables`).
- **`resolveAxes(attr, model)`** ([variegation.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/variegation.ts)) is the new single source of truth for axis geometry (`{ axes:{name,labels,dim}[], dims, strides, mins, total }`); a legacy table resolves as N=2 with identical numbers, so all the old `resolveKeyLabels` call sites migrate to ONE helper and can't drift. Also new there: `normalizeLookupTablePayload` (dims-aware sibling of `normalizeLookupTable`), `buildLookupTablePayload` (the ONE worker-payload builder — legacy ships labels+values, axes-mode ships `dims`/`mins`/`data`), `randomFillTableData` (THE shared seeded xorshift32 fill — editor + Overseer), `remapTableDataAxis` / `remapTableDataForAxesChange` (the structural cascades). Caps: `MAX_LOOKUP_AXES=6`, `MAX_INT_RANGE_SPAN=4096`, `MAX_LOOKUP_TABLE_ENTRIES=1048576`.
- **Table Lookup ports** ([LookupInteractionNode.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/LookupInteractionNode.ts)): static max-6 `axis_0..axis_5` integer inputs, sliced/relabeled per the referenced table by the **shared shaper `applyLookupAxisPorts`** (consumed by BOTH CaNode + effectivePorts — the buildExtraSlotPorts dual-consumption pattern; the expression-node static-port-slice discipline). Legacy tables keep `labelA`/`labelB` (existing wires never move); axes-mode hides those + shows the axis names. `compile.ts` pre-resolve bakes `_dims`/`_mins` (comma-joined strings — NodeConfig is scalars-only) parsed by `lookupNodeDims`/`lookupNodeMins`.
- **Emit** (JS / WASM / WebGPU lattice + agent WASM/WebGPU): axes-mode = per-axis **saturating clamp** `clamp((axisₖ|0) − minₖ, 0, dimₖ−1)` + the row-major multiply-add. Legacy = raw (no clamp), byte-identical. Layout regions (`wasm/layout.ts interactionTableOffsets`, `webgpu/layout.ts` varAux, `agentEngine.ts` lookupTableOffset, `agentWebgpu/layout.ts` auxF32) all carry `dims`/`mins` and size Π dims via `resolveAxes` (the layout-lockstep single-resolution rule). `interactionTableMap` (Table Map) supports **N=2 axes only** (its shape is two parallel index arrays; N>2 → validation badge + empty output). **Fixed a pre-existing agent-WebGPU bug** in the same emitter: it read ports `'row'`/`'col'` instead of the def's `labelA`/`labelB`, so it always read the clamped [0,0] cell.
- **Worker** ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts)): `InteractionTablePayload`/`UpdateLookupTableMsg` gain `dims`/`data`/`mins`; init + `updateLookupTable` normalize via `normalizeLookupTablePayload` (view sized Π dims). **THE load-bearing worker fix**: the worker's `computeMemoryLayout` lookupTables input MUST size a multi-axis region by `dims` (a multi-axis payload's rowLabels/colLabels are EMPTY → the old `t.rowLabels.length||1` path reserved **1 cell**, truncating the whole table to `tableData[0]` AND desyncing every offset baked into the WASM module — the model compiled + round-tripped its table but ran inert, growing 0 cells). Legacy tables take the unchanged rowLabels/colLabels path.
- **Editor**: [AttributesPanelContent.tsx](src/modeler/panels/AttributesPanelContent.tsx) — an **Axes-mode select** (Rows×columns ↔ Multi-axis) with explicit one-shot conversions (legacy→multi flattens tableValues→tableData; multi→legacy for N=2 rebuilds the sparse map), `LookupAxesEditor` (name + source per axis, **append / remove-LAST only** — never reorder, the multi-attr-slots discipline: storage + node ports are positional), `KeySourceField` gains **Integer range** (min/max). [LookupTableEditor.tsx](src/modeler/panels/LookupTableEditor.tsx) — a multi-axis **slice view** (the LAST two axes span the grid — deterministic vs. row-major storage; outer axes get prev/next+select steppers) reusing the per-valueType cell widgets, + the **Randomize block** (Seed + 🎲 reroll, Density, integer Max) shared by both modes via `randomFillTableData` (legacy fills the sparse map, axes-mode fills tableData; `tableRoll` metadata records the roll). Live during a sim run (the simulator right-panel editor carries tableData/tableRoll through `handleInteractionTableEdit`).
- **Cascades** ([ModelContext.tsx](src/model/ModelContext.tsx)): axes-mode structural `tableData` remaps — tag rename/reorder on a referenced tag axis, own-axes edits (intRange grow/shrink/shift + custom renames, via label-name matching), removed tag attr collapses its axis to `single` — all via the shared `remapTableData*` helpers.
- **Randomize is design-time** (writes `tableData` into the model, saved in `.gcaproj`, reproducible; same seed+density+shape ⇒ identical table on any machine). The Overseer **`ovRandomizeTable`** node (plan PR4) is the runtime-only sibling for automated rule-space search — re-rolls the WORKER's table without dirtying the model (like a slider), journaling each {seed, density}; see the Overseer node-family section.

### The Accretor sample ([scripts/gen-accretor.mjs](scripts/gen-accretor.mjs) → `public/models/Accretor.gcaproj`)
**300³** constant-boundary 3D grid (27M cells — practical because the model ships with **Skip Isolated Empty Cells** ON: empty attr `state`, empty value 0, range = radius-1 chebyshev, which provably covers the rule since growth requires faceCount ≥ 1; verified byte-identical to the full loop at a fixed RNG seed, inits ~1.9 s, ~10 gens/s), tag `state` (empty/A/B), **three neighbourhoods** (faces 6 / edges 12 / corners 8 as explicit `coords3d` + same-length 2D projections). The rule is a 4-axis Lookup Table (State intRange 0..2 × Faces 0..6 × Edges 0..12 × Corners 0..8 = 2457 entries, `valueType:'tag'` → `valueTagAttributeId:'state'`), pre-rolled by the script's inline copy of `randomFillTableData` (seed **318**, density **0.13** — chosen by a seed search for a rule where BOTH the random-asymmetric AND the mirror-symmetric seed grow a substantial structure to the edge ~50 gens; `tableRoll` lets the user re-roll in the editor). Step graph: `state==0` gate → 3× `getNeighborsAttribute → groupCounting(greater, compare defaults 0 = count occupied)` → `faces≥1` gate → `lookupInteraction(state,f,e,c)` → `setAttribute(state)`; a sibling edge-stop branch reads the `border` cell attribute → `AND(occupied)` → `stopEvent` (pauses when the structure reaches a border). Standalone Output Mapping: empty → alpha 0 (voxel-culled), A green / B magenta via `categoricalColor` + alpha `(state!=0)*255`. Linked frequency indicator (trackedValues A,B).
- **GRID-VOLUME-INDEPENDENT seeding via TWO init roots** (both compute from the LIVE grid dims, so a simulator Resize re-centres/re-detects everything — NO baked centre/bounds): (1) a **per-cell Init Event** flags the `border` cell attr (a new bool the step reads) via the folded coord `dblₖ = |2·coord − maxₖ|` (arithmeticOperator `*2` → `− max` → `abs`; INTEGER — no `floor` node, arith has none — `= maxₖ` only at coord 0 or maxₖ, so `dblₖ >= maxₖ` on any axis ⇒ an outer face); the Stop Event fires when a `border` cell becomes occupied. (2) a **[Grid Init Event](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/GridInitEventNode.ts)** (`gridInit`, runs ONCE) scatters the seed: `Loop(SEED_N=60) { getRandom offset ∈ [−3,3]³ + value ∈ {A,B}; Set Cell (at Position) at centre+offset }` — the centre = `width/2` etc. from the gridInit `width`/`height`/`depth` outs.
- **Symmetric vs asymmetric seed** via the **`symmetricSeed` bool MODEL attribute** (a live toggle — flip it + Reset). ASYMMETRIC writes only the primary point. SYMMETRIC additionally writes the point's **7 other octahedral mirror cells** (a `conditional(symmetricSeed)` gating 7 more `Set Cell` nodes) with the **SAME** value — the mirror coord is `(dim-1) − primary` (reflect across the TRUE grid centre `(W-1)/2`; mirroring across `W/2` is off by ½ and only ~37% symmetric). Because the value is drawn ONCE per loop iteration and reused across all 8 writes, the seed is BOTH **random** (`getRandom` re-rolls every Reset — the RNG stream advances across Resets) AND **mirror-symmetric** → the structure grows symmetric. This is the whole point of moving the seed to the Grid Init Event: **a once-run event can draw one random value and mirror it — a per-cell event fundamentally cannot** (mirrored cells run at different points in the per-cell RNG stream, so they'd never match; the old per-cell shell `(dblX+dblY+dblZ) mod 3` was symmetric but NOT random). Verified in the real worker (JS + WASM): asymmetric grows to the edge (re-rolls each Reset); symmetric grows to the edge with **100% mirror-X/Y/Z + 100% point symmetry** AND re-rolls each Reset (two Resets → different seeds, both 100% symmetric); 24³ (resized) → seed re-centres. `getRandom` integer supports negative `min` (`floor(rand·(max−min+1))+min`), and RNG nodes are loop-pinned (drawn fresh each iteration).
- **Ships an Overseer Rule Explorer** (`overseerConfig.enabled`, seedPolicy `'fixed'` so the centre seed is constant across the sweep and the series isolates the RULE): `Clear Series → forEachInArray(ovSweepValues [8 known-good seeds]) { ovRandomizeTable(rule, seed=element, density 0.13) → ovResetBoard → ovRunUntilStop(600) → ovCollectSample(accreted ← Read A + Read B) → ovLog }` — the automated "re-roll until something interesting grows" workflow; each seed is journaled (reproducible in the editor). Verified: 8 seeds → a rule→size histogram.
- **Verified end-to-end in the real worker** — JS: asym grows to the edge + re-rolls each Reset, sym 100%-octahedral + re-rolls; WASM (default): sym 100%-mirror + edge-stop; the multi-axis rule compiles on JS/WASM/WebGPU. Legacy Chromatography (2-axis) unregressed.

### Verification tooling
- [scripts/check-compile-identity.mjs](scripts/check-compile-identity.mjs) — `--capture`/`--compare <baseline>`: hashes every library model's emitted output (JS/WASM/WGSL + agent + overseer) — the byte-identity regression proof standard for any compiler-touching change.
- [scripts/test-ndtable.mjs](scripts/test-ndtable.mjs) — helper unit tests (fill determinism, the remap cascades, payload normalize) + a synthetic 3-axis coded-index model RUN on JS and real WASM in Node (bit-identical, saturating clamps proven with out-of-range inputs; the WGSL emit checked).

## Particle Life (samples + matrix-play UX — branch `Improvements`)

The classic Particle Life sandbox ([sandbox-science.com/particle-life](https://sandbox-science.com/particle-life); source verified against their `SandboxScience-master` repo) as TWO shipped library samples (**Particle Life** 2D + **Particle Life 3D**, [scripts/gen-particle-life.mjs](scripts/gen-particle-life.mjs) — ONE parameterized builder emits both), plus the enabling features that make "playing with the interaction matrices" first-class. Plan + mockup: [docs/PLAN_PARTICLE_LIFE.md](docs/PLAN_PARTICLE_LIFE.md)/`.html`.

### The model (faithful to their force law)
- Agents-only (`gridCells:false`), torus; `species` = an agent TAG attribute (K=6) keying **THREE asymmetric species×species float lookup tables**: `rules` (∈[−1,1] — the matrix), `attractMin` (per-pair repulsion-core radius, rolled 3..6), `attractMax` (per-pair range, rolled 8..16). **rmin/rmax are PER-PAIR TABLES in Particle Life, not scalars.**
- Force per neighbour at distance d (their `particleComputeForces.wgsl` exactly): `d < minR → repel·(d/minR − 1)` (always repulsive, rule-independent); `d < maxR → tent` peaking `rule` at `(minR+maxR)/2`, zero at both ends; accumulated along the torus-shortest offset. Graph: Get Nearby Agents(queryRadius ← model attr) → For Each → Get Agent Offset → 3× Table Lookup → 2× Compare + 2× valueSwitch + Expressions → Apply Force.
- Integration: **graph-side friction** (Get Velocity → `v·f` → Set Velocity, `f` = a bounded model attr ⇒ a live slider) + engine `momentum: 1.0` ⇒ `v = f·v + (Δt/η)·ΣF` — their `v += F·k; v *= friction` family. Engine physics OFF (Particle profile, collision off, `maxBonds:0`, `useBondingPhysics:false`, `repulsionStiffness:0` + `bondStiffness:0` so `clampAgentDt` keeps `dt = timeStep = 1`). Spawn: Agent Init → Loop (**Count wired** from model attr `N`) → Create Agent(rand·worldW, rand·worldH[, rand·worldD]) → random species → Add To World; `N` applies on Reset.
- Sliders: `N` / `forceFactor` / `repel` / `friction` / `queryRadius`. Tuned defaults (browser-measured): forceFactor 0.05, friction 0.8, repel 1.5 → vmax ≈ 2 units/step → species-sorted clusters (density 1.6× uniform; same-species neighbour fraction 0.40 vs 0.17 null). **`queryRadius` must stay ≤ `neighbourQueryRadius` (24, the hash ceiling) and ≥ the attractMax roll range** — a wired query radius above the config ceiling silently misses far pairs (documented in the model description; no validation yet). 4 presets = named matrices (`interactionTables` on `rules` only) ported from their `rulesGenerator.ts`.
- Verified: all six agent compile surfaces; JS↔WASM bit-parity (parity harness globs both samples → permanent coverage); WASM gate+instantiate; WebGPU behaviour+force shaders device-compile 0 errors (2D+3D); browser ~110 steps/s at 1800 agents (real worker), 3D spans the full volume, 0 NaN. NB the gen script mints fresh node ids per run, so re-running it churns compile-identity hashes for these two models only (expected, like every gen script).

### Three latent compiler parity bugs it surfaced (all fixed)
1. **`compileAgentGraph` never ran the lookup-table geometry pre-resolve** — Table Lookup / Table Map on the AGENT graph baked `_colCount` = 1, so the JS agent emit indexed `row·1+col` and read the WRONG cells of any multi-column table (WASM/WebGPU resolve dims from the layout and were right — JS was the broken target). Fix: `preResolveLookupTableNode` extracted to module scope in [compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/compile.ts), called by BOTH the cell pre-resolve and `compileAgentGraph` (cell side proven byte-identical). No shipped sample had used Table Lookup on the agent graph.
2. **`getAgentOffset.distance` emitted `Math.hypot` on JS** vs the WASM emitter's `f64.sqrt`-of-squared-sum — hypot is correctly-rounded, so Distance consumers diverged by ULPs (bit-parity broken). JS now emits `Math.sqrt(dx*dx+dy*dy[+dz*dz])` in the WASM op order. Boids' emitted text changed as a dead const only (it never wires Distance). **Rule: agent JS emits must never use `Math.hypot` — match the WASM associativity.**
3. **`getCurvature`** had the same hypot pattern (fixed via the spawned follow-up task + a permanent bonded-population parity synthetic).

### Matrix-play UX (the enabling features)
- **Signed/ranged Randomize** (`tableRoll.rangeMin`/`rangeMax` + `TableFillPolicy.rangeMin/rangeMax`): float tables roll uniform in [Min, Max) — signed ranges (−1..1) are the Particle Life rules matrix. Absent range ⇒ **bit-identical** to the historical (0,1) draw (proven in [scripts/test-ndtable.mjs](scripts/test-ndtable.mjs)); the Overseer `ovRandomizeTable` runtime re-roll reads the stored range (mirrors the integer `max` policy).
- **Lookup-table axes + value-tag sources bind AGENT tag attributes**: `resolveKeyLabels` / `resolveValueTagOptions` ([variegation.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/variegation.ts) `findTagAttrById`) search `model.attributes` THEN `agentAttributes` (cell/model wins on collision) — all six compile surfaces + the worker payload inherit from the one resolver. `KeySourceField` + the value-tag dropdown offer agent tag attrs when the Agents topology is on. The UPDATE/REMOVE_ATTRIBUTE lookup-table cascades were extracted into shared helpers (`remapLookupTableForTagChange` / `detachLookupTableFromRemovedTag` in [ModelContext.tsx](src/model/ModelContext.tsx)) now ALSO invoked by UPDATE/REMOVE_AGENT_ATTRIBUTE — species renames/reorders/removals remap all three tables.
- **Matrix-play view** ([LookupTableEditor.tsx](src/modeler/panels/LookupTableEditor.tsx), both call sites — Attributes panel + the simulator **LEFT**-panel Model Attributes section): float tables default to a diverging-color grid (red = repel / cyan = attract over dark neutral, |value|/scale = saturation; scale = the declared roll range else the live data extent), **horizontal drag-on-cell** adjust (range/150 per px, clamped), click select + Ctrl multi-select, a shared slider editing the selection or ALL cells (their "All Types"), hover value tooltips, `# Values` toggles back to the NumberField grid. Square 2-axis tables add **Fill pattern** ([src/model/matrixGenerators.ts](src/model/matrixGenerators.ts) — seeded xorshift32 ports of their generator catalogue: uniform/symmetric random, snake, rock-paper-scissors, chains, bipartite, hub-and-spokes, shells, anti-symmetric swirl, dimers, triad flocks) + Zero/Symmetrize/Transpose/Negate/Mutate. Editor-only (compilers untouched, byte-identity green); edits stream live via the existing `updateLookupTable` path. Symmetric tables show + edit the mirrored cells in matrix view (writes go through the same mirror rule).

### Deferred (documented candidates)
K-stepper compound control (resize species tagOptions + palette + all three matrices in one action); an attract/repel force brush; a queryRadius-vs-maxR validation; Pointer-Lock drag; per-pair min ≤ max cross-table constraint. *(The 2D point-splat render fast path AND PR7c GPU residency have since SHIPPED — the perf round on `optimize`; 50k PL runs ~20 ms/gen resident.)*

## Ant Necrophoresis — the discrete, mass-conserving rebuild (branch `improvements`, 2026-07-28)

The shipped sample was **rebuilt from scratch** ([scripts/gen-ant-necrophoresis.mjs](scripts/gen-ant-necrophoresis.mjs)). The original was reported as nonsensical and it was: `corpse` was a FLOAT field, pick-up/drop were `affectCellsUnder` **add/subtract 1 over an r = 0.8 disk under a FRACTIONALLY-positioned agent** (so a pick could take 1 from each of several cells and a drop add 1 to a different number of cells), and pick-up was gated only on the neighbourhood MEAN — never on the cell actually holding a corpse. So it subtracted from empty cells (negative densities), created mass on every mismatched pick/drop pair, and rendered as a smeared decimal cloud that grew past what was seeded. There was no discrete item, no conservation, and no cell-level pick/place.

### The rebuilt design — conservation is STRUCTURAL, not tuned
**The invariant: `corpses on the grid + corpses being carried = constant`.** Four load-bearing choices:
- **`corpse` is an INTEGER cell attribute holding 0 or 1** — a discrete item, never a density. The linked Output Mapping spans 0..1.
- **Ants live on EXACT INTEGER cell positions.** They are spawned at integer cells by the Agent Init Event (`floor(u·W)`, `floor(u·H)` via Get Grid Dimensions, so it is grid-size independent) and moved one cell per step by **Set Agent Position** with a hand-written torus wrap (`(x+dx) − floor((x+dx)/W)·W`). The engine integrator is inert (`customForcesOnly`, `momentum 0`, no forces/growth/bonds, `agentCapabilities.motion:'static'`), so its unconditional `xNext = wrap(x + v)` pass just re-writes the same integer — **but it MUST run**, because `swapPositions` commits `xNext` over `x` every step, so a Set-Position write would otherwise be reverted. **Because the position is exactly integral, an `r = 0.5` disk covers EXACTLY ONE cell** (own cell at distance 0; the 4-neighbours at 1 > 0.5) — that is what makes Read/Affect Cells Under a single-cell read/write instead of a blob. (Contrast the old **deposit-radius gotcha**: at a FRACTIONAL position radius 0 writes NOTHING and ~0.71 writes 1–4 cells depending on the fraction.)
- **Both writes are `set`, gated on the opposite cell state**: pick-up requires `cell == 1` and writes `set 0`; drop requires `cell == 0` and writes `set 1`. `set` (not add/subtract) means the field is structurally confined to {0, 1}; the gates mean every write is a genuine transfer. Each ant carries ≤ 1 corpse (`carrying` bool agent attr).
- **The agent target is WASM (and the grid WASM too) — a deliberate exception to the library compile-target policy.** The one-corpse-per-transfer guarantee relies on the **SEQUENTIAL** agent loop (JS/WASM): a second ant on the same cell reads the field AFTER the first ant's deposit. A parallel WebGPU dispatch reads a snapshot, so two ants could both claim one corpse (~1 same-cell pair per step at 120 ants on 6400 cells) and slowly manufacture mass. The grid stays WASM because the cell rule is EMPTY (corpses don't move on their own) — a WebGPU grid would add a per-gen field-bridge round-trip for nothing.

Rule (Deneubourg et al. 1991 / Bonabeau et al.), with `f` = the occupied FRACTION over the sensing disk (`readCellsUnder` mean of a 0/1 field): empty-handed on a corpse → `P(pick) = (k1/(k1+f))²` (falls with density); carrying on an empty cell → `P(drop) = (f/(k2+f))²` (rises with density). Autocatalytic → cemeteries. Live model attributes: Ants / Seed Density (both apply on Reset) / Sense Radius / k1 / k2. A linked TOTAL indicator "Corpses on ground" makes the conserved pool visible (it can only ever dip by at most the ant count).

### TWO cross-target compiler defects this model surfaced (BOTH NOW FIXED IN THE COMPILER)
The model was first reshaped to avoid them; the compiler was fixed afterwards — see **"Agent-compiler branch-scope + RNG-order fixes"** for the root causes, the fixes and the permanent regression synthetics. The shapes were:
1. **RNG DRAW ORDER diverged between the JS and WASM agent compilers** — JS sink-analysis hoisted a draw whose uses sat at agent-loop top into the pre-flow value block (topo order) while WASM emitted at the flow use site, so a graph with a draw inside a branch plus a draw after it advanced the shared `_rs` stream in a different order per target and every downstream decision diverged. (Local Variables did NOT help: `setVariable` pins the assignment, not where JS emits the `getRandom`.)
2. **A value used BOTH inside a branch AND after it was emitted INSIDE the branch by the JS agent compiler** → `ReferenceError: _v… is not defined`. Two shapes: a `valueSwitch` feeding a `setAttribute` inside the `then` AND the post-conditional colour; and a `getVariable` read consumed in BOTH branches force-emitting its shared pure input (`readCellsUnder`) into the FIRST branch.

**The model keeps its defect-free shape on purpose** (it is also the better model): ONE random draw per agent per step, split into a direction index `k = floor(u·9)` and the leftover fraction `roll = u·9 − k`; and a colour index computed purely as `c + act·(1−2c)` rather than reusing the branch's value or re-reading the attribute after the write. Re-reading an attribute after a write in the same step is still not advisable — it depends on hazard pinning agreeing across targets.

The decision is therefore **BRANCH-FREE**: one roll, one probability (`valueSwitch` on carrying), one cell test, one `AND`, one conditional whose body does the transfer — instead of two mirrored branches.

### Verified
`scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs` **JS↔WASM bit-parity ✓ on all samples**; `scripts/check-agent-wasm-gate.mjs` GATE✓ COMPILE✓ INST✓. **Real worker, WASM grid + WASM agents, in-browser**: seeded 778 corpses + 120 ants; at **generation 42 002** the field held 767–768 corpses with 10–11 carried — **TOTAL exactly 778, unchanged**, `nonBinary` cells **0**, `fractionalPos` **0** (ants still exactly on integer cells after 42k moves), 120 alive, 0 worker errors. Clustering deepened over the same run: mean occupied-Moore-neighbours per occupied cell **0.96 (random baseline) → 2.72 @ gen 2k → 4.64 @ gen 42k**, densest 5×5 patch **24 of 25 cells** — real cemeteries. (Composited pixels were not eyeballed — the Browser pane was occluded; the "Corpses on ground" indicator read 768, matching the measured field.)
