## Agent-compiler branch-scope + RNG-order fixes (branch `improvements`, 2026-07-28)

Two latent defects in the **JS agent compiler's value placement**, surfaced by the Ant Necrophoresis rebuild and fixed here. Both broke the project's own JS↔WASM **bit-parity** invariant for the agent target (one by crashing, one by silently diverging). Touched: [volatileHoist.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/volatileHoist.ts), [sinkAnalysis.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/sinkAnalysis.ts), [compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/compile.ts) — **no WASM / WebGPU / worker / engine file** (`agent.wasm.bytes`, `agent.webgpu.shader`, `wasm.bytes`, `webgpu.shader` and `js.stepCode` are byte-identical for every library model; only three agent models' `agent.behaviourCode` moved, and only textually — see Verified).

### Defect 1 — `usedVolatiles` over-skipped `next`, so a branch never claimed its own volatile
`computeVolatileHoist` assigns each volatile value to the FIRST member of its LCA scope whose *subtree* uses it, and the compilers force-emit it there. `usedVolatiles(F)` deliberately skipped F's `next` port (an earlier fix: a value read AFTER a loop must belong to the later sibling, not the loop). But the skip applied at EVERY recursion depth — so a volatile consumed by the **2nd or later statement of a branch body** was invisible to the branch-opening node. Ownership fell through to a LATER top-level sibling, the value was scheduled AFTER the branch that reads it, and the JS compiler's inline fallback then declared it INSIDE the branch → `ReferenceError: _v… is not defined` on the post-branch read.
- **Fix:** `usedVolatiles(flowNodeId, includeNext)`. The top-level call (a member of the LCA scope) passes `false` — the next-chain is a later sibling, preserving the loop fix. Every RECURSIVE call passes `true` — anything reached through a BRANCH port lives in a nested block where the next-chain is simply the rest of that block's statements. Cache key carries the flag.
- **This helper is SHARED with the agent WASM/WebGPU compilers** (hazard pinning), so the fix also makes a hazard-pinned read dominate its branch there. No shipped model's WASM/WGSL output changed.

### Defect 2 — the force-emit dragged a SHARED pure input into one branch
While force-emitting a volatile, `forceVolatileCurrentScope` routes EVERY emission (including non-volatile inputs reachable only through the volatile) to the current flow position — necessary because such an input is never pre-emitted and its own sink buffer was already flushed. But when that input is used from BOTH branches (sink scope `CELL_TOP`), pinning it inside the first branch left the sibling referencing an out-of-scope `const`.
- **Fix** ([compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/compile.ts) `routeValueEmit`): under the force window, a NON-volatile node whose own sink scope is `CELL_TOP` routes to `valueLines` instead. It is pure, so cell-top always dominates, and `valueLines` is assembled ABOVE `flowLines` — safe to push to mid-walk. **A sink-scope inversion is impossible** (a producer's use-set is a superset of its consumer's, so its LCA is at-or-above), and anything depending on a volatile IS volatile — so a cell-top-sink pure value can only depend on other cell-top-sink pure values.
- Branch-scoped inputs (the documented `getRandom`-feeding-volatile-arithmetic case) keep the current position — unchanged.

### Defect 3 — RNG DRAW ORDER: JS hoisted, WASM/WebGPU emit at the use site
Evaluating an RNG node is a **side effect** (it advances the shared `_rs` stream), so WHERE it is emitted decides where the draw lands. The agent WASM/WebGPU compilers emit a draw at its flow USE SITE (RNG is in `AGENT_VALUE_NO_HOIST`); JS sink analysis hoisted a draw whose uses all sit at agent-loop top into the pre-flow value block, ordered TOPOLOGICALLY. A graph with a draw inside a branch and another after it therefore advanced the stream in a different order per target — each target individually a correct random walk, but bit-parity gone and every downstream decision diverging. Neither `setVariable` (pins the assignment, not the draw) nor reordering the cell-top block can fix it: WASM interleaves branch-local draws BETWEEN top-level ones, which a hoisted block cannot express.
- **Fix** ([compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/compile.ts)): for a **`behaviourStep`** root, seed every RNG node into the volatile set. That routes it through `volatileHoist` — *"emit at the LCA flow scope, immediately before the consuming flow node"* — which IS flow order, matching the other targets by construction. A branch-local draw stays in its branch (volatileHoist deliberately never hoists past loops or out of branches), so entropy is still spent only when the branch runs.
- **`isRngNode(node)` is now exported from [sinkAnalysis.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/sinkAnalysis.ts)** — ONE predicate shared by `isLoopPinned` (never hoist a draw out of a loop body) and this seeding (never hoist a draw above its consuming flow node). They must agree or the two rules disagree about the same node.
- **SCOPE — `behaviourStep` only, deliberately.** It is the sole root the WASM/WebGPU agent compilers emit (agent init / division event / agent output mappings are JS-on-CPU on EVERY target, so they cannot diverge), and the **CELL grid is already in lockstep** because its WASM/WebGPU compilers consume this same sink analysis. Widening it would rewrite the RNG stream of many library models for zero correctness gain.

### Verified
- **Two permanent synthetics in [scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs](scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs)** — `[synthetic] RNG draw order (branch draw + post-branch draws)` (a branch-local draw + two post-branch draws, each stored to an agent attribute so any reordering mismatches) and `[synthetic] Branch scope (value used inside AND after a branch)` (covers BOTH defect 1 — a `valueSwitch` read by the 2nd statement of a branch and again after it — and defect 2 — a `getVariable` read in both branches over a shared pure input). **Negative-controlled**: with the fixes reverted, the first MISMATCHES (`attr_sel js=0 wasm=-1`) and the second throws `ReferenceError: _v… is not defined`; with them, both pass at 30 and 250 steps.
- **Byte-identity** ([scripts/check-compile-identity.mjs](scripts/check-compile-identity.mjs), 24 models): every cell-grid surface unchanged, every `agent.wasm.bytes` / `agent.webgpu.shader` unchanged. Only **Ant Necrophoresis / Boids / Chemotaxis** `agent.behaviourCode` differ — exactly the three agent models with RNG. **Their runtime behaviour is unchanged**, proven two ways: (a) the WASM modules are byte-identical and JS↔WASM parity was green BEFORE and AFTER, so `JS_before == WASM == JS_after`; (b) the emitted diffs are textual relocations only — the draws move as a contiguous block preserving their relative order with no other draw interleaved (Boids/Chemotaxis), and Ant's hazard-volatile `r_carrying` read regroups within the same straight-line prefix, above the only write.
- All other harnesses green: `parity-agent-force` (7), `check-agent-wasm-gate` (9/9), `test-agent-abi` (28), `audit-agent-layout`, `test-ndtable`, `verify-sparse-stepping`, `test-grid-dimensions`, `test-loop-index`, `test-cross-agent-writes`, `test-positional-collision`, `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit`.
- **Real worker, in-browser, on the JS agent target** (the changed path): Ant Necrophoresis at generation 20 000 — corpses on ground 742 + carried 12 = **754, exactly the seeded total**, 0 non-binary cells, 120 ants still on exact integer positions, clustering 3.74 vs the 0.93 random baseline; **Boids** flocks to polarization **0.9984**; **Chemotaxis** builds its field (0 → 26.9) and aggregates (occupied bins 170 → 107, cluster-sum-of-squares 332 → 610). Zero worker errors throughout.

### The invariant the fixes preserve — ONE Get Random node = ONE draw, shared
A `getRandom` (or any RNG node) is drawn **once per cell / per agent per step**, and EVERY consumer sees that same number no matter where in the flow it is read — inside a branch, after it, in a sibling branch, or through an intermediate expression. Flow-ordering a draw moves WHERE it is emitted; it never splits it into two draws (the per-node `compiled` dedup + the value cache still hold). Verified after the fixes:
- **Cell grid**: one `getRandom` feeding a `then` write, an `else` write and a post-branch write emits exactly ONE `_rs` advance per cell and all three consumers reference the same `_v<id>`. (The cell path was untouched by these fixes — the RNG seeding is scoped to `behaviourStep` — so this is unchanged behaviour, confirmed rather than altered.)
- **Agents, JS**: the same four shapes (both-top-level / branch+after / then+else / via-expression) each emit exactly one draw per agent, and the consumers read equal values.
- **Agents, JS ↔ WASM**: the `[synthetic] RNG sharing (one draw, many consumers across scopes)` entry in [scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs](scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs) covers a pre-branch read + both sibling branches + a post-branch read + an expression-mediated read. **It carries a VALUE `invariant` (see below), not just cross-target agreement** — the WASM/WebGPU agent compilers drop their value cache at branch exit (`enterCacheScope`/`exitCacheScope`), so a re-emit there would be a genuine second draw that parity alone could never see if both targets did it identically.

**New harness capability — a per-entry `invariant(store)` hook** in `parity-agent-wasm.mjs`, run on BOTH stores (JS and wasmBacked) after each step and reported as `INVARIANT(js|wasm) <message>`. Use it whenever an entry asserts a SEMANTIC property rather than "the two targets agree" — parity is a mirror test and passes happily when both sides are equally wrong. Negative-controlled: pointing the post-branch consumer at a SECOND `getRandom` makes it fail with `top 0.317… !== afterBranch 0.750…`.

### Rule of thumb this leaves behind
When adding a node whose EVALUATION is a side effect (an RNG draw, a mutable-storage read), it must be **flow-ordered on every target** — add it to `isRngNode` (or seed it into the volatile closure) rather than letting sink analysis hoist it. And when touching `usedVolatiles`, remember the two roles of `next`: a later sibling at the caller's own scope, but part of the block everywhere below.

---

## Skip Isolated Empty Cells (opt-in large-grid optimization — branch `sim_agent_fixes`)

The step is normally **O(total cells)** — at 300³ (27M cells) every generation runs the full rule (incl. the 26-neighbour gather) on every cell even though the vast majority are isolated empty cells that provably can't change, and the precomputed neighbour-index tables are `total×nSz×4` bytes (**2.8 GB / ~10 s init at 300³**). This opt-in feature makes the step **O(active cells)** (the growing SURFACE + the structure, not the volume) and replaces the tables with inline neighbour computation. Plan + phase log: [docs/PLAN_LARGE_GRID_PERF.md](docs/PLAN_LARGE_GRID_PERF.md). **OFF by default → every existing model byte-identical** (proven by check-compile-identity per phase). Measured: the Accretor at **300³ inits in ~1.9 s (was ~10 s) with no 2.8 GB table and steps ~10 gens/s** (the remaining per-gen cost is the O(total) bulk copy + linked-indicator scan + colours ship, not the rule).

### The user contract (Properties → Execution → "Skip Isolated Empty Cells", CA-grid models only)
When enabled the user MUST pick: the **empty attribute** + the **empty value** (what "empty" means — tag/bool/int/float, type-adaptive widget), and a **processing range** (a neighbourhood OR a radius+metric). A cell is **ACTIVE** iff it's within the (symmetrized) range of a non-empty cell; only active cells run the Generation Step + the (post-batch) colour pass. **Painting is NEVER gated** — every mutation handler writes directly, then rebuilds the active set (a painted isolated cell activates + is processed next step — verified: painting a border cell at 300³ fired the model's edge-stop from that cell's own step). The range must ⊇ the rule's read-neighbourhood (the user's responsibility — it's opt-in; e.g. the Accretor's radius-1 Moore box covers its face/edge/corner reads because growth requires faceCount ≥ 1).

### Scope + gating
- `ModelProperties.skipIsolatedEmpty?: SkipIsolatedEmptyConfig` ([types.ts](src/model/types.ts)) — `{ enabled, emptyAttributeId, emptyValue, rangeKind: 'neighborhood'|'radius', neighborhoodId?, radius?, radiusMetric? }`. Additive; ModelContext cascades clear/remap on attribute/neighbourhood delete + tagOptions edits.
- **`sparseSteppingEnabled(model)`** ([sparseStepping.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/sparseStepping.ts) — its own tiny module so wasm/layout.ts imports it without a cycle; re-exported from compile.ts) = enabled + **synchronous** + gridCells + **no glyphs**. Async is rejected by design (single-buffer + shuffled order is incompatible with list iteration); **glyph models are excluded** (the per-pass glyph zero-fill assumes a FULL repaint — a sparse loop would erase inactive cells' glyphs; they keep the full loop, correct but not accelerated). **WebGPU ignores the feature** (runs full — correct, since sparse==full; a GPU stream-compaction pass is a possible follow-up). **Any config change forces a FULL worker reinit** (SimulatorView `needsFullInit`) — the config drives the baked layout + step signature. The worker's mirror predicate (`sieParamsPresent` + the initGrid layout flag) matches ALL four terms.
- **Sub-attributes under sparse**: sub-attrs normally skip the bulk copy (their per-cell conditional copy scrubs non-matching cells) — but the per-cell copy only runs for ACTIVE cells, so in sparse mode sub-attrs JOIN the bulk copy on BOTH JS (`sparseBulk` gate in bulkCopyLines) and WASM (`!layout.sparseStepping &&` in emitBulkCopyLines). Active cells still scrub (their conditional copy overwrites on top — identical); inactive cells carry storage forward, which is INVISIBLE behind the parent-match guard (reads, iteration, linked indicators all apply it). Without this, an inactive cell's w-buffer held two-generations-old data after the swap.

### The active set ([src/simulator/engine/activeSet.ts](src/simulator/engine/activeSet.ts) — shared by the worker AND the Node harness)
Reference-counted: `nearCount[idx]` = # of non-empty cells within range; `list`/`member`/`count` + `staleCount`→`compactActiveSet` (so non-monotonic models stay correct — counts go up AND down). `buildActiveOffsets` (neighbourhood coords symmetrized ∪ origin, or a radius ball by chebyshev/manhattan/euclidean), `rebuildActiveSet` (O(total) scan — init/reset/gridInit/loadState/every mutation via `refreshColorsAfterInputJS`), `applyTransition` (per empty↔non-empty flip, ±1 dilation). **`ActiveSet.list` is a VIEW over wasmMemory at `layout.activeListOffset`** — the sparse WASM step reads the live list via the baked offset and the JS step reads the same view through its arg: zero per-step copies. In `runStep`, transitions are detected AFTER the step fn and BEFORE the buffer swap (readAttrs = pre-step, writeAttrs = post-step — true on JS ref-swap AND WASM attrsA/attrsB).

### Compile changes (JS + WASM lockstep; OFF = byte-identical)
- **JS** ([compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/compile.ts)): the sync step + the colour pass emit a dual loop — `if (_activeList) { for over the list } else { full 0..total }` — with `_activeList`/`_activeCount` appended LAST to the params. Linked-indicator aggregation is NOT embedded when sparse (it would only see active cells) — the worker runs the full-grid `computeLinkedIndicatorsFromBuffer` instead (the WASM pattern).
- **WASM** ([wasm/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/wasm/compile.ts)): loop entries (step + outputMapping) gain a 2nd `activeCount` i32 param — `>= 0` iterates the active-list region, `-1` = the full loop (the worker's sentinel). The historical loop is extracted into `emitCellLoop` (byte-identical when non-sparse); the body is emitted twice (once per branch — emitBody is re-entrant). A 5th func type `(i32,i32)->()` is appended ONLY in sparse modules.
- **Colour pass**: SPARSE only for the post-step-batch pass (`runColorPass(true)` — inactive cells never step, so their colour inputs are provably frozen); **FULL** for paint / reset / model-attr edit / viewer switch / load (any event that can change an inactive cell's colour) and **always for glyph models** (the per-pass glyph zero-fill would erase inactive cells' glyphs). `buildLoopArgs(useActiveList)` passes a null list to force full.

### Inline neighbours (the 300³ unblock)
When the feature is on, the per-neighbourhood region holds **`nSz` PACKED NIs** (packNI/packNI3 — [wasm/layout.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/wasm/layout.ts) sizes it `size×4` instead of `total×size×4`), filled once by the worker ([sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts) `buildNeighborIndices`' sparse branch — one fill serves both targets, the JS arg is a view over the same region; the constant-boundary sentinel fill is kept). Emitters decode each slot inline — **the exact torus-wrap / constant-sentinel math the big table precomputed**, so results are byte-identical: JS via `CompileContext.inlineNbr` + `niCellExprStmts` ([GetNeighborsAttributeNode](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/GetNeighborsAttributeNode.ts) + [GetNeighborAttributeByTagNode](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/GetNeighborAttributeByTagNode.ts)); WASM via `pushInlineNbrCellIdx` (slot's packed NI → the existing `pushNiCellIdx`) at all 6 reachable table-read sites (byTag / groupStatement / random pick / the aggregate main loop / median-scratch / sub-attr-random-scratch / groupCounting-indexes). **Aggregate FUSION is disabled in sparse mode on JS** (the fused builders read the per-cell table; unfused, the gather materialises through the inline-aware scratch fill — negligible on sparse cells). `setNeighborhoodAttribute` is async-only → structurally unreachable in (sync-only) sparse mode. The `_activeList`/`_activeCount`/packed-`nIdx` args are appended/threaded through `buildLoopParams`/`buildCellParams`/the OM params + the worker's `buildLoopArgs`/`buildCellArgs` (ABI-mirror discipline; `sieParamsPresent` is the worker's mirror predicate, and the layout flag rides `computeMemoryLayout(..., sparseStepping)` — set BEFORE `initGrid` from the init message).

### Verification ([scripts/verify-sparse-stepping.mjs](scripts/verify-sparse-stepping.mjs) — run `--wasm` for the full matrix)
The proof standard for any change touching this feature: a feature-OFF compile (classic table) is the REFERENCE; the feature-ON arms — JS sparse-list, JS inline-full (null list), WASM sparse (real instantiated module, list in wasm memory), WASM inline-full (-1) — must produce **byte-identical grids after every step**, on 2D + 3D minimal accretion models that stay genuinely sparse (maxActive ~36-48%), plus a layout-shrink assertion. Also verified END-TO-END through the real worker: Accretor ON == OFF at a fixed RNG seed (identical grid hashes, JS + WASM); a too-small range (corners) ≠ full (proves the gating is real); painting isolated cells works at 300³.

### Gotchas (several found by the adversarial review + fixed)
- The **active set covers the whole STRUCTURE + shell, not just the surface** — a non-empty cell is always active (range 0 of itself). For the Accretor, frozen interior cells still step but short-circuit cheaply (isEmpty gate). Skipping frozen non-empty cells would be a different (model-specific) contract — the feature's name is the contract: only isolated EMPTY cells are skipped.
- **Agent-topology models are EXCLUDED** (in the predicate + both worker mirrors): the field bridge deposits into the cell read buffer OUTSIDE the step, so the active set can never see those transitions — a deposit into an isolated cell would silently never activate it. Agent models keep the full loop.
- **Constant boundary + a NON-empty boundary value on the empty attribute → worker falls back to full** (setupActiveSet sentinel guard): every border-adjacent cell can read the always-non-empty sentinel, a state NO range choice covers. The common empty-boundary configuration (the Accretor) is unaffected.
- **`sieColorDirtyAll`** forces the next colour pass FULL after (a) a `compactActiveSet` (a just-emptied cell removed from the list before the batch-end pass would keep its stale colour) and (b) `updateModelAttrs`/`updateLookupTable` (both are legitimate OM inputs — inactive cells' colours may need recomputing). Cleared once a full pass runs.
- **Incremental linked indicators (the REAL 300³ payoff — found by benchmarking, not review):** the per-gen full-grid indicator scan (`computeLinkedIndicatorsFromBuffer`) costs **~775 ms/gen at 27M cells** — it DWARFED the sparse step (~5 ms) and made the feature look useless end-to-end (OFF 1302 → ON 813 ms/gen, a perceptual wash — the user-reported "no significant difference"). Fixed two ways in [sim.worker.ts](src/simulator/engine/sim.worker.ts): (1) **sparse-incremental** — when EVERY linked def is eligible (plain cell attr, non-spatial, `total` or `frequency` over bool/tag/integer — float-freq bins depend on the global min/max, genuinely non-incremental), the aggregates are maintained from the SAME O(active) diff loop that maintains the active set (`sieLinkedFreq`/`sieLinkedTotal` raw-numeric Maps + `emitSieLinkedResults` producing the scan-identical string-keyed shape; baseline recount in `rebuildActiveSetFromGrid`); exact per-generation values → works at ANY Gens/Frame, accumulated included. (2) **batch-tail deferral** — when incremental doesn't apply and no linked def is `accumulated`, the batch loops pass `runStep(deferIndicatorScan=true)` and the scan+spatial run ONCE at the batch tail (`indicatorScanPending`) — identical observables (the stepped message / end conditions / Overseer all read once per batch); gated to `(callWasm || sieParamsPresent)` so the plain-JS target (which embeds the aggregation in the loop) is untouched. Measured at 300³: **OFF 1302 → ON 40 ms/gen (G/F 20) / 167 ms per frame (G/F 1, now dominated by the 108 MB colours ship + voxel rescan — render, not simulation)**. Parity verified end-to-end: grid hash AND indicator values identical ON-incremental (1-gen batches) vs OFF-scan (5-gen deferred batches).
- **Observability (`sieActive`)**: every `stepped` message carries the live active-cell count (`-1` = feature configured but NOT engaged — excluded combination / invalid config — full loop running); the simulator stats overlay renders **"◩ N active (x%)"** or an amber **"◩ skip-empty inactive"**. This exists because silent fallbacks made "is it even on?" unanswerable — keep it working when touching the feature.
- **Where the feature CANNOT help (tell users, don't debug):** MNCA-class models — dense soups with huge neighbourhoods (the range must cover the LARGEST read-neighbourhood; dilating a soup by radius ~10 covers ~the whole grid → active ≈ 100% = pure overhead, and on the JS target fusion is disabled → measurably WORSE) — and any model on the WebGPU target (documented no-op). The feature's home turf is compact structures growing through a large empty volume (accretion, crystal growth, maze builders).
- **Range size is capped at 30000 offsets** (worker bail + the UI radius max 15): `nearCount` is Uint16 — an oversized range could overflow it in dense regions and a wrap would drop genuinely-active cells at the next compaction.
- Per-gen costs that remain O(total): the sync bulk copy (w←r, ~30 ms/gen at 27M). Per-FRAME (amortized by Gens/Frame): the colours ship (108 MB) + the main-thread voxel-renderer rescan. The indicator scan is NO LONGER per-gen (see the incremental/deferral bullet above).
- `readRegion` reads layer 0 only (pre-existing 2D-clipboard limitation — noted while verifying paint at 300³).

---

## Overseer (Experiment Orchestration) — the THIRD graph (branch `sim_agent_fixes`)

The **Overseer** is an opt-in third rule graph that authors experiment protocols AROUND the simulation — replicate statistics (N seeded runs → mean ± std), parameter sweeps, run-until-stop protocols, capture — implementing docs/PLAN_OVERSEER.md (PR0–PR2 scope + the PR1 sample). **The Cells/Agents graphs are the physics; the Overseer is the scientist.** Everything is gated by **`model.overseerConfig?.enabled`** (Properties → Execution → "Use Overseer") — with it OFF there is ZERO trace of the feature anywhere (no graph pill, no palette nodes, no right-panel tab strip — the panel is just the Controls, as before; verified: loading a normal model over an overseer model removes every trace). All schema additive; legacy files load unchanged.

### The core posture — a DRIVER, not a compile target
The Overseer runs at EXPERIMENT tempo (a few node evaluations per run) as an **async JS program on the MAIN thread**, commanding the sim worker through the **existing message protocol** exactly like the transport bar does (post a step batch → await `stepped` → decide). The CA keeps running on whichever compile target the model selects — **the ALL-TARGET rule is satisfied by construction** (same stance as the agent colour pass). Verified end-to-end on ALL THREE targets through the real worker: the same seeded experiment is **bit-reproducible on JS and WASM (bit-IDENTICAL to each other)** and statistically reproducible on WebGPU (per-cell PCG — the documented stance).

### Schema ([types.ts](src/model/types.ts), all additive)
`CAModel.overseerGraphNodes?`/`overseerGraphEdges?` (serialized compact-inline like the agent graph) + **`overseerConfig?: OverseerConfig`** = `{ enabled, seedPolicy?: 'none'|'fixed'|'sequential', baseSeed?, maxSnapshotSlots? }`. `NodeRequirements.overseer?: boolean` gates the node family. ModelContext: `SET_OVERSEER_GRAPH` + `UPDATE_OVERSEER_CONFIG` (+ both useMemo dep arrays), LOAD_MODEL seed guards, and **`patchAllNodes` scans `overseerGraphNodes`** (its result now carries the graph and all 13 consumer sites SPREAD it — `{ ...model, ...patched }` — so adding a future graph can''t silently miss a cascade site).

### Editor (third pill + allowlist gating)
`ActiveGraphKind` = `'cells' | 'agents' | 'overseer'` ([graphState.ts](src/modeler/vpl/graphState.ts) + [modelerUiState.ts](src/modeler/modelerUiState.ts)). GraphEditor reuses the whole Agents-graph swap machinery: the amber **Overseer** pill (shown only when enabled; the strip now renders when `agentsTopo || overseerOn`), the graph-swap effect + scheduleSync/flushSync routing to `setOverseerGraph`, the graph-kind-tagged clipboard, the fall-back effect when the feature is disabled mid-session, and `experiment` in `SINGLETON_NODE_TYPES`. **Availability on the Overseer graph is an explicit ALLOWLIST** (`OVERSEER_UNIVERSAL_TYPES` in [nodeValidation.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/nodeValidation.ts)): overseer nodes + the context-free universal subset (sequence/conditional/loop/forEachInArray/switch + getConstant/arithmetic/expression/Compare/logic/getRandom/valueSwitch/proportionMap/interpolation/arrayElement/arrayLength/getModelAttribute). EVERY per-cell/per-agent node is excluded (the allowlist IS the safety boundary); overseer nodes are invisible on the other graphs and when the feature is off; `isMacroAvailableOnGraph` gained the overseer arm.

### The node family (20, `requirements: { overseer: true }`, prefix `Ov*`)
**Root**: `experiment` (white, singleton — runs on the panel''s Run Experiment). **Run control** (indigo `#3949ab`): `ovResetBoard`, `ovRunGenerations` (Count inline — a **FIXED-count run**: a Stop Event does NOT halt it, so every replicate reaches the same iteration = the fixed developmental time point an ensemble average needs; Run Until Stop is the detector-gated node), `ovRunUntilStop` (Max Gens; value outs `atGeneration` + `stoppedBy` 0=cap/1=stopEvent/2=endCondition — action RESULTS, captured once as function-scope lets), `ovSetSeed`, `ovSetModelAttribute` (runtime-only slider semantics — never dirties the model), **`ovRandomizeTable`** (runtime-only re-roll of a Lookup Table's values from a Seed at a Density — the SAME deterministic `randomFillTableData` as the editor's Randomize block; the rule-space-search primitive, journals each {seed, density}; `O.randomizeTable` → `deps.randomizeTable` resolves axes + value policy in SimulatorView and posts `updateLookupTable`, multi-axis and legacy), `ovLoadPreset` (live-apply only; structural presets journal + skip). **Measurement** (teal `#00838f`): `ovReadIndicator` (config indicatorId + category for freq maps — bool/tag categories get a select, numeric buckets a text input; spatial indicators excluded like End Conditions), `ovGetGeneration`. **Data/stats** (purple `#6a1b9a`): `ovCollectSample` (series + run/experiment scope), `ovCollectSpatial` (capture a SPATIAL indicator''s whole per-position curve for one category as one replicate — see "Spatial aggregate" below), `ovClearSeries`, `ovSeriesStat` (mean/std/min/max/median/sum/count/ci95), `ovSweepValues` (list or linspace → array out; feed forEachInArray). **Capture** (blue-grey `#546e7a`): `ovLog` (`{value}`/`{gen}` placeholders), `ovScreenshot`, `ovStartRecording`/`ovStopRecording`. **Control**: `ovStopExperiment` (no NEXT). CaNode config blocks + `detectMissingConfig` cases for all reference-typed configs. Catalogue: **141 selectable** node types (144 − 3 hidden macro) / 45 agent / 20 overseer.

### Spatial aggregate — replicate-averaged chromatograms (mean ± σ)
`ovCollectSpatial` ([OvCollectSpatialNode.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/OvCollectSpatialNode.ts)) captures a SPATIAL indicator''s (`xAxis` rows/columns/layers) current per-position-bin curve for ONE category as one run of a named **spatial series** (`O.sampleSpatial(series, indicatorId, category, chart)`). Its validation is the inverse of `ovReadIndicator` (requires a SPATIAL indicator + a category). The runtime ([overseerRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/overseerRuntime.ts) `spatialSeries` map + `spatialAggregate(name)`) stacks the per-run curves and computes a **per-bin mean + sample-σ (n−1)**; the panel ([ExperimentsPanel.tsx](src/simulator/ExperimentsPanel.tsx) `SpatialAggregateChart`) renders each **Chart group** (series sharing a `chart` name overlay on one canvas) as: faint individual replicate curves + a σ band + the bold mean per series, palette by insertion order. `SimulatorView.spatialMeta(indicatorId)` resolves the X-axis label (axisName + binSize) from the indicator def + live dims. CSV export is long-format (`series,run,bin,value`); JSON carries the aggregates. The **Chromatography** model ships the headline use: 12 seeded replicates × Reset → Run 300 → capture S1+S2 → Run to 600 → capture S1+S2, aggregated into two mean±σ chromatograms (verified in-browser: S1 [weak B-affinity] peak migrates bin 4→7 down the column while S2 [strong] lags at bin 1→2, a ~5-bin two-peak separation — the paper''s Fig. 3 as an ensemble average, S1 leads S2). **Gotcha**: `ovRunGenerations` being detector-gated would truncate the fixed-time snapshot and pollute the ensemble with the model''s spurious early-elution (a solute wrapping up the vertical torus) — hence Run Generations is a strict fixed-count run.

### The compiler ([compiler/overseer/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/overseer/compile.ts))
`compileOverseerGraph(nodes, edges, model)` → `{ driverCode, error }` where driverCode is the async function BODY for `new AsyncFunction('O', body)` (null when no `experiment` root — not an error). Front-end = the standard pipeline (`expandMacros → collapseReroutes → expandMultiAttrs`). **Value nodes reuse their per-node JS `compile()` verbatim** (lockstep semantics with the cell/agent compilers; the driver preamble provides `modelAttrs` + `_rs` for getModelAttribute/getRandom). **Values are RE-EVALUATED at every flow statement that consumes them** (each statement is wrapped in its own `{ … }` block, so `const _v<id>` redeclares per statement) — a Read Indicator consumed after a Run reads the POST-run value; the exceptions are ACTION RESULT ports (Run Until Stop) and For-Each element/index, which are captured once (`let _v<id>_<port>` hoisted / loop locals) and referenced afterwards. `if (O.aborted) return;` injected after every await + at loop back-edges. Flow walk mirrors the cell compiler''s semantics for conditional/loop/forEach/switch (both switch modes + firstMatchOnly) and walks `next` pass-throughs. Defence-in-depth: any reachable non-allowlisted node type → compile error. Verified by [scripts/test-overseer-compile.mjs](scripts/test-overseer-compile.mjs) — 18 assertions running the emitted driver against a mock O (statistics loop, sweep × until-stop with action results, conditional + stop, guards, RNG determinism).

### The runtime ([engine/overseerRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/overseerRuntime.ts)) + worker correlation
`OverseerRuntime` attaches its OWN worker listener (workers support multiple listeners — SimulatorView''s handler is untouched) and correlates its batches via **`reqId`**: `StepMsg`/`ResetMsg` gained `reqId?: number`, echoed on the resulting `stepped` (module `stepAckId` consumed by `sendColors` — set in the step/reset cases, carried across the async WebGPU/agent batch tails) and on the step case''s `stopEvent` posts. After each ack the runtime waits ONE macrotask so a trailing `stopEvent` (posted right after `sendColors`) is observed before deciding "no stop". Batches are bounded (`OV_BATCH` 500 gens) so Abort lands within one batch. **Seed policy**: `maybeAutoSeed` posts `setRngSeed` BEFORE each `reset` (fixed = base; sequential = base + reset-count) unless the graph ran Set Random Seed since the last reset. **Series** (run/experiment scope; run-scoped clear at each Reset) + **Journal** (capped 2000) + CSV (long format) / JSON export. `O.indicator` reads the runtime''s OWN last-stepped copy (no ordering dependence on SimulatorView''s handler); end conditions checked per batch via the passed `evalEndConditions`.

### SimulatorView wiring + the Experiments panel ([ExperimentsPanel.tsx](src/simulator/ExperimentsPanel.tsx))
The panel is titled **"Overseer Experiments"** and, when enabled, lives on its OWN tab of the shared right panel — SimulatorView's `rightPanelTab: 'controls' | 'experiments'` swaps the panel between the "Controls" view (brush + layers + indicators) and the "Overseer Experiments" view (mirroring the modeler's per-panel tabs). The tab strip (`.rightPanelTabs` / `.rightPanelTab` in SimulatorView.module.css) renders ONLY when `overseerEnabled`; with the feature off the panel is just the controls (as before, no tabs) and an effect resets `rightPanelTab` to `'controls'`. The ExperimentsPanel root carries the `rightSectionIndicators` grow class so it fills the panel (its body scrolls) as the sole tab content. Panel contents: Run/Abort, live status (runs · gen · elapsed), scrolling Journal, Series table (n/mean/std/min/max), an auto-rendered **`ScalarSeriesChart` per scalar series** (a HISTOGRAM of the value distribution across the runs with the mean marked — the classic replicate-distribution figure — toggleable per series to a per-run SEQUENCE view; state in the panel's `scalarModes`, default `'hist'`, series with ≥2 samples), the spatial aggregate charts, CSV/JSON export, and a hint when the graph has no Experiment root. So an experiment produces real FIGURES (histograms + spatial mean±σ curves), not just a text report. SimulatorView compiles the graph in a `useMemo([model, overseerEnabled])`, builds the runtime deps at Run time (worker access, `evalEndConditions`, `handleModelAttrChange`, the live-preset predicate mirroring `handleLoadPreset`''s structural check, screenshot/recording pass-throughs, `runtimeModelAttrsLatest` snapshot ref), and enforces **mutual exclusion**: `sendNextStep`/`handleStep`/`handleReset` early-return while an experiment runs; the `stopEvent` handler suppresses its notice (the runtime journals it); ANY model change / worker reinit / manual recompile / unmount **aborts the experiment** (`abortExperiment` at the model-effect top, `initWorkerWithDimensions`, `handleRecompile`, the unmount cleanup).

### setRngSeed is now a SUPPORTED worker message (bug fixed)
Promoted from DEV-only. **The bug the E2E caught**: the WASM step/init read the RNG from the in-memory cell at `layout.rngStateOffset`, synced from `rngState[0]` only at `initGrid` — so seeding was a silent no-op on the WASM target (identical seed policies gave different WASM replicate statistics across two runs while JS reproduced bit-exactly). The handler now also writes the wasmMemory cell and re-derives the WebGPU per-cell PCG streams via `seedRngState`. Post-fix verification (real worker, 5-run experiment, sequential seeds): JS 453.4 ± 15.534 bit-reproducible; **WASM bit-IDENTICAL to JS** (453.4/15.534/440/479) and reproducible; WebGPU 460.8 ± 15.707 reproducible.

### Sample + harness + verification recipes
**`GoL Replicate Statistics`** ([scripts/gen-overseer-stats.mjs](scripts/gen-overseer-stats.mjs) → public/models/): Conway GoL 60×60 (p=0.35 random-soup Init Event, linked OM, linked freq indicator with trackedValues) + the Overseer protocol `Clear Series → Loop ×20 { Reset → Run 200 → Collect ← Read population[true] } → Log mean → Log std`, sequential seed base 4242. Verified in-browser on WASM: 20 replicates in 1.9 s, survivors 283.7 ± 58.6 (n=20, range 131–397), second run bit-identical. The dev harness `compileAll` gained an `overseer.driverCode` surface + the migrate guards. **Gotchas for future work**: (1) the runtime''s deps close over the model at Run time — fine because any model change aborts; (2) a no-Output-Mapping model''s reset/mutation refresh runs an extra STEP (pre-existing worker behaviour), so per-run generation counts read slightly above the requested N — consistent across runs, harmless for statistics; (3) Run Experiment while the tab is hidden keeps stepping (the auto-pause only gates the play loop) — deliberate; (4) snapshot slots (`ovSaveSnapshot`/`ovLoadSnapshot`), `ovWriteRegion`, `ovApplyImage`, overseer-scoped Local Variables, result CHARTS, the parallel worker pool (plan PR4) and the EA sample (plan PR5) are NOT yet implemented — see docs/PLAN_OVERSEER.md for their design.

## Get Grid Dimensions (universal node — Cells AND Agents, all 6 compile surfaces)

A value node exposing the SIZE of the world the rule runs in: **Width**, **Height** and (3D only) **Depth**. It is **UNIVERSAL** — available on BOTH the Cells graph (the lattice grid size) and the Agents graph (the agent world size; the agent world IS the cell grid **1:1**, so the two report the same numbers, and the node renders as **"Get World Dimensions"** there via `agentLabel`/`agentDescription`). It reads no per-cell / per-agent state, so it works in **every event on both graphs** (Step / Init / Grid Init / Input Mapping / Output Mapping — Behaviour Step / Agent Init / Division Event / Agent Output Mapping).

**Center outputs** (`centerX`/`centerY`/`centerZ` = ⌊W/2⌋ / ⌊H/2⌋ / ⌊D/2⌋, matching the hand-wired `size/2`-then-truncate idiom): opt-in via the **`withCenter` config checkbox** ("Output center") — but the checkbox is **UI declutter ONLY**: `hiddenPorts` hides the three ports when off, while **every compiler emits the centres UNCONDITIONALLY** (three cheap loop-invariant constants), so a wire into a centre port keeps working if the checkbox is later unticked (the stale-edge-on-hidden-output trap). `centerZ` is additionally hidden in 2D (value 0 there). Per-surface emit: JS cell `Math.floor(W/2)`; WASM/WebGPU cell bake the floored literal; JS agent `Math.floor(_fieldW/2)` (centerZ from the derived depth); agentWasm `floor(field* × 0.5)` (exact for integer dims ⇒ JS↔WASM bit-parity); agentWebgpu `floor(control.field* * 0.5)` (f32, exact for grid-scale ints).

**Why:** a rule that wants to be grid-size INDEPENDENT (seed the middle, normalise a coordinate to 0..1, fade by distance from an edge, keep agents inside the world) previously had to hard-code the dimensions — silently wrong the moment the user hits the simulator's **Resize** — or route them through the one event root that happens to expose them (Grid Init's width/height/depth, Init Event's maxX/maxY, Agent Init's World Width/Height). The values are always **LIVE**: nothing is baked into the `.gcaproj`, and a simulator Resize recompiles through the `dimsModel` override, so the node reports the real dims.

### Emit — no ABI change anywhere; W/H/D were already in scope on all six surfaces
- **JS cell** ([GetGridDimensionsNode.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/GetGridDimensionsNode.ts)): the `W` / `H` / `D` step params (`D` only exists in a 3D signature → 2D emits the literal `1`, keeping 2D byte-identical).
- **WASM cell** ([wasm/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/wasm/compile.ts)): `i32` constants baked from `ctx.model.properties` (exactly how `initEvent` bakes maxX/maxY).
- **WebGPU cell** ([webgpu/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/webgpu/compile.ts)): `i32` literals baked from `ctx.layout.gridWidth/Height/Depth` (`gridDepth` is 1 in 2D).
- **JS agent**: `_fieldW` / `_fieldH`. **Depth derives from `_fieldTotal`** — `_fieldD` is **NOT in the Agent Init Event's ABI** ([agentAbi.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentAbi.ts)'s trailing 3D block omits it for `kind === 'init'`), so a naive `_fieldD` emit would `ReferenceError` the moment the node is dropped in an Agent Init Event. The derived form `((_fieldW > 0 && _fieldH > 0) ? Math.round(_fieldTotal / (_fieldW * _fieldH)) : 1)` is correct in **all four** agent roots — the SAME trick the Agent Init Event's own World Depth port uses.
- **WASM agent** ([agentWasm/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWasm/compile.ts)): the behaviour's `fieldW` / `fieldH` / `fieldD` f64 params (always present; `fieldD` = `s.worldDepth`, i.e. 1 in 2D). In `AGENT_WASM_SUPPORTED_TYPES`.
- **WebGPU agent** ([agentWebgpu/compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/agentWebgpu/compile.ts)): `control.fieldW` / `fieldH` / `fieldD` (always in the Control uniform). In `AGENT_WEBGPU_SUPPORTED_TYPES`.

### `CompileContext.agentGraph` (new)
A universal node's `compile()` has no idea which ROOT it sits under, but it must emit against the right ABI. `agentGraph` is true for **all FOUR** agent roots (`AGENT_ROOT_TYPES` in [compile.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/compile.ts)) — **distinct from `agentRoot`**, which is `undefined` on `agentOutputMapping` (it only tags the three roots whose live-agent guard differs). Any future universal node that reads the same thing from a different ABI on the two graphs should branch on `agentGraph`, NOT on `agentRoot` (an agent OM graph would otherwise emit the cell `W`/`H` and blow up).

### Registration checklist (what a multi-output universal value node needs)
`MULTI_OUTPUT_TYPES` (3 ports) ✓; **NOT** `NEVER_INVARIANT` — it has no value inputs and reads only function PARAMS, so loop-invariance correctly hoists it out of the per-cell / per-agent loop (contrast `getCellPosition`, which reads the per-cell `_row`/`_col` and MUST be `NEVER_INVARIANT`); **NOT** `NEVER_PURE_TYPES` — the dims are immutable, so two instances CSE-merge (a free win); **NOT** `LATTICE_ONLY_TYPES` + **no `requirements`** = the definition of "universal" (available on both graphs); **NOT** in `OVERSEER_UNIVERSAL_TYPES` — the overseer driver's preamble provides no `W`/`H`, and baking them would go stale after a simulator Resize. No `detectMissingConfig` case (no config). `hiddenPorts` hides `depth` in 2D via `is3dModelLike`.

### Latent bug fixed in the same pass
`agentWebgpu`'s `emitBehaviourStep` had **no `myZ` case**, so a 3D model wiring **Behaviour Step → Z** silently read `0.0` on the WebGPU agent target while JS/WASM read `_agentZ[idx]`. No shipped model wires it (byte-identity confirms), so this was latent. Gated on `ctx.is3d` (the `z` SoA run only exists in 3D).

### Verification
- **[scripts/test-grid-dimensions.mjs](scripts/test-grid-dimensions.mjs)** (the regression standard for this node): availability (universal / hidden on Overseer / Depth hidden in 2D); **cells 2D + 3D compiled AND RUN** — the JS step and a **real instantiated WASM module in Node** both leave every cell holding exactly W/H/D, JS↔WASM bit-identical; WGSL bakes the literals; **agents 2D + 3D** — the JS behaviour loop RUN (every agent holds W/H/D), the **Agent Init Event RUN through the real spawn idiom** (Create Agent lands at x=W, y=H, z=D — the `_fieldD`-absent trap), both agent gates accept the node, both modules/shaders compile.
- **[scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs](scripts/parity-agent-wasm.mjs)**: a permanent synthetic 3D grid-dims agent model (`buildGridDimsModel`) → 13/13 JS↔WASM bit-parity. NB parity alone would pass if BOTH targets were wrong — the value assertions live in `test-grid-dimensions.mjs`.
- **[scripts/check-compile-identity.mjs](scripts/check-compile-identity.mjs)**: all 23 library models byte-identical on every surface.

## 3D auto-zoom (the dolly sibling of auto-orbit)

Auto-orbit spins the camera; **auto-zoom** dollies it — and it has the SAME shape: it travels in ONE direction at the slider's rate (negative = zoom in, positive = zoom out, 0 = stopped) and **STOPS at the distance limit**, so it can't keep zooming forever. It is deliberately NOT an oscillation. The point is unattended recordings: start the camera close, turn both on, and it orbits + slowly pulls out as the model grows (the Softology Accretor videos).

- **The dolly is MULTIPLICATIVE** — `dist *= exp(speed * dt)` — so it reads as a constant-rate zoom at every distance (a linear `dist += speed*dt` would crawl when far out and lurch when close in). `speed` is therefore in **e-folds/second**: the default 0.15 pulls out from 1.9 to the far limit in ~20 s; the slider spans -1..1. Because `dist` is a **multiple of the largest grid dimension** ([gl3d.ts](src/simulator/render/gl3d.ts) `setCamera`), the motion is grid-size independent for free. The clamp to `[MIN_CAM_DIST, MAX_CAM_DIST]` IS the "limit" — no separate range control.
- **State**: `AutoZoom3D { on, speed }` + `DEFAULT_AUTOZOOM3D` in [gl3d.ts](src/simulator/render/gl3d.ts); `zoom3d` state + `zoom3dRef` in [SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx). **NOT persisted** — matching auto-orbit (a camera animation that resumed itself on every load would surprise), which also sidesteps the settings-persist **declaration-order trap** (any state feeding the persist effect must be declared ABOVE it, ~line 977).
- **A wheel zoom mid-flight COMPOSES for free** — the loop SCALES whatever distance the camera is at rather than SETTING it from a stored base, so a manual zoom simply carries on from where the user lands. (An earlier oscillating design needed a `zoomBaseRef` + `zoomPhaseRef` + an `autoZoomBaseFrom` re-baseline on every wheel/Reset to avoid stomping the user; the one-way dolly needs none of that. If you ever reintroduce an absolute `cam.dist = f(...)` form, that whole re-baselining problem comes back.)
- **ONE rAF loop drives BOTH animations** (the effect gate is `!is3D || (!orbit3d.on && !zoom3d.on)`) — two independent loops would each call `draw()` every frame and **double the redraw rate** when both are on. It reads its params through refs so a slider drag doesn't restart the loop; `last` resets to 0 while hidden so a tab-away can't apply a huge `dt` and fling the camera (the dt is also clamped to 0.1 s).
- **Shared camera consts** (same file): the distance clamp `MIN_CAM_DIST` (0.2) / `MAX_CAM_DIST` (40) and the default camera `defaultCamera3d()` are now exported consts/factory instead of literals duplicated between the wheel handler, the initial `cam3dRef`, and Reset view — so the wheel and auto-zoom **can't drift apart**. **Reset view now restores the default IN PLACE** rather than replacing `cam3dRef.current`, which used to strand every holder of the old object (the DEV `window.__sim3dCamera` hook among them — it reads a stale camera after a reset otherwise).
- Renderer-layer only: **2D untouched**, no compiler / worker / schema impact.
- **Verified in-browser** on Life3D. NB the Browser pane is **occluded** (`document.hidden`), which **suspends the real rAF** AND makes `readPixels` on the default framebuffer return all-zero — so drive the loop with an **rAF shim + synthetic timestamps**, and prove the render path by **spying on `Gl3DRenderer.setCamera`** rather than reading pixels. Results: at the default +0.15 the distance climbs monotonically 1.9 → 4.0 → 8.5 → 18.0 → 38.2 and **stops dead at 40**, staying there for another 20 s; at -0.3 it dollies in and **stops at 0.2**; speed 0 is stopped; a wheel zoom mid-flight applies (0.20 → 0.22) and the next frame **carries on inward from the new distance** (0.2199) instead of overriding it; with both animations on, **one rAF + exactly one `setCamera` per frame** (60 frames → 60 calls, 60 distinct distances reaching the renderer) while yaw spins and dist pulls out. Zero console errors.

## Viewer pill / preset menu / hemifield polish (branch `improvements`, 2026-07-28)

Three user-feedback items. All additive; the compilers are untouched (`check-compile-identity`: 24 models byte-identical on every surface).

- **The Simulator Instructions pill is hidden in the standalone VIEWER.** `SimulatorView` gained an optional **`hideInstructionsPill`** prop, set by [ViewerApp.tsx](src/viewer/ViewerApp.tsx). The viewer renders its OWN top-left `ⓘ Info` button (rAF-anchored just right of the settings ear, so it lands at ~left 22px) and the pill sits at `left: 36px` with a higher-z-index Info button on top of it — they collided, AND the viewer's About panel already renders `properties.instructions`, so the pill is redundant there. In the MAIN app the pill is clear of the panel-expand ear in BOTH panel states (measured: ear right 14–16px vs pill left 36–38px). **Also fixed in passing:** the popover header contained a raw `—` in JSX TEXT (not an expression), so it RENDERED the literal escape — now `{'—'}`.
- **Preset rows declutter behind a `…` menu + a new Rename.** Each simulator preset row is now **`[name] [Load] […] [⋮⋮]`**; the five inline icon buttons moved into `.presetMenu` (a `position: fixed` popover measured from its trigger so the scrolling panel body can't clip it — width MUST match the `PRESET_MENU_W` const used to right-align it; `PRESET_MENU_H` decides drop-below vs flip-above). One open at a time, dismissed by a capture-phase outside `pointerdown` or Escape (the context-menu pattern). Items: **Overwrite with current state · Rename… · Duplicate · Export (.gcapreset) · Delete** (danger-styled, still routed through the existing `ConfirmDialog`). **Rename** reuses `PresetSaveDialog` in a metadata-only mode — a new **`hideGridOption`** prop hides the include-grid checkbox — and dispatches `updatePreset(id, {name, description})`, never capturing sim state. The row's `title`, the `◉` grid marker and the reorder drag are unchanged.
- **FOV sensing polish (three parts).**
  - **Optional per-node `config.visionColor`** (`#rrggbb`) on BOTH `getAgentsInView` + `senseHemifield` — **DISPLAY-ONLY**, edited by the `VisionColorRow` block in CaNode (a cosmetic RGB `<input type=color>` + a `⟳` reset that writes `''`). `SimulatorView`'s `visionCones` memo resolves it to an `"r,g,b"` `tint` and the draw() wedge block uses `cone.tint ?? TINTS[ci % TINTS.length]`. **No compiler on any target reads it**; both nodes are already in `NEVER_PURE_TYPES`, so the new config key can't perturb an accessor-CSE purity key either.
  - **Radius discoverability.** The `Radius` input was ALREADY there on both nodes (a port with an inline number widget, default 5) — the reason it reads as missing is that **port labels are a global toggle that is OFF by default**, so an inline widget renders as an unlabelled box. Fixed generally: CaNode's `.inlineWidgetWrapper` now carries **`title={port.label}`**, so hovering ANY inline widget names its port. Both nodes' `description` strings now spell out Radius (how far) vs Half-angle° (how wide).
  - **New sample `Boids — Hemifield Vision`** ([scripts/gen-boids-hemifield.mjs](scripts/gen-boids-hemifield.mjs)) — a Braitenberg boids variant whose steering is 100% left/right agent COUNTS, and the multi-cone display demo (THREE FOV nodes, three `visionColor`s). Agents-only, 2D torus 120×120, 300 agents, `agentTarget: 'webgpu'` (gate accepts). Rule: `turn = KFAR·(Lfar−Rfar) − KNEAR·(Lnear−Rnear)` applied along the heading's LEFT normal `(-vy, vx)` (matching Sense Hemifield's own `cross = hx·dy − hy·dx ≥ 0 ⇒ Left` convention) — veer toward distant company (green, r16 ±55°), away from near crowding (red, r4.5 ±110°) — plus velocity matching over a `Get Agents In View` cone (cyan, r12 ±70°), a cruise-speed self-propulsion term, and a little wander (which also cold-starts the at-rest seed, where a zero heading makes every cone omnidirectional AND every turn force zero). **Soft-sphere collision is ON (`collision: 'soft'`, stiffness 0.4 ≤ the `clampAgentDt` budget) with NO bonds/springs/growth/adhesion** — the STEERING is pure hemifield; the body just stops agents sitting inside one another (without it, medianNN collapsed to ~1.0 with 15% of agents overlapping = visual mush). Carries `properties.instructions` pointing at `Show vision = All`.

**Verification.** tsc + `npm run build`; `check-compile-identity` 24/24 unchanged (the new model is reported NEW and ignored); `parity-agent-wasm` (all samples + synthetics, incl. the new model) ✓; `check-agent-wasm-gate` GATE✓COMPILE✓INST✓; `verify-agent-render` ✓. In-browser: the preset menu's five actions all drive real state (rename lands in the row, export downloads `<name>.gcapreset`, delete confirms, duplicate appends, overwrite confirms); the pill renders + opens with a correct em dash and never overlaps the ear; the Cone-color picker round-trips and its `⟳` restores the palette. **The sample is alive** — a `stepped`-driven worker run to generation 1600 (0 errors, 0 NaN, 300 alive): sustained motion (mean per-step displacement 0.12–0.18, never freezes), persistent clustering far above the random baseline (`clusterSq` 1810–3070 and 55–80 occupied bins of 144, vs ~925 / ~126 for a uniform scatter), NO overlap (median nearest-neighbour 2.0–3.1 against a 1.8 contact distance, 0% under 0.5), and a swarm that progressively COORDINATES (common-drift fraction 0.16 → 0.95 — milling clusters organising into a directed migrating stream). With `Show vision = All` the draw path emits exactly **900 arcs = 3 cones × 300 agents**, filled `rgba(80,200,255,.1)` / `rgba(255,100,100,.1)` / `rgba(120,232,120,.1)` — the three per-node `visionColor`s, NOT the automatic palette.

### FOLLOW-UP FIX — the cones rendered as full CIRCLES (the snapshot-contents invariant)
User-reported right after the batch: every cone drew as a circle. **Root cause: the render snapshot is SLIM — `snapshotAgentsForRender` gated `vx`/`vy` on `includeSprites`** (the P2 slim pass: "the sprite orientToVelocity heading is their only consumer"). The hemifield sample has no sprites ⇒ `snap.vx` was a **length-0** array ⇒ `snap.vx?.[id] ?? 0` ⇒ a zero heading for every agent ⇒ the cone code's own **legal** "still agent senses omnidirectionally" rule fired ⇒ a full circle, with no error anywhere.
- **Fix:** `snapshotAgentsForRender(store, includeSprites, includeVelocity)` ships vx/vy when **either** is set; the worker passes a new module flag `agentSnapshotVelocity`, set by a new **`setAgentSnapshotVelocity`** message that SimulatorView posts when `showVision` changes **and re-publishes in `initWorkerWithDimensions`** (a fresh worker defaults to OFF — the `setSimLayers` discipline). Default OFF ⇒ the payload stays byte-identical for every other model.
- **THE VERIFICATION LESSON (this is why the batch's check passed a broken feature):** the original test counted `ctx.arc` CALLS — but **both** branches call `arc` once per agent (cone = `moveTo` + `arc(…, a−half, a+half)`, circle = `arc(…, 0, 2π)`), so "900 arcs = 3 cones × 300 agents" was equally consistent with 900 circles. **Only the arc's SWEEP distinguishes them.** The re-verification spies on the arc ARGUMENTS: post-fix the histogram is `{110°: 300, 140°: 300, 220°: 300}` (= 2×the three half-angles 55/70/110) with **zero 360° entries**, and `snap.vx.length === 300`. Negative control (post `setAgentSnapshotVelocity {on:false}` and re-measure): `vx.length === 0` and **all 900 arcs are 360°** — the exact reported symptom, reproduced on demand.
### FOLLOW-UP ROUND 2 — cone lifecycle, the left/right midline, and multi/draggable agent inspectors
- **The EDIT-target cone now follows the Edit brush.** The `inspected` scope added `editTargetIdRef` unconditionally, so after switching away from Edit the cone lingered on that agent while its dashed highlight (correctly gated on `brushTarget==='agents' && mode==='edit' && scope==='single'` in `drawCursorLayer`) had already vanished. The cone now uses the SAME three-term condition, so highlight and cone appear/disappear together; the target itself is preserved, so returning to Edit restores both. Verified in-app: Edit → 3 cones (one per FOV node), switch to Add → **0**, back to Edit → 3.
- **Dotted centre line.** Each wedge draws a faint dashed bisector along the heading (skipped for an omnidirectional cone, which has no left/right split) so the halves Sense Hemifield counts are readable. Verified: 900 dashed strokes = one per cone (3 nodes × 300 agents).
- **Agent inspectors are now MULTI + DRAGGABLE + Close all** — the agent-layer twin of the cell inspect popovers, in a new [InspectAgentPopover.tsx](src/simulator/InspectAgentPopover.tsx) (reuses `InspectCellPopover.module.css`; header-drag with viewport clamp, Esc-closes-the-focused-one, Close all). `agentInspect`/`agentState` (one popover, one state) became **`agentPopovers[]` (pinned) + `agentSweepPopover` (transient) + an id-keyed `agentStatesRef` map**, with ONE ~3 Hz poll covering every open id. Dimension- and target-agnostic by construction: both the 2D and 3D pick paths feed the same state, and the data comes from `getAgentState`, which the worker answers identically on JS/WASM/WebGPU (and which already joins the one-shot GPU staleness readers). The 3D inspect RINGS are now derived from every open popover (`syncInspectAgents3dRef`), so all pinned agents stay ringed — and an agent-move no longer wipes the rings.
  - **Fast-click bug found + fixed by that work:** `commitAgentSweep` read the sweep from a ref MIRRORED AT RENDER, but a real click fires `mousedown`+`mouseup` inside one frame — before any re-render — so the commit saw `null` and **silently pinned nothing**. The ref is now assigned SYNCHRONOUSLY in `openAgentInspector`/`clear`/`commit` (it LEADS the state instead of mirroring it). This is the general trap: **a pointer handler that reads state written by an earlier handler in the same gesture must read a synchronously-written ref, never a render-time mirror.** Verified with zero-delay clicks: 3 popovers pin (#5/#60/#140), Close all clears them; header-drag moves a popover by exactly the drag delta.
- **Guarded permanently** by the **B12** block in [scripts/verify-agent-render.mjs](scripts/verify-agent-render.mjs) (6 source invariants: the engine flag + the `includeSprites || includeVelocity` rule + the worker's pass-through + the message handler + the toggle post + the reinit re-publish). **Generalise the rule: a render-snapshot consumer must REQUEST every field it reads — an un-requested field is a length-0 array that silently reads as 0, not an error.**

## Quick-wins batch (branch `improvements`, 2026-07-27)

Nine small independent features/UX items from the TODO triage. All additive; the compiler changes are byte-identity-proven (`check-compile-identity` 25/25 unchanged) and the agent parity harness stays green.

- **Math node floor / ceil / round ops (all 5 emit surfaces)** — added to `arithmeticOperator` on JS cell ([ArithmeticOperatorNode.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/ArithmeticOperatorNode.ts)), WASM cell, WebGPU cell, agentWasm, agentWebgpu (+ the CaNode op dropdown and `ARITHMETIC_UNARY_OPS`, which `hiddenPorts` + the collapsed label consume). **The parity rule: `round` emits `floor(x + 0.5)` on EVERY target** — never JS `Math.round` / WASM `f64.nearest` / WGSL `round()` (banker's rounding diverges on .5 cases) — the SAME convention the Expression node's `round()` already used (its emitters were the reference; no Expression change was needed, `floor/ceil/round` were already there). floor/ceil use native intrinsics (WASM opcodes 0x9C/0x9B — no new host imports; `OP_F64_CEIL` added to the wasm + agentWasm import lists). No `trunc` — the op set deliberately mirrors the Expression function set. Verified by [scripts/test-math-int-ops.mjs](scripts/test-math-int-ops.mjs): JS + a REAL instantiated WASM module in Node produce the hand-computed values (incl. round(2.5)=3, round(−2.5)=−2) bit-identically; WGSL emit checked for `floor(`/`ceil(`/the `floor(x + 0.5)` form and the ABSENCE of `round(`.
- **Lookup-table random-fill Min for Integer/Tag values** — `tableRoll.min?` ([types.ts](src/model/types.ts)) + `TableFillPolicy.intMin?` ([variegation.ts](src/modeler/vpl/compiler/variegation.ts) `randomFillTableData`): integer/tag entries draw uniform over `[intMin, valueCount]`; **absent (or 1) ⇒ bit-identical to the historical `1 + floor(next()*count)` draw** (same single RNG advance, proven in the extended [scripts/test-ndtable.mjs](scripts/test-ndtable.mjs) — old seeds keep reproducing their tables). With a min the raw (unclamped) max is used, so negative integer ranges (−5..−2) work; degenerate min > max collapses to min; tag min is an option INDEX (0 admits the "empty" option 0 into rolls). Editor: a Min `NumberField` in [LookupTableEditor.tsx](src/modeler/panels/LookupTableEditor.tsx)'s Randomize block for integer AND tag (integer Max lost its ≥1 clamp); the roll stores `min` (integer + tag). The Overseer `ovRandomizeTable` dep in SimulatorView passes `intMin: attr.tableRoll?.min` so a runtime re-roll reproduces the editor's policy.
- **Set Agent Sprite `setAlpha` facet** ([SetAgentSpriteNode.ts](src/modeler/vpl/nodes/SetAgentSpriteNode.ts) + the CaNode checkbox): writes the agent COLOUR's alpha byte `colors[_t*4+3]` (Uint8ClampedArray clamps) — the same alpha the sprite render already multiplies by, so it fades/hides sprites per agent with zero new SoA/ABI; a later colour pass (agent OM) overrides it, documented in the tooltip/Help. JS-engine write like the other facets (no WASM/WebGPU emit — the established Set Agent Sprite posture).
- **Browser back exits the macro view** ([GraphEditor.tsx](src/modeler/vpl/GraphEditor.tsx)): one same-document `history.pushState` entry per macro level, RECONCILED from `currentScope` in an effect (enter pushes; a UI exit — breadcrumb/Undo Macro — consumes entries via one `history.go(delta)` with a `suppressPopRef` token so its popstate doesn't double-exit); the `popstate` listener pops exactly one level when inside a macro (decrementing `historyDepthRef` BEFORE the scope change so the reconcile effect no-ops) and no-ops at root (normal history behaviour). Orphaned entries after an unmount mid-macro are harmless (same-document state pops the app ignores); a remount re-pushes for the restored scope.
- **Renameable reroute labels** ([RerouteNodeComponent.tsx](src/modeler/vpl/RerouteNodeComponent.tsx)): the node context menu's Rename already worked for reroutes (`data.label`, wholesale-serialized) — the label just wasn't RENDERED. Now a small muted `.label` pinned above the dot (pointer-events none; `.reroute` gained `position: relative`), shown when non-empty; the tooltip carries it too. **A NEW reroute defaults its label to the relayed PORT's name** (`defaultRerouteLabel` in [GraphEditor.tsx](src/modeler/vpl/GraphEditor.tsx), applied by both creation paths via `makeRerouteNode`'s optional label param): a reroute of a reroute inherits THAT reroute's (possibly user-renamed) label, and an UNNAMED upstream reroute is walked past to the original port (cycle-guarded). Verified in-app: `Get Cell Attribute.value` → reroute labeled "Value", chained reroute inherits it.
- **Collapsible Properties sections** ([PropertiesPanelContent.tsx](src/modeler/panels/PropertiesPanelContent.tsx) `CollapsibleSection` + `.sectionTitleCollapsible`/`.sectionChevron` in PanelContent.module.css): Structure / Execution / Indicators / Variegated collapse via their title row (chevron). Collapsed bodies stay **MOUNTED** (`display: none`) — the Indicators list is a controlled master-detail child whose selection/effects must not reset. Persisted as a string[] in `localStorage['genesisca_properties_collapsed']` keyed by stable ids. `IndicatorsPanelSection` gained a `hideTitle` prop (the wrapper renders the title; `bare` variant avoids double `.section` chrome).
- **FPS / Gens-per-Frame popup sliders** ([SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx) + `.speedPopup*` css): the inline transport sliders became compact readout BUTTONS ("FPS 60" / "G/F 1") opening a small popover with a VERTICAL slider (a rotated horizontal range in a fixed wrap — robust vs writing-mode/orient) + the value + the ∞ checkbox. **Opens on HOVER of the wrapper too** (pointerenter/pointerleave; the popover sits FLUSH above the trigger — `bottom: 100%`, NO gap, because enter/leave are DOM-tree based and a visual gap is a hit-test hole that fires pointerleave crossing into the popover) and click still toggles. **Interacting with the slider while ∞ is ticked UNTICKS it** (the slider is never disabled — grabbing it means "I want this value"). One popover at a time (`speedPopup` state); dismissed by leave, capture-phase outside pointerdown, or Escape (capture + stopPropagation so Esc doesn't fire the simulator's reset). Everything carries `data-sim-overlay`. Value semantics/persistence unchanged; the orphaned `.transportSpeedLabel`/`.transportSlider` css was removed.
- **2D axes indicator** (SimulatorView `show2dAxes`, persisted in `genesisca_sim_settings`; a `⊾` toggle next to gridlines, 2D only): draws the grid ORIGIN (cell 0,0) dot + growth axes in `draw()`'s 2D branch — columns red → right, rows green → down — **matching the 3D axes convention**, primary tile only in infinity mode. Each axis spans the **FULL grid edge** with an arrowhead and a `C (n)` / `R (n)` label carrying the dimension count (dark-halo text, tip-anchored INSIDE the grid so it stays on-screen), and the block sits **AFTER the agent drawing** (drawAgentsOverlay / the A1 direct-render blit / the E2 composite blit) so nothing covers it. The state's sync effect also calls `draw()` (the onClick's own draw runs before the ref sync — the toggle must land while paused).
- **3D axes follow-ups** (feedback round): (1) **agent-sphere FREE mode never drew the scene wireframes** — `overlaysOnly` skipped `renderOverlays` unconditionally, but only the L1 VOXEL free mode has a worker line pass drawing them; the agent-sphere free mode (Particle Life 3D) lost axes/grid/bounds entirely and they "appeared only on interaction" (the frame-mode pin). Fix: `setOverlaysOnly(on, wireframesExternal)` ([gl3d.ts](src/simulator/render/gl3d.ts)) — gl3d keeps drawing the wireframes in overlays-only mode unless the worker owns them; `draw()` passes `wireframesExternal = voxelFree` only. Structurally verified: agent-free draws, voxel-free skips, frame draws (2× — colour + the agentsInFront depth-restore). (2) **Axis extension + arrowhead now SCALE with the grid** (`ext = 1.2 + maxDim*0.02`, `hl = 0.7 + maxDim*0.025`) — the fixed 0.7-cell arrowheads were invisible at 300³. Mirrored byte-identically in gl3d `renderOverlays`, `renderAxisLabels`' ext, AND the worker's `buildVoxelOverlayVerts` ([webgpuRuntime.ts](src/simulator/engine/webgpuRuntime.ts)) — the free/frame flip must not move the axes. (3) **The 3D axis tip labels now carry the C/R/D LETTER + the count** (`C 300`): `pushDigitsNdc` renders GLYPHS letter polylines alongside the 7-segment digits.
- **Compile-target chip** (SimulatorView stats overlay): shows the RESOLVED targets — grid (`WebGPU`/`WASM`/`JS` from properties) + `agents …` (via `agentTargetOf` + the two agent gates, **memoised on [model]** — the gates flatten the agent graph, too costly per stepped re-render); agents-only models hide the grid part. Honesty arm: the `useWebGPUStatus` handler records `ready:false` while WebGPU is enabled → the chip turns amber `WebGPU✗` (device/init failure fallback).
- Docs synced: HelpView (Math ops, Randomize Min, sprite alpha facet, transport popovers, axes+chip, reroute rename, macro back), README (transport line), NODES_REFERENCE (Math row #36 + Set Agent Sprite row #115, which also caught up on the rotation/scale facets).
- NOT done from the quick-wins list: "make app open full-screen" — deferred pending clarification (browser fullscreen is user-gesture-gated; PWA/Tauri window config is a different feature).

## Medium-features batch (branch `improvements`, 2026-07-28 — five features, one commit each)

The "handoff" batch (the spawned agent hit the session limit, so it was implemented inline). All additive; harnesses green per feature.

- **Simulator Instructions** (`59f12f1`): `ModelProperties.instructions?` (additive) — a multiline "Simulator Instructions" textarea in the Info panel; when non-empty the Simulator shows an **ⓘ Instructions** pill (top-left, `left: 36px` clear of the panel-expand ear) opening a dismissible `pre-wrap` card (`.instructionsBtn`/`.instructionsPopover` in SimulatorView.module.css; capture-phase outside-pointerdown + Esc-with-stopPropagation dismissal; closes on model load, keyed on `properties.name`). The standalone viewer's About panel renders it too. The pill+card share a `display: contents` wrapper ref so the outside-dismiss treats them as one region.
- **Preset export/import `.gcapreset`** (`4351101`): [fileOperations.ts](src/model/fileOperations.ts) `PresetFile` (`{schemaVersion: 1, name, description?, preset}`) + `downloadPresetFile`/`readPresetFile` (Tauri Save-As via saveTextFile; import regenerates the preset id, normalizes embedded-state depth like readStateFile, accepts a bare Preset object). UI: a `⤓` export button per preset row + an "Import Preset…" button in the simulator Presets block. The preset object travels VERBATIM so the documented load semantics (grid-carrying = dims-authoritative, parameter-only = grid-less) are preserved exactly.
- **Drag-and-drop onto the app** (`14a6495`): window-level dragenter/dragover/dragleave/drop in [App.tsx](src/App.tsx) (preventDefault so the browser never navigates; a counter-based `dragActive` full-window overlay). Routing by extension: `.gcaproj`/`.json`/`.html(.htm)` → the FileMenu/PWA-file-handler load flow (readModelFile + the `pendingLibLoad` unsaved-changes ConfirmDialog + `afterLoad`); `.gcastate` → a "Replace the simulation state?" ConfirmDialog then the transport-bar Load State path via a **`genesis-load-state-file`** CustomEvent; `.gcapreset` → **`genesis-import-preset-file`**; images → **`genesis-open-image-file`** (the Ctrl+V clipboard seam into the Map Image to Cells dialog). SimulatorView consumes the three events in one latest-ref effect. `handleDroppedFile` goes through a latest-ref so the once-registered listeners never act on stale closures.
- **Agent copy/paste** (`71a4501`, 2D — 3D is a follow-up like the cell clipboard): Ctrl+C/X with the AGENT brush target collects the footprint ids (`agentsInShapeAt`, hovered-agent fallback) and batch-reads their FRESH spec via a new **`readAgents`** worker message (which JOINS the one-shot staleness READERS beside getAgentState — a free-mode copy is never stale); the `agentsRead` reply stashes relative-to-anchor specs (position/radius/velocity/attr values) in `agentClipboardRef`; cut kills the sources only after the read captured them. Ctrl+V posts a new **`pasteAgents`** message with PER-AGENT specs (unlike seedAgents' shared sets), composed from `allocAgentSlot`/`initAgentSlot`/`applyAgentSets` (the compiled-fn ABI + SoA layout untouched), torus-wrapped, `agentOverflow` on capacity. `pasteAgents` is in `AGENT_GPU_DEFER_TYPES` (deferral + residency re-upload for free). Verified through the real worker: bit-exact velocities/positions; per-agent attr sets proven with an inverted-value paste on GoL-on-agents.
- **Hemifield / vision-cone display** (`afe3d02`): a **Show vision** selector (Off / Inspected agent / All, persisted as `showVision` in `genesisca_sim_settings`) in the 2D agent controls, rendered only when the agent graph (top-level + macro internals) contains an FOV node. The `visionCones` `useMemo([model])` extracts per-node halfAngle/radius (inline `_port_radius` when unwired, else the `neighbourQueryRadius` fallback — a wired radius isn't knowable at render time). draw() renders translucent wedges (velocity heading; zero heading or halfAngle ≥ 180° → full circle, matching the nodes' omni rule; Facing/Wired heading sources approximated by velocity — documented; All capped at 1500; one tint per node). **`updateAgentUiSync` gained a want-term while not Off** (the cones read the agent snapshot, which free-running direct-render models don't ship).

## Library compile-target policy: WebGPU where supported, else WASM (2026-07-27)

Every shipped library model now sets its compile target(s) by ONE rule — **WebGPU wherever the compile gates accept it, else WASM** — applied to BOTH the grid target (`useWebGPU`/`useWasm`) and the agent target (`centerBased.agentTarget`). Surveyed + applied mechanically (a throwaway script compiled every model with each target forced and read the gates); the matching **gen-\* scripts were updated in lockstep** so a re-run doesn't revert (gen-ant-necrophoresis / -chemotaxis / -boids / -gol-agents / -particle-life / -tissue / -tissue3d gained `agentTarget: 'webgpu'` and/or `useWebGPU: true`; gen-life3d + gen-overseer-stats flipped to WebGPU). Resulting state:
- **Grid = WebGPU** on every synchronous grid model (Accretor, Coagulation, Elementary CA 1D, Extended Wireworld, GoL, Golly-style, GoL Replicate Statistics, Gray-Scott, Kelp War, Life3D, MNCA, Chemotaxis). **Grid = WASM** on the four ASYNC models (Amphiphile, Chromatography, gas_particles, snake — WebGPU rejects async by fundamentals). Agents-only models keep their (inert) grid flags untouched.
- **Agents = WebGPU** on the agent samples (Boids, GoL-on-agents, Chemotaxis, the three Tissues, both Particle Lifes). **Ant Necrophoresis is the documented EXCEPTION on BOTH layers — WASM grid + WASM agents** (see its section below): its discrete pick-up/drop is only exactly mass-conserving under the SEQUENTIAL agent loop, which the parallel GPU dispatch cannot give; and with an empty cell rule a WebGPU grid would only add per-gen field-bridge round-trips. NB the Tissues are bonded/dividing ⇒ NOT residency-eligible ⇒ the per-gen GPU path with its upload/readback — correct but possibly slower than JS at their small populations (the documented posture; the Properties hint says so). Ant + Chemotaxis with BOTH layers on WebGPU engage the **E1b GPU field bridge** (verified live post-change: `gpuBridge` counting, 0 CPU fallback, shared device refCount 2). Life3D on WebGPU now also gets the **L1 voxel free-mode render** (verified: `voxelRenderStatus active:true`).
- **`Agent WASM Drift Test` was REMOVED from the library** (user call: useless as a default model). Its gen script stays ([scripts/gen-agent-wasm-drift.mjs](scripts/gen-agent-wasm-drift.mjs)); the parity/gate harnesses glob `public/models` so they adapt automatically, and the deterministic-parity coverage lives in the harness's synthetics. README/Help sample lists updated (nine agent samples).
- Older per-model target statements earlier in this file (e.g. Life3D "flipped to useWasm:true", Chemotaxis "WASM field + JS agents", Particle Life `agentTarget: 'wasm'`) are HISTORICAL narration — this policy supersedes them.

## Direct-render resize re-attach: keep the OLD canvas until the ack commits (the panel-resize frozen-frame fix, 2026-07-27)

**The bug (user-reported on Particle Life + Accretor — i.e. every direct-render family):** resizing a simulator side panel (or the window) changes the display size, which forces a canvas RE-ATTACH on all four worker-presented render paths (2D A1 agent render, E2 composite, 3D agent spheres, L1 voxels — a transferred OffscreenCanvas has fixed dims). The old code flipped `*ActiveRef = false` + dropped the canvas ref immediately, so for the whole handshake (transfer → worker setup → ack → commit) the display fell back to the CPU paths — **whose data free mode deliberately let go STALE** (no snapshots / no colours cross the wire), showing a frozen, badly outdated frame. Both UI-sync drivers **early-return while the render is inactive**, so nothing could even request fresh data during the gap; a slow/deferred/failed attach froze the display indefinitely while the sim kept running.

**The fix ([SimulatorView.tsx](src/simulator/SimulatorView.tsx)) — two rules:**
1. **Keep the OLD canvas through the gap.** The worker keeps presenting into the old OffscreenCanvas until the new attach lands worker-side, so the old canvas is LIVE — the resize blocks now call `maybeAttachAgentCanvas(true)` / `maybeAttachVoxelCanvas(true)` (a new `reattach` param that bypasses the is-active guard) WITHOUT flipping the active refs or dropping the canvas: 2D keeps blitting it (briefly mis-sized), 3D keeps compositing it (CSS-stretched). The attach functions therefore NO LONGER remove the prior DOM canvas up front — the **ack COMMIT removes it** (both `agentRenderStatus` 3D and `voxelRenderStatus` commit branches). Dead-canvas paths (runtime rebuild via `agentRuntimeReady`, WebGPU death via `useWebGPUStatus ready:false` — which also now drops the kept voxel canvas) still remove explicitly.
2. **A FAILED attach forces the sync ON.** New `forceAgentUiSyncOn` / `forceGridUiSyncOn` helpers (mirror assigned in the same statement as the post — the B11 mirror invariant; the harness census in [scripts/verify-agent-render.mjs](scripts/verify-agent-render.mjs) sanctions them) are called from the failure ack branches, so a permanent CPU fallback receives fresh snapshots/colours (the worker's OFF→ON handlers ship one immediately) instead of freezing on the ancient frame. Since the normal drivers early-return while the render is inactive, the one-shot ON sticks — which is exactly right for a dead render path.

**Verified live** (real worker + GPU, occluded pane → protocol/structural evidence): Particle Life (A2) — a viewport resize triggers `attachAgentCanvas` → `ack:true`, and an arc-spy shows ~7 arcs total across the gap (cursor ring) instead of the ~1800/frame a stale CPU-overlay fallback would draw; Accretor (L1 voxel, 300³ WebGPU) — resize → `attachVoxelCanvas` → `ack:true`, the voxel layer ends at exactly 1 canvas (commit removed the old), and `stepped` still ships NO colours (stayed in free mode — no spurious readback). Zero console/GPU errors. tsc + `verify-agent-render` + `verify-render-uniform-layouts` green.
